Symmetries and Higher-Form Connections in Derived Differential Geometry


Abstract
We introduce a general definition of higher-form connections on principal ∞ \infty -bundles in differential geometry. This is achieved by developing the formal differentiation and integration of maps from smooth manifolds to derived stacks with sufficient deformation theory. That allows us to introduce the Atiyah L ∞ L_{\infty} -algebroid of a principal ∞ \infty -bundle and establish its global sections as the L ∞ L_{\infty} -algebra of the derived higher symmetry group of the bundle. We define the space of p p -form connections on an ∞ \infty -bundle as the space of order p p splittings of its Atiyah L ∞ L_{\infty} -algebroid. We demonstrate that our new concept of derived geometric p p -form connections recovers the known notion of connections on higher U ⁡ ( 1 ) {\mathrm{U}}(1) -bundles defined via Čech–Deligne differential cocycles. We further relate the L ∞ L_{\infty} -algebras of derived higher symmetries of higher U ⁡ ( 1 ) {\mathrm{U}}(1) -bundles and higher Courant algebroids. Some applications in higher gauge theory and in supergravity are mentioned.

Contents

1
Introduction

1.1
Towards higher-form connections

Connections on principal bundles form a cornerstone of differential geometry and have been adopted in many other areas.
They describe, for instance, geodesics and curvature in Riemannian and Lorentzian geometry, gauge fields in physics, the path signature in data science, and Chern–Weil characteristic classes in differential topology, among many further applications.
If
P\longrightarrow M
is a principal bundle, a connection on
P
essentially describes how to compare the fibres
P_{|x}
and
P_{|y}
at the start and end point of any smooth path
\gamma
in
M
.
More precisely, a connection describes this only for infinitesimally short paths—i.e. on tangent vectors—and these data can then be integrated to the desired comparison along all paths.

Additional data is necessary when the fibres of
P
have higher internal structure.
As a first example, suppose that
P
is a bundle whose fibres are groupoids (which may be topological or smooth in some sense).
Then the comparison map
{\mathrm{PT}}_{\gamma}\colon P_{|x}\longrightarrow P_{|y}
associated to
\gamma
is a functor, and we are free to specify, for each homotopy
h\colon\gamma\longrightarrow\gamma^{\prime}
of paths with fixed endpoints, a natural isomorphism
{\mathrm{PT}}_{h}\colon{\mathrm{PT}}_{\gamma}\longrightarrow{\mathrm{PT}}_{\gamma^{\prime}}
as part of the connection data.
Infinitesimally, we now need to specify the action of the connection not only on tangent vectors (which yields
{\mathrm{PT}}_{\gamma}
), but also on pairs of tangent vectors (which yields
{\mathrm{PT}}_{h}
).
Geometrically, a classical
1
-form connection on a principal bundle
P
consists of locally defined
1
-forms valued in the Lie algebra of the structure group of
P
.
In contrast, a 2-form connection on a categorified principal bundle
P
, whose fibres are groupoids, consists of locally defined 1-forms and 2-forms taking values in the Lie 2-algebra of the structure 2-group of
P
.

Connections on bundles whose fibres are smooth spaces with higher internal structure are by no means exotic, but appear in many instances in mathematics and its applications in physics, as well as increasingly other areas.
Examples include (differential) string structures in the study of topological modular forms, positive scalar curvature manifolds
[
83
,
5
]
and supergravity
[
86
]
, conjectural higher-dimensional versions of the path signature in data science
[
52
,
56
,
9
]
, higher gauge theory
[
13
,
8
]
, and in gauging higher-form symmetries in quantum field theory
[
44
,
79
,
4
,
80
]
.

In their most general form, spaces which combine smoothness and higher internal structure form the objects of an
\infty
-category
{\mathscr{X}}
.
Usually
{\mathscr{X}}
has particularly nice properties making it into an
\infty
-topos.
As a first example, one could take
{\mathscr{X}}={\mathscr{S}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{M}\hskip-0.56917pt\mathrm{fd}})
to be the
\infty
-category of sheaves of
\infty
-groupoids on the site of manifolds and open coverings.
This is sufficient for a range of questions whose answers can be found in higher, but non-derived, geometry.
In particular, in any
\infty
-topos
{\mathscr{X}}
we can find, for any group object
G
, a classifying object
{\mathrm{B}}G\in{\mathscr{X}}
.
The
\infty
-groupoid of principal
\infty
-bundles on an object
X\in{\mathscr{X}}
with structure group
G
is then given by the mapping space
[
66
,
20
,
21
]

{\mathscr{B}\hskip-0.56917pt\mathrm{un}}(X;G)={\mathscr{X}}(X,{\mathrm{B}}G)\ .

(1.1)

Given that a good theory of principal bundles exists at this level of generality, we arrive at the following very natural and mathematically pressing question:

How can one define connections on principal
\infty
-bundles?

Despite its broad range of applications—including but not limited to the examples mentioned above—no general theory of connections on principal
\infty
-bundles exists to date.
Various partial answers and definitions of such higher connections have been given in the literature, for instance in
[
13
,
52
,
84
,
81
,
39
,
1
,
15
,
53
,
94
,
11
,
23
]
.
However, each of these setups make restrictive assumptions on the bundles or their structure groups, or only describe flat higher-form connections (i.e.
\infty
-local systems).

Our approach to the definition of higher connections on a bundle is based on the relationship with the infinitesimal symmetries of the bundle.
To better illustrate our approach and results, we begin by briefly recalling this perspective in the case of classical principal bundles.

Symmetries and connections from Atiyah algebroids.

Let
G
be a connected Lie group and
P\longrightarrow M
a principal
G
-bundle over a manifold
M
. A
symmetry of the pair
(P,M)
is a diffeomorphism
\phi\colon M\longrightarrow M
together with an isomorphism of principal bundles
\psi\colon P\longrightarrow\phi^{*}P
.
This defines an infinite-dimensional Lie group
{\mathsf{Sym}}(P)
of symmetries of
P
, which is part of a short exact sequence of Lie groups

\displaystyle 1\longrightarrow\mathsf{Aut}(P)\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Sym}}(P)\longrightarrow\mathsf{Diff}(M)\longrightarrow 1\ ,

(1.2)

where
\mathsf{Aut}(P)
is the group of automorphisms, or gauge transformations, of
P
and
\mathsf{Diff}(M)
is the group of diffeomorphisms of
M
(see for example
[
10
, Section 3]
for more details).

Differentiating at the identity leads to a short exact sequence of Lie algebras

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow{\mathrm{Ad}}(P)\longrightarrow{\mathrm{At}}(P)\overset{\rho}{\longrightarrow}TM\longrightarrow 0\ ,

(1.3)

where
TM
denotes the Lie algebra of vector fields on
M
,
{\mathrm{Ad}}(P)
is the Lie algebra of sections of the adjoint bundle of
P
, and
{\mathrm{At}}(P)
is the Lie algebra of
G
-invariant vector fields on
P
(or equivalently sections of
TP/G
). The exact sequence (
1.3
) controls infinitesimal symmetries of
P
. A crucial observation is that this sequence is
local
on
M
, in the sense that it is even a short exact sequence of
Lie algebroids
on
M
.
The Lie algebroid
{\mathrm{At}}(P)
is called the
Atiyah algebroid
of
P

[
3
]
.

The set of connections on
P
can then be expressed as
[
52
]

{\mathrm{Con}}(P)={\mathscr{L}\hskip-0.85355pt\mathrm{ie}}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{M}\big(Q^{{(1)}}TM,\,{\mathrm{At}}(P)\big)\ ,

(1.4)

the set of maps from the free Lie algebroid on the tangent bundle
TM
to the Atiyah algebroid
{\mathrm{At}}(P)
.
This is the same as vector bundle morphisms
TM\longrightarrow{\mathrm{fgt}}{\mathrm{At}}(P)
, where we forget the Lie algebroid structure on
{\mathrm{At}}(P)
.
A connection
A\colon Q^{{(1)}}TM\longrightarrow{\mathrm{At}}(P)
is flat precisely if
A
respects the brackets, i.e. it even induces a map of Lie algebroids
TM\longrightarrow{\mathrm{At}}(P)
.

In the present paper we develop a theory of higher-form connections on higher bundles from first principles, taking as its foundations this perspective on classical connections.
To every higher principal bundle we assign an
Atiyah
L_{\infty}
-algebroid
encoding its infinitesimal symmetries and define connections in terms of certain partial splittings, as we explain in more detail below.

1.2
Summary of main results

Heuristically, a
p
-form connection on a principal
\infty
-bundle on a manifold
M
should define parallel transports along infinitesimal paths, their homotopies, homotopies of these homotopies, and so on up to dimension
p
.
That is, such a
p
-form connection on a bundle
x\colon M\longrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}G
can be understood as a map from an order
p
truncation of the infinitesimal path
\infty
-groupoid of
M
to an infinitesimal version of the
\infty
-groupoid which describes equivalences between the fibres of
x
.
In this paper we make this picture rigorous and justify that it yields the correct notion of connections.
For
p=1
it matches the classical case described in Section
1.1
above:
the free Lie algebroid
Q^{{(1)}}TM
can be seen as the
1
-truncation of the infinitesimal path groupoid of
M
, and the Atiyah algebroid
{\mathrm{At}}(P)
is exactly the object which describes infinitesimal equivalences between fibres of
P
.
The main results of this paper can be summarised as follows.

Derived differential geometry and Atiyah
\boldsymbol{L_{\infty}}
-algebroids.

The first part of this paper develops a theory which allows us to form and compute the analogue of the Atiyah algebroid for a principal
\infty
-bundle
x\colon M\longrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}G
in maximum generality.
We achieve this by passing to a derived differential geometric setting: using differential graded (dg)
C^{\infty}
-rings we set up a theory of
\infty
-sheaves on the
\infty
-category
{\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}}
of
inf-manifolds
, a version of derived infinitesimally thickened manifolds we introduce, with the main aim of obtaining geometric objects which have a good deformation theory.

To that end, given a map
x\colon M\longrightarrow X
from a smooth manifold to a suitable
\infty
-sheaf
X
, we first construct the tangent complex
T_{x}X
on
M
.
We do this in a way that avoids using the cotangent complex and thus having to dualise (Definition
2.76
).
In the case where
X
satisfies a certain Schlessinger condition and admits a well-behaved tangent complex for all maps from smooth manifolds (Definitions
2.68
and
2.82
), we say that
X

has deformation theory
, or is a
dt stack
.
Our first main result is Theorem
3.2
, which is the key to forming Atiyah algebroids of higher bundles. A slightly reduced version of Theorem
3.2
can be stated as

Theorem 1.5

(Theorem
3.2
)

Let
M
be a smooth manifold and let
x\colon M\longrightarrow X
be a map to a stack with deformation theory.
The relative tangent complex

T(M/X)\coloneqq{\mathrm{hofib}}(TM\longrightarrow T_{x}X)\ ,

which is a priori a dg
C^{\infty}(M)
-module, has a natural lift to a complete
L_{\infty}
-algebroid on
M
.
This determines a right adjoint functor of
\infty
-categories

T(M/-)\colon\mathrm{St}^{\mathrm{dt}}_{M/}\longrightarrow L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{M}^{{\mathrm{cpl}},\infty}\ ,

from stacks with deformation theory under
M
to complete
L_{\infty}
-algebroids on
M
.

In Theorem
3.2
we also show that the left adjoint of
T(M/-)
is fully faithful and identify its essential image, as well as the global functions on any
\infty
-sheaf in this essential image.
We then introduce

Definition 1.6

Given a smooth manifold
M
and a map
x\colon M\longrightarrow X
to a stack with deformation theory, the
L_{\infty}
-algebroid
T(M/X)
is the
Atiyah
L_{\infty}
-algebroid
of
x
.

Along the way we develop several important results about
\infty
-sheaves on
{\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}}
, including formal versions of the Inverse Function and Submersion Theorems (Propositions
2.87
and
2.102
, respectively), as well as formally smooth maps (Definition
2.99
), formally smooth
\infty
-groupoids (Definition
2.104
) and their deformation theories.

Moduli of geometric structures on manifolds, globally.

Theorem
1.5
contains, in particular, a classification of formal moduli problems over a manifold
M
in terms of
L_{\infty}
-algebroids on
M
.
Indeed, if we view a dt stack
X
as a moduli space of geometric data, then a map
x\colon M\longrightarrow X
is an
M
-point in this moduli space.
By the internal hom adjunction, the same data is encoded by a map
*\longrightarrow{\operatorname{Hom}}(M,X)
, which is a single point in the moduli stack of
X
-structures on
M
.
However, we need to remember that some of the deformations of the map
x\colon M\longrightarrow X
come purely from permuting points in
M
by infinitesimal diffeomorphisms.
In this global picture that is manifested by the canonical action of the diffeomorphism group
\mathsf{Diff}(M)
on
{\operatorname{Hom}}(M,X)
.
We show that the moduli problem defined by the induced morphism

x^{\dashv}\colon*\longrightarrow{\operatorname{Hom}}(M,X)\,\big/\,\mathsf{Diff}(M)

to the quotient is the correct global version of the moduli problem associated to
x\colon M\longrightarrow X
. Again a reduced version of our actual result can be stated as

Theorem 1.7

(Theorem
3.14
)

Let
x\colon M\longrightarrow X
be a map from a smooth manifold to a stack with deformation theory.

(1)

The quotient
{\operatorname{Hom}}(M,X)/\mathsf{Diff}(M)
is a stack with deformation theory.

(2)

The moduli problem associated to
x^{\dashv}\colon*\longrightarrow{\operatorname{Hom}}(M,X)/\mathsf{Diff}(M)
is controlled by an
L_{\infty}
-algebra with a canonical map to the Lie algebra of vector fields on
M
.
It is equivalent over this Lie algebra to the global sections of the Atiyah
L_{\infty}
-algebroid
T(M/X)
of
x
, which controls the local moduli problem (see Theorem
1.5
).

Theorem
1.7
has the following consequence, which provides further justification to view
T(M/X)
as a derived generalisation of the classical Atiyah Lie algebroid:
note that the loop stack of
x^{\dashv}\colon*\longrightarrow{\operatorname{Hom}}(M,X)/\mathsf{Diff}(M)
agrees with the group stack
{\mathsf{Sym}}(x)
of derived higher symmetries of
x
.
Theorem
1.7
then implies, in particular, that the
L_{\infty}
-algebra of
{\mathsf{Sym}}(x)
consists precisely of the global sections of the Atiyah
L_{\infty}
-algebroid and comes with an appropriate morphism to the vector fields on
M
.
As in the classical case, we thus observe that the finite symmetries of
x
are global objects on
M
, whereas upon passing to infinitesimal symmetries they enhance to local objects, amounting to the enhancement of structure from an
L_{\infty}
-algebra to an
L_{\infty}
-algebroid.
It is this enhancement of structure of infinitesimal symmetries which allows us to define higher-form connections.

So far little is known about how to explicitly compute the
L_{\infty}
-algebra associated to a formal moduli problem.
Only passing in the opposite direction is computationally understood and explicit.
Here we make partial progress on this general problem by computing the induced brackets on the cohomology
\mathrm{H}^{\bullet}(T(*/Y))
in all degrees via an intricate interaction between deformation theory and Whitehead brackets (Proposition
3.30
), where
y\colon*\longrightarrow Y
is a point of a stack with deformation theory (in particular, we may take
Y={\operatorname{Hom}}(M,X)/\mathsf{Diff}(M)
).
This is likely to be of wider interest.

Higher-form connections and their curvatures.

Now that we have a general notion of the Atiyah algebroid in place, the next step in making precise the heuristic perspective on
p
-form connections sketched at the beginning of this section is to find the order
p
truncation of
TM
:
at the level of cocommutative dg coalgebras (cdgcs), the
L_{\infty}
-algebroid
TM
corresponds to its Chevalley–Eilenberg cdgc
{\mathrm{Sym}}_{\mathbbm{R}}(TM[1])
.
This has a straightforward order
p
truncation
{\mathrm{Sym}}_{\mathbbm{R}}^{\leqslant p}(TM[1])
: it is obtained by projecting away all tensor powers greater than
p
.
Dually, its Chevalley–Eilenberg cdga corresponds to the truncated de Rham algebra
\Omega^{\leqslant p}(M)
.
It turns out that there is another
L_{\infty}
-algebroid, denoted
Q^{{(p)}}TM
, which corresponds to
{\mathrm{Sym}}_{\mathbbm{R}}^{\leqslant p}(TM[1])
(up to equivalence at the level of cdgcs).
We understand this
L_{\infty}
-algebroid as the order
p
truncation of
TM
.
Dually, in terms of dg algebras, the Chevalley–Eilenberg algebra of
Q^{{(p)}}TM
simply corresponds to the order
p
truncation
\Omega^{\leqslant p}(M)
of the de Rham complex.

Given any morphism
x\colon M\longrightarrow X
from a smooth manifold to a stack with deformation theory, we can now make the above heuristics fully rigorous through

Definition 1.8

The
\infty
-groupoid of
p
-form connections on
x\colon M\longrightarrow X
is the mapping space

{\mathrm{Con}}_{p}(x)\coloneqq L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{M}^{\infty}\big(Q^{(p)}TM,\,T(M/X)\big)

(1.9)

in the
\infty
-category of
L_{\infty}
-algebroids on
M
.

We then present several fundamental aspects of this notion of connection.
We define the field strength
(p{+}1)
-form of a
p
-form connection as the obstruction of the
p
-form connection to lift to a
(p{+}1)
-form connection (Definition
4.58
) and check that this recovers the classical definition of field strength of a connection.
We also show that null homotopies of the field strength
(p{+}1)
-form correspond exactly to extensions of the
p
-form connection to a
(p{+}1)
-form connection (Proposition
4.61
), adding to the interpretation of the fake curvature condition of higher connections, and show that when
x\colon M\longrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}G
is a principal
\infty
-bundle its higher gauge group acts canonically on the space of
p
-form connections on
x
(Definition
4.101
and Remark
4.103
).
We expect that this notion of higher connections also generalises straightforwardly beyond the case where
M
is a smooth manifold.

Computing spaces of connections on higher
{\mathrm{U}}(1)
-bundles.

In the second part of this paper we explore applications of our theory of
p
-form connections on higher bundles, and illustrate the relationship between derived symmetries of geometric structures and possible weakened versions of the notion of a connection.

First, we provide an independent verification of the derived geometric framework of connections in the case of higher
{\mathrm{U}}(1)
-bundles.
These are also called
(n{-}1)
-gerbes and are classified by maps

x\colon M\longrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}^{n}{\mathrm{U}}(1)

to a smooth delooping of the circle group
{\mathrm{U}}(1)
.
For this special type of principal
\infty
-bundles there already exists a fully developed theory of higher-form connections which, crucially, is entirely independent of the derived geometric formalism we set up here.
Concretely, an
n
-form connection on
x\colon M\longrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}^{n}{\mathrm{U}}(1)
is given by enhancing the map
x
to a differential cocycle, which we model using Čech–Deligne cocycles (for background, see for instance
[
12
,
41
,
39
,
85
,
18
]
).
In a similar vein, one can also endow
x
with
p
-form connections for any
p\in\mathbbm{N}
.
It was observed in
[
16
]
that in this Čech–Deligne approach, for each
(n{-}1)
-gerbe on
M
there is an entire
\infty
-groupoid of
p
-form connections, which we denote by
{\mathrm{Con}}_{p}^{\mathrm{\check{C}D}}(x)
.
We compute the Atiyah
L_{\infty}
-algebroid
T(M/{\mathrm{B}}^{n}{\mathrm{U}}(1))
in Corollary
5.13
, strictify it to a dg Lie algebroid on
M
in Theorem
5.27
, and then by an explicit computation of mapping spaces of
L_{\infty}
-algebroids we prove

Theorem 1.10

(Theorem
5.55
)

Let
x\colon M\longrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}^{n}{\mathrm{U}}(1)
be an
(n{-}1)
-gerbe on a smooth manifold
M
.
For any
p\in\mathbbm{N}
, there is a canonical equivalence

{\mathrm{Con}}_{p}^{\mathrm{\check{C}D}}(x)\simeq{\mathrm{Con}}_{p}(x)

(1.11)

between the
\infty
-groupoids of
p
-form connections on
x
in the Čech–Deligne sense and
p
-form connections on
x
in the derived geometric sense introduced in the present paper.

In particular, we obtain an
L_{\infty}
-algebraic model for differential cohomology.

Higher Courant algebroids and weak connections.

We conclude by exploring the relationship of the theory developed in this paper to higher Courant algebroids and symmetries of higher
{\mathrm{U}}(1)
-bundles with
p
-form connections. Higher Courant algebroids provide a geometric framework for unifying the diffeomorphism and higher-form gauge symmetries together with duality symmetries, as well as the different flavours of fields, in many supergravity theories. One can associate a higher Courant algebroid to any
(n{-}1)
-gerbe through a choice of
(n{-}1)
-form connection, extending Hitchin’s generalised tangent bundle construction for
n=2

[
48
]
to all
n\geqslant 2
(Remark
6.19
); from this perspective, a choice of extension of the
(n{-}1)
-form connection to an
n
-form connection is a splitting of the short exact sequence of vector bundles in which the higher Courant algebroid sits. This association is crucial to the formulation of semi-classical flux quantization conditions in supergravity, and to higher geometric prequantization.

Of particular relevance to the present work is that the bracket of a higher Courant algebroid has a canonical extension to a Lie
n
-algebra that naturally projects to the Lie
n
-algebra underlying the Atiyah
L_{\infty}
-algebroid of a corresponding
(n{-}1)
-gerbe (Theorem
6.54
). Compared to the Atiyah
L_{\infty}
-algebra which controls the symmetries of an
(n{-}1)
-gerbe on its own, we argue that the Courant
L_{\infty}
-algebra controls the symmetries of the
(n{-}1)
-gerbe which also preserve a choice of
(n{-}1)
-form connection (Conjectures
6.87
and
6.90
). For
n=2
, similar statements were proven in
[
37
]
.

Because in this case the objects whose deformation theory we aim to compute contain differential form data, they do not have deformation theory and so only the global version of the moduli problem exists.
That is, we have to describe them globally as maps
y\colon*\longrightarrow{\operatorname{Hom}}_{v}(M,X)/\mathsf{Diff}(M)=Y
, where
{\operatorname{Hom}}_{v}
denotes a ‘concretified’ version of the internal hom whereby families of differential forms on
M
over a parameter space
U
act only on tangent vectors along
M
and not
U
.
We present a general way to compute the deformation theory of such maps in cases where
X
has an
\mathbbm{E}_{\infty}
-group structure (Proposition
6.61
).
This results in
L_{\infty}
-algebras
T(*/Y)
over the Lie algebra of vector fields on
M
which do not stem from
L_{\infty}
-algebroids.
Among other examples, we compute the deformation theory of a gerbe with
1
-form connection (i.e. with a connective structure) and prove that the resulting Lie 2-algebra is equivalent to the Lie 2-algebra of global sections of the Courant algebroid associated to the gerbe (Example
6.99
), thus recovering the main theorem of
[
28
]
.

In the spirit of Definition
1.8
we can, however, still consider order
p
splittings of the map of
L_{\infty}
-algebras
T(*/Y)\longrightarrow TM
which are
C^{\infty}(M)
-linear and view these as a weakened notion of
p
-form connections.
We show that a
{\mathrm{U}}(1)
-bundle with connection admits a
1
-form connection in this weakened sense if and only if the original connection is flat, in which case the additional weak
1
-form connection agrees with the original connection (Example
6.93
).
We also show that a weak
1
-form connection on a gerbe with connective structure is the same as an enhanced curving in the sense of
[
86
]
(Example
6.99
).
An enhanced curving canonically splits into a standard curving and a symmetric
2
-tensor on
M
, which in special cases will be a Riemannian or Lorentzian metric on
M
.
In particular, weak
1
-form connections on gerbes with connective structure may provide a unification of the
B
-field and metric in NSNS supergravity when the Ševera class of the associated Courant algebroid is integral (see also
[
86
,
16
]
).

1.3
Outline of the paper

For the convenience of the reader, as an aid in navigating this paper we provide a brief overview of the structure of its remaining contents.

In Section
2
we collect material on
L_{\infty}
-algebroids on manifolds and the geometry of derived stacks which serves as the foundation for our main results in later sections.
This section consists of both old and new material.
It includes a review of the homotopy theory of
L_{\infty}
-algebroids and dg-
C^{\infty}
-rings, focussing on square zero extensions.
In Section
2.3
we then introduce the
\infty
-category
{\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}}
of derived and infinitesimally thickened manifolds which serves as the foundation for our theory of derived stacks.
Section
2.4
covers tangent complexes and introduces stacks with (quasi-coherent) deformation theory, and Section
2.5
contains various constructions and examples of stacks with (quasi-coherent) deformation theory.

In Section
3
we recall key results about formal moduli problems over commutative differential graded algebras from
[
68
,
70
]
and build on these to establish the formal differentiation of maps
x\colon M\longrightarrow X
from a smooth manifold to a stack with deformation theory.
Such differentiation results in an
L_{\infty}
-algebroid structure on the relative tangent complex
T(M/X)
.
We also provide a formal integration, thus identifying the essential image of the differentiation
\infty
-functor.
Moreover, we show that the global sections of the
L_{\infty}
-algebroid
T(M/X)
agree with the
L_{\infty}
-algebra of the group of derived symmetries of
x\colon M\longrightarrow X
.
We include several computational tools and constructions in Section
3.2
, and show how the brackets on the cohomology of the tangent complex of a formal moduli problem can be computed explicitly via methods from rational homotopy theory.
Proofs of the main theorems in this section are deferred to Appendices
A
and
B
.

In Section
4
we recall the notion of an order
p
morphism of
L_{\infty}
-algebroids from
[
69
]
and present our definition of a
p
-form connection on
L_{\infty}
-algebroids
{\mathfrak{g}}
over a unital commutative dg algebra
A
(Definition
4.14
).
We are then able to define the space of
p
-form connections on a morphism
x\colon M\longrightarrow X
to a stack with deformation theory as the space of order
p
connections on its associated Atiyah
L_{\infty}
-algebroid
T(M/X)
(Definition
4.21
).
The section also contains model categorical tools for computing spaces of order
p
connections and includes several examples.
With the definitions established, we extract the curvature of an order
p
connection as its failure to already be an order
(p{+}1)
-connection (Definition
4.58
and Proposition
4.61
).
Finally, we show that spaces of order
p
connections are functorial with respect to automorphisms of the map
x\colon M\longrightarrow X
.

Section
5
contains two models of the Atiyah
L_{\infty}
-algebroid of higher
{\mathrm{U}}(1)
-bundles
x\colon M\longrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}^{n}{\mathrm{U}}(1)
, one as an
L_{\infty}
-algebroid concentrated in degrees
0
and
-n+1
with a non-trivial
(n{+}1)
-bracket, and one as a dg Lie algebroid concentrated in degrees
-n+1,\ldots,0
(Corollary
5.13
and Theorem
5.27
).
The proof that these models are equivalent occupies Appendix
C
.
Subsequently, we establish in Theorem
5.55
that the space of
p
-form connections on any
n
-bundle
x\colon M\longrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}^{n}{\mathrm{U}}(1)
in our new derived geometric sense is canonically equivalent to the space of
p
-form connections on
x
as obtained from the classical Čech–Deligne description of differential cocycles.
The proof of this theorem is an involved computation, which we carry out in Appendix
E
and which relies on an explicit fibrant simplicial resolution of the target
L_{\infty}
-algebroid established in Appendix
D
.

Section
6
concerns the higher derived symmetries of higher
{\mathrm{U}}(1)
-bundles with fixed
p
-form connections and the question of which additional connection-like structures these symmetries induce.
Crucially, since we fix part of a connection as part of the data here, we are not able to differentiate the classifying map
x\colon M\longrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}^{n-p}\,{\mathrm{B}}_{\nabla}^{p}{\mathrm{U}}(1)
of the geometric structure as before, but only the point
x^{\dashv}
which
x
defines in an (appropriately defined) moduli stack of
{\mathrm{U}}(1)
-principal
n
-bundles with
p
-form connection on
M
(the key result being Proposition
6.61
).
Thus the morphism
x
does not admit connections in the sense of Section
4
, but we can still define a relaxed notion of connection using
x^{\dashv}
instead.
In the case where
x
classifies a gerbe with connective structure (i.e. with a 1-form connection), these weakened 1-form connections on
x^{\dashv}
consist of a curving on the gerbe and a symmetric 2-tensor on the base manifold
M
(compare the results in
[
86
]
).
As first steps towards generalising these results, we survey higher Courant algebroids (Section
6.1
) as well as a higher version of Hitchin’s generalised tangent bundle construction (Remark
6.19
), and use the homotopy transfer theorem to generalise Zambon’s
L_{\infty}
-algebras of higher Courant algebroids
[
95
]
to associate
L_{\infty}
-algebras to
{\mathrm{U}}(1)
-principal
n
-bundles with
(n{-}1)
-form connections (Section
6.2
and Proposition
6.45
).
Finally, we show that there is a projection from Zambon’s Courant
L_{\infty}
-algebra associated to such an
n
-bundle with
(n{-}1)
-form connection onto the
L_{\infty}
-algebra of global sections of the
n
-bundle’s Atiyah
L_{\infty}
-algebroid.
This leaves a few further questions open, which we include as Conjectures
6.87
,
6.90
and
6.112
.

1.5
Notation and conventions

This paper is engulfed in the language of derived geometry,
\infty
-categories and model categories. To aid the reader in following our exposition, below we summarise some of the underlying concepts as well as our notation and conventions.

•

For
n\in\mathbbm{N}
, we write
\Sigma_{n}
for the symmetric group of degree
n
, and
\mathrm{Sh}(k,n-k)\subseteq\Sigma_{n}
for the subset of
(k,n{-}k)
-shuffles with
0\leqslant k\leqslant n
, i.e. permutations
\sigma\in\Sigma_{n}
such that
\sigma(1)<\cdots<\sigma(k)
and
\sigma(k+1)<\cdots<\sigma(n)
. We put
\mathrm{Sh}(0,n)=\mathrm{Sh}(n,0)=\{{\mathrm{id}}\}
. We write
\epsilon(\sigma)
for the sign of a permutation
\sigma
.

•

We use cohomological conventions, so that a
complex
will always mean a
cochain complex
. Consequently, objects like the de Rham complex are in non-negative degrees while our main
L_{\infty}
-algebroids of interest will be in non-positive degrees.
Explicitly, for a graded vector space
C
we use the desuspension convention
({\mathsf{\Omega}}C)^{i}=(C[-1])^{i}=C^{i-1}
. We write
|c|\in\mathbbm{Z}
for the degree of a homogeneous element
c\in C
; that is, if
c\in C^{i}
then
|c|=i
.

•

We abbreviate ‘differential graded’ to dg, ‘differential graded Lie algebra’ to dgla, ‘commutative differential graded algebra’ to cdga and ‘cocommutative differential graded coalgebra’ to cdgc.

•

We write
{{\mathbbe{\Delta}}}
for the simplex category of finite ordinals
[p]=\{0,1,\dots,p\}
and order-preserving maps. A simplicial object in a (
\infty
-)category
\mathscr{C}
is a functor
{{\mathbbe{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{op}}\longrightarrow\mathscr{C}
, where the superscript
op
indicates the opposite category; a cosimplicial object is a functor
{{\mathbbe{\Delta}}}\longrightarrow\mathscr{C}
. A simplicial set is a simplicial object in the category of sets
\mathscr{S}\mathrm{et}
, and the category of simplicial sets is denoted by
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{et}_{\hskip-0.85355pt\Delta}}=\mathrm{Fun}({{\mathbbe{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathscr{S}\mathrm{et})
. For
p\in\mathbbm{N}_{0}
, the standard
p
-simplex is the simplicial set
\Delta^{p}={{\mathbbe{\Delta}}}(-,[p])
induced by the Yoneda embedding. We write
\Lambda_{i}^{p}\subset\partial\Delta^{p}\subset\Delta^{p}
for the
i
-th horn. A Kan complex is a fibrant simplicial set, i.e. a simplicial set satisfying horn-filling conditions.

•

An
\infty
-category is a simplicial set satisfying the inner horn-lifting conditions (see e.g.
[
26
,
58
]
). If
\mathscr{C}
is an
\infty
-category and
c,d\in\mathscr{C}
, we write
\mathscr{C}(c,d)
for the
\infty
-categorical mapping space in
\mathscr{C}
.
For an ordinary category
{\mathscr{M}}
with objects
x,y\in{\mathscr{M}}
, we write
{\mathscr{M}}(x,y)
for the set of morphisms
x\longrightarrow y
in
{\mathscr{M}}
.

•

A model category is a category with three specified classes of morphisms — fibrations, cofibrations and weak equivalences — which satisfy axioms reminiscent of surjections, injections and weak homotopy equivalences of topological spaces. If
{\mathscr{M}}
is a model category, an object
x\in{\mathscr{M}}
is called fibrant if the unique map from
x
to the terminal object is a fibration and cofibrant if the unique map from the initial object to
x
is a cofibration. For any object
x\in{\mathscr{M}}
, there is a cofibrant object
Q(x)
and an acyclic fibration
Q(x)\longrightarrow x
(a weak equivalence), called a cofibrant resolution of
x
. There is dual notion called fibrant resolution.

•

If
{\mathscr{M}}
is a model category and
x,y\in{\mathscr{M}}
, we write
\mathrm{Map}_{\mathscr{M}}(x,y)
for the model categorical mapping space. We sometimes write
{\mathscr{M}}^{\infty}={\mathscr{M}}[W^{-1}]=\operatorname{Ho}{\mathscr{M}}
for its
\infty
-categorical localisation at the weak equivalences.
Then there is an equivalence of spaces
{\mathscr{M}}^{\infty}(x,y)\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{\mathscr{M}}(x,y)
, for each
x,y\in{\mathscr{M}}
.

•

Throughout this paper we work over the field of real numbers
\mathbbm{R}
. Let
A
be a cdga over
\mathbbm{R}
.
We write
{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}
for the category of unbounded dg
A
-modules and
{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{A}^{\infty}={\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}[W^{-1}]
for its
\infty
-categorical localisation at the quasi-isomorphisms.
The morphism sets in
{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}
are denoted
{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}(C,D)
, whereas we write the hom complex as
{\operatorname{Hom}}_{A}(C,D)
.
The
\infty
-categorical mapping space is
{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{A}^{\infty}(C,D)
.

•

We write
{\mathscr{M}\hskip-0.56917pt\mathrm{fd}}
for the category of smooth manifolds and smooth maps. For
M\in{\mathscr{M}\hskip-0.56917pt\mathrm{fd}}
, we write
\mathrm{Open}(M)
for the category of open subsets of
M
and inclusions; the category
\mathrm{Open}(M)
is a poset.

•

We denote exponential objects inside a category or an
\infty
-category
\mathscr{C}
by
Y^{X}
.

•

We use the
C^{\infty}
-version of the Serre–Swan theorem to identify vector bundles over a manifold
M
with finitely generated projective modules over the ring of smooth functions
C^{\infty}(M)
.

•

We sometimes use the algebraic notational convention for the tangent bundle of a manifold
M\in{\mathscr{M}\hskip-0.56917pt\mathrm{fd}}
:
we write
TM
to denote the tangent complex of the
C^{\infty}
-ring
C^{\infty}(M)
; that is,
TM
is the
C^{\infty}(M)
-module of smooth vector fields on
M
.

•

When working with dg
C^{\infty}
-rings, we use
\otimes
to denote the tensor product of the underlying cdgas and
\amalg
to denote the coproduct, or
C^{\infty}
-algebraic tensor product, of the dg
C^{\infty}
-rings.

•

In this paper we use three categories of
L_{\infty}
-algebroids over a cgda
A
:

\diamond

the category
L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}
of
L_{\infty}
-algebroids with morphisms the bracket-preserving maps of the underlying complexes,

\diamond

the category
L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}
of
L_{\infty}
-algebroids with their
\infty
-morphisms, which are the same as morphisms of the associated cdgcs satisfying a certain compatibility with the anchor and an
A
-linearity condition (see Definition
2.11
and Remark
2.13
for details), and

\diamond

the
\infty
-categorical localisation
L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\infty}=L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}[W^{-1}]\simeq L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}[W^{\prime}{}^{-1}]
, where the first localisation is at the quasi-isomorphisms and the second is at the
\infty
-equivalences.

•

We write
{\mathrm{DK}}\colon\mathrm{Ch}_{\leqslant 0}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{b}}_{\Delta}
for the Dold–Kan correspondence, where
\mathrm{Ch}_{\leqslant 0}
is the category of non-positively graded cochain complexes and
{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{b}}_{\Delta}
is the category of simplicial abelian groups.
For a more compact notation, if
{\mathrm{DK}}
is applied to a complex which is not concentrated in non-positive degrees, we will implicitly let
{\mathrm{DK}}
first truncate the complex at level zero and then apply the usual Dold–Kan construction.

•

The
\infty
-category
\mathscr{S}
of spaces (or equivalently
\infty
-groupoids) is the homotopy coherent nerve of the simplicially enriched category of Kan complexes. It arises as the
\infty
-categorical localisation of the Kan–Quillen model structure on simplicial sets.

•

If
\mathscr{C}
is an
\infty
-category and
x\in\mathscr{C}
, we denote by
\mathscr{C}_{/x}
(resp.
\mathscr{C}_{x/}
) the
\infty
-category of objects in
\mathscr{C}
over (resp. under)
x
, i.e. with a map to (resp. from)
x
.

•

In this paper, by a stack we will always mean an
\infty
-stack. We write
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}
(resp.
{\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}
) for the
\infty
-category of stacks (resp. prestacks) on a site.

•

We denote
\infty
-morphisms between
L_{\infty}
-algebroids by a squigly arrow
\,\relbar\joinrel\leadsto\,
. We write
\simeq
for weak equivalences of objects, and
\cong
for isomorphisms.

•

We write
\int\mathscr{F}\longrightarrow\mathscr{C}
for the left fibration classified by an
\infty
-functor
\mathscr{F}:\mathscr{C}\longrightarrow\mathscr{S}
.

2
Deformation theory

To introduce our definition of connections on
\infty
-bundles, we need a formalism of derived
C^{\infty}
-geometry which admits a classification theorem of formal moduli problems over manifolds.
In this section we begin by developing some foundational material on
C^{\infty}
-rings and
L_{\infty}
-algebroids as well as their homotopy theory.
We introduce a notion of infinitesimally thickened manifolds suitable for our purposes,
\infty
-sheaves and formally smooth
\infty
-groupoids thereon, as well as their tangent complexes. This follows ideas from derived algebraic geometry
[
45
,
88
]
, but some additional care is needed in the differential-geometric setting.
In Section
3
below we build on these foundations to prove classification results for deformation problems over manifolds.

2.1
Homotopy theory of
L_{\infty}
-algebroids

The purpose of this section is to give a brief review of the homotopy theory of
L_{\infty}
-algebroids over a smooth manifold
M
. To be able to use model-categorical tools, it will be useful to first consider
L_{\infty}
-algebroids over
M
from a purely algebraic perspective in terms of their
C^{\infty}(M)
-modules of global sections. Since vector bundles over
M
can be identified with finitely generated projective
C^{\infty}(M)
-modules by the
C^{\infty}
-version of the Serre–Swan theorem
[
65
, Chapter 11]
, this purely algebraic approach specialises to more geometric definitions of
L_{\infty}
-algebroids over
M
in terms of vector bundles, or more generally sheaves; we will come back to this point in Definition
2.24
.

Let us fix a cdga
A
over
\mathbbm{R}
and let
T_{A}
denote its complex of derivations.
We will write
\pounds_{X}\colon A\longrightarrow A
for the action of a derivation
X\in T_{A}
on
A
. The commutator bracket and pointwise multiplication by an element in
A
endow the complex
T_{A}
with the structure of a dg Lie algebra and a dg
A
-module.
When
A=C^{\infty}(M)
, this reproduces the usual Lie algebra of vector fields on
M
which we also denote by
TM=T_{C^{\infty}(M)}
; then
\pounds_{X}
is the Lie derivative along a vector field
X\in TM
.

We refer to
[
54
]
for background on
L_{\infty}
-algebras.

Definition 2.1

Let
A
be a cdga over
\mathbbm{R}
.
An
L_{\infty}
-algebroid
over
A
is a dg
A
-module
{\mathfrak{g}}
together with

(1)

a map of dg
A
-modules
\rho\colon{\mathfrak{g}}\longrightarrow T_{A}
called the
anchor map
, and

(2)

an
\mathbbm{R}
-linear
L_{\infty}
-algebra structure on
{\mathfrak{g}}
, with
n
-ary brackets
[-,\dots,-]:\text{\Large$\wedge$}_{\mathbbm{R}}^{n}\,{\mathfrak{g}}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{g}}
of degree
2{-}n
satisfying homotopy Jacobi identities, such that
\rho
is a
strict
map of
L_{\infty}
-algebras (in particular, all
n
-ary brackets with
n\geqslant 3
are sent to zero) and such that for all
x_{i}\in{\mathfrak{g}}
and
a\in A
:

\displaystyle[x_{1},a\,x_{2}]

\displaystyle=(-1)^{|a|\,|x_{1}|}\,a\,[x_{1},x_{2}]+\pounds_{\rho(x_{1})}(a)\,x_{2}\ ,

\displaystyle[x_{1},\dots,x_{n-1},a\,x_{n}]

\displaystyle=(-1)^{|a|\,(|x_{1}|+\dots+|x_{n-1}|)}\,a\,[x_{1},\dots,x_{n}]\quad\text{for }n\geqslant 3\ .

An
L_{\infty}
-algebroid is
fibrant
if its anchor map
\rho:{\mathfrak{g}}\longrightarrow T_{A}
is a fibration, i.e. it is surjective.

A (
strict
)
map of
L_{\infty}
-algebroids
is a map of dg
A
-modules
{\mathfrak{g}}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{h}
that commutes with the anchor maps and preserves the
L_{\infty}
-structure.
We denote the category of
L_{\infty}
-algebroids over
A
and strict maps by
L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}
.
A morphism of
L_{\infty}
-algebroids is a
quasi-isomorphism
if its underlying map of complexes is a quasi-isomorphism.

Example 2.2

An
L_{\infty}
-algebroid whose
n
-ary brackets vanish for all
n\geqslant 3
is a
dg Lie algebroid
. One can show that every
L_{\infty}
-algebroid is connected by a zig-zag of quasi-isomorphisms of
L_{\infty}
-algebroids to a dg Lie algebroid
[
69
, Corollary 3.10]
.

\triangleleft

In Definition
2.1
we allow for
L_{\infty}
-algebroids that are unbounded both in the positive and in the negative direction. However, the positive and non-positive parts play a very different role: the positive part encodes a
derived
structure while the non-positive part encodes a (formally smooth)
stacky
structure. An
L_{\infty}
-algebroid whose underlying complex is concentrated in degrees
[-(n{-}1),\dots,0]
is sometimes called a
Lie
n
-algebroid
.

Proposition 2.3 (
[
69
, Theorem 3.1]
)

The category of
L_{\infty}
-algebroids over
A
carries a cofibrantly generated left semi-model structure in which a map is a weak equivalence if it is a quasi-isomorphism and a fibration if it is degreewise surjective.

Definition 2.4

We denote by

L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\infty}=L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}[W^{-1}]

(2.5)

the
\infty
-category obtained as the (
\infty
-categorical) localisation of the category of
L_{\infty}
-algebroids at the weak equivalences.

Remark 2.6

When
A=C^{\infty}(M)
for a manifold
M
, the category of
non-positively graded

L_{\infty}
-algebroids carries a (full) model structure in which the weak equivalences are the quasi-isomorphisms, and the fibrations are maps that are surjective in negative degrees. Indeed, this follows from the transfer argument from
[
89
]
: every non-positively graded
L_{\infty}
-algebroid
{\mathfrak{g}}
is fibrant and admits a functorial path object, obtained from the
L_{\infty}
-algebroid
{\mathfrak{g}}\boxtimes\Omega^{*}[\Delta^{1}]
from
[
89
, Lemma 2.5]
by taking its canonical truncation (
0
-cycles and elements in negative degree).

The evident inclusion into all
L_{\infty}
-algebroids is then a left Quillen functor,
1
1

1

A
left Quillen functor
is a left adjoint functor that preserves (acyclic) fibrations and weak equivalences.
whose derived unit map is a weak equivalence. Consequently, the model category of non-positively graded
L_{\infty}
-algebroids over
A=C^{\infty}(M)
(with strict maps) describes the full
\infty
-subcategory of
L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\infty}
on the
L_{\infty}
-algebroids with vanishing cohomology in positive degrees.

\triangleleft

Proposition
2.3
implies that the
\infty
-category
L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\infty}
can be studied using (most of) the usual computational tools from model category theory. For example, the mapping space
L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\infty}\big({\mathfrak{g}},\mathfrak{h})
can be computed using a cofibrant replacement of
{\mathfrak{g}}
and a fibrant simplicial resolution of
\mathfrak{h}
. In fact, there is a rather explicit model for this mapping space in terms of
\infty
-morphisms
of
L_{\infty}
-algebroids.

To describe this, let us start by recalling that any
\mathbbm{R}
-linear
L_{\infty}
-algebra has an associated
Chevalley–Eilenberg coalgebra
, given by the reduced
2
2

2

The
reduced
symmetric coalgebra arises from the full symmetric coalgebra by projecting out the degree zero part.

cofree non-unital cocommutative differential graded coalgebra

{\mathrm{CE}}_{*}({\mathfrak{g}})=\Big(\overline{{\mathrm{Sym}}}_{\mathbbm{R}}({\mathfrak{g}}[1]),\delta_{{\mathrm{CE}}}\Big)\ ,

(2.7)

with the shuffle coproduct (see for instance
[
54
, Equations (2.28) and (2.31)]
), and differential
\delta_{{\mathrm{CE}}}
given onto cogenerators by the differential of
{\mathfrak{g}}
as well as all higher brackets
[
63
, Proposition 10.1.20]
.
Given two
\mathbbm{R}
-linear
L_{\infty}
-algebras, an
\infty
-morphism
{\mathfrak{g}}\,\relbar\joinrel\leadsto\,\mathfrak{h}
is then given by a map of (non-unital) cocommutative dg coalgebras

\phi\colon{\mathrm{CE}}_{*}({\mathfrak{g}})\longrightarrow{\mathrm{CE}}_{*}(\mathfrak{h})\ .

(2.8)

Note that without differential
\phi
is uniquely determined by an
\mathbbm{R}
-linear map
\overline{{\mathrm{Sym}}}_{\mathbbm{R}}({\mathfrak{g}}[1])\longrightarrow\mathfrak{h}
of degree
+1
.

Compatibility with the differential then translates into the following condition:
the complex of
\mathbbm{R}
-linear maps
{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathbbm{R}}({\mathrm{CE}}_{*}({\mathfrak{g}}),\mathfrak{h}\big)
has the structure of an
L_{\infty}
-algebra, with
n
-ary bracket
[\phi_{1},\dots,\phi_{n}]_{{\operatorname{Hom}},n}
computed as the composition

[\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{n}]_{{\operatorname{Hom}},n}=[-,\dots,-]_{\mathfrak{h},n}\circ(\phi_{1}\otimes\dots\otimes\phi_{n})\circ{\mathsf{\Delta}}^{n-1}_{\mathfrak{g}}

(2.9)

with the
n
-ary bracket of
\mathfrak{h}
and the
(n{-}1)
-fold coproduct of
{\mathrm{CE}}_{*}({\mathfrak{g}})
.
Then a degree one
\mathbbm{R}
-linear map
\phi\colon{\mathrm{CE}}_{*}({\mathfrak{g}})\longrightarrow\mathfrak{h}
is an
\infty
-morphism
{\mathfrak{g}}\,\relbar\joinrel\leadsto\,\mathfrak{h}
if and only if it satisfies the
Maurer–Cartan equation
(see for example
[
33
, Lemma 2.9]
)

\mathrm{d}_{\operatorname{Hom}}\phi+\sum_{n\geqslant 2}\,\frac{[\phi,\dots,\phi]_{{\operatorname{Hom}},n}}{n!}=0\ .

(2.10)

Here
\mathrm{d}_{\operatorname{Hom}}
is the differential induced on the complex
{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathbbm{R}}({\mathfrak{g}},\mathfrak{h})
from the differentials on
{\mathfrak{g}}
and
\mathfrak{h}
.

Finally, we extend the notion of an
\infty
-morphism from
L_{\infty}
-algebras to
L_{\infty}
-algebroids in this vein.

Definition 2.11

Let
{\mathfrak{g}}
and
\mathfrak{h}
be two
L_{\infty}
-algebroids over a cdga
A
.
An
\infty
-morphism

\phi\colon{\mathfrak{g}}\,\relbar\joinrel\leadsto\,\mathfrak{h}
consists of a map of
\mathbbm{R}
-linear cocommutative dg coalgebras

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{CE}}_{*}({\mathfrak{g}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{CE}}_{*}(\mathfrak{h})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{CE}}_{*}(T_{A})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{CE}}_{*}(\rho_{{\mathfrak{g}}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{CE}}_{*}(\rho_{\mathfrak{h}})}

(2.12)

such that the underlying map of non-unital graded coalgebras
\overline{{\mathrm{Sym}}}_{\mathbbm{R}}({\mathfrak{g}}[1])\longrightarrow\overline{{\mathrm{Sym}}}_{\mathbbm{R}}(\mathfrak{h}[1])
descends to an
A
-linear map of graded coalgebras
\overline{{\mathrm{Sym}}}_{A}({\mathfrak{g}}[1])\longrightarrow\overline{{\mathrm{Sym}}}_{A}(\mathfrak{h}[1])
. The category of
L_{\infty}
-algebroids over
A
and
\infty
-morphisms is denoted
L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}
.

Remark 2.13

Equivalently, an
\infty
-morphism of
L_{\infty}
-algebroids is given by the data of a degree one map of graded
A
-modules
\phi\colon\overline{{\mathrm{Sym}}}_{A}({\mathfrak{g}}[1])\longrightarrow\mathfrak{h}
such that

(1)

the
\mathbbm{R}
-linear map underlying
\phi
defines a Maurer–Cartan element in
{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathbbm{R}}\big(\overline{{\mathrm{Sym}}}_{\mathbbm{R}}({\mathfrak{g}}[1]),\mathfrak{h}[1]\big)
(see (
2.10
)), and

(2)

the composition
\overline{{\mathrm{Sym}}}_{A}({\mathfrak{g}}[1])\longrightarrow\mathfrak{h}\xrightarrow{\ \rho_{\mathfrak{h}}\ }T_{A}
is given by
\rho_{{\mathfrak{g}}}
on the linear copy
{\mathfrak{g}}[1]
and vanishes on all higher tensor powers of
{\mathfrak{g}}[1]
.

\triangleleft

Lemma 2.14 (
[
69
, Lemmas 5.8 and 5.10]
)

For any
L_{\infty}
-algebroid
{\mathfrak{g}}
, there exists another
L_{\infty}
-algebroid
Q{\mathfrak{g}}
with the following universal property: there is a natural bijection between strict maps of
L_{\infty}
-algebroids
Q{\mathfrak{g}}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{h}
and
\infty
-morphisms
{\mathfrak{g}}\,\relbar\joinrel\leadsto\,\mathfrak{h}
; that is

L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}(Q{\mathfrak{g}},\mathfrak{h})\cong L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}({\mathfrak{g}},\mathfrak{h})\ .

(2.15)

Furthermore,
Q{\mathfrak{g}}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{g}}
is a cofibrant resolution if
{\mathfrak{g}}
is cofibrant as a dg
A
-module.

We will give a more explicit description of
Q{\mathfrak{g}}
in Section
4.1
.

Remark 2.16

Suppose that
A
is concentrated in degree zero, and recall that any bounded-above complex of projective
A
-modules is cofibrant.
In the particular case where
A=C^{\infty}(M)
, this means that any bounded-above, and in particular every finite, complex of finite-rank vector bundles on
M
determines such a cofibrant module.

\triangleleft

Corollary 2.17

Let
{\mathfrak{g}}
and
\mathfrak{h}
be
L_{\infty}
-algebroids and suppose that
{\mathfrak{g}}
is cofibrant as a dg
A
-module.
Then the derived mapping space can be computed by the simplicial set of
\infty
-morphisms

L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\infty}\big({\mathfrak{g}},\mathfrak{h})\simeq L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}^{\mathrm{dg}}_{A}\big(Q{\mathfrak{g}},\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}\,\big)\simeq L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}\big({\mathfrak{g}},\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}\,\big)\ ,

(2.18)

where
\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}
is any Reedy fibrant simplicial resolution of
\mathfrak{h}
.

Remark 2.19

Depending on the situation, there are various ways to produce a Reedy fibrant simplicial resolution
\widehat{\mathfrak{h}}
:

(1)

If
\mathfrak{h}
is fibrant, i.e.
\mathfrak{h}\longrightarrow T_{A}
is surjective, then one can construct an explicit fibrant simplicial resolution using polynomial differential forms
[
69
, Lemma 5.24]
.

(2)

If
A=C^{\infty}(M)
, and
{\mathfrak{g}}
and
\mathfrak{h}
are both concentrated in non-positive degrees, then it suffices to construct a Reedy fibrant resolution of
\mathfrak{h}
in the model structure on non-positively graded
L_{\infty}
-algebroids from Remark
2.6
.

(3)

If
\mathfrak{h}\longrightarrow T_{A}
is a
dg abelian extension
of
T_{A}
in the sense of Definition
D.29
, then one can use the simplicial resolution from Proposition
D.107
.
\triangleleft

The usual de Rham algebra has a natural analogue for
L_{\infty}
-algebroids.

Definition 2.20

If
{\mathfrak{g}}
is an
L_{\infty}
-algebroid over
A
, its
Chevalley–Eilenberg algebra

{\mathrm{CE}}^{*}({\mathfrak{g}})
(or
de Rham algebra
) is the cdga given as follows:

•

the underlying graded algebra is given by the
A
-linear dual of the
A
-linear cofree cdgc on
{\mathfrak{g}}[1]
:

{\mathrm{CE}}^{*}({\mathfrak{g}})={\operatorname{Hom}}_{A}\big({\mathrm{Sym}}_{A}({\mathfrak{g}}[1]),A\big)\ .

(2.21)

•

the differential is given by

\displaystyle(\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{CE}}\alpha)(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}_{A}\big(\alpha(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})\big)+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\hskip 2.0pt\pm\,\pounds_{\rho(x_{k})}\alpha(x_{1},\dots,\widehat{x_{k}},\dots,x_{n})

\displaystyle\quad\,-\sum_{k\geqslant 1}\ \sum_{\sigma\in\mathrm{Sh}(k,n-k)}\pm\hskip 2.0pt\alpha\Big([x_{\sigma(1)},\dots,x_{\sigma(k)}],x_{\sigma(k+1)},\dots,x_{\sigma(n)}\Big)\ .

Here the permutation
\sigma
ranges over all
(k,n{-}k)
-shuffles, where
\pm
denotes the Koszul sign associated to the permutation of the elements
x_{i}\in{\mathfrak{g}}[1]
, and
[x_{i}]=\mathrm{d}_{{\mathfrak{g}}[1]}(x_{i})
denotes the differential of
{\mathfrak{g}}[1]
. The hat indicates omission of the corresponding entry.

There is a natural bijection between
n
-cocycles
\alpha\in Z^{n}{\mathrm{CE}}^{*}({\mathfrak{g}})
and
\infty
-morphisms

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\rho,\alpha)\colon{\mathfrak{g}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{A}\oplus A[n-1]}

where
T_{A}\oplus A[n-1]
is the dg Lie algebroid with zero differential and bracket

\big[(X,a)\,,\,(Y,b)\big]=\big([X,Y]\,,\,\pounds_{X}(b)-\pounds_{Y}(a)\big)\ .

Indeed, the
T_{A}
-component of this
\infty
-morphism is fixed to be the anchor map
\rho
, and the second component is given by an
A
-linear map

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha\colon{\mathrm{Sym}}_{A}({\mathfrak{g}}[1])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A[n]\ .}

Unravelling the definitions as in
[
69
, Example 6.18]
, one sees that
\alpha
is an
n
-cocycle in the Chevalley–Eilenberg complex if and only if
(\rho,\alpha)
is an
\infty
-morphism. One can refine this to an equivalence between mapping spaces.

Lemma 2.22

Let
{\mathfrak{g}}
be an
L_{\infty}
-algebroid which is cofibrant as a dg
A
-module. Then there is a natural equivalence of mapping spaces

L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\infty}\big({\mathfrak{g}},T_{A}\oplus A[n-1]\big)\simeq{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}^{\infty}_{\mathbbm{R}}\big(\mathbbm{R}[-n],{\mathrm{CE}}^{*}({\mathfrak{g}})\big)\ .

The right mapping space can be presented explicitly as the image of a complex under the Dold–Kan correspondence as

{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}^{\infty}_{\mathbbm{R}}\big(\mathbbm{R}[-n],{\mathrm{CE}}^{*}({\mathfrak{g}})\big)\simeq{\mathrm{DK}}\big(Z^{n}{\mathrm{CE}}^{*}({\mathfrak{g}})\longleftarrow{\mathrm{CE}}^{n-1}({\mathfrak{g}})\longleftarrow\cdots\big)\ .

Indeed, this simplicial set is isomorphic to the simplicial set of maps
{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{\mathbbm{R}}^{{\mathrm{dg}}}\big(C_{*}(\Delta^{\bullet})[-n],{\mathrm{CE}}^{*}({\mathfrak{g}})\big)
out of a cofibrant cosimplicial resolution of the complex
\mathbbm{R}[-n]
, where
C_{*}(\Delta^{p})
denotes the (normalised Moore) complex of singular chains on the
p
-simplex
\Delta^{p}\in{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{et}_{\hskip-0.85355pt\Delta}}
, for
p\in\mathbbm{N}_{0}
.

Proof.

It follows from
[
69
, Corollary 6.16]
that
{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}^{\infty}_{\mathbbm{R}}\big(\mathbbm{R}[-n],{\mathrm{CE}}^{*}({\mathfrak{g}})\big)
is naturally equivalent to the space of sections of the projection

\pi\colon{\mathfrak{g}}\oplus A[n-1]={\mathfrak{g}}\times_{T_{A}}\big(T_{A}\oplus A[n-1]\big)\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{g}}\ .

Since
\pi
is the homotopy pullback of
T_{A}\oplus A[n-1]\longrightarrow T_{A}
along
\rho\colon{\mathfrak{g}}\longrightarrow T_{A}
and
T_{A}
is the terminal
L_{\infty}
-algebroid, this space of sections is equivalent to the space of maps from
{\mathfrak{g}}
to
T_{A}\oplus A[n-1]
.
∎

Finally, let us consider the special case where
A=C^{\infty}(M)
is the ring of smooth functions on a smooth manifold
M
. In this case, the definition of
L_{\infty}
-algebroid is a bit too algebraic: a general
L_{\infty}
-algebroid over
C^{\infty}(M)
cannot be studied locally on
M
using descent methods. For this reason, it will often be convenient to add the condition below.
In the case where
A=C^{\infty}(M)
we also write

L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{M}\coloneqq L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{C^{\infty}(M)}\ ,\quad L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{M}^{\mathrm{dg}}\coloneqq L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{C^{\infty}(M)}^{\mathrm{dg}}\quad\text{and}\quad L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{M}^{\infty}\coloneqq L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{C^{\infty}(M)}^{\infty}\ .

(2.23)

Definition 2.24

Let
M
be a smooth manifold and
E
a dg
C^{\infty}(M)
-module. Let
\mathscr{E}
be the induced presheaf of complexes on
M
given by

\mathscr{E}(U)=C^{\infty}(U)\otimes_{C^{\infty}(M)}E\ ,

for any open subset
U\subset M
, and let
E^{\mathrm{cpl}}
be the associated
\infty
-sheaf of complexes. The dg
C^{\infty}(M)
-module
\Gamma(M,E^{\mathrm{cpl}})
of global sections of
E^{\mathrm{cpl}}
is the
completion
of
E
, and
E
is
complete
if the map
E\longrightarrow\Gamma(M,E^{\mathrm{cpl}})
is a quasi-isomorphism.
We denote the full subcategory of
{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{C^{\infty}(M)}^{\mathrm{dg}}
on the complete modules by
{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{C^{\infty}(M)}^{{\mathrm{dg}},{\mathrm{cpl}}}
.

An
L_{\infty}
-algebroid is
complete
if its underlying complex of
C^{\infty}(M)
-modules is complete, and we denote the full subcategory of complete
L_{\infty}
-algebroids by
L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{M}^{{\mathrm{dg}},{\mathrm{cpl}}}
.

One can think of complete
L_{\infty}
-algebroids as (the global sections of)
sheaves
of
L_{\infty}
-algebroids over
M
.

Lemma 2.25 (
[
68
, Lemma 5.1.33]
)

For any smooth manifold
M
, the functors sending
E\longmapsto E^{\mathrm{cpl}}
and taking global sections give rise to an adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\mathrm{cpl}}:{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{C^{\infty}(M)}^{\infty}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{C^{\infty}_{M}}^{\infty}:\Gamma(M,-)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\perp}

between the
\infty
-categories of dg
C^{\infty}(M)
-modules and complexes of sheaves on
M
that are dg modules over the sheaf
C^{\infty}_{M}
of
C^{\infty}
-functions on
M
.
The right adjoint
\Gamma(M,-)
is fully faithful with essential image consisting precisely of the complete
C^{\infty}(M)
-modules.

In particular, this shows that completion is an idempotent operation.

Remark 2.26

Since the sheaf of
C^{\infty}
-functions on
M
has partitions of unity, its module sheaves have vanishing higher sheaf cohomology. Consequently, for any
-\infty\leqslant i\leqslant j\leqslant\infty
, both functors in the adjunction of Lemma
2.25
preserve complexes with cohomology in the range
[i,j]
. This implies the following pleasant properties of completion:

(1)

If
E^{\mathrm{cpl}}
is the completion of
E
, then
\mathrm{H}^{i}(E^{\mathrm{cpl}})
is the completion of
\mathrm{H}^{i}(E)
; in particular,
\mathrm{H}^{i}(E^{\mathrm{cpl}})
is complete. Consequently,
E
is complete if and only if each cohomology module
\mathrm{H}^{i}(E)
is complete.

(2)

If
E
is a complex of
C^{\infty}(M)
-modules given in each degree by a complete module in the sense of Joyce
[
50
, Definition 5.25]
, then
E
is complete in the sense of Definition
2.24
. Most importantly, this implies that any complex of finite rank projective modules (i.e. vector bundles) is complete.

(3)

Let
f\colon M\longrightarrow N
be a map between smooth manifolds. Then there is a natural adjoint pair between their categories of complete modules

\hbox to186.72pt{\vbox to19.37pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 93.35947pt\lower-9.68251pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

Here
f_{*}
is given by restriction of scalars along
C^{\infty}(N)\longrightarrow C^{\infty}(M)
and
f^{*}(E)
is the completion of the module
C^{\infty}(M)\otimes^{h}_{C^{\infty}(N)}E
. Under the identification from Lemma
2.25
, this adjunction is identified with the direct image and inverse image of module sheaves; the functor
f_{*}
does not need to be derived because it is exact (by partitions of unity).
\triangleleft

2.2
Homotopy theory of dg
C^{\infty}
-rings

Perhaps the simplest non-trivial example of a Lie algebroid on a smooth manifold
M
is the following: let
\pi\colon M\longrightarrow N
be a submersion and consider the subbundle

T(M/N)=\ker\big(\mathrm{d}\pi\colon TM\longrightarrow\pi^{*}TN\big)

of vector fields on
M
that are tangent to the fibres of
\pi
. Then
T(M/N)
has the natural structure of a Lie algebroid, since vector fields tangent to the fibres of
\pi
are closed under the commutator bracket.
The purpose of this and the following sections is to describe a general method for associating an
L_{\infty}
-algebroid (well-defined up to quasi-isomorphism) to
any
map from a manifold
M
to a higher differentiable stack which admits sufficient deformation theory.

To do this in a functorial way, we will need a sufficiently formal way to define ‘infinitesimal objects’ such as the tangent bundle of a smooth manifold.
One way to achieve this is via synthetic differential geometry, by enlarging the site of smooth manifolds to also include ‘infinitesimally thickened manifolds’.
In fact, to ensure sufficient functoriality of our constructions, we will need to go one step further and also include ‘
derived
infinitesimally thickened manifolds’ to our site.
Since each smooth manifold
M
is uniquely determined by its
C^{\infty}
-ring of smooth functions, we will use the algebraic formalism of dg
C^{\infty}
-rings from
[
29
]
to describe such objects.

Definition 2.27

A
dg
C^{\infty}
-ring

A
is an
\mathbbm{R}
-linear commutative dg algebra concentrated in non-positive degrees, equipped with a compatible
C^{\infty}
-ring structure on its degree zero part
A^{0}
.

Recall that a
C^{\infty}
-ring is a set
A
equipped with an operation
f_{*}\colon A^{\times n}\longrightarrow A
for every
C^{\infty}
-function
f\colon\mathbbm{R}^{n}\longrightarrow\mathbbm{R}
such that composition is respected (see e.g.
[
64
]
or
[
50
]
for detailed accounts). In particular, the operations associated to polynomial maps
\mathbbm{R}^{n}\longrightarrow\mathbbm{R}
endow any dg
C^{\infty}
-ring
A
with the structure of a cdga over
\mathbbm{R}
.

Example 2.28

Let us mention the following examples of dg
C^{\infty}
-rings:

(1)

For every smooth manifold
M
, its smooth functions form a dg
C^{\infty}
-ring concentrated in degree zero, which we again denote by
C^{\infty}(M)
.

(2)

If
A
is a dg
C^{\infty}
-ring and
I\subseteq A
is any dg ideal in the usual algebraic sense, then
A/I
inherits the structure of a dg
C^{\infty}
-ring by
[
64
, Proposition 1.2]
.

(3)

Consider a dg Artin (or Weil) algebra over
\mathbbm{R}
, i.e. a finite-dimensional cdga of the form
A=\mathbbm{R}[x_{1},\dots,x_{n}]/I
where
I\subseteq(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})
is an ideal such that for all
i
,
x_{i}^{k_{i}}\in I
for some
k_{i}
. Here the
x_{i}
can have any non-positive degree, and there can be a non-zero differential.
Then
A
has the canonical structure of a dg
C^{\infty}
-ring (the
C^{\infty}
-ring structure on
A^{0}
arises from
[
64
, Theorem 3.17]
).

(4)

The tensor product
C^{\infty}(M)\otimes\mathbbm{R}[x_{1},\dots,x_{n}]/I
of a dg Artin algebra and the function algebra of a smooth manifold is again a dg
C^{\infty}
-ring.

(5)

Suppose that
{\mathfrak{g}}
is an
L_{\infty}
-algebroid over a smooth manifold
M
, and let

\tau^{\leqslant 0}{\mathrm{CE}}^{*}({\mathfrak{g}})=\Big[Z^{0}\big({\mathrm{CE}}^{*}({\mathfrak{g}})\big)\longleftarrow{\mathrm{CE}}^{-1}({\mathfrak{g}})\longleftarrow\cdots\Big]

be the truncation of its Chevalley–Eilenberg algebra to non-positive degrees. Then
\tau^{\leqslant 0}{\mathrm{CE}}^{*}({\mathfrak{g}})
has the structure of a dg
C^{\infty}
-ring
[
68
, Lemma 4.1.20]
.

(6)

If
A
is a dg
C^{\infty}
-ring and
M
is a non-positively graded dg
A
-module, then the square-zero extension
A\oplus M
has a natural
C^{\infty}
-ring structure by
[
30
, Proposition 2.42]
. Explicitly, the
C^{\infty}
-structure on
A^{0}\oplus M^{0}
is given by

f_{*}\big(a_{1}+m_{1},\dots,a_{n}+m_{n}\big)=f_{*}\big(a_{1},\dots,a_{n}\big)+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\,\Big(\frac{\partial f}{\partial x_{i}}\Big)_{*}(a_{1},\dots,a_{n})\cdot m_{i}\ .

(7)

For any dg
C^{\infty}
-ring
A
, its zeroth cohomology
\mathrm{H}^{0}(A)
has the structure of an ordinary
C^{\infty}
-ring. We define the
reduction

A_{\mathrm{red}}
of
A
to be the quotient of
\mathrm{H}^{0}(A)
by its ideal of nilpotent elements (we will not need any more subtle notion of reduction):

A_{\mathrm{red}}\coloneqq\mathrm{H}^{0}(A)\,\big/\,\mathrm{nilradical}\ .

There is a natural map of dg
C^{\infty}
-rings
A\longrightarrow A_{\mathrm{red}}
.

\triangleleft

The category of dg
C^{\infty}
-rings supports a model structure
[
29
, Theorem 6.10]
whose weak equivalences are the quasi-isomorphisms and fibrations are maps that are surjective in negative degree. The cofibrant objects are retracts of dg
C^{\infty}
-rings of the form

\Big(C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{S})[x_{\alpha}],\mathrm{d}\Big)

with arbitrary differential
\mathrm{d}
.
Here the
x_{\alpha}
are generators in negative degree, and for any set
S
we denote by
C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{S})
the ring of functions
f\colon\prod_{s\in S}\,\mathbbm{R}\longrightarrow\mathbbm{R}
that factor as

\prod_{s\in S}\,\mathbbm{R}\,\relbar\joinrel\twoheadrightarrow\,\mathbbm{R}^{\times k}\longrightarrow\mathbbm{R}\ .

The first map projects onto finitely many factors and the second map is
C^{\infty}
.
Any dg
C^{\infty}
-ring is a quotient of such a quasi-free dg
C^{\infty}
-ring.

Definition 2.29

We write
{\mathrm{C}^{\infty}\hskip-0.59998pt\mathscr{R}\mathrm{ing}}^{\mathrm{dg}}
for the category of dg
C^{\infty}
-rings endowed with the above model structure, and
{\mathrm{C}^{\infty}\hskip-0.59998pt\mathscr{R}\mathrm{ing}}^{\infty}={\mathrm{C}^{\infty}\hskip-0.59998pt\mathscr{R}\mathrm{ing}}^{\mathrm{dg}}[W^{-1}]
for its associated
\infty
-category.

Remark 2.30

For any diagram of dg
C^{\infty}
-rings

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}

in which
A\longrightarrow A^{\prime}
is surjective on
\mathrm{H}^{0}
, the map
A^{\prime}\otimes^{h}_{A}B\longrightarrow A^{\prime}\amalg^{h}_{A}B
from the derived tensor product of the underlying cdgas to the homotopy pushout of dg
C^{\infty}
-rings is an equivalence. Indeed, in this case one can model
A\longrightarrow A^{\prime}
by a cofibration in which one only adds generators in negative degree, so that
A
and
A^{\prime}
are isomorphic
C^{\infty}
-rings in degree
0
; in this case, the pushout of dg-
C^{\infty}
-rings is simply given by the tensor product.

\triangleleft

For our purposes it will suffice to consider dg
C^{\infty}
-rings that arise as nilpotent extensions of the function ring of a smooth manifold.
To make this more precise, let us recall how to describe square-zero extensions of dg
C^{\infty}
-rings (see for instance
[
68
, Definition 2.2.20]
or
[
88
, Definition 1.2.1.6]
).

Definition 2.31

Let
A
and
B
be dg
C^{\infty}
-rings, and let
I
be a dg
A
-module in non-positive degrees. A homotopy pullback diagram in the
\infty
-category
{\mathrm{C}^{\infty}\hskip-0.59998pt\mathscr{R}\mathrm{ing}}^{\infty}
of the form

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{\eta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\oplus I[1]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({\mathrm{id}},0)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta}

(2.32)

exhibits
B_{\eta}
as a
square-zero extension
of
B
by the dg
A
-module
I
.

Remark 2.33

The homotopy pullback in Definition
2.31
can be identified more explicitly as follows:
suppose that the map
\eta
in
{\mathrm{C}^{\infty}\hskip-0.59998pt\mathscr{R}\mathrm{ing}}^{\infty}
arises from a (strict) map of dg
C^{\infty}
-rings (one can always assume this by replacing
B
with a cofibrant resolution)

\eta=\eta_{A}\oplus\eta_{I}\colon B\longrightarrow A\oplus I[1]\ .

Such a map of dg
C^{\infty}
-rings corresponds to a map of dg
C^{\infty}
-rings
\eta_{A}:B\longrightarrow A
, so that we can view
I
as a dg
B
-module, together with a
derivation

\eta_{I}\colon B\longrightarrow I[1]
, i.e. a map such that

\eta_{I}(b_{1}\,b_{2})=b_{1}\cdot\eta_{I}(b_{2})+(-1)^{|b_{1}|\,|b_{2}|}\,b_{2}\cdot\eta_{I}(b_{1})\ ,\quad\eta_{I}\big(f_{*}(b_{1},\dots,b_{n})\big)=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\,\Big(\frac{\partial f}{\partial x_{i}}\Big)_{*}(b_{1},\dots,b_{n})\cdot\eta_{I}(b_{i})\ ,

where the last equation only applies to elements
b_{i}\in B
in degree zero.
One can then compute the homotopy pullback by resolving the right vertical map by

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\oplus\text{Cone}({\mathrm{id}}_{I})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\oplus I[1]\ ,}

whose kernel is
I
; here
{\mathrm{Cone}}
denotes the mapping cone of a map of complexes.
The homotopy pullback is then given by
B\oplus I
with differential

\mathrm{d}(b+m)=\mathrm{d}_{B}(b)+\eta_{I}(b)+\mathrm{d}_{I}(m)

for all
b\in B
and
m\in I
.

Conversely, suppose that
I
is a square-zero ideal in a dg
C^{\infty}
-ring
B^{\prime}
, and let
B\longrightarrow B^{\prime}/I
be a quasi-free resolution. Then the base change
B\times_{B^{\prime}/I}B^{\prime}\,\relbar\joinrel\twoheadrightarrow\,B

is a (strict) square-zero extension of dg
C^{\infty}
-rings which is quasi-isomorphic to
B^{\prime}\longrightarrow B^{\prime}/I
.
That
B
is quasi-free allows us to lift generators, and so one can identify
B\times_{B^{\prime}/I}B^{\prime}\cong B\oplus I
with differential determined by a certain derivation
\eta_{I}\colon B\longrightarrow I[1]
. In other words, up to quasi-isomorphism every square-zero extension arises from a homotopy pullback as in Definition
2.31
.

\triangleleft

Lemma 2.34

Fix a homotopy pullback square (
2.32
) and a dg
C^{\infty}
-ring
C
, and consider the induced square of homotopy coproducts

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C\amalg^{h}B_{\eta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C\amalg^{h}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C\amalg^{h}B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C\amalg^{h}(A\oplus I[1])}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{id}}\amalg({\mathrm{id}},0)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{id}}\amalg\eta}

If the map
\mathrm{H}^{0}\eta\colon\mathrm{H}^{0}(B)\longrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{0}(A\oplus I[1])\cong\mathrm{H}^{0}(A)
is surjective, then this square is a homotopy pullback that exhibits
C\amalg^{h}B_{\eta}
as the square-zero extension of
C\amalg^{h}B
by the dg
C^{\infty}
-ring
(C\amalg^{h}A)\otimes^{h}_{A}I
.

Proof.

We first show that the square is a homotopy pullback, for which it suffices to work at the level of cdgas.
Since
\eta
is surjective on
\mathrm{H}^{0}
, all maps in the square (
2.32
) are surjective on
\mathrm{H}^{0}
. By Remark
2.30
, the square in the statement of the lemma is then obtained from (
2.32
) by applying the functor

(C\amalg^{h}B_{\eta})\otimes^{h}_{B_{\eta}}(-)\simeq(C\amalg^{h}B_{\eta})\amalg^{h}_{B_{\eta}}(-)\ .

(2.35)

It thus suffices to check that the derived tensor product preserves homotopy pullbacks of maps that induce a surjection on
\mathrm{H}^{0}
. This can be verified at the level of non-positively graded
B_{\eta}
-modules, where a square is a pullback if it is a pushout, and the converse holds whenever two parallel maps in the square are surjective on
\mathrm{H}^{0}
(see
[
62
, Corollary C.1.2.6]
); it then follows since the derived tensor product preserves homotopy pushouts.

A similar argument shows that at the level of dg algebras, the map

{\mathrm{id}}\amalg({\mathrm{id}},0)\colon C\amalg^{h}A\longrightarrow C\amalg^{h}(A\oplus I[1])

(2.36)

is equivalent to the canonical map

({\mathrm{id}},0)\colon C\amalg^{h}A\longrightarrow(C\amalg^{h}A)\oplus(C\amalg^{h}A)\otimes^{h}_{A}I[1]

(2.37)

to the trivial square-zero extension.
It follows from
[
22
, Theorem 9.3]
that this is also an equivalence as maps of dg
C^{\infty}
-rings. This shows that the square of coproducts indeed classifies a square-zero extension by
(C\amalg^{h}A)\otimes^{h}_{A}I
.
∎

Lemma 2.38

Let
B\longrightarrow A
be a morphism of dg
C^{\infty}
-rings with the following two properties:

(a)

the induced morphism
\mathrm{H}^{0}(B)\longrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{0}(A)
is surjective with nilpotent kernel
J
;

(b)

there exists an
N\in\mathbbm{N}
such that
\mathrm{H}^{-l}(B)\longrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{-l}(A)
is an isomorphism for all
l>N
.

Then:

(1)

The map
B\longrightarrow A
decomposes (up to equivalence) as a finite sequence of square-zero extensions by shifted
A_{\rm red}
-modules.

(2)

If
C\longrightarrow C^{\prime}
is a map of dg
C^{\infty}
-rings under
B
such that the map of pushouts
A\amalg_{B}C\longrightarrow A\amalg_{B}C^{\prime}
is an equivalence, then
C\longrightarrow C^{\prime}
is an equivalence.

Proof.

For assertion (1), we may assume that
B\longrightarrow A
is surjective, with kernel
I
. For each
n\geqslant 0
, let us consider the following two sub-complexes of
I
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I(n)=\big[0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z^{-n}I}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I^{-n-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots\big]\ ,}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I^{\prime}(n)=\big[0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{d}(I^{n-1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I^{-n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I^{-n-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots\big]\ .}

(2.39)

Both define dg ideals of
B
, so that we obtain a tower of dg
C^{\infty}
-rings

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B/I^{\prime}(n)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B/I(n)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B/I^{\prime}(n-1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B/I(0)=A\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

Each map
B/I(n)\longrightarrow B/I^{\prime}(n-1)
is a quasi-isomorphism because it is surjective with kernel given by the acyclic complex
I^{-n}/Z^{-n}I\longrightarrow\mathrm{d}(I^{-n})
. Furthermore, the map
B\longrightarrow B/I(n)
is a quasi-isomorphism for all sufficiently large
n
, as
\mathrm{H}^{-l}(I)=0
for all
l\in\mathbbm{N}
sufficiently large (since
\mathrm{H}^{-l}(f)
is an isomorphism for all
l\in\mathbbm{N}
sufficiently large).

It therefore suffices to verify that each map
B/I^{\prime}(n)\longrightarrow B/I(n)
decomposes into a finite sequence of square-zero extensions by
\mathrm{H}^{0}(A)
-modules. Let us write
B^{\prime}\longrightarrow B/I(n)
for this map and
K=\mathrm{H}^{-n}(I)[n]
for its kernel, which is an
\mathrm{H}^{0}(B)
-module. Since the kernel
J
of the map
\mathrm{H}^{0}(B)\longrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{0}(A)
satisfies
J^{p}=0
for some
p\in\mathbbm{N}
, we then obtain a finite sequence of dg
C^{\infty}
-rings

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B/I^{\prime}(n)=B^{\prime}/J^{p}\cdot K}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B^{\prime}/J^{p-1}\cdot K}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B^{\prime}/J\cdot K}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B^{\prime}/K=B/I(n)\ .}

Each map in this sequence is a (strict) square-zero extension by a shift of an
\mathrm{H}^{0}(A)
-module of the form
J^{k}\cdot K/J^{k+1}\cdot K
. By Remark
2.33
, these are also square-zero extensions in the sense of Definition
2.31
.

For assertion (2), note that
A\amalg^{h}_{B}C\simeq A\otimes^{h}_{B}C^{\prime}
since
B\longrightarrow A
is surjective on
\mathrm{H}^{0}
.
It therefore suffices to show that
A\otimes^{h}_{B}(-)
detects equivalences between non-positively graded dg
B
-modules.
This follows because
B\longrightarrow A
is the limit of a finite tower of square-zero extensions by
A
-modules
I_{k}
in increasingly negative degrees:
tensoring up, this induces a natural tower for each non-positively graded
B
-module
E
such that
E=\operatorname{lim}_{k\in\mathbbm{N}_{0}}E_{k}
and equipped with natural equivalences

\mathrm{fib}(E_{k}\longrightarrow E_{k-1})\simeq I_{k}\otimes_{A}(A\otimes^{h}_{B}E).

So if
f\colon E\longrightarrow E^{\prime}
is a morphism of non-positively graded dg
B
-modules such that
(A\otimes^{h}_{B}f)
is an equivalence, then
f
induces an equivalence
\mathrm{fib}(E_{k}\longrightarrow E_{k-1})\xrightarrow{\ \simeq\ }\mathrm{fib}(E^{\prime}_{k}\longrightarrow E^{\prime}_{k-1})
on the homotopy fibre, for each
k\in\mathbbm{N}_{0}
.
Thus
f
is itself an equivalence.
∎

Remark 2.40

The same argument shows that, in the situation of Lemma
2.38
, any non-positively graded dg
B
-module
E
can be obtained as an iterated extension of dg
A
-modules.

\triangleleft

Corollary 2.41

Let
A
be a dg
C^{\infty}
-ring such that
A_{\mathrm{red}}\cong C^{\infty}(M)
for a smooth manifold
M
. The natural map
\epsilon\colon A\longrightarrow A_{\mathrm{red}}=C^{\infty}(M)
admits a (non-canonical) section up to homotopy.

Proof.

The map
\epsilon
decomposes into a tower of maps

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon\colon A=\underset{n\in\mathbbm{N}_{0}}{\operatorname{lim}}\,\tau^{\geqslant-n}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{\geqslant-n}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{\geqslant 0}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{\mathrm{red}}\ .}

It will suffice to construct a compatible family of sections
s_{n}\colon A_{\mathrm{red}}\longrightarrow\tau^{\geqslant-n}(A)
for each
n\geqslant 0
;
the desired section is then given by taking the limit.
We will produce this family of sections by induction, using Lemma
2.38
, which implies that the maps
\tau^{\geqslant 0}(A)\longrightarrow A_{\mathrm{red}}
and
\tau^{\geqslant-n}(A)\longrightarrow\tau^{\leqslant 1-n}(A)
all decompose into finite sequences of square-zero extensions by
A_{\mathrm{red}}
-modules.

For the inductive step, it therefore suffices to check that we can lift these sections against square-zero extensions. For this, let us consider a diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{\eta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{\mathrm{red}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{\mathrm{red}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{\mathrm{red}}\oplus I[1]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({\mathrm{id}},0)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{s}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta}

where the square is a pullback exhibiting
B_{\eta}
as a square-zero extension of
B
. To find the dashed lift, we have to show that the map
\eta\circ s\colon A_{\mathrm{red}}\longrightarrow A_{\mathrm{red}}\oplus I[1]
factors over
({\mathrm{id}},0)\colon A_{\mathrm{red}}\longrightarrow A_{\mathrm{red}}\oplus I[1]
.

Now recall from Remark
2.33
that the map
\eta\circ s
is classified by a derivation
A_{\mathrm{red}}=C^{\infty}(M)\longrightarrow I[1]
.
In turn, this is determined uniquely by a map
\phi\colon\Omega^{1}(M)\longrightarrow I[1]
in the
\infty
-category of
C^{\infty}(M)
-modules (see Example
2.77
below, as well as
[
68
, Example 2.2.18]
).
Under these identifications, the space of factorisations of
\eta\circ s
over
({\mathrm{id}},0)
is equivalent to the space of null-homotopies of
\phi
.
Since
\Omega^{1}(M)
is a projective
C^{\infty}(M)
-module (hence cofibrant) and
I[1]
is concentrated in negative degrees, the map
\phi
is necessarily null-homotopic. We can therefore factor
\eta\circ s
over
({\mathrm{id}},0)
and find a lift
\tilde{s}
. ∎

2.3
Background from derived
C^{\infty}
-geometry

Using the elements from the homotopy theory of dg
C^{\infty}
-rings from Section
2.2
, we will now set up a version of derived
C^{\infty}
-algebraic geometry for infinitesimal thickenings of smooth manifolds.
Ideally, one would like to say that for every dg
C^{\infty}
-ring
A
such that
A_{\mathrm{red}}\cong C^{\infty}(M)
