for a smooth manifold
M
, there is an infinitesimal thickening
{\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}(A)
of
M
. However, such dg
C^{\infty}
-rings need not behave well with respect to descent, and we need to impose a completeness condition similar to Definition
2.24
.

Definition 2.42

Let
A
be a dg
C^{\infty}
-ring such that
\mathrm{H}^{0}(A)
is a nilpotent extension of
C^{\infty}(M)
for some smooth manifold
M
. Consider the (
\infty
-)presheaf of dg
C^{\infty}
-rings

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{O}}_{A,\mathrm{pre}}\colon\mathrm{Open}(M)^{\mathrm{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{C}^{\infty}\hskip-0.59998pt\mathscr{R}\mathrm{ing}}^{\infty}\ ,}

(2.43)

sending each open subset
U\subset M
to the dg
C^{\infty}
-ring obtained from
A
by inverting all elements in
\mathrm{H}^{0}(A)
whose image under

\mathrm{H}^{0}(A)\longrightarrow C^{\infty}(M)\longrightarrow C^{\infty}(U)

is invertible
[
68
, Lemma 5.1.13]
. Let
{\mathscr{O}}_{A}
be the associated sheaf. Then
A
is
complete
if the natural map

A=\Gamma(M,{\mathscr{O}}_{A,\mathrm{pre}})\longrightarrow\Gamma(M,{\mathscr{O}}_{A})

(2.44)

of global sections is an equivalence of dg
C^{\infty}
-rings.
Similarly, a dg
A
-module
E
is
complete
if the natural map
E\longrightarrow\Gamma(M,\mathscr{E})
is an equivalence of dg
A
-modules, where
\mathscr{E}
is the associated sheaf of
{\mathscr{O}}_{A}\otimes_{A}E
.

This coincides with the notion of completeness for dg
C^{\infty}
-rings and their modules from
[
50
,
68
,
82
]
. As shown there, the functors
A\longmapsto\Gamma(M,{\mathscr{O}}_{A})
and
E\longmapsto\Gamma(M,\mathscr{E})
define left adjoints to the inclusions of the full subcategories of complete objects; we will refer to them as
completion
.

Remark 2.45

The presheaf
{\mathscr{O}}_{A,\mathrm{pre}}
can be made more explicit via a choice of section
C^{\infty}(M)\longrightarrow A
as in Corollary
2.41
.
Indeed, such a section gives rise to natural maps

\mu\colon C^{\infty}(U)\amalg_{C^{\infty}(M)}A\longrightarrow{\mathscr{O}}_{A,\mathrm{pre}}(U)\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mu^{\prime}\colon C^{\infty}(U)\otimes_{C^{\infty}(M)}A\longrightarrow{\mathscr{O}}_{A,\mathrm{pre}}(U)

(2.46)

of
C^{\infty}
-rings and cdgas, respectively (recall that when we take the algebraic tensor product
\otimes
of
C^{\infty}
-rings, rather than their pushouts, we only remember their underlying cdga structures). The maps
\mu
and
\mu^{\prime}
are both equivalences, since they become equivalences after taking the pushout (tensor product) along the map
A\longrightarrow C^{\infty}(M)
(see Lemma
2.38
). In particular, it follows that
A
is complete if and only if it is complete as a dg
C^{\infty}(M)
-module (for any choice of section), and similarly for a dg
A
-module. Remark
2.26
then shows that completion has good homological properties; for example, the cohomology groups of the completion of
A
can be identified with the completions of the
C^{\infty}(M)
-modules
\mathrm{H}^{i}(A)
.

\triangleleft

Definition 2.47

The
\infty
-category of
inf-manifolds
,

{\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}}\subseteq{\mathrm{C}^{\infty}\hskip-0.59998pt\mathscr{R}\mathrm{ing}}^{\infty,\mathrm{op}}\ ,

is the opposite of the full subcategory of
{\mathrm{C}^{\infty}\hskip-0.59998pt\mathscr{R}\mathrm{ing}}^{\infty}
on those dg
C^{\infty}
-rings
A
such that

(1)

A_{\mathrm{red}}=C^{\infty}(M_{\mathrm{red}})
for a smooth manifold
M_{\mathrm{red}}
,

(2)

\mathrm{H}^{0}(A)\longrightarrow A_{\mathrm{red}}
is a nilpotent extension,

(3)

A
is complete, and

(4)

\mathrm{H}^{*}(A)
is concentrated in finitely many degrees.

For any such dg
C^{\infty}
-ring
A
, we write
{\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}(A)
for the corresponding inf-manifold and for any inf-manifold
M
, we write
{\mathscr{O}}(M)
for the corresponding dg
C^{\infty}
-ring. We also write

M_{\mathrm{red}}={\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}(A_{\mathrm{red}})

for the smooth manifold underlying an inf-manifold
M={\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}(A)
.

Example 2.48

Let
M
be a smooth manifold and

A=\mathbbm{R}[x_{1},\dots,x_{n}]\,\big/\,I

a dg Artin algebra. Then the dg
C^{\infty}
-ring
C^{\infty}(M)\otimes_{\mathbbm{R}}A
is a nilpotent extension of
C^{\infty}(M)
and complete (since it is a finite complex of free
C^{\infty}(M)
-modules). Geometrically,

{\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}\big(C^{\infty}(M)\otimes_{\mathbbm{R}}A\big)\simeq M\times{\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}(A)

looks like the product of a smooth manifold and an infinitesimally thickened point.

\triangleleft

Remark 2.49

Removing the boundedness condition (4) from Definition
2.47
leads to another notion of nilpotent thickenings of smooth manifolds; let us refer to these as
almost inf-manifolds
. The
\infty
-category of almost inf-manifolds
{\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}}^{\mathrm{alm}}
has the advantage of being closed under the following operations:

(1)

Let us say that a sequence of almost inf-manifolds

M_{0}\longrightarrow M_{1}\longrightarrow\cdots

is
almost eventually constant
if for every
n\geqslant 0
, the tower of dg
C^{\infty}
-rings

\tau^{\geqslant-n}\,{\mathscr{O}}(M_{0})\longleftarrow\tau^{\geqslant-n}\,{\mathscr{O}}(M_{1})\longleftarrow\cdots

is eventually constant.
The basic example is (the opposite of) the Postnikov tower. Then
\operatorname{colim}_{k\in\mathbbm{N}_{0}}M_{k}
is an almost inf-manifold, with

{\mathscr{O}}\Big(\underset{k\in\mathbbm{N}_{0}}{\operatorname{colim}}\,M_{k}\Big)=\underset{k\in\mathbbm{N}_{0}}{\operatorname{lim}}\,{\mathscr{O}}(M_{k})\ .

(2)

Let
N\longrightarrow M
be a submersion between smooth manifolds and
M^{\prime}\longrightarrow M
a map from an almost inf-manifold. Then there exists a fibre product
M^{\prime}\times_{M}N
in
{\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}}^{\mathrm{alm}}
, which coincides with the usual fibre product if
M^{\prime}
is a smooth manifold:
indeed,
{\mathscr{O}}(M^{\prime}\times_{M}N)
is simply the completion of the dg
C^{\infty}
-ring
A={\mathscr{O}}(M^{\prime})\amalg^{h}_{{\mathscr{O}}(M)}{\mathscr{O}}(N)
, which has
A_{\mathrm{red}}=C^{\infty}(M^{\prime}_{\mathrm{red}}\times_{M}N)
.

The restriction functor
{\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}}^{\mathrm{alm}})\longrightarrow{\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}})
has a fully faithful right adjoint given by right Kan extension, which can also be presented as sending
X\colon{\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}}\longrightarrow\mathscr{S}
to the presheaf
{\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}(A)\longmapsto\operatorname{lim}_{n\in\mathbbm{N}_{0}}X({\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}(\tau^{\geqslant-n}A))
. The essential image of this right adjoint consists of presheaves
Y
such that
Y(\operatorname{colim}_{k\in\mathbbm{N}_{0}}M_{k})\simeq\operatorname{lim}_{k\in\mathbbm{N}_{0}}Y(M_{k})
for every almost eventually constant sequence of almost inf-manifolds. Essentially all presheaves of geometric interest have this property.
Furthermore, this embedding restricts to the level of
\infty
-sheaves with respect to the Grothendieck topology below.

\triangleleft

Definition 2.50

A map of inf-manifolds
j\colon U={\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}(B)\longrightarrow{\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}(A)=M
is an
open embedding
if
U_{\mathrm{red}}\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,M_{\mathrm{red}}
is an open embedding of smooth manifolds and the map
A\longrightarrow B
exhibits
B
as the initial complete dg
C^{\infty}
-ring under
A
with the following property: each element in
\mathrm{H}^{0}(A)
whose image under
\mathrm{H}^{0}(A)\longrightarrow C^{\infty}(M_{\mathrm{red}})\longrightarrow C^{\infty}(U_{\mathrm{red}})
is invertible is sent to an invertible element of
\mathrm{H}^{0}(B)
.

For every inf-manifold
M
, the full subcategory of
{\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}}_{/M}
on the open embeddings is equivalent to the poset of open subsets of the smooth manifold
M_{\mathrm{red}}
: for every open subspace
U_{\mathrm{red}}\subseteq M_{\mathrm{red}}
, the corresponding open subspace of
M
is
{\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}({\mathscr{O}}_{A}(U_{\mathrm{red}}))
where
{\mathscr{O}}_{A}
is the sheaf from Definition
2.42
.

We endow
{\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}}
with a Grothendieck topology by declaring that a family of open embeddings
\{U_{a}\longrightarrow M\}_{a\in\varLambda}
is a
cover
if and only if
\{U_{a,\mathrm{red}}\longrightarrow M_{\mathrm{red}}\}_{a\in\varLambda}
is an open cover of the underlying smooth manifold in the usual sense. There is then an adjoint pair

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota:{\mathscr{M}\hskip-0.56917pt\mathrm{fd}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}}:r\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\perp}

(2.51)

where
\iota
is the natural inclusion (which is, in particular, fully faithful) and
r
sends each inf-manifold
M
to its underlying smooth manifold
M_{\mathrm{red}}
.
Both functors preserve open covers and hence induce adjunctions between
\infty
-categories of
\infty
-sheaves:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{M}\hskip-0.56917pt\mathrm{fd}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}})\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\perp}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{*}=r^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota^{*}=r_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\perp}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\perp}

(2.52)

Because
{\mathscr{M}\hskip-0.56917pt\mathrm{fd}}
has finite products and
\iota
preserves these (as a consequence of Remark
2.49
), the functor
\iota_{!}
preserves finite products as well.
Furthermore,
r^{*}
has a further right adjoint
r_{*}
, since
r
has the covering lifting property (see for example
[
71
, Appendix A]
or
[
2
, Section A.2]
for background).
In the terminology of
[
76
, Definition 4.2.1]
, this equips the
\infty
-topos
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{M}\hskip-0.56917pt\mathrm{fd}})
with
differential cohesion
.
In practise, we will often leave the functor
\iota
implicit and treat
{\mathscr{M}\hskip-0.56917pt\mathrm{fd}}
as an
\infty
-subcategory of
{\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}}
.

We will always consider
\infty
-sheaves on smooth manifolds as
\infty
-sheaves on inf-manifolds via the functor
\iota_{!}
. For example,
{\mathrm{B}}^{n}{\mathrm{U}}(1)
can be viewed as a higher stack on inf-manifolds, given by the
n
-fold delooping of
{\mathrm{U}}(1)
viewed as an inf-manifold that happens to be an ordinary smooth manifold.

The adjunctions (
2.52
) give rise to two idempotent endofunctors on
\mathrm{Sh}_{\infty}({\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}})
whose value on
X
we will denote by

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{\mathrm{red}}=\iota_{!}\,\iota^{*}X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r^{*}\,\iota^{*}X=X_{\mathrm{dR}}\ ,}

(2.53)

and refer to respectively as the
reduced stack
and the
de Rham stack
of
X
. The de Rham stack is given explicitly by

X_{\mathrm{dR}}(M)=X(M_{\mathrm{red}})\ ,

(2.54)

for each inf-manifold
M
.
As a pleasant property, forming the de Rham stack
X\longmapsto X_{\mathrm{dR}}
preserves both limits and colimits.
Moreover, if
M
is a smooth manifold with a map
x\colon M\longrightarrow X
, then there is a canonical map

X(M)\simeq{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}})(\iota_{!}M,X)\longrightarrow{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}})(\iota_{!}M,\iota_{!}\,\iota^{*}X)\simeq X_{\mathrm{red}}(M)\ ,

(2.55)

where we used that, for any functor of
\infty
-categories
F\colon\mathscr{C}\longrightarrow\mathscr{D}
, the Yoneda embeddings
h
satisfy
h_{F(c)}\simeq F_{!}\,h_{c}
for each
c\in\mathscr{C}
, and that
{\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}}
is subcanonical (Proposition
2.56
below).
In other words, any point of
X
from a smooth manifold factors through
X_{\mathrm{red}}
.

Proposition 2.56

The Grothendieck topology on
{\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}}
is subcanonical, i.e. every representable presheaf is a sheaf.

Proof.

Let
M
and
N
be inf-manifolds and let
\pazocal{U}
be a covering sieve of
M
.
We have to verify that

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}}(M,N)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underset{U\in\pazocal{U}}{\operatorname{lim}}\ {\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}}(U,N)}

(2.57)

is an equivalence. In terms of dg
C^{\infty}
-rings, this corresponds to the assertion that

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{C}^{\infty}\hskip-0.59998pt\mathscr{R}\mathrm{ing}}^{\infty}\big({\mathscr{O}}(N),{\mathscr{O}}(M)\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underset{U\in\pazocal{U}}{\operatorname{lim}}\ {\mathrm{C}^{\infty}\hskip-0.59998pt\mathscr{R}\mathrm{ing}}^{\infty}\big({\mathscr{O}}(N),{\mathscr{O}}(U)\big)}

(2.58)

is an equivalence.
It therefore suffices to verify
{\mathscr{O}}(M)\simeq\operatorname{lim}_{U\in\pazocal{U}}\,{\mathscr{O}}(U)
, which holds exactly by completeness of
{\mathscr{O}}(M)
(Definition
2.42
).
∎

Example 2.59

Every
\infty
-sheaf
X\in{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}})
has an associated
function algebra

\Gamma(X,{\mathscr{O}}_{X})
, given by an—a priori
unbounded
—commutative dg algebra over
\mathbbm{R}
(well-defined up to quasi-isomorphism).
In abstract terms, this is defined as follows.
Let
{\mathrm{cdga}}^{\infty}_{\mathbbm{R}}
denote the
\infty
-category associated to the model category
{\mathrm{cdga}}_{\mathbbm{R}}^{\mathrm{dg}}
of unbounded cdgas (with its model structure inherited from that on
{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{\mathbbm{R}}^{\mathrm{dg}}
along the forgetful functor).
Then there is a functor

{\mathscr{O}}\colon{\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}}^{\mathrm{op}}\longrightarrow{\mathrm{cdga}}_{\mathbbm{R}}^{\infty}\ ,\qquad{\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}(A)\longmapsto A\ ,

(2.60)

which sends each inf-manifold to the corresponding dg
C^{\infty}
-ring and then applies the forgetful functor from dg
C^{\infty}
-rings to cdgas (which we suppress in the notation).

Recall that any open cover
\{U_{a}\longrightarrow M\}_{a\in\varLambda}
of an inf-manifold is determined by an open cover of the underlying manifold.
Thus, for each cover
\{U_{a}\longrightarrow M\}_{a\in\varLambda}
, the map

{\mathscr{O}}(M)\longrightarrow\underset{[n]\in{{\mathbbe{\Delta}}}}{\operatorname{holim}}\ \prod_{a_{0},\dots,a_{n}\in\varLambda}{\mathscr{O}}(U_{a_{0}\cdots a_{n}})

(2.61)

is a quasi-isomorphism by completeness of
{\mathscr{O}}(M)
.
Here we have used the notation
U_{a_{0}\cdots a_{n}}=\bigcap_{i=0}^{n}\,U_{a_{i}}
.
This implies that
{\mathscr{O}}
extends to an adjoint pair

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{co}\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pec}}:{\mathrm{cdga}}_{\mathbbm{R}}^{\infty}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}})^{\mathrm{op}}:\Gamma(-,{\mathscr{O}})\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\perp}

(2.62)

Explicitly, if
X
can be written as a homotopy colimit

X=\underset{\alpha}{\operatorname{hocolim}}\ {\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}(A_{\alpha})

of inf-manifolds, then

\Gamma(X,{\mathscr{O}}_{X})\simeq\underset{\alpha}{\operatorname{holim}}\ A_{\alpha}\ .

The left adjoint
{\mathrm{co}\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pec}}
can be thought of as sending a cdga to the corresponding affine stack in the sense of Toën
[
87
]
(called coaffine stack by Lurie
[
59
]
).

\triangleleft

Example 2.63

The affine stack
{\mathrm{co}\mathscr{S}\mathrm{pec}}(\mathbbm{R}[x_{n}])
associated to a polynomial algebra on a single degree
n
generator is given by the stack
{\mathrm{B}}^{n}\mathbbm{R}
. It then follows from the adjunction (
2.62
) that for each
n\geqslant 0
,

\displaystyle{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}})\big(X,{\mathrm{B}}^{n}\mathbbm{R}\big)

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{{\mathrm{cdga}}_{\mathbbm{R}}}\big(\mathbbm{R}[x_{n}],{\mathscr{O}}(X)\big)

(2.64)

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}^{\mathrm{dg}}_{\mathbbm{R}}}\big(\mathbbm{R}[-n],{\mathscr{O}}(X)\big)

(2.65)

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathrm{DK}}\Big(\tau^{\leqslant 0}\big(\Gamma(X,{\mathscr{O}}_{X})[n]\big)\Big)

(2.66)

is the space corresponding to the non-positively graded complex

0\longleftarrow Z^{n}\Gamma(X,{\mathscr{O}}_{X})\longleftarrow\Gamma(X,{\mathscr{O}}_{X})^{n-1}\longleftarrow\cdots

(2.67)

under the Dold–Kan correspondence, where
Z^{n}\Gamma(X,{\mathscr{O}}_{X})
sits in degree zero.

\triangleleft

2.4
Infinitesimal structure

The infinitesimal thickenings of smooth manifolds included in
{\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}}
allow us to study the infinitesimal structure of a stack on
{\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}}
. For this to be well-behaved, we will impose an analogue of the Schlessinger condition, as studied for example in
[
45
]
in the algebro-geometric setting.

Definition 2.68

Let
S
be a class of morphisms in
{\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}}
, and let
S_{0}
be the class of morphisms
g\colon{\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}(A)\longrightarrow{\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}(A^{\prime})
in
{\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}}
such that the induced map
\mathrm{H}^{0}(A^{\prime})\longrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{0}(A)
is surjective with nilpotent kernel.
A prestack
X\colon{\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}}^{\mathrm{op}}\longrightarrow\mathscr{S}
satisfies the
Schlessinger condition along morphisms in
S
if
X
sends any pushout square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}(B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}(A^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}(B^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

(2.69)

in
{\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}}
, with
g\in S_{0}
and
f\in S
, to a pullback square of spaces.

Remark 2.70

There are various choices for the class of maps along which we impose the Schlessinger condition; see Definition
2.82
for two choices that will be of interest to us.
For now, we let
S=S_{0}
be the class of morphisms; prestacks satisfying the Schlessinger condition along all maps in
S_{0}
are often called
infinitesimally cohesive
.

In this situation, since
\ker(\mathrm{H}^{0}(f))
and
\ker(\mathrm{H}^{0}(g))
are nilpotent, all corners of (
2.69
) have the same underlying smooth manifold.
One can therefore think of the Schlessinger condition as an infinitesimal gluing condition.

\triangleleft

One of the Schlessinger condition’s main features is that it gives rise to a well-behaved notion of tangent space.
To see this, recall that the tangent space
T_{x}M
of a smooth manifold can be identified with the set of first order infinitesimal paths in
M
around
x
.
The
\infty
-category
{\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}}
allows us to describe such first order infinitesimal paths as maps out of the inf-manifold
{\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}(\mathbbm{R}[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})
. More generally, by giving
\epsilon
a negative cohomological degree, one obtains spaces of derived first order infinitesimal paths.
If
X
is infinitesimally cohesive, the resulting spaces of first order paths can be organised into a
linear
object—just as for ordinary smooth manifolds—using the
\infty
-categorical construction we now describe.

Definition 2.71

Let
F\colon\mathscr{C}\longrightarrow\mathscr{D}
be an
\infty
-functor.

(1)

F
is
excisive
if it sends all pushout squares in
\mathscr{C}
to pullback squares in
\mathscr{D}
.
We write
\mathrm{Exc}(\mathscr{C},\mathscr{D})
for the full
\infty
-subcategory of the
\infty
-functor category
{\operatorname{Fun}}(\mathscr{C},\mathscr{D})
on the excisive functors.

(2)

If
\mathscr{C}
also has a final object, then
F
is
reduced excisive
if it additionally preserves the final object.
We write
\mathrm{Exc}_{*}(\mathscr{C},\mathscr{D})
for the full
\infty
-subcategory of
{\operatorname{Fun}}(\mathscr{C},\mathscr{D})
on the reduced excisive functors.

Remark 2.72

Let
A
be a non-positively graded cdga and let
{\mathscr{P}\mathrm{erf}}^{\leqslant 0}_{A}
denote its
\infty
-category of non-positively graded
perfect
modules, i.e. the smallest subcategory of
{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{A}^{\infty}
containing
A
which is closed under retracts and finite homotopy colimits.
It follows from Remark
2.26
(2) that if
A=C^{\infty}(M)
, for a smooth manifold
M
, then each perfect
A
-module is also complete.

Every
A
-module
E
determines a functor

\Phi_{E}\colon{\mathscr{P}\mathrm{erf}}_{A}^{\leqslant 0}\longrightarrow\mathscr{S}\ ,\qquad\Phi_{E}(I)={\mathrm{DK}}\big(E\otimes_{A}I\big)\ .

The discussion in
[
62
, Construction 17.5.1.1]
shows that this determines an equivalence

{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{A}^{\infty}\ \xrightarrow{\ \simeq\ }\ \mathrm{Exc}_{\ast}\big({\mathscr{P}\mathrm{erf}}^{\leqslant 0}_{A},\mathscr{S}\big)\subseteq{\operatorname{Fun}}\big({\mathscr{P}\mathrm{erf}}^{\leqslant 0}_{A},\mathscr{S}\big)\ ,\qquad E\longmapsto\Phi_{E}

(2.73)

between the
\infty
-category of
A
-modules and the
\infty
-category of reduced excisive functors
{\mathscr{P}\mathrm{erf}}^{\leqslant 0}_{A}\longrightarrow\mathscr{S}
.
\triangleleft

Now let
x\colon M\longrightarrow X
be a map from an inf-manifold
M
to a prestack
X
.
Let
{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{A}^{\leqslant 0,\infty}
denote the
\infty
-category of non-positively graded dg
A
-modules.
For any non-positively graded, bounded and complete
{\mathscr{O}}(M)
-module
I
, we write

{\mathrm{Der}}_{X}(x;I)=X\big({\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}({\mathscr{O}}(M)\oplus I)\big)\times_{X(M)}\{x\}

(2.74)

for the space of first order paths around
x
parametrised by
{\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}({\mathscr{O}}(M)\oplus I)
.

When
X
is infinitesimally cohesive, the functor
{\mathrm{Der}}_{X}(x;-)\colon{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{{\mathscr{O}}(M)}^{\leqslant 0,\infty}\longrightarrow\mathscr{S}
is reduced excisive:
first, note that
{\mathrm{Der}}_{X}(x;-)
is reduced.
Then by
[
61
, Proposition 1.4.2.13]
it suffices to check that it sends suspension pushouts to pullbacks.
We can see this as follows:
in
{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}^{\leqslant 0,\infty}_{{\mathscr{O}}(M)}
, the canonical square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I[1]}

(2.75)

is both cartesian and cocartesian.
Taking square-zero extensions by the corners of this square produces a commutative square of dg
C^{\infty}
-rings which is a pullback whose legs are surjective on
\mathrm{H}^{0}
with nilpotent kernel, and so its image under
{\mathrm{Der}}_{X}(x;-)
is a pullback square of
\infty
-groupoids, as desired.

Thus there exists a unique (in the
\infty
-categorical sense)
{\mathscr{O}}(M)
-module
T_{x}X
which presents the restriction of
{\mathrm{Der}}_{X}(x;-)
to
{\mathscr{P}\mathrm{erf}}_{{\mathscr{O}}(M)}^{\leqslant 0}
under the equivalence (
2.73
).
We can therefore make

Definition 2.76

Let
X
be an infinitesimally cohesive prestack, and let
x\colon M\longrightarrow X
be a map from an inf-manifold.
The
tangent complex
of
X
at the point
x
is the unique object
T_{x}X\in{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{{\mathscr{O}}(M)}^{\infty}
equipped with a natural equivalence

{\mathrm{DK}}\big(T_{x}X\otimes_{{\mathscr{O}}(M)}I\big)\simeq{\mathrm{Der}}_{X}(x;I)\ ,

for
I\in{\mathscr{P}\mathrm{erf}}_{{\mathscr{O}}(M)}^{\leqslant 0}
.

Taking
I={\mathscr{O}}(M)[n]
, one sees that, up to equivalence, the complex

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z^{n}T_{x}X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(T_{x}X)^{n-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}

corresponds under the Dold–Kan correspondence to the space
{\mathrm{Der}}_{X}(x;{\mathscr{O}}(M)[n])
of shifted tangent vectors at
x
.

Example 2.77

If the functor
{\mathrm{Der}}_{X}(x;-)\colon{\mathscr{P}\mathrm{erf}}_{{\mathscr{O}}(M)}^{\leqslant 0}\longrightarrow\mathscr{S}
is
corepresented
by a dg
{\mathscr{O}}(M)
-module
L
, then
T_{x}X
is the
{\mathscr{O}}(M)
-linear dual
L^{\vee}
.
For example, for any map
x\colon M\longrightarrow N
to a smooth manifold, the functor
{\mathrm{Der}}_{N}(x;-)
is corepresented by the module
\Omega^{1}(N)\otimes_{C^{\infty}(N)}C^{\infty}(M)\simeq\Gamma(M,x^{*}T^{*}N)
of
1
-forms. It follows that
T_{x}N
is the module of sections of the pullback of the tangent bundle
TN
along
x
.

\triangleleft

To study the dependence of the tangent complex
T_{x}X
on the basepoint
x
, it will be convenient to use a more global version of the above definitions. Restricting attention to basepoints
x\colon M\longrightarrow X
where
M
is a smooth manifold (which suffices for our purposes), let us consider the diagram of
\infty
-categories

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{P}\mathrm{erf}}^{\leqslant 0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}^{\leqslant 0,\infty}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{M}\hskip-0.56917pt\mathrm{fd}}^{\mathrm{op}}\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\kappa}

(2.78)

Here
{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}^{\leqslant 0,\infty}
is the Grothendieck construction of the
\infty
-functor
{\mathscr{M}\hskip-0.56917pt\mathrm{fd}}^{\mathrm{op}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{C}\mathrm{at}}_{\infty}
which sends a manifold
M
to its
\infty
-category of bounded, complete, and non-positively graded dg
C^{\infty}(M)
-modules
{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}^{\leqslant 0,\infty}_{C^{\infty}(M)}
; that is, it is the
\infty
-category of pairs
(M,I)
of a smooth manifold
M
and a bounded, complete, and non-positively graded dg
C^{\infty}(M)
-module
I
, with morphisms
(M,I)\longrightarrow(N,J)
given by a smooth map
f\colon N\longrightarrow M
and a
C^{\infty}(M)
-linear map
I\longrightarrow C^{\infty}(M)\otimes_{C^{\infty}(N)}J
.
Likewise,
{\mathscr{P}\mathrm{erf}}^{\leqslant 0}
is the full
\infty
-subcategory of
{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}^{\leqslant 0,\infty}
on those objects
(M,I)
where
I
is perfect.

Every prestack
X
gives rise to a functor

{\mathrm{Der}}_{X}\colon{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}^{\leqslant 0,\infty}\longrightarrow\mathscr{S}\ ,\qquad(M,I)\longmapsto X\big({\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}(C^{\infty}(M)\oplus I)\big)\ .

If
X
is an
\infty
-sheaf, then
{\mathrm{Der}}_{X}
is an
\infty
-sheaf for the following Grothendieck topology on
({\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}^{\leqslant 0,\infty})^{\mathrm{op}}
:
the open covers of
(M,I)
are of the form
(U_{a},j_{a}^{*}I)
with
j_{a}\colon U_{a}\longrightarrow M
an open cover in the usual sense and
j_{a}^{*}I
the pullback of the complete module
I
(see Remark
2.26
(3)).

The fibre of
{\mathrm{Der}}_{X}(M,I)\longrightarrow{\mathrm{Der}}_{X}(M,0)\simeq X(M)
over a point
x
is precisely the space
{\mathrm{Der}}_{X}(x;I)
from (
2.74
). In particular, the restriction of
{\mathrm{Der}}_{X}
to the full subcategory
{\mathscr{P}\mathrm{erf}}^{\leqslant 0}
encodes the data of all the tangent complexes of
X
at basepoints parametrised by smooth manifolds. In addition, one can use the tangent complex to approximate the values of
{\mathrm{Der}}_{X}
on non-perfect modules:
recall the inclusion
\infty
-functor
\kappa
introduced in (
2.78
).

Lemma 2.79

Let
X
be an infinitesimally cohesive prestack,
M
a smooth manifold and
I
a bounded, complete, and non-positively graded
C^{\infty}(M)
-module.
Consider the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\kappa_{!}\,\kappa^{*}{\mathrm{Der}}_{X}(M,I)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Der}}_{X}(M,I)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Der}}_{X}(M,0)=X(M)}

Then for any point
x\in X(M)
, the induced map between the fibres over
x
of the diagonal arrows can be identified with a natural map

{\mathrm{DK}}\big(T_{x}X\otimes_{C^{\infty}(M)}I\big)\longrightarrow{\mathrm{Der}}_{X}(x;I)\ .

(2.80)

Proof.

It follows by the pointwise formula for the left Kan extension that

\kappa_{!}\,\kappa^{*}{\mathrm{Der}}_{X}(M,I)\simeq\underset{(N,J)\in{\mathscr{P}\mathrm{erf}}^{\leqslant 0}_{/(M,I)}}{\operatorname{colim}}\,{\mathrm{Der}}_{X}(N,J)\simeq\underset{J\in({\mathscr{P}\mathrm{erf}}^{\leqslant 0}_{C^{\infty}(M)})_{/I}}{\operatorname{colim}}\,{\mathrm{Der}}_{X}(M,J)\ ,

where we have used that the inclusion

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big({\mathscr{P}\mathrm{erf}}^{\leqslant 0}_{C^{\infty}(M)}\big)_{/I}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{P}\mathrm{erf}}^{\leqslant 0}_{/(M,I)}}

(2.81)

is cofinal (see the proof of
[
58
, Lemma 4.3.3.9]
).
The first overcategory is taken with respect to the inclusion
{\mathscr{P}\mathrm{erf}}^{\leqslant 0}_{C^{\infty}(M)}\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}^{\leqslant 0,\infty}_{C^{\infty}(M)}
.
Since
\big({\mathscr{P}\mathrm{erf}}^{\leqslant 0}_{C^{\infty}(M)}\big)_{/I}
has finite colimits, it is a filtered
\infty
-category.
It follows that the colimit is a filtered colimit and commutes with taking fibres. Consequently, the fibre of
\kappa_{!}\,\kappa^{*}{\mathrm{Der}}_{X}(M,I)
over a point
x
can be identified with

\underset{J\in({\mathscr{P}\mathrm{erf}}^{\leqslant 0}_{C^{\infty}(M)})_{/I}}{\operatorname{colim}}\,{\mathrm{Der}}_{X}(x;J)\simeq\underset{J\in({\mathscr{P}\mathrm{erf}}^{\leqslant 0}_{C^{\infty}(M)})_{/I}}{\operatorname{colim}}\,{\mathrm{DK}}\big(T_{x}X\otimes_{C^{\infty}(M)}J\big)\simeq{\mathrm{DK}}\big(T_{x}X\otimes_{C^{\infty}(M)}I\big)\ ,

where the last equivalence uses that the Dold–Kan correspondence and the tensor product preserve filtered colimits.
∎

Using this, we now specify two classes of (pre)stacks whose infinitesimal structure is controlled by the tangent complex.

Definition 2.82

A prestack
X
has
deformation theory
(resp.
quasi-coherent deformation theory
) if it satisfies:

(1)

(
Schlessinger condition
) It satisfies the Schlessinger condition from Definition
2.68
along the class of maps
f\colon{\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}(A)\longrightarrow{\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}(B)
in
{\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}}
such that
\mathrm{H}^{0}(B)\longrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{0}(A)
is surjective (resp. along all maps
f\colon{\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}(A)\longrightarrow{\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}(B)
).

(2)

(
Tangent representability
) The map
\kappa_{!}\,\kappa^{*}{\mathrm{Der}}_{X}\longrightarrow{\mathrm{Der}}_{X}
from Lemma
2.79
induces an equivalence on associated
\infty
-sheaves on
{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}^{\leqslant 0,\infty}
.

We will abbreviate the term ‘prestacks with (quasi-coherent) deformation theory’ to (
qc
)
dt
prestacks.

The prestack
X
has
strictly
(
quasi-coherent
)
deformation theory
, or is a
strict
(
qc
)
dt prestack
, if it satisfies condition (1) and the map in (2) is an equivalence even before sheafifying.

Remark 2.83

By Lemma
2.38
, every map in the class
S
from Definition
2.68
decomposes into a finite sequence of square-zero extensions.
In turn, every square-zero extension
{\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}(A)\longrightarrow{\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}(A_{\eta})
of inf-manifolds arises as the pushout of the canonical projection
{\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}(A\oplus I[1])\longrightarrow{\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}(A)
.
To check that a prestack has deformation theory, it therefore suffices to verify that it sends a pushout (
2.69
) to a pullback in the case where
A^{\prime}\longrightarrow A=A^{\prime}\oplus I[1]
is the zero map and
\mathrm{H}^{0}(B)\longrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{0}(A)
is surjective. The same argument applies to quasi-coherent deformation theory.

\triangleleft

We will be mainly interested in the case where
X
is a stack. In this case, the tangent representability condition (2) in Definition
2.82
implies that the tangent complex controls the infinitesimal structure of
X
through

Proposition 2.84

Let
X
be a dt stack and let
x\colon M\longrightarrow X
be a map from a smooth manifold.
Then for every bounded, complete, and non-positively graded
C^{\infty}(M)
-module
I
, there is a natural equivalence

{\mathrm{DK}}\big((T_{x}X\otimes_{C^{\infty}(M)}I)^{\mathrm{cpl}}\big)\simeq{\mathrm{Der}}_{X}(x;I)

where the domain is the Dold–Kan image of the completion of
T_{x}X\otimes_{C^{\infty}(M)}I
.

That is, while the comparison morphism (
2.80
) generally fails to be an equivalence if
I
is not perfect, if
X
is a dt stack the morphism still becomes an equivalence upon completing the module
T_{x}X\otimes_{C^{\infty}(M)}I
.

Proof.

The values of the comparison map (
2.80
) at the open subspaces
j\colon U\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,M
determine a natural transformation of presheaves on
M
of the form

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{DK}}\big(T_{j^{*}x}X\otimes_{C^{\infty}(U)}j^{*}I\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Der}}_{X}(j^{*}x;j^{*}I)\ .}

Since
X
is a stack, the codomain of this map is an
\infty
-sheaf on
M
. By tangent representability, the codomain is the associated sheaf of the domain. The associated sheaf of the domain can be identified explicitly: since
{\mathrm{DK}}
preserves associated sheaves (as a consequence of Remark
2.26
), it corresponds to the associated sheaf of the presheaf of modules
U\longmapsto T_{j^{*}x}\otimes_{C^{\infty}(U)}j^{*}I
, i.e. the tensor product of the two sheaves sending
U
to
T_{j^{*}x}
and
j^{*}I
. By Lemma
2.25
, the global sections of this tensor product of sheaves is the
completion
of
T_{x}X\otimes_{C^{\infty}(M)}I
.
∎

Remark 2.85

The proof of Proposition
2.84
explains why we do not require the map
\kappa_{!}\,\kappa^{*}{\mathrm{Der}}_{X}\longrightarrow{\mathrm{Der}}_{X}
to be an equivalence in Definition
2.82
: the comparison maps (
2.80
) are seldom equivalences when
X
is a stack. Indeed, their codomain satisfies descent, but the domain need not since the tensor product does not commute with infinite homotopy limits.

\triangleleft

The Schlessinger condition (1) in Definition
2.82
allows us to relate tangent complexes at different points of a given dt stack
X
through

Proposition 2.86

Let
X
be a dt stack and let
x\colon M\longrightarrow X
be a map from a smooth manifold. Then for every map of smooth manifolds
f\colon N\longrightarrow M
, there is a natural map of complete
C^{\infty}(N)
-modules

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}T_{x}X=\big(T_{x}X\otimes_{C^{\infty}(M)}C^{\infty}(N)\big)^{\mathrm{cpl}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{f^{*}x}X\ ,}

which is an equivalence if
f
is an open or closed embedding.
If
X
is even a qcdt stack, then this map is an equivalence for all
f\colon N\longrightarrow M
.

In other words, when
X
is a qcdt stack, the tangent complexes
T_{x}X
, where
x
ranges over all morphisms from a smooth manifold to
X
, define a quasi-coherent sheaf on
X_{\mathrm{red}}
(the restriction of
X
along the inclusion
{\mathscr{M}\hskip-0.56917pt\mathrm{fd}}\subset{\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}}
).

Proof.

Let
I
be a perfect non-positively graded
C^{\infty}(N)
-module and let
f_{*}I
be its underlying
C^{\infty}(M)
-module. Using Proposition
2.84
, we obtain a natural map

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{DK}}\big((T_{x}X\otimes_{C^{\infty}(M)}f_{*}I)^{\mathrm{cpl}}\big)\simeq{\mathrm{Der}}_{X}(x;f_{*}I)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Der}}_{X}\big(f^{*}x;I\big)\simeq{\mathrm{DK}}(T_{f^{*}x}X\otimes_{C^{\infty}(N)}I)\ ,}

which arises by restriction along
{\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}(C^{\infty}(N)\oplus I)\longrightarrow{\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}(C^{\infty}(M)\oplus f_{*}I)
. This natural transformation of reduced excisive functors on
{\mathscr{P}\mathrm{erf}}^{\leqslant 0}_{C^{\infty}(N)}
is equivalent to a map of
C^{\infty}(N)
-modules of the form
f^{*}T_{x}X\longrightarrow T_{f^{*}x}X
(see Remark
2.72
).

The Schlessinger condition implies that
{\mathrm{Der}}_{X}(x;f_{*}I)\longrightarrow{\mathrm{Der}}_{X}\big(f^{*}x;I\big)
is an equivalence whenever
X
is a qcdt stack or whenever
X
is a dt stack and
f
is a closed embedding (so that
f^{*}:C^{\infty}(M)\longrightarrow C^{\infty}(N)
is surjective). In both cases it follows that
f^{*}T_{x}X\simeq T_{f^{*}x}X
. Proposition
2.84
and its proof imply that if
X
is a dt stack, then also
f^{*}T_{x}X\simeq T_{f^{*}x}X
whenever
f
is an open embedding.
∎

In particular, it follows that dt stacks admit a formal version of the Inverse Function Theorem given by

Proposition 2.87

Let
f\colon Y\longrightarrow X
be a map of dt stacks. Then
f
is an equivalence if and only if

(1)

the map
Y_{\mathrm{red}}\longrightarrow X_{\mathrm{red}}
is an equivalence, and

(2)

for every map
y\colon M\longrightarrow Y
from a smooth manifold, the map
T_{y}Y\longrightarrow T_{f(y)}X
is an equivalence.

Remark 2.88

By Proposition
2.86
, as soon as condition (2) holds for a point
y\colon M\longrightarrow Y
, it automatically also holds for the restrictions of
y
to all open and all closed submanifolds of
M
.
If
X
is a qcdt stack, then it automatically holds for all points of
X
from manifolds which factor through
y
.

\triangleleft

Proof.

Let
\mathscr{K}
be the class of inf-manifolds
U
such that
Y(U)\longrightarrow X(U)
is an equivalence. Since
Y_{\mathrm{red}}\simeq X_{\mathrm{red}}
by assumption, this class contains all smooth manifolds.
Together with the fact that
f
induces equivalences on tangent complexes, this implies that the induced map
\kappa^{*}{\mathrm{Der}}_{Y}\longrightarrow\kappa^{*}{\mathrm{Der}}_{X}
is a natural equivalence (because the map is an equivalence on all fibres by Lemma
2.79
).
Since
Y
and
X
are dt stacks,
{\mathrm{Der}}_{Y}
and
{\mathrm{Der}}_{X}
are the associated sheaves of
\kappa_{!}\,\kappa^{*}{\mathrm{Der}}_{Y}
and
\kappa_{!}\,\kappa^{*}{\mathrm{Der}}_{X}
by tangent representability. Consequently
{\mathrm{Der}}_{Y}\longrightarrow{\mathrm{Der}}_{X}
is an equivalence as well, which means that
\mathscr{K}
contains all square-zero extensions
{\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}(C^{\infty}(M)\oplus I)
of a smooth manifold
M
by a bounded, complete, and non-positively graded
C^{\infty}(M)
-module
I
.

Because
X
and
Y
have deformation theory, the class
\mathscr{K}
is then stable under square-zero extensions by bounded, complete, and non-positively graded
C^{\infty}(M)
-modules, for any smooth manifold
M
. Using Lemma
2.38
(and taking completions of all dg
C^{\infty}
-rings involved), one finds that any inf-manifold can be obtained by such a sequence of square-zero extensions, so that
\mathscr{K}
contains all inf-manifolds.
∎

2.5
Examples and constructions of stacks with deformation theory

Let us now turn to examples of stacks with (quasi-coherent) deformation theory, as well as several ways of constructing such stacks that will be important later in this paper.

Example 2.89

Every smooth manifold
N
has strictly quasi-coherent deformation theory (see Definition
2.82
).
Indeed, any representable stack satisfies the Schlessinger condition along all maps
{\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}(A)\longrightarrow{\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}(B)
, since
{\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}}(-,N)
sends all pushouts to pullbacks.

For condition (2) of Definition
2.82
, we note that for any map of smooth manifolds
x\colon M\longrightarrow N
, the functor
{\mathrm{Der}}_{N}(x;-)
is corepresented by the finite-type projective module
L=\Omega^{1}(N)\otimes_{C^{\infty}(N)}C^{\infty}(M)
.
By Example
2.77
, the map (
2.80
) is then the natural map
L^{\vee}\otimes_{C^{\infty}(M)}I\longrightarrow{\operatorname{Hom}}_{C^{\infty}(N)}(L,I)
.
It is an equivalence because finite-type projective modules are dualisable.

\triangleleft

Example 2.90

Recall the notion of de Rham stack from (
2.53
).
For each stack
X\in{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}})
, the de Rham stack
X_{\mathrm{dR}}
has strictly quasi-coherent deformation theory. The Schlessinger condition is satisfied because
{\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}(A)_{\mathrm{red}}={\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}(A^{\prime})_{\mathrm{red}}
, so that
X_{\mathrm{dR}}({\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}(A))\simeq X_{\mathrm{dR}}({\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}(A^{\prime}))
, and likewise for
B
and
B^{\prime}
(in the notation of Definition
2.68
).
From (
2.74
) we see
{\mathrm{Der}}_{X_{\mathrm{dR}}}(x;I)\simeq*
, for any point
x
and any non-positively graded, bounded, and perfect module
I
, since
{\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}({\mathscr{O}}(M)\oplus I)_{\mathrm{red}}={\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}({\mathscr{O}}(M))
.
Therefore the domain and codomain of (
2.80
) are both contractible, implying condition (2) of Definition
2.82
.

\triangleleft

To produce more examples of (pre)stacks with quasi-coherent deformation theory and help with computations later on, we record the following properties of (qc)dt prestacks and stacks:
we write
{\operatorname{Hom}}
for the internal hom, or mapping (pre)stack, in both
{\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}})
and
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}})
.

Proposition 2.91

(1)

If
X
is a dt (resp. qcdt) prestack, the associated stack
\widetilde{X}
is also dt (resp. qcdt).

(2)

The classes of prestacks and stacks with (quasi-coherent) deformation theory are each closed under finite limits and filtered colimits.

(3)

If
X
is a (pre)stack with deformation theory and
M
is a smooth manifold, then the mapping stack
{\operatorname{Hom}}(M,X)
has deformation theory as well.
For every morphism
x^{\dashv}\colon N\longrightarrow{\operatorname{Hom}}(M,X)
from a smooth manifold, adjoint to a morphism
x\colon M\times N\longrightarrow X
, there is an equivalence of complete
C^{\infty}(N)
-modules

T_{x^{\dashv}}{\operatorname{Hom}}(M,X)\simeq\pi_{N*}\big(T_{x}X\big)\ ,

where
\pi_{N}\colon M\times N\longrightarrow N
is the canonical projection.

Assertion (3) does not hold for qcdt prestacks, because Lemma
2.34
does not hold without the condition that
\mathrm{H}^{0}(B)\longrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{0}(A)
is surjective.

Proof.

For (1), let us first consider the Schlessinger condition. Given a pushout square of inf-manifolds as in (
2.69
), together with an open subspace
U\longrightarrow{\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}(B^{\prime})
, restricting to
U
gives another pushout square of inf-manifolds. Furthermore, if the map
\mathrm{H}^{0}(B)\longrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{0}(A)
was surjective in the original square, then the resulting map for the square of restrictions is surjective as well.
Varying
U
and applying
X
to each of the four corners of the square gives a commuting square of presheaves on the underlying manifold
{\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}(B^{\prime})_{\mathrm{red}}
. Since
X
has (quasi-coherent) deformation theory, this square of presheaves is a pullback square. Applying the same construction to
\widetilde{X}
provides a commuting square of
\infty
-sheaves on
{\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}(B^{\prime})_{\mathrm{red}}
, which is obtained from the square for
X
by taking associated sheaves. The result now follows from the fact that
\infty
-sheafification is left exact. (See
[
68
, Lemma 6.1.6]
for a similar proof.)

For tangent representability, note that the map
{\mathrm{Der}}_{X}\longrightarrow{\mathrm{Der}}_{\widetilde{X}}
induces an equivalence on associated sheaves. Since
\kappa
preserves covers and has the covering lifting property, it follows that
\kappa_{!}\,\kappa^{*}{\mathrm{Der}}_{X}\longrightarrow\kappa_{!}\,\kappa^{*}{\mathrm{Der}}_{\widetilde{X}}
induces an equivalence on associated sheaves as well. The result then follows from the two-out-of-three property.

For assertion (2), note that the Schlessinger condition is a pullback condition. Consequently, any limit or filtered colimit of prestacks satisfying the Schlessinger condition will do so as well. Tangent representability follows from the fact that
\kappa_{!}\,\kappa^{*}{\mathrm{Der}}_{X}
and
{\mathrm{Der}}_{X}
(as well as their associated sheaves) both preserve fibre products and pointwise colimits in the variable
X
.

For (3), we will use the fully faithful inclusion
{\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}})\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,{\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}}^{\mathrm{alm}})
from Remark
2.49
. Since this inclusion preserves mapping stacks, we may view
X
and
M
as (representable) presheaves on almost inf-manifolds and work with
{\operatorname{Hom}}(M,X)
in
{\mathscr{PS}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}}^{\mathrm{alm}})
. The Schlessinger condition for
{\operatorname{Hom}}(M,X)
then follows by adjunction: for any square of the form (
2.69
), taking the product of all its objects with the smooth manifold
M
produces another square of the form (
2.69
), by Lemma
2.34
. The result then follows from the fact that
X
sends such squares to pullback squares.

For the tangent representability condition, let us fix a map
x^{\dashv}\colon N\longrightarrow{\operatorname{Hom}}(M,X)
, adjoint to a map
x\colon M{\times}N\longrightarrow X
. Using Lemma
2.34
, for each
C^{\infty}(N)
-module
I
there is a natural equivalence

{\mathrm{Der}}_{{\operatorname{Hom}}(M,X)}(x^{\dashv};I)\simeq{\mathrm{Der}}_{X}\big(x;C^{\infty}(M\times N)\otimes^{h}_{C^{\infty}(N)}I\big)\ .

By Remark
2.72
and Definition
2.76
this implies that
T_{x^{\dashv}}{\operatorname{Hom}}(M,X)\simeq\pi_{N*}\big(T_{x}X\big)
, and that there are equivalences

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{DK}}\big(T_{x^{\dashv}}{\operatorname{Hom}}(M,X)\otimes^{h}_{C^{\infty}(N)}I\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Der}}_{{\operatorname{Hom}}(M,X)}(x^{\dashv};I)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{DK}}\big((\pi_{N*}T_{x}X)\otimes^{h}_{C^{\infty}(N)}I\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Der}}_{X}\big(x;C^{\infty}(M\times N)\otimes^{h}_{C^{\infty}(N)}I\big)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

The bottom map induces equivalences on
\infty
-sheaves on
N
because
X
is a dt prestack, so that the same holds for the top map.
This completes the proof by Lemma
2.79
.
∎

Example 2.92

Let
M
be a smooth manifold, and consider the functor

\Omega^{p}_{v}(M)\colon{\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}}^{\mathrm{op}}\longrightarrow\mathscr{S}

sending each inf-manifold
U
to the space of vertical
p
-forms on
U\times M
, i.e. the
p
-forms with legs along
M
only. Algebraically, this space can be identified under the Dold–Kan correspondence as

\Omega^{p}_{v}(M)(U)\simeq{\mathrm{DK}}\big({\mathscr{O}}(U\times M)\otimes^{h}_{C^{\infty}(M)}\Omega^{p}(M)\big)\ .

Then
\Omega^{p}_{v}(M)
is a stack with deformation theory (but not with quasi-coherent deformation theory). Indeed, to verify the Schlessinger condition along maps
{\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}(A)\longrightarrow{\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}(B)
such that
\mathrm{H}^{0}(B)\longrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{0}(A)
is surjective, we use Remark
2.83
to reduce to the case where
A=A^{\prime}\oplus I[1]
, so that
B^{\prime}=B_{\eta}
is a square-zero extension of
B
. We then have to verify that the square of complexes
is a homotopy pullback, which follows from Lemma
2.34
and the fact that
\Omega^{p}(M)
is a projective
C^{\infty}(M)
-module.

For tangent representability, since
\Omega^{p}_{v}(M)
has the natural structure of an abelian group (in fact an
\mathbbm{R}
-module) and unravelling the definitions, one sees that for any point
\alpha\colon N\longrightarrow\Omega^{p}_{v}(M)
from a smooth manifold
N
, there are canonical equivalences

T_{\alpha}\Omega^{p}_{v}(M)\simeq T_{0}\Omega^{p}_{v}(M)\simeq\Omega^{p}_{v}(N\times M)=C^{\infty}(N\times M)\otimes_{C^{\infty}(M)}\Omega^{p}(M)\ .

Similarly

{\mathrm{Der}}_{\Omega^{p}_{v}(M)}(\alpha;I)\simeq{\mathrm{DK}}\big(I\otimes_{C^{\infty}(N)}C^{\infty}(N\times M)\otimes_{C^{\infty}(M)}\Omega^{p}(M)\big)\ ,

(2.93)

which shows that the maps (
2.80
) are equivalences.

\triangleleft

Example 2.94

For every smooth manifold
M
, consider the cochain complex of stacks in cochain degrees
[0,\dim(M)-p]
given by

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega^{p}_{v}(M)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega^{p+1}_{v}(M)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega^{\dim(M)}_{v}(M)\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{d}_{v}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{d}_{v}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{d}_{v}}

where
\mathrm{d}_{v}
is the de Rham differential along
M
.
This associates a cochain complex of simplicial
\mathbbm{R}
-modules to each inf-manifold
U
. Under the cosimplicial Dold–Kan correspondence, this corresponds to a (coskeletal) cosimplicial diagram in
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}})
, and we define
\Omega^{\geqslant p}_{v}(M)\colon{\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}}^{\mathrm{op}}\longrightarrow\mathscr{S}
to be its limit. Explicitly,
\Omega^{\geqslant p}_{v}(M)
acts as

U\longmapsto{\mathrm{DK}}\Big(\tau^{\leqslant 0}\,\mathrm{Tot}\big({\mathscr{O}}(U\times M)\otimes^{h}_{C^{\infty}(M)}\Omega^{\geqslant p}(M),\mathrm{d}_{v}\big)\Big)\ ,

where
\Omega^{\geqslant p}(M)
denotes the complex of differential forms on
M
in form degrees at least
p
.
For example, its value on a
smooth
manifold
U
is the set of vertically closed vertical
p
-forms on
U\times M\longrightarrow U
.

The stack
\Omega^{\geqslant p}_{v}(M)
has deformation theory, since it is a finite limit of dt stacks. Similarly to Example
2.92
, the tangent complex at a map
\alpha\colon N\longrightarrow\Omega^{\geqslant p}_{v}(M)
is equivalent to the complex
\Omega^{\geqslant p}_{v}(N\times M)
with the vertical de Rham differential.

\triangleleft

Example 2.95

In contrast to Examples
2.92
and
2.94
, now consider the functor
\Omega^{1}\colon{\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}}\longrightarrow\mathscr{S}
which assigns to each inf-manifold its space of
1
-forms. This functor does
not
have deformation theory. Indeed, consider the pullback square of square-zero extensions of dg
C^{\infty}
-rings

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbbm{R}[\epsilon_{0}]\,\big/\,\epsilon_{0}^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbbm{R}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbbm{R}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbbm{R}[\epsilon_{-1}]\,\big/\,\epsilon_{-1}^{2}}

(2.96)

The bottom right corner is the free dg
C^{\infty}
-ring on a generator of degree
-1
(which squares to zero by the Koszul sign rule). Its space of
1
-forms corresponds under the Dold–Kan correspondence to the complex

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbbm{R}\,\epsilon_{-1}\,\mathrm{d}\epsilon_{-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbbm{R}\,\mathrm{d}\epsilon_{-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

(2.97)

concentrated in degrees
-1
and
-2
.
Since the space of
1
-forms on
\ast={\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}(\mathbbm{R})
is zero, the homotopy pullback of spaces of
1
-forms is given by the two-term complex
\mathbbm{R}\,\epsilon_{-1}\,\mathrm{d}\epsilon_{-1}\longrightarrow\mathbbm{R}\,\mathrm{d}\epsilon_{-1}
in degrees
0
and
-1
. This is not weakly equivalent to the space of
1
-forms on
{\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}(\mathbbm{R}[\epsilon_{0}]/\epsilon_{0}^{2})
, whose set of path components is the two-dimensional vector space
(\mathbbm{R}[\epsilon_{0}]/\epsilon_{0}^{2})\,\mathrm{d}\epsilon_{0}
.

An analogous argument for the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbbm{R}[\epsilon_{1,0},\ldots,\epsilon_{p,0}]\,\big/\,\{\epsilon_{i,0}\,\epsilon_{j,0}\,|\,i,j=1,\ldots,p\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbbm{R}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbbm{R}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbbm{R}[\epsilon_{1,-1},\ldots,\epsilon_{p,-1}]\,\big/\,\{\epsilon_{i,-1}\,\epsilon_{j,-1}\,|\,i,j=1,\ldots,p\}}

(2.98)

shows that

\Omega^{p}\colon{\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}}\longrightarrow\mathscr{S}

does not have deformation theory for any
p\in\mathbbm{N}
.

\triangleleft

Finally, the class of qcdt prestacks also includes quotients by formally smooth higher groupoids.

Definition 2.99

A map between prestacks
f\colon Y\longrightarrow X
is
formally smooth
if in any square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}(A^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

(2.100)

where
\mathrm{H}^{0}(A^{\prime})\longrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{0}(A)
is surjective with nilpotent kernel, there exists a dashed lift as indicated.

Remark 2.101

By Lemma
2.38
, a map between prestacks with deformation theory is formally smooth if and only if it has the right lifting property against all maps
{\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}(A\oplus I[1])\longrightarrow{\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}(A)
. In fact, it suffices to check this when
A=C^{\infty}(M)
for a smooth manifold: Remark
2.40
shows that any morphism
{\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}(A\oplus I[1])\longrightarrow{\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}(A)
can be further decomposed into a sequence of square-zero extensions by modules over
A_{\mathrm{red}}=C^{\infty}(M)
, i.e. into pushouts of maps of the form
{\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}(C^{\infty}(M)\oplus J[1])\longrightarrow{\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}(C^{\infty}(M))
for a
C^{\infty}(M)
-module
J
.

\triangleleft

We obtain a formal version of the Submersion Theorem given by

Proposition 2.102

Let
f\colon Y\longrightarrow X
be a map between qcdt stacks. Then
f
is formally smooth if and only if for any map from a smooth manifold
y\colon M\longrightarrow Y
, the fibre of the map of tangent complexes
T_{y}Y\longrightarrow T_{f(y)}X
has vanishing cohomology in positive degrees.

Proof.

By Remark
2.101
,
f
is formally smooth if and only if there exists a lift

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}(C^{\infty}(M)\oplus I[1])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}

for every smooth manifold
M
and complete non-positively graded
C^{\infty}(M)
-module
I
. In this case, the lifting condition is equivalent to the map

{\mathrm{Der}}_{Y}(M,0)\longrightarrow{\mathrm{Der}}_{Y}(M,I[1])\times_{{\mathrm{Der}}_{X}(M,I[1])}{\mathrm{Der}}_{X}(M,0)

having non-empty fibres. Unravelling the definitions using Proposition
2.84
, this means that the map

\big(T_{y}Y\otimes_{C^{\infty}(M)}I[1]\big)^{\mathrm{cpl}}\longrightarrow\big(T_{f(y)}X\otimes_{C^{\infty}(M)}I[1]\big)^{\mathrm{cpl}}

has fibre with cohomology concentrated in strictly negative degrees, for every point
y
and every choice of
I
. Because completion can be computed at the level of cohomology groups (Remark
2.26
), this is equivalent to the fibre of
T_{y}Y\longrightarrow T_{f(y)}X
having cohomology concentrated in non-positive degrees.
∎

Example 2.103

Any submersion between smooth manifolds
\pi\colon M\longrightarrow N
is formally smooth: this follows directly from Proposition
2.102
and the fact that for any map of smooth manifolds
f\colon V\longrightarrow M
, the map of tangent bundles
f^{*}TM\longrightarrow f^{*}\,\pi^{*}TN
is surjective.

\triangleleft

Definition 2.104

Let
X_{\bullet}
be a simplicial diagram of prestacks with deformation theory. Then
X_{\bullet}
is a
formally smooth
\infty
-groupoid
if for each horn inclusion the map

X_{n}\longrightarrow X(\Lambda^{n}_{i})=\underset{\Delta^{k}\longrightarrow\Lambda^{n}_{i}}{\operatorname{holim}}\ X_{k}

(2.105)

is formally smooth.

Proposition 2.106

Let
X_{\bullet}
be a formally smooth
\infty
-groupoid. If each
X_{n}
has (quasi-coherent) deformation theory, then the colimit
|X_{\bullet}|
has (quasi-coherent) deformation theory as well and

T_{x}\big|X_{\bullet}\big|\simeq\big|T_{x}X_{\bullet}\big|

for all
x\colon M\longrightarrow X_{0}\longrightarrow|X_{\bullet}|
.

Proof.

First, recall that a morphism
K\longrightarrow L
of simplicial spaces is called a
realisation fibration
if, for every map of simplicial spaces
S\longrightarrow L
, the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{|S\times_{L}K|}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{|K|}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{|S|}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{|L|}}

(2.107)

is homotopy cartesian in
\mathscr{S}

[
72
]
.
In other words, the canonical morphism
|S\times_{L}K|\longrightarrow|S|\times_{|L|}|K|
is an equivalence of spaces.

For the Schlessinger condition, consider a pushout square (
2.69
) whose image under each
X_{n}
is a pullback square. It remains to verify that this pullback is preserved under taking geometric realisations. This follows from the formal smoothness conditions: these imply that the map of simplicial spaces
X_{\bullet}({\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}(A^{\prime}))\longrightarrow X_{\bullet}({\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}(A))
is a Kan fibration of simplicial spaces
[
6
, Definition 3.2]
, and thus also a realisation fibration
[
6
, Theorem 3.17]
.
Tangent representability and the description of the tangent complex follows from
{\mathrm{Der}}_{|X_{\bullet}|}\simeq|{\mathrm{Der}}_{X_{\bullet}}|
and
\kappa_{!}\,\kappa^{*}{\mathrm{Der}}_{|X_{\bullet}|}\simeq|\kappa_{!}\,\kappa^{*}{\mathrm{Der}}_{X_{\bullet}}|
.
∎

Example 2.108

Every (non-derived, higher) differentiable stack
X
has deformation theory: indeed, it arises as the geometric realisation (computed in stacks) of a Lie
\infty
-groupoid
X_{\bullet}
, each of whose horn-filling maps
X_{n}\longrightarrow X(\Lambda^{n}_{i})
is a submersion and hence formally smooth.

One can use Proposition
2.106
and Example
2.89
to compute (locally) the tangent complex of higher differentiable stacks. For example, let
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
be a Lie groupoid and
x\colon M\longrightarrow[M/\mathscr{G}]
the canonical map to the associated quotient stack. Then
T_{x}[M/\mathscr{G}]
is the complex of vector bundles over
M
corresponding under the Dold–Kan correspondence to the simplicial vector bundle

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots\ }
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e^{*}T\big(\mathscr{G}\times_{M}\mathscr{G})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e^{*}T\mathscr{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TM\ ,}

where
e\colon M\longrightarrow\mathscr{G}\times_{M}\dots\times_{M}\mathscr{G}
denotes the iterated degeneracy maps. Under the Dold–Kan correspondence, this corresponds precisely to the two-term complex

{\mathfrak{g}}\longrightarrow TM

given in degree
-1
by the Lie algebroid
{\mathfrak{g}}
of
\mathscr{G}
and with differential given by the anchor map.

\triangleleft

3
Lie differentiation and infinitesimal symmetries

In this section we discuss how one can use derived
C^{\infty}
-geometry to associate an
L_{\infty}
-algebroid to a map
x\colon M\longrightarrow X
from a smooth manifold to a dt stack.
One can think of the morphism
x\colon M\longrightarrow X
as classifying a certain type of geometric structure on
M
.
The
L_{\infty}
-algebroid we produce can then be understood as the
L_{\infty}
-version of its Atiyah algebroid.
This perspective is the starting point for the theory of connections on higher bundles we develop in the later sections.
Furthermore, the
L_{\infty}
-algebra of global sections of this
L_{\infty}
-algebroid can be thought of as the
L_{\infty}
-algebra of infinitesimal symmetries of the map
x
. We will start by stating the main (formal) results and illustrating how they can be used in examples. The technical details behind the main theorems are discussed in Appendix
A
(Theorem
3.2
) and in Appendix
B
(Theorem
3.14
).

3.1
Formal differentiation and integration

The relevance of the derived geometric formalism we developed Section
2
to the problem of differentiating higher stacks rests in the following result.

Definition 3.1

Let
M
be a smooth manifold.
We write

\mathrm{St}^{\mathrm{qcdt}}_{M/}\subseteq\mathrm{St}^{\mathrm{dt}}_{M/}\subseteq{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}})_{M/}

for the full
\infty
-subcategories on those morphisms
M\longrightarrow X
where
X
is a dt (resp. qcdt) stack.

Given a dg
C^{\infty}
-ring
A
, let
{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}^{{\mathrm{cpl}},\infty}_{A}\subset{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}^{\infty}_{A}
denote the full
\infty
-subcategory on the complete dg
A
-modules, and let
L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{{\mathrm{cpl}},\infty}\subset L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\infty}
denote the full
\infty
-subcategory on those
L_{\infty}
-algebroids whose underlying dg
A
-module is complete.

Theorem 3.2

Let
M
be a smooth manifold and let
x\colon M\longrightarrow X
be a map to a dt stack.
Then the
relative tangent complex

T(M/X)\coloneqq{\mathrm{hofib}}(TM\longrightarrow T_{x}X)\ \in\ \big({\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}^{{\mathrm{cpl}},\infty}_{C^{\infty}(M)}\big)_{/TM}

has a natural lift to an object in the
\infty
-category
L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{M}^{{\mathrm{cpl}},\infty}
of complete
L_{\infty}
-algebroids over
M
. This determines a right adjoint functor of
\infty
-categories

T(M/-)\colon\mathrm{St}^{\mathrm{dt}}_{M/}\longrightarrow L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{M}^{{\mathrm{cpl}},\infty}\ ,

whose left adjoint is fully faithful and has essential image given by the full subcategory of
\mathrm{St}^{\mathrm{dt}}_{M/}
on those morphisms
M\longrightarrow X
where
X
is a qcdt stack such that
M\longrightarrow X_{\mathrm{red}}
is an equivalence.

Furthermore, for any morphism
M\longrightarrow X
in this essential image, there is an equivalence

\Gamma(X,\mathscr{O}_{X})\simeq{\mathrm{CE}}^{*}\big(T(M/X)\big)

between the function cdga of
X
(Example
2.59
) and the Chevalley–Eilenberg cdga of
T(M/X)
.

An algebro-geometric version of part of this theorem appears as
[
45
, Proposition V.1.4.2]
; combined with
[
70
, Theorem 8.2]
, this identifies the notion of
L_{\infty}
-algebroid used there with the one from Definition
2.4
.
When
M=\ast
is a point, this recovers the principle that for any point
x\colon\ast\longrightarrow X
of a suitable stack, the shifted tangent complex
T_{x}X[-1]
has the structure of an
L_{\infty}
-algebra (see for example
[
46
,
60
]
).
Here the shift arises from the equivalences

T(*/X)\coloneqq{\mathrm{hofib}}(0\longrightarrow T_{x}X)\simeq T_{x}X[-1]\ .

(3.3)

When
G
is a (finite-dimensional) Lie
\infty
-group, an explicit description of the Lie differentiation of the map
\ast\longrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}G
is given in
[
73
]
. When
M
is a manifold, its seems more difficult to describe the Lie differentiation of a map
M\longrightarrow X
explicitly, because such maps are typically presented as maps out of a hypercover of
M
. In the special case where
M\longrightarrow X
arises from a map
M\longrightarrow X_{\bullet}
of Lie
\infty
-groupoids, without taking a hypercover of
M
, then the Lie differentiation given by Theorem
3.2
probably coincides with the one proposed by Ševera
[
77
,
57
]
.

Example 3.4

The de Rham stack
M_{\mathrm{dR}}
is (by definition) the terminal stack equipped with a map
M\longrightarrow M_{\mathrm{dR}}
inducing an equivalence on reductions. In addition,
M_{\mathrm{dR}}
has strictly quasi-coherent deformation theory by Example
2.90
. It follows that
M_{\mathrm{dR}}
corresponds under the equivalence of Theorem
3.2
to the terminal Lie algebroid
TM
; that is, there is a canonical equivalence

T(M/M_{\mathrm{dR}})\simeq TM\ .

Similarly, the initial Lie algebroid
0
corresponds to
M
itself, i.e. it is the relative tangent complex of the identity map on
M
.

\triangleleft

Corollary 3.5

For any smooth manifold
M
and retract diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}

(3.6)

where
X
is a dt stack, the relative tangent bundle
T(M/X)
has the natural structure of a complete
C^{\infty}(M)
-linear
L_{\infty}
-algebra. In fact, there is an equivalence between complete
C^{\infty}(M)
-linear
L_{\infty}
-algebras and retract diagrams (
3.6
) where
X
is a qcdt stack such that
M\longrightarrow X_{\mathrm{red}}
is an equivalence.

Proof.

Theorem
3.2
and Example
3.4
show that
T(M/X)
defines a complete
L_{\infty}
-algebroid over the zero Lie algebroid, that is, a complete
C^{\infty}(M)
-linear
L_{\infty}
-algebra.
∎

The second part of Theorem
3.2
quantifies how much of the structure of
X
can be recovered from the
L_{\infty}
-algebroid
T(M/X)
. It essentially asserts that
T(M/X)
encodes the infinitesimal structure of
X
supported at
M
, or more precisely its formal completion.

Definition 3.7

Let
M\longrightarrow X
be any map in
\mathrm{Sh}_{\infty}({\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}})
. The
formal completion
of
X
along
M
is the fibre product

X^{\wedge}_{M}\coloneqq X\times_{X_{\mathrm{dR}}}M_{\mathrm{dR}}\ .

(3.8)

Evaluating
X^{\wedge}_{M}
at an inf-manifold
U
, one sees that

X^{\wedge}_{M}(U)=X(U)\times_{X(U_{\mathrm{red}})}M(U_{\mathrm{red}})

describes the infinitesimal structure of
X
around
M
. In particular
X^{\wedge}_{M}\simeq X
as soon as
M_{\mathrm{red}}\simeq X_{\mathrm{red}}
.

Corollary 3.9

Let
x\colon M\longrightarrow X
be a map from a smooth manifold to a qcdt stack. Then the counit map of the adjunction from Theorem
3.2
is equivalent to the map
X^{\wedge}_{M}\longrightarrow X
.

In particular, the data of the
L_{\infty}
-algebroid
T(M/X)
completely recovers precisely the formal completion
X^{\wedge}_{M}
. When
X
is not a qcdt stack, the counit map is much less explicit.

Proof.

Since
X
has quasi-coherent deformation theory,
X^{\wedge}_{M}
is a stack with quasi-coherent deformation theory whose reduction is equivalent to
M
. Since
T(M/X^{\wedge}_{M})\longrightarrow T(M/X)
is an equivalence as a direct consequence of (
2.74
) and Definition
3.7
, the result follows from Theorem
3.2
.
∎

Remark 3.10

As an application of Corollary
3.9
, suppose that
{\mathfrak{g}}
is a complete
L_{\infty}
-algebroid over
M
and that
f\colon N\longrightarrow M
is a map of smooth manifolds. Then
f^{*}{\mathfrak{g}}\times^{h}_{f^{*}TM}TN
can be endowed with a natural
L_{\infty}
-algebroid structure: if
M\longrightarrow X
is the qcdt stack associated to
{\mathfrak{g}}
by Theorem
3.2
, then
f^{*}{\mathfrak{g}}\times_{f^{*}TM}TN
is the underlying complex of the
L_{\infty}
-algebroid of
X^{\wedge}_{N}
.

\triangleleft

Every
L_{\infty}
-algebroid
{\mathfrak{g}}
over a smooth manifold
M
gives rise to an
\mathbbm{R}
-linear
L_{\infty}
-algebra
\Gamma(M,{\mathfrak{g}})
by taking global sections; in terms of the Serre–Swan perspective we are employing, this amounts to forgetting the
C^{\infty}(M)
-linear structure on
{\mathfrak{g}}
while remembering its
\mathbbm{R}
-linear structure and brackets. For example, when
{\mathfrak{g}}=TM
is the tangent bundle, we recover the Lie algebra of vector fields on
M
.
At the same time, this
\mathbbm{R}
-linear Lie algebra should also arise in a different way: if we consider the diffeomorphism group
\mathsf{Diff}(M)
as an object in
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}})
, then the Lie algebra of vector fields should be its Lie differentiation. One can make this more precise through

Definition 3.11

Let

\mathsf{Diff}(M)\colon{\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}}^{\mathrm{op}}\longrightarrow\mathscr{S}

be the stack sending each inf-manifold
U
to the space of equivalences relative to
U
, i.e. the space of commutative triangles in
{\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}}
of the form

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U\times M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U\times M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\pi_{U},\phi)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{U}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{U}}

Lemma 3.12

The stack
\mathsf{Diff}(M)
has deformation theory and is formally smooth, i.e.
\mathsf{Diff}(M)\longrightarrow\ast
is formally smooth in the sense of Definition
2.99
.

Proof.

By Lemma
2.38
(2), a map
(\pi_{U},\phi)\colon U\times M\longrightarrow U\times M
is an equivalence (over
U
) if and only if its pullback
U_{\mathrm{red}}\times M\longrightarrow U_{\mathrm{red}}\times M
is a diffeomorphism of smooth manifolds. This implies that the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Diff}(M)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Hom}}(M,M)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Diff}(M)_{\mathrm{dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Hom}}(M,M)_{\mathrm{dR}}}

(3.13)

is a pullback. Examples
2.89
and
2.90
together with Proposition
2.91
then imply that
\mathsf{Diff}(M)
is a pullback of qcdt stacks, and hence a qcdt stack itself.

It follows from Proposition
2.102
that
\mathsf{Diff}(M)
is formally smooth if for each map
\phi^{\dashv}\colon U\longrightarrow\mathsf{Diff}(M)
from a smooth manifold
U
, the tangent complex
T_{\phi^{\dashv}}\mathsf{Diff}(M)
has vanishing positive cohomology. Using the pullback square (
3.13
) and Proposition
2.91
(3), one then deduces

T_{\phi^{\dashv}}\mathsf{Diff}(M)\simeq T_{\phi^{\dashv}}{\operatorname{Hom}}(M,M)\simeq\pi_{U*}(T_{\phi}M)\ .

In other words, this is the complete
C^{\infty}(U)
-module underlying the
C^{\infty}(U\times M)
-module
\phi^{*}TM
, where
\phi\colon U\times M\longrightarrow M
is adjoint to
\phi^{\dashv}
. This module is concentrated in degree
0
, so the result follows.
∎

Composition of equivalences endows
\mathsf{Diff}(M)
with the structure of an
\infty
-group, and we will write
{\mathrm{B}}\mathsf{Diff}(M)
for the corresponding classifying stack. Similarly, each mapping stack
{\operatorname{Hom}}(M,X)
carries a natural right action
\varrho
of
\mathsf{Diff}(M)
by pre-composition, encoded by a simplicial diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Hom}}(M,X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Hom}}(M,X)\times\mathsf{Diff}(M)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varrho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}}

where
\pi_{1}
is the projection to the first factor.
Lemma
3.12
and Proposition
2.91
imply that for any dt stack
X
and any manifold
M
, this is a formally smooth
\infty
-groupoid. Proposition
2.106
then implies that the corresponding quotient stack
{\operatorname{Hom}}(M,X)/\mathsf{Diff}(M)

[
66
, Definition 3.1]
is a dt stack as well. In particular, taking
X=\ast
shows that
{\mathrm{B}}\mathsf{Diff}(M)
is a dt stack itself. We give a more precise description of the stacks
{\mathrm{B}}\mathsf{Diff}(M)
and
{\operatorname{Hom}}(M,X)/\mathsf{Diff}(M)
in Appendix
B
.

Every morphism
x\colon M\longrightarrow X
induces a basepoint

x^{\dashv}\colon\ast\longrightarrow{\operatorname{Hom}}(M,X)\,\big/\,\mathsf{Diff}(M)\ .

We may then give a stacky interpretation of the functor taking the
L_{\infty}
-algebra of global sections of an
L_{\infty}
-algebroid.
In the following, if
\mathscr{C}
is an
\infty
-category and
x,z\in\mathscr{C}
, we write
\mathscr{C}_{x{/\hskip-2.84544pt/}z}
for the
\infty
-category of objects under
x
and over
z
, i.e. diagrams
x\longrightarrow y\longrightarrow z
in
\mathscr{C}
. We also write
L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}}^{\infty}_{\mathbbm{R}}
for the
\infty
-category of
\mathbbm{R}
-linear
L_{\infty}
-algebras.

Theorem 3.14

There is a commutative diagram of right adjoint functors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{St}^{\mathrm{dt}}_{M/}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{St}^{\mathrm{dt}}_{\ast{/\hskip-2.84544pt/}{\mathrm{B}}\mathsf{Diff}(M)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{M}^{{\mathrm{cpl}},\infty}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}}^{\infty}_{\mathbbm{R}}\big)_{/\Gamma(M,TM)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{*}\,t^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T(M/-)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T(\ast/-)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{fgt}}}

(3.15)

where the vertical maps arise from Theorem
3.2
, the bottom functor forgets the
C^{\infty}(M)
-linear structure, and the top functor
p_{*}\,t^{*}
sends
x\colon M\longrightarrow X
to the formal completion of
{\operatorname{Hom}}(M,X)/\mathsf{Diff}(M)
at the basepoint
x^{\dashv}
.

Example 3.16

The Lie differentiation of
\ast\longrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}\mathsf{Diff}(M)
in the sense of Theorem
3.2
recovers the Lie algebra of vector fields on
M
, as one would expect: this follows by comparing the two images of the terminal object of
\mathrm{St}^{\mathrm{dt}}_{M/}
in (
3.15
) using Example
3.4
.

\triangleleft

Remark 3.17

Theorem
3.14
provides the following unified derived perspective on symmetries of geometric structures on a manifold
M
:
first, we may consider the global symmetries of
x\colon M\longrightarrow X
.
These are not local on
M
, as
U\longmapsto\mathsf{Diff}(U)
does not define a sheaf (nor even a presheaf) on
M
, and so the global symmetries are encoded in a group object in
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}})
, or equivalently its classifying object.
Differentiating, we obtain the
L_{\infty}
-algebra of the symmetry group.
Theorem
3.14
states, in particular, that the
infinitesimal
symmetries
are
local, i.e. that the
L_{\infty}
-algebra of the higher derived symmetry group has an enhancement to an
L_{\infty}
-algebroid on
M
, and that the functor
T(M/-)
computes precisely this local enhancement.

Geometrically, the left-hand vertical functor in (
3.15
) treats the deformation theory of the map
x\colon M\longrightarrow X
as a family of deformation problems in
X
, parametrised by the points of
M
.
It keeps track of deformations which merely arise from permuting the indexing set by derived diffeomorphisms of
M
.
In contrast, the right-hand vertical functor treats the deformation theory of the map
x\colon M\longrightarrow X
as the deformations of a single point
x^{\dashv}\colon*\longrightarrow{\operatorname{Hom}}(M,X)/\mathsf{Diff}(M)
in the moduli stack of geometric structures on
M
classified by
X
, modulo their equivalences and diffeomorphisms of
M
.
Theorem
3.14
implies

T\big(*\,\big/\,({\operatorname{Hom}}(M,X)\,/\,\mathsf{Diff}(M))\big)\simeq\Gamma\big(M,T(M/X)\big)

(3.18)

and makes precise the relationship between these two deformation problems.

\triangleleft

The first part of Theorem
3.2
allows us, in particular, to associate an
L_{\infty}
-algebroid on
M
which controls the deformation theory of any morphism
x\colon M\longrightarrow X
from a manifold to a dt stack.
Viewing
X
as the classifying stack of a certain type of geometric structure on
M
, Theorem
3.14
suggests that the
L_{\infty}
-algebroid
T(M/X)
should then be understood as the generalisation of the Atiyah algebroid of a classical principal bundle.
This motivates

Definition 3.19

Given a morphism
x\colon M\longrightarrow X
from a smooth manifold to a dt stack
X
, the relative tangent complex
T(M/X)
is its
Atiyah
L_{\infty}
-algebroid
.

A key insight and justification of Definition
3.19
is, as we will show in the later sections of this paper, that it provides access to what we believe is the most general definition of higher-form connections on higher geometric structures, such as principal
\infty
-bundles.

3.2
Properties and examples

Let us now record some properties and examples of the Lie differentiation procedure provided by Theorem
3.2
, starting with

Proposition 3.20

The Lie differentiation from Theorem
3.2
satisfies the following properties:

(1)

Lie differentiation preserves homotopy limits: for any diagram
M\longrightarrow X_{\alpha}
of dt stacks under
M
,

T\Big(M\,\big/\,\underset{\alpha}{\operatorname{holim}}\ X_{\alpha}\Big)\simeq\underset{\alpha}{\operatorname{holim}}\ T(M/X_{\alpha})\ .

(3.21)

(2)

Lie diffentiation preserves filtered homotopy colimits.

(3)

Given a formally smooth
\infty
-groupoid
X_{\bullet}\colon{{\mathbbe{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{op}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}})
and a map
M\longrightarrow X_{0}
, there is an equivalence of
L_{\infty}
-algebroids

T\big(M/|X_{\bullet}|\big)\simeq\big|T(M/X_{\bullet})\big|\ .

(3.22)

Proof.

Since the forgetful functor from
L_{\infty}
-algebroids to complexes respects homotopy limits and sifted
3
3

3

A colimit is
sifted
if it commutes with finite coproducts.
homotopy colimits, the properties readily follow from the fact that taking tangent complexes (Definition
2.76
) preserves limits and filtered colimits (by (
2.74
) and Theorem
3.2
) as well as geometric realisations of formally smooth
\infty
-groupoids (see Proposition
2.106
).
∎

Example 3.23

Let
M
be a smooth manifold and consider a retract diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r}

of dt stacks under
M
. This gives rise to a retract diagram of
L_{\infty}
-algebroids

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{g}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{h}={\mathfrak{g}}\oplus\mathfrak{n}}

on
M
, where
\mathfrak{n}
denotes the kernel of
\mathfrak{h}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{g}}
. Then
T(M/X\times^{h}_{Y}X)
can be identified as the complex

{\mathfrak{g}}\oplus\mathfrak{n}[-1]\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{g}}\longrightarrow TM\ ,

with the
L_{\infty}
-structure

\big[(x_{1},y_{1})\,,\,\dots\,,\,(x_{n},y_{n})\big]=\Big([x_{1},\dots,x_{n}]\,,\,\sum\limits_{k=1}^{n}\,[x_{1},\dots,y_{k},\dots,x_{n}]\Big)\ .

Indeed, we can compute
T(M/X\times^{h}_{Y}X)\simeq{\mathfrak{g}}\times^{h}_{\mathfrak{h}}{\mathfrak{g}}
as a homotopy pullback of
L_{\infty}
-algebroids. To do this, we factor the morphism
{\mathfrak{g}}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{h}
as the composition
{\mathfrak{g}}\longrightarrow\tilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{h}
of a weak equivalence followed by a fibration, as follows. Let

\tilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}=\mathfrak{h}\oplus\mathfrak{n}[-1]

with differential

\mathrm{d}(z,y)=(\mathrm{d}z,\pi(z)+\mathrm{d}y)\ ,

where
\pi\colon\mathfrak{h}={\mathfrak{g}}\oplus\mathfrak{n}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{n}
is the projection. This comes equipped with an
L_{\infty}
-algebroid structure where

\big[(z_{1},y_{1})\,,\,\dots\,,\,(z_{n},y_{n})\big]=\Big([z_{1},\dots,z_{n}]\,,\,\sum\limits_{k=1}^{n}\,[z_{1},\dots,y_{k},\dots,z_{n}]\Big)\ .

One readily checks that the inclusion of
{\mathfrak{g}}
and the projection to
\mathfrak{h}
preserve the brackets. The pullback
{\mathfrak{g}}\times_{\mathfrak{h}}\tilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}
then coincides with the
L_{\infty}
-algebroid given above.

If
X=M
and
x={\mathrm{id}}_{M}
, we can think of

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}

as a bundle over
M
with a specified global section.
In that case,
M\times^{h}_{Y}M
produces the stack of fibrewise loops in
Y
based at the global section.
Then
{\mathfrak{g}}=0
is the trivial
L_{\infty}
-algebroid (by Example
3.4
), and so
T(M/M\times^{h}_{Y}M)\simeq{\mathfrak{g}}\times^{h}_{\mathfrak{h}}{\mathfrak{g}}
is equivalent to
\mathfrak{n}[-1]
with trivial brackets.

\triangleleft

Example 3.24

Suppose that
G
is a dt stack that admits the structure of an
\infty
-group. Let
