e\colon\ast\longrightarrow G
be the unit. Then the Atiyah
L_{\infty}
-algebroid

T(\ast/G)\simeq T_{e}G[-1]

is a
trivial

L_{\infty}
-algebra
[
45
, Chapter 6, Proposition 1.7.2]
(where
T(*/G)
is the relative tangent complex of the canonical morphism
e\colon*\longrightarrow G
). Indeed, we can identify
T(\ast/G)\simeq T\big(\ast/(\ast\times_{{\mathrm{B}}G}\ast)\big)
with the homotopy pullback
0\times^{h}_{T(\ast/{\mathrm{B}}G)}0
of
L_{\infty}
-algebras. Example
3.23
shows that this homotopy pullback is trivial.

\triangleleft

Example 3.25

The same analysis as in Example
3.24
applies when
M
is a manifold and
e\colon M\longrightarrow G
can be identified with the unit map for some
\infty
-group in the
\infty
-category
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}})_{/M}
of stacks over
M
. In this case, the
C^{\infty}(M)
-linear
L_{\infty}
-algebra
T(M/G)
(Corollary
3.5
) is the trivial
L_{\infty}
-algebra.

For instance, this shows that for any closed embedding of smooth manifolds
M\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,N
, the shifted normal bundle

T(M/N)\simeq(T_{M}N/TM)[-1]

is a trivial
L_{\infty}
-algebroid. Indeed,
T(M/N)
only depends on an open neighbourhood of
M
inside
N
, and so using a tubular neighbourhood we can reduce to the case where
M\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,N
is the zero section of a vector bundle on
M
(which has a fibrewise group structure over
M
).

\triangleleft

Remark 3.26

Example
3.24
shows that for a dt stack
G
that admits some group structure, the
shifted
tangent complex
T_{e}G[-1]
is a trivial
L_{\infty}
-algebra. The usual tangent complex
T_{e}G
normally has a
non-trivial

L_{\infty}
-structure that depends on the group structure on
G
: this
L_{\infty}
-structure arises by differentiating the map
\ast\longrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}G
to the classifying stack of
G
. It reproduces, for example, the usual Lie bracket on the tangent space
T_{e}G
of a Lie group
G
(by Example
3.33
below).

Conversely, this gives the following informal interpretation of the Lie differentiation of
x\colon\ast\longrightarrow X
: it can be identified with the
L_{\infty}
-algebra

T(\ast/X)\simeq T_{x}({\mathsf{\Omega}}_{x}X)

of the based loop space
{\mathsf{\Omega}}_{x}X
endowed with the group structure given by composition of loops. If
X
has itself the structure of a group, then
{\mathsf{\Omega}}_{x}X
has an
\mathbbm{E}_{2}
-structure, which trivialises all brackets.

\triangleleft

Remark 3.27

In light of Remark
3.26
we revisit the interpretation of Theorem
3.14
in terms of infinitesimal symmetries. Suppose that
x\colon M\longrightarrow X
is a map to a dt stack. The
L_{\infty}
-algebra underlying
T(M/X)
can be identified with the Lie differentiation of the basepoint inclusion
x^{\dashv}:\ast\longrightarrow{\operatorname{Hom}}(M,X)/\mathsf{Diff}(M)
. The loop space of
{\operatorname{Hom}}(M,X)/\mathsf{Diff}(M)
is precisely the group stack

{\mathsf{Sym}}(x)=\mathsf{Diff}_{/X}(M,M)

(3.28)

of derived diffeomorphisms of
M
relative to
X
.
(In the present setting this is a derived enhancement of the smooth higher symmetry groups investigated in
[
10
,
20
,
16
]
.)
By the above arguments one can think of the
L_{\infty}
-algebra underlying
T(M/X)
as the tangent Lie algebra of the group stack
\mathsf{Diff}_{/X}(M,M)
.

In particular, if
X={\mathrm{B}}G
for a smooth group stack
G
, so that
x\colon M\longrightarrow X
classifies a
G
-bundle
P\longrightarrow M
, then a point in
\mathsf{Diff}_{/X}(M,M)
corresponds to a diffeomorphism
\phi\colon M\longrightarrow M
together with an equivalence of
G
-bundles
P\simeq\phi^{*}P
.

\triangleleft

Let us now describe a way to identify the Lie bracket at the level of homotopy groups. To this end, suppose that
x\colon\ast\longrightarrow X
is a point of a dt stack and let us write
{\mathsf{\Omega}}_{x}^{m}X
for the
m
-fold loop stack at the basepoint
x
. For any
a\leqslant 0
, let us write

D^{1}_{a}={\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}\big(\mathbbm{R}[\epsilon_{a}]\,\big/\,\epsilon_{a}^{2}\big)

for the infinitesimal line, where
\epsilon_{a}
has degree
a
. There is a canonical basepoint
\ast\longrightarrow D^{1}_{a}
, and the tangent complex classifies maps out of such infinitesimal lines: we can identify

\mathrm{H}^{-a-m}\big(T(\ast/X)\big)\cong\big[(D^{1}_{a},\ast)\,,\,({\mathsf{\Omega}}^{m+1}_{x}X,\ast)\big]

with the set of homotopy classes of pointed maps
D^{1}_{a}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{\Omega}}_{x}^{m+1}X
(since this set of homotopy classes agrees with
\pi_{0}{\mathrm{Der}}_{{\mathsf{\Omega}}_{x}^{m+1}X}(x;\mathbbm{R}[a])
and Definition
2.76
).

Given two infinitesimal lines we will write

D^{1}_{a}\vee D^{1}_{b}={\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}\big(\mathbbm{R}[\epsilon_{a},\epsilon_{b}]\,\big/\,\epsilon_{a}^{2},\epsilon_{b}^{2},\epsilon_{a}\,\epsilon_{b}\big)\qquad\text{and}\qquad D^{1}_{a}\times D^{1}_{b}={\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}\big(\mathbbm{R}[\epsilon_{a},\epsilon_{b}]\,\big/\,\epsilon_{a}^{2},\epsilon_{b}^{2}\big)

for the wedge sum and the product, respectively.
Given two pointed maps
\alpha\colon D^{1}_{a}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{\Omega}}^{m+1}_{x}X
and
\beta\colon D^{1}_{b}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{\Omega}}^{n+1}_{x}X
, let us now consider the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{1}_{a}\vee D^{1}_{b}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{1}_{a}\times D^{1}_{b}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{\Omega}}^{m+1}_{x}X\times{\mathsf{\Omega}}_{x}^{n+1}X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ast}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{1}_{a+b}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{\Omega}}_{x}^{m+n+1}X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha\times\beta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Wh}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[\alpha,\beta]_{\mathrm{Wh}}}

(3.29)

Here
\mathrm{Wh}
denotes the Whitehead product, induced by the attaching map
S^{m+n+1}\longrightarrow S^{m+1}\vee S^{n+1}
for the
(m{+}n{+}2)
-cell of
S^{m+1}\times S^{n+1}
. The composition
D^{1}_{a}\vee D^{1}_{b}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{\Omega}}_{x}^{m+n+1}X
is equivalently described by an
(m{+}n{+}1)
-fold loop in the space of pointed maps
D^{1}_{a}\vee D^{1}_{b}\longrightarrow X
given as the Whitehead product of an
(m{+}1)
-fold loop and an
(n{+}1)
-fold loop.

However, this mapping space is an infinite loop space, so all Whitehead products vanish. By the Schlessinger condition, we therefore obtain a map
[\alpha,\beta]_{\mathrm{Wh}}\colon D^{1}_{a+b}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{\Omega}}_{x}^{m+n+1}X
out of the pushout.

Proposition 3.30

For each
a,b\leqslant 0
, the map
[\alpha,\beta]_{\mathrm{Wh}}\colon D^{1}_{a+b}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{\Omega}}_{x}^{m+n+1}X
represents the image of
(\alpha,\beta)
under the Lie bracket on cohomology:

[-,-]\colon\mathrm{H}^{-a-m}\big(T(\ast/X)\big)\,\times\,\mathrm{H}^{-b-n}\big(T(\ast/X)\big)\longrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{-a-b-m-n}\big(T(\ast/X)\big)\ .

(3.31)

That is, the formula described by (
3.29
) computes the graded Lie algebra structure on all cohomology groups of
T(*/X)
.

Proof.

Let us write
{\mathfrak{g}}
for a dg Lie algebra modelling
T(\ast/X)
. It follows from (the proof of) Theorem
3.2
and
[
24
, Example 7.20]
that for any non-positively graded dg Artin algebra
A
with maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}_{A}
, there is a natural equivalence of spaces

\mathrm{St}^{\mathrm{dt}}\big({\mathscr{S}\hskip-0.29999pt\mathrm{pec}}(A),X\big)\times_{\mathrm{St}^{\mathrm{dt}}(\ast,X)}\{x\}\simeq\mathrm{MC}_{\bullet}\big({\mathfrak{g}}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{A})\ .

Here the right hand side denotes the Maurer–Cartan space from
[
46
]
: it is obtained by tensoring with the simplicial cdga
\Omega^{*}[\Delta^{\bullet}]
of polynomial differential forms on simplices and taking Maurer–Cartan elements.

Using this, the construction of (
3.29
) boils down to the following: we can identify
\alpha
and
\beta
with maps

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{MC}_{\bullet}\big({\mathfrak{g}}\otimes(\epsilon_{a},\epsilon_{b},\epsilon_{a}\,\epsilon_{b})\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{MC}_{\bullet}\big({\mathfrak{g}}\otimes(\epsilon_{a})\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{MC}_{\bullet}\big({\mathfrak{g}}\otimes(\epsilon_{b})\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{m+1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha=\alpha^{\prime}\cdot\epsilon_{a}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta=\beta^{\prime}\cdot\epsilon_{b}}

where
(\epsilon_{a})
is the maximal ideal of
{\mathscr{O}}(D^{1}_{a})
, while
\alpha^{\prime}
and
\beta^{\prime}
correspond to cocycles in
{\mathfrak{g}}
via the Dupont contraction
[
7
, Section 11.2]
. Next we take the Whitehead product of the resulting pair of elements in the homotopy groups of the space
\mathrm{MC}_{\bullet}\big({\mathfrak{g}}\otimes(\epsilon_{a},\epsilon_{b},\epsilon_{a}\,\epsilon_{b})\big)
, or more precisely of its connected component containing the basepoint. This connected component is a nilpotent rational space, whose Lie model is the nilpotent dg Lie algebra
\tau^{\leqslant 0}\big({\mathfrak{g}}\otimes(\epsilon_{a},\epsilon_{b},\epsilon_{a}\,\epsilon_{b})\big)
.

It now follows from
[
7
, Theorems 12.41 and 12.42]
that the Whitehead product of
\alpha
and
\beta
is given by the Lie bracket in
{\mathfrak{g}}\otimes(\epsilon_{a},\epsilon_{b},\epsilon_{a}\,\epsilon_{b})
. In other words, the Whitehead product is given up to homotopy by

[\alpha^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}]_{{\mathfrak{g}}}\cdot\epsilon_{a}\,\epsilon_{b}\colon S^{m+n+1}\longrightarrow\mathrm{MC}_{\bullet}\big({\mathfrak{g}}\otimes(\epsilon_{a},\epsilon_{b},\epsilon_{a}\,\epsilon_{b})\big)\ ,

where
[-,-]_{\mathfrak{g}}
is the Lie bracket of
{\mathfrak{g}}
. This class is indeed null modulo
\epsilon_{a}\,\epsilon_{b}
, and we find that the class
[\alpha,\beta]_{\mathrm{Wh}}
can be represented up to homotopy by

[\alpha,\beta]_{\mathrm{Wh}}=[\alpha^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}]_{{\mathfrak{g}}}\cdot\epsilon_{a}\,\epsilon_{b}\colon S^{m+n+1}\longrightarrow\mathrm{MC}_{\bullet}\big({\mathfrak{g}}\otimes(\epsilon_{a}\,\epsilon_{b})\big)\ .

It follows that
[\alpha,\beta]_{\mathrm{Wh}}
indeed presents the Lie bracket on
\mathrm{H}^{*}({\mathfrak{g}})
.
∎

Proposition
3.30
is particularly useful for determining the Lie bracket on the non-positive cohomology groups of
T(\ast/X)
: in this case we can take
\epsilon_{a}
and
\epsilon_{b}
to be of degree zero and work in a (non-derived) setting of synthetic differential geometry.

Example 3.32

Suppose that
G\in{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}})
is a formally smooth dt stack equipped the structure of an
\infty
-group. By Proposition
2.106
the classifying stack
{\mathrm{B}}G
has deformation theory, and the tangent complex
T(\ast/{\mathrm{B}}G)\simeq T_{e}({\mathsf{\Omega}}{\mathrm{B}}G)=T_{e}G
is concentrated in non-positive degrees. The Lie bracket
\mathrm{H}^{m}(T_{e}G)\times\mathrm{H}^{n}(T_{e}G)\longrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{m+n}(T_{e}G)
on cohomology can be obtained entirely using the Whitehead product of maps
\alpha\colon D^{1}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{\Omega}}_{e}^{m}G
and
\beta\colon D^{1}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{\Omega}}_{e}^{n}G
.

This is well-known for
m=n=0
: in this case there are two (non-derived) infinitesimal paths
\alpha,\beta\colon D^{1}\longrightarrow G
at the identity and their Lie bracket is obtained as the unique factorisation

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{1}\times D^{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G\times G}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha\times\beta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})\longmapsto\epsilon_{1}\,\epsilon_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(g,h)\longmapsto g\,h\,g^{-1}\,h^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[\alpha,\beta]}

so that the Whitehead product is the commutator
[
64
, Theorem V.1.6]
.

\triangleleft

Example 3.33

Let
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
be a Lie groupoid and let
x\colon M\longrightarrow X=[M/\mathscr{G}]
be the canonical map to the associated stack. Example
2.108
identifies the morphism
TM\longrightarrow T_{x}X
with the evident inclusion
TM\longrightarrow({\mathfrak{g}}\longrightarrow TM)
, where
{\mathfrak{g}}
is the Lie algebroid of
\mathscr{G}
and the differential is its anchor map. Consequently the map
T(M/X)\longrightarrow TM
coincides with the anchor map
\rho\colon{\mathfrak{g}}\longrightarrow TM
.

For degree reasons, an
L_{\infty}
-algebroid structure on
{\mathfrak{g}}
is just the datum of a Lie bracket. To show that this recovers the usual Lie algebroid structure on
{\mathfrak{g}}
, we use Theorem
3.14
and consider the Lie differentiation of
x^{\dashv}\colon\ast\longrightarrow{\operatorname{Hom}}(M,X)/\mathsf{Diff}(M)
. Since we already know that the underlying module is
{\mathfrak{g}}
(concentrated in degree
0
), we can use Proposition
3.30
to identify the Lie bracket exactly as in Example
3.32
.

By Remark
3.27
, the loop stack
\mathsf{Diff}_{/X}(M,M)={\mathsf{\Omega}}_{x^{\dashv}}\big({\operatorname{Hom}}(M,X)/\mathsf{Diff}(M)\big)
can be identified with the stack sending each inf-manifold
U
to the space of dashed maps in the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U\times M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U\times M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[M/\mathscr{G}]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x\circ\pi_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x\circ\pi_{2}}

which are equivalences and make both triangles commute up to homotopy. When
U
is non-derived, the space of such lifts is a set: for the top left triangle, commutativity is simply a condition expressing
\phi
as a map of the form
(\pi_{1},\phi^{\prime})
for
\phi^{\prime}\colon U\times M\longrightarrow M
. Since the quotient stack
[M/\mathscr{G}]
sends each non-derived
U
to a groupoid, there is a set of homotopies making the bottom right triangle commute: they correspond to the set of
bisections

g\colon U\times M\longrightarrow\mathscr{G}

of the Lie groupoid
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s,t:\mathscr{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
over
\phi^{\prime}
, i.e. maps such that
s\circ g=\pi_{2}
and
t\circ g=\phi^{\prime}
.

Proceeding as in Example
3.32
, it now suffices to take two first order infinitesimal families of bisections
g,h\colon D^{1}\times M\longrightarrow\mathscr{G}
(sending the basepoint to the identity) and consider the map

D^{1}\times D^{1}\times M\longrightarrow\mathscr{G}\ ,\qquad(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2},m)\longmapsto g(\epsilon_{1},m)\,h(\epsilon_{2},m)\,g(\epsilon_{1},m)^{-1}\,h(\epsilon_{2},m)^{-1}\ .

The
\epsilon_{1}\,\epsilon_{2}
term precisely gives the usual Lie bracket of the Lie algebroid
{\mathfrak{g}}
(see
[
25
, Section 15.4]
). For an alternative proof of the fact that Theorem
3.2
indeed reproduces the usual Lie algebroid associated to a Lie groupoid, see
[
68
, Corollary 6.3.33]
.

\triangleleft

4
Connections and curvature on principal
\infty
-bundles

In this section we present a general derived differential geometric approach to higher-form connections and curvatures on geometric structures on manifolds.
More precisely, we describe geometric structures on a manifold
M
in terms of morphisms
x\colon M\longrightarrow X
in
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}})
from
M
to a dt stack
X
(see Definition
2.82
), where we understand
X
as classifying a certain type of geometric structure on
M
.
In particular, for the classifying stack of a Lie group
X={\mathrm{B}}G
we recover classical connections on principal
G
-bundles, while in Section
5
we compute explicitly how our new definition of higher-form connections recovers all spaces of
p
-form connections on higher principal bundles classified by
X={\mathrm{B}}^{n}{\mathrm{U}}(1)
for any
n\in\mathbbm{N}
(i.e.
(n{-}1)
-gerbes).

4.1
p
-form connections on geometric structures

Let
M
be a smooth manifold and
X
a dt stack.
We will define the space of
p
-form connections on
x\colon M\longrightarrow X
in terms of the associated Atiyah
L_{\infty}
-algebroid
T(M/X)
(see Theorem
3.2
and Definition
3.19
).
It is worth emphasising that once this
L_{\infty}
-algebroid has been associated to
x
, our definition is purely algebraic.
We thus present it first at the level of
L_{\infty}
-algebroids.

To that end, let
A
be a cdga over
\mathbbm{R}
.
We recall from
[
69
, Section 5]
(see in particular the proof of Proposition 5.17 therein) the chain of functors and natural transformations

Q^{(1)}\longrightarrow Q^{(2)}\longrightarrow\cdots\longrightarrow Q^{(\infty)}=Q\ ,

(4.1)

where each
Q^{(p)}
is a functor (of ordinary categories)

Q^{(p)}\colon L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}\longrightarrow L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}\ .

(4.2)

The functor
Q
is the left adjoint in an adjunction
[
69
, Lemma 5.8]

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q:L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}:\iota\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\perp}

(4.3)

The functors
Q^{(p)}
can be described as follows
[
69
, Section 5]
:
let

F\colon\big({\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}\big)_{/T_{A}}\longrightarrow L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}

(4.4)

denote the functor which takes the free
L_{\infty}
-algebroid on a dg
A
-module with its anchor map to
T_{A}
(see e.g.
[
52
]
).
Then the underlying dg
A
-module and brackets of
Q^{(p)}{\mathfrak{g}}
are the same as those of

F\Big({\mathrm{Sym}}^{1\leqslant\bullet\leqslant p}_{A}\big({\mathfrak{g}}[1]\big)[-1]\Big)\ ,

(4.5)

but the differential is not the differential produced by
F
.
Instead, the differential on
Q^{(p)}{\mathfrak{g}}
is obtained by observing that the differential on
Q{\mathfrak{g}}
restricts to
Q^{(p)}{\mathfrak{g}}\subset Q{\mathfrak{g}}
, for each
p\in\mathbbm{N}
.
On
Q{\mathfrak{g}}
the differential reads as
[
69
, Equations (5.11) and (5.12)]

\mathrm{d}_{Q{\mathfrak{g}}}(x_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes x_{n})=\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}(x_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes x_{n})+\kappa(x_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes x_{n})\ ,

(4.6)

where

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}(x_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes x_{n})&=\sum_{k=1}^{n}\,\pm\,x_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}(x_{k})\otimes\cdots\otimes x_{n}\ ,\\[4.0pt]
\kappa(x_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes x_{n})&=-\sum_{k\geqslant 2}\ \sum_{\sigma\in{\rm Sh}(k,n-k)}\,\pm\,[x_{\sigma(1)},\ldots,x_{\sigma(k)}]_{{\mathfrak{g}},k}\otimes x_{\sigma(k+1)}\otimes\cdots\otimes x_{\sigma(n)}\\
&\quad\,+\sum_{k\geqslant 2}\,\frac{1}{k!}\,\big[{\mathsf{\Delta}}^{k}(x_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes x_{n})\big]_{F,k+1}\ .\end{split}

(4.7)

Here
[-]_{{\mathfrak{g}},k}
is the
k
-ary bracket on
{\mathfrak{g}}
,
{\mathsf{\Delta}}^{k}
is the
k
-fold comultiplication in
\overline{{\mathrm{Sym}}}_{A}({\mathfrak{g}}[1])
and
[-]_{F,k}
is the
k
-ary formal bracket on the free
L_{\infty}
-algebroid.
Note that
\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}
and the double sum in (
4.7
) combine into the Chevalley–Eilenberg differential on the cdgc
\overline{{\mathrm{Sym}}}_{\mathbbm{R}}({\mathfrak{g}}[1])
.

Example 4.8

If
A
and
{\mathfrak{g}}
are both concentrated in degree zero, then

Q^{(1)}{\mathfrak{g}}=\big(F({\mathfrak{g}}),\mathrm{d}_{\overline{{\mathrm{Sym}}}_{A}({\mathfrak{g}})|\leqslant 1}\big)=F({\mathfrak{g}})

(4.9)

agrees with the free Lie algebroid over
A
on
{\mathfrak{g}}
.
This will allow us to recover Kapranov’s approach to connections
[
52
]
.

\triangleleft

Let
\mathfrak{h}
be an
L_{\infty}
-algebroid over
A
.
We let
{\mathrm{CE}}_{\leqslant p}(\mathfrak{h})\subset{\mathrm{CE}}_{*}(\mathfrak{h})
denote the sub-dg coalgebra on polynomials of degree at most
p
.
Since morphisms out of
L_{\infty}
-algebroids of the form
Q^{(p)}\mathfrak{h}
will be central to the computations in the remainder of this paper, we briefly describe them explicitly through

Lemma 4.10

Let
{\mathfrak{g}}
and
\mathfrak{h}
be
L_{\infty}
-algebroids over
A
.
For each
p\in\mathbbm{N}
, there are canonical natural bijections between

(1)

the set of morphisms
L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}(Q^{(p)}\mathfrak{h},{\mathfrak{g}})
,

(2)

the set of morphisms of
\mathbbm{R}
-linear cdgcs

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{CE}}_{\leqslant p}(\mathfrak{h})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{CE}}_{*}({\mathfrak{g}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{CE}}_{*}(T_{A})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{CE}}_{\leqslant p}(\rho_{\mathfrak{h}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{CE}}_{*}(\rho_{{\mathfrak{g}}})}

(4.11)

such that the underlying map of non-unital graded coalgebras
\overline{{\mathrm{Sym}}}_{\mathbbm{R}}(\mathfrak{h}[1])\longrightarrow\overline{{\mathrm{Sym}}}_{\mathbbm{R}}({\mathfrak{g}}[1])
descends to an
A
-linear map of graded coalgebras
\overline{{\mathrm{Sym}}}_{A}(\mathfrak{h}[1])\longrightarrow\overline{{\mathrm{Sym}}}_{A}({\mathfrak{g}}[1])
, and

(3)

the set of degree one maps of graded
A
-modules
\phi\colon\overline{{\mathrm{Sym}}}^{\,\leqslant p}_{A}(\mathfrak{h}[1])\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{g}}
such that

(i)

the underlying
\mathbbm{R}
-linear map defines a Maurer–Cartan element in
{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathbbm{R}}\big(\overline{{\mathrm{Sym}}}_{\mathbbm{R}}(\mathfrak{h}[1]),{\mathfrak{g}}[1])\big)
(see (
2.10
)), and

(ii)

the composition
\overline{{\mathrm{Sym}}}_{A}(\mathfrak{h}[1])\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{g}}\xrightarrow{\ \rho_{{\mathfrak{g}}}\ }T_{A}
is given by
\rho_{\mathfrak{h}}
on the linear copy
\mathfrak{h}[1]
and vanishes on all higher tensor powers of
\mathfrak{h}[1]
.

Proof.

This follows readily by the description of the differential on
Q^{(p)}\mathfrak{h}
and the fact that
{\mathrm{CE}}_{*}({\mathfrak{g}})
is a cofree cocommutative dg coalgebra, together with Definition
2.11
and Remark
2.13
.
∎

Definition 4.12

For
p\in\mathbbm{N}\cup\{\infty\}
, an
order
p
morphism of
L_{\infty}
-algebroids

\mathfrak{h}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{g}}
is an element of

L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{(p)}(\mathfrak{h},{\mathfrak{g}})\coloneqq L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}\big(Q^{(p)}\mathfrak{h},{\mathfrak{g}}\big)\ .

(4.13)

We can now introduce an algebraic definition of
p
-form connections through

Definition 4.14

Let
A
be a unital commutative dg algebra (cdga) over
\mathbbm{R}
.
Let
{\mathfrak{g}}
be an
L_{\infty}
-algebroid over
A
.
The
space of
p
-form connections on
{\mathfrak{g}}
is the mapping space

{\mathrm{Con}}_{p}({\mathfrak{g}})\coloneqq\mathrm{Map}_{L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}}\big(Q^{(p)}T_{A},{\mathfrak{g}}\big)\simeq L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\infty}\big(Q^{(p)}T_{A},{\mathfrak{g}}\big)\ .

(4.15)

This definition becomes particularly accessible for
A
-cofibrant
L_{\infty}
-algebroids
[
69
, Definition 5.16]
.

Definition 4.16

An
L_{\infty}
-algebroid
\mathfrak{h}
over
A
is
A
-cofibrant
if its underlying dg
A
-module is cofibrant.

Remark 4.17

In the situation of Definition
4.14
, if
T_{A}
is
A
-cofibrant then we can compute the space of
p
-form connections on
{\mathfrak{g}}
as

{\mathrm{Con}}_{p}({\mathfrak{g}})\cong L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}\big(Q^{(p)}T_{A},\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}\big)\cong L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}\big(Q^{(p)}T_{A},\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}\big)\ ,

(4.18)

where the isomorphisms are in the homotopy category
\operatorname{Ho}{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{et}_{\hskip-0.85355pt\Delta}}
and
\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}
is any fibrant simplicial resolution of
{\mathfrak{g}}
in
L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}
.
Indeed, given an
A
-cofibrant
L_{\infty}
-algebroid
\mathfrak{h}\in L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}
, the
L_{\infty}
-algebroid
Q^{(p)}\mathfrak{h}
is cofibrant for each
p\in\mathbbm{N}\cup\{\infty\}
(see the proof of
[
69
, Proposition 5.17]
).
This implies the first isomorphism in (
4.18
), since we can now compute the mapping space by choosing a fibrant simplicial resolution
\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}
of
{\mathfrak{g}}
.
To pass from strict to
\infty
-morphisms of
L_{\infty}
-algebroids, we can further apply the functor
Q
to the source (this produces a weekly equivalent cofibrant
L_{\infty}
-algebroid by
[
69
, Proposition 5.22(c)]
) to obtain the second isomorphism in
\operatorname{Ho}{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{et}_{\hskip-0.85355pt\Delta}}
, since

L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}^{\mathrm{dg}}_{A}\big(QQ^{(p)}T_{A},\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}\big)\cong L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}\big(Q^{(p)}T_{A},\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}\big)

(4.19)

by the defining property of
Q
.

\triangleleft

Example 4.20

Let
M
be a connected manifold, and set
A=C^{\infty}(M)
(regarded as a cdga concentrated in degree zero).
For each vector bundle
E\longrightarrow M
, the
C^{\infty}(M)
-module of global sections
\Gamma(M,E)
is finitely generated and projective
[
65
, Theorem 12.32]
.
Since the projective model structure on
{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}^{\mathrm{dg}}_{A}
is inherited from that on
{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}^{\mathrm{dg}}_{\mathbbm{R}}
along the forgetful functor, each projective
A
-module is cofibrant.
In particular,
TM=T_{C^{\infty}(M)}
is a
C^{\infty}(M)
-cofibrant
L_{\infty}
-algebroid.

\triangleleft

Let again
M
be a smooth manifold and
x\colon M\longrightarrow X
a morphism to a dt stack (see Definition
2.82
).
Combining the algebraic Definition
4.14
with the derived geometric differentiation of maps
x\colon M\longrightarrow X
from Theorem
3.2
, we introduce a derived geometric definition of higher-form connections on geometric structures through

Definition 4.21

Let
x\colon M\longrightarrow X
be an object in
\mathrm{St}^{\mathrm{dt}}_{M/}
.
The
space of
p
-form connections on
x
is

{\mathrm{Con}}_{p}(x)\coloneqq{\mathrm{Con}}_{p}\big(T(M/X)\big)\ .

(4.22)

Example 4.23

An important class of target stacks consists of the classifying stacks for higher principal bundles:
let
G_{\bullet}
be a group object in
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}})
whose underlying object
G=G_{1}\in{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}})
has (quasi-coherent) deformation theory.
Recall that a group object in an
\infty
-topos is in particular a groupoid object in the sense of
[
58
, Definition 6.1.2.7]
.
By
[
62
, Example A.5.2.5]
, for any groupoid object
X_{\bullet}
the morphisms

X_{n}\longrightarrow X(\Lambda^{n}_{i})=\underset{\Delta^{k}\longrightarrow\Lambda^{n}_{i}}{\operatorname{holim}}\ X_{k}

(4.24)

are equivalences.
Since any equivalence in
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}})
is formally smooth (Definition
2.99
),
G_{\bullet}
is a formally smooth
\infty
-groupoid (Definition
2.104
).
Moreover, since
G_{n}\simeq(G_{1})^{\times n}
for each
n\in\mathbbm{N}_{0}
, by Proposition
2.91
(2) each
G_{n}
for
n\in\mathbbm{N}_{0}
as well as the homotopy colimit

{\mathrm{B}}G=\underset{[n]\in{{\mathbbe{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{op}}}{\operatorname{hocolim}}\ G_{n}

(4.25)

are stacks with (quasi-coherent) deformation theory as well.

\triangleleft

The stack
{\mathrm{B}}G
classifies
G
-principal
\infty
-bundles in
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}})

[
66
,
67
]
(see also
[
21
]
for an overview).
In Example
4.26
below we consider the cases where
G
is an ordinary Lie group and show how we recover the classical theory of connections.
In Section
5
we will compute the spaces of
p
-form connections on morphisms
x\colon M\longrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}^{n}{\mathrm{U}}(1)
and show that these recover the higher groupoids of
p
-form connections on higher
{\mathrm{U}}(1)
-bundles.

Example 4.26

Let
G
be a Lie group and

x_{P}\colon M\longrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}G

the classifying map for a principal
G
-bundle
P\longrightarrow M
.
This is, a priori, a morphism in
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{M}\hskip-0.56917pt\mathrm{fd}})
.
It induces a morphism
\iota_{!}\,x_{P}\colon M\longrightarrow\iota_{!}{\mathrm{B}}G
in
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}})
under the functor
\iota_{!}
from (
2.52
).
4
4

4

Since
\iota_{!}
preserves finite products and is a left adjoint, it preserves group objects and there is a canonical equivalence
\iota_{!}{\mathrm{B}}G\simeq{\mathrm{B}}(\iota_{!}G)
for each group object
G
in
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{M}\hskip-0.56917pt\mathrm{fd}})
.
In particular, it commutes with forming classifying objects.

In computations involving objects in
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{M}\hskip-0.56917pt\mathrm{fd}})
we will often omit the functor
\iota_{!}
for ease of notation.

The Atiyah
L_{\infty}
-algebroid of
x_{P}:M\longrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}G
is the traditional Atiyah algebroid of
P

[
68
, Corollary 6.4.33]

\displaystyle T(M/{\mathrm{B}}G)=\mathfrak{X}(P)^{G}\longrightarrow TM\ ,

(4.27)

where
\mathfrak{X}(P)^{G}
denotes the
G
-invariant vector fields on
P
.
We also write

{\mathrm{At}}(P)=T(M/{\mathrm{B}}G)\ .

(4.28)

The space of 1-form connections on
P
in Definition
4.21
is the mapping space

{\mathrm{Con}}_{1}(P):={\mathrm{Con}}_{1}(x_{P})=\mathrm{Map}_{L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{M}^{\mathrm{dg}}}\big(Q^{(1)}TM,{\mathrm{At}}(P)\big)\ .

(4.29)

The
L_{\infty}
-algebroid
Q^{(1)}TM
was computed in Example
4.8
.
Both
Q^{(1)}TM
and
T(M/{\mathrm{B}}G)
are concentrated in degree zero.
Hence we can compute the derived mapping space in the model structure on
L_{\infty}
-algebroids concentrated in non-positive degrees from Remark
2.6
.
To do this, we have to pick a Reedy fibrant simplicial resolution of
{\mathrm{At}}(P)
.
As a consequence of Remark
2.19
we can choose the constant resolution in this case.
By Remark
4.17
and Example
4.20
it is not necessary to choose a cofibrant resolution of the source
L_{\infty}
-algebroid
Q^{(1)}TM
.
Altogether we find

\displaystyle{\mathrm{Con}}_{1}(P)\cong L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{M}^{\mathrm{dg}}\big(Q^{(1)}TM,{\mathrm{At}}(P)\big)\cong{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}^{\mathrm{dg}}_{C^{\infty}(M)}\big(TM,{\mathrm{At}}(P)\big)\ ,

(4.30)

where for the last isomorphism we used that
Q^{(1)}TM
is the free Lie algebroid on
TM
, see Example
4.8
.
Hence our notion of a 1-form connection on a principal
G
-bundle
P\longrightarrow M
from Definition
4.21
reduces to the usual definition of a connection on
P
in the formulation of Kapranov
[
52
]
, i.e. using the free Lie algebroid on the tangent bundle of
M
.

By the same arguments, for
p\geqslant 2
the space of
p
-form connections on
P
is

{\mathrm{Con}}_{p}(P)=L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{M}^{\mathrm{dg}}\big(Q^{(p)}TM,{\mathrm{At}}(P)\big)\ .

(4.31)

An element of this set is the same as a morphism
TM\longrightarrow{\mathrm{At}}(P)
of Lie algebroids on
M
, which are in one-to-one correspondence with
flat
connections on
P
.

\triangleleft

Example 4.32

Another important class of examples consists of principal 2-bundles whose structure 2-group is a crossed module

G=\big(G_{-1}\,,\,G_{0}\,,\,\delta\colon G_{-1}\longrightarrow G_{0}\,,\,\alpha\colon G_{-1}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Aut}(G_{0})\big)

of Lie groups.
Using that all manifolds are qcdt stacks by Example
2.89
and that all submersions of manifolds are formally smooth (Example
2.103
), together with Proposition
2.106
, we see that any such crossed module gives rise first to a (2-coskeletal) simplicial Lie group and then to a qcdt stack
{\mathrm{B}}G
.
Thus for each classifying map

x\colon M\longrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}G

of a crossed module 2-bundle, there is an associated Atiyah
L_{\infty}
-algebroid

{\mathrm{At}}(x)=T(M/{\mathrm{B}}G)\ .

Here we consider the case where
x
classifies the trivial
G
-bundle and check that our new notion of
2
-form connection on
x
recovers the known one in the literature (see for instance
[
93
]
).
Since
x
is trivial, the Atiyah
L_{\infty}
-algebroid is

{\mathrm{At}}(x)=\big({\mathfrak{g}}_{-1}\otimes C^{\infty}(M)\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{g}}_{0}\otimes C^{\infty}(M)\oplus TM\big)\ ,

(4.33)

which lies in degrees
-1
and
0
, where the brackets are induced from those of the corresponding Lie 2-algebra
{\mathfrak{g}}
and the canonical actions of
TM
on itself as well as on
C^{\infty}(M)
.
Using Lemma
4.10
, it follows that the vertices of
{\mathrm{Con}}_{2}(x)
are given by pairs

\big(A^{(1)}\in\Omega^{1}(M;{\mathfrak{g}}_{0})\,,\,A^{(2)}\in\Omega^{2}(M;{\mathfrak{g}}_{-1})\big)

satisfying

\delta A^{(2)}=\mathrm{d}A^{(1)}+[A^{(1)},A^{(1)}]\ ,

(4.34)

as expected.

\triangleleft

Remark 4.35

In principle, we can compute the Atiyah algebroid and space of connections for a higher principal bundle
P\colon M\longrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}G
with general higher structure group
G
from a Whitehead-like decomposition of
{\mathrm{B}}G
and the compatibility of all our constructions with pullbacks.
For example, if
K
is a compact, simple and simply-connected Lie group, then the string 2-group
{\mathrm{String}}(K)
fits into the pullback

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{B}}{\mathrm{String}}(K)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{B}}K}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{B}}^{3}{\mathrm{U}}(1)}

(4.36)

where the map
{\mathrm{B}}K\longrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}^{3}{\mathrm{U}}(1)
classifies the Chern–Simons 2-gerbe on
{\mathrm{B}}K
.
From this description we see that a
{\mathrm{String}}(K)
-bundle
P_{{\mathrm{String}}(K)}\colon M\longrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}{\mathrm{String}}(K)
on a manifold
M
is the same as a principal
K
-bundle
P_{K}
on
M
together with a trivialisation of the pullback of the Chern–Simons 2-gerbe to its total space
P_{K}
.
By Theorem
3.2
the Atiyah
L_{\infty}
-algebroid
T(M/{\mathrm{B}}{\mathrm{String}}(K))
is equivalent to the pullback
T(M/{\mathrm{B}}K)\times_{T(M/{\mathrm{B}}^{3}{\mathrm{U}}(1))}*
.

It follows that a 1-form connection on
P_{{\mathrm{String}}(K)}
is specified by a connection on
P_{K}
together with a compatible trivialisation of the induced 1-form connection on the Chern–Simons 2-gerbe on
M
. If we consider instead
p
-form connections for
p>1
, then the induced connection on
P_{K}
must be flat, which presents an obstruction to their existence.
In particular, we recover the well-known fact that geometric string structures on
P_{K}
are not the same as 2-form connections on the underlying string structure
P_{{\mathrm{String}}(K)}
.

\triangleleft

Definition
4.21
also allows us to define
p
-form connections only along group actions or foliations:
let
x\colon M\longrightarrow X
be a morphism from a smooth manifold to a dt stack.
Let
\mathfrak{h}
be an
L_{\infty}
-algebroid on
M
with a morphism
\mathfrak{h}\longrightarrow TM
in
L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{M}
.
We can then form the homotopy pullback

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{h}\times^{h}_{TM}T(M/X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T(M/X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{h}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TM}

(4.37)

Definition 4.38

The space of
p
-form connections on
x
along
\mathfrak{h}
is

\displaystyle{\mathrm{Con}}_{p}(x;\mathfrak{h})

\displaystyle=(L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{M}^{\infty})_{/\mathfrak{h}}\big(Q^{(p)}\mathfrak{h},\,\mathfrak{h}\times^{h}_{TM}T(M/X)\big)

(4.39)

\displaystyle\simeq L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{M}^{\infty}\big(Q^{(p)}\mathfrak{h},\,T(M/X)\big)\ .

(4.40)

Example 4.41

If
G
is a higher smooth group acting on
M
with associated
L_{\infty}
-algebra
{\mathfrak{g}}
, there is an induced infinitesimal action
{\mathfrak{g}}\otimes{\mathscr{O}}(M)\longrightarrow TM
.
A
p
-form connection on
x
along
{\mathfrak{g}}\otimes{\mathscr{O}}(M)
can be viewed as an order
p
infinitesimal lift of the
G
-action on
M
to the geometric structure on
M
classified by
x
.

\triangleleft

Example 4.42

Another source of
L_{\infty}
-algebroids on
M
consists of foliations of
M
, or equivalently integrable distributions in
TM
.
Definition
4.38
allows us to define
p
-form connections on
x
along any integrable distribution in
TM
.

A particularly interesting case is the following:
suppose that
M
comes endowed with a submersion
s\colon M\longrightarrow B
.
Then the fibres of
s
are submanifolds of
M
and
\ker(s_{*})\subset TM
is an integrable distribution.
If we understand
s\colon M\longrightarrow B
as encoding a
B
-parametrised family of smooth manifolds
(M|b)_{b\in B}
and the morphism
x\colon M\longrightarrow X
as a
B
-parametrised family of geometric structures
x_{M|b}\colon M|b\longrightarrow X
, then a
p
-form connection along
\ker(s_{*})
can be viewed as a
B
-family of
p
-form connections on
x_{M|b}
.
In particular, this provides a general and perhaps surprisingly simple description of concretification (see
[
76
,
17
]
), i.e. a description of smooth families of geometric structures with connections on manifolds where the connection data is defined not on the total space of a family of manifolds but only along its fibres.
Finally, if the family
M\longrightarrow B
is a bundle with fibre
F
, then this example is a sub-case of Example
4.41
where
G=\mathsf{Diff}(F)
acts canonically on
M
along the fibres.

\triangleleft

Remark 4.43

On every smooth manifold
M
there is a universal
p
-form connection in the sense of Definition
4.14
, for each
p\in\mathbbm{N}
.
It consists of the identity morphism

\pazocal{A}_{p,\mathrm{univ}}={\mathrm{id}}\colon Q^{(p)}TM\longrightarrow Q^{(p)}TM\ .

(4.44)

We expect that these universal connections (or translation-invariant versions thereof) furnish the classical path signature for
p=1
and higher-dimensional versions thereof for higher values of
p
(see e.g.
[
56
,
9
]
).

\triangleleft

4.2
Curvature forms

We now derive the curvature of the
p
-form connections we introduced in Definitions
4.14
and
4.21
.
To that end, fix a morphism
x\colon M\longrightarrow X
in
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}})
from a smooth manifold to a dt stack, and consider a
p
-form connection
\tau
on
x
.
Its curvature appears as the obstruction to lifting
\tau
to a
(p{+}1)
-form connection.
To see this, we study the data required to extend an order
p
morphism out of an
L_{\infty}
-algebroid
\mathfrak{h}
to an order
(p{+}1)
morphism, or equivalently a map out of
Q^{(p)}\mathfrak{h}
to a map out of
Q^{(p+1)}\mathfrak{h}
.
This is accomplished by means of
[
69
, Proposition 5.17]
(or rather its proof), which describes
Q^{(p+1)}\mathfrak{h}
as a pushout of
Q^{(p)}\mathfrak{h}
.
Recall the free-forgetful adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F:({\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}})_{/T_{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Lie}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}:U\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\perp}

(4.45)

where the right-hand side is the category of dg Lie algebroids over
A
(the full subcategory of
L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}
on those
L_{\infty}
-algebroids whose
m
-ary brackets vanish for all
m>2
).

Lemma 4.46 (
[
69
, Proof of Proposition 5.17]
)

Let
A
be a cdga over
\mathbbm{R}
, and
\mathfrak{h}
an
L_{\infty}
-algebroid over
A
.
For each
p\in\mathbbm{N}_{0}
there is a pushout of
L_{\infty}
-algebroids

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\big({\mathrm{Sym}}_{A}^{p+1}(\mathfrak{h}[1])[-2]\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q^{(p)}\mathfrak{h}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\big({\mathrm{Sym}}_{A}^{p+1}(\mathfrak{h}[1])[-2,-1]\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q^{(p+1)}\mathfrak{h}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\kappa}

(4.47)

where the bottom-left vertex is the free
L_{\infty}
-algebroid on a shifted cone of the dg
A
-module
{\mathrm{Sym}}_{A}^{p+1}(\mathfrak{h}[1])
.
Both free dg Lie algebroids on the left are formed using the zero anchor map, and the map
\kappa
was defined in (
4.7
).
If
\mathfrak{h}
is
A
-cofibrant, then this diagram is even a homotopy pushout.

To describe the curvature of a
p
-form connection, we will now present a dual version of this statement as follows.
Let
\tau\colon\mathfrak{h}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{g}}
be an order
p
morphism of
L_{\infty}
-algebroids over
A
.
To extend
\tau
to an order
(p{+}1)
morphism
\tau^{\prime}\colon\mathfrak{h}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{g}}
, by Lemma
4.10
it suffices to specify a map

\tau^{\prime}_{p+1}\colon{\mathrm{Sym}}_{A}^{p+1}(\mathfrak{h}[1])\longrightarrow\ker(\rho_{{\mathfrak{g}}})[1]

(4.48)

such that
\tau+\tau^{\prime}_{p+1}
is a Maurer–Cartan element in
{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{\mathbbm{R}}^{\mathrm{dg}}({\mathrm{CE}}_{\leqslant p+1}(\mathfrak{h}),{\mathfrak{g}})
.
Observe from (
2.9
) and (
2.10
) that in the Maurer–Cartan equation in question all the brackets involving
\tau^{\prime}_{p+1}
vanish, since
{\mathrm{CE}}_{\leqslant p+1}(\mathfrak{h})
vanishes in degrees less than
-(p{+}1)
.
Hence the Maurer–Cartan equation becomes

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}\circ\tau^{\prime}_{p+1}-\tau^{\prime}_{p+1}\circ\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{h}}=-\left(\mathrm{d}_{\operatorname{Hom}}\tau+\sum_{m=2}^{p}\,\frac{1}{m!}\,[\tau,\dots,\tau]_{{\operatorname{Hom}},m}\right)\ ,

(4.49)

where the differential
\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}
on the left-hand side is that on the complex
{\mathfrak{g}}
, while
\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{h}}
is—by a slight abuse of notation—induced from the extension of the differential of
\mathfrak{h}
to
{\mathrm{Sym}}^{\leqslant p+1}_{A}(\mathfrak{h}[1])
.
In other words, the left-hand side can be written as

\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}\circ\tau^{\prime}_{p+1}-\tau^{\prime}_{p+1}\circ\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{h}}=\mathrm{d}_{\operatorname{Hom}}\tau^{\prime}_{p+1}\ .

(4.50)

Since by assumption
\tau
is a Maurer–Cartan element in
{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{\mathbbm{R}}({\mathrm{CE}}_{\leqslant p}(\mathfrak{h}),{\mathfrak{g}})
, the only non-trivial part of the Maurer–Cartan equation involves the component
{\mathrm{Sym}}^{p+1}_{A}(\mathfrak{h}[1])\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{g}}
.
We write
F^{p}(\tau)
for this component of the right-hand side of the equation.
Explicitly

F^{p}(\tau)=\tau\circ\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{h}}+\sum_{m=2}^{p}\,\frac{1}{m!}\,[\tau,\dots,\tau]_{{\operatorname{Hom}},m|{\mathrm{Sym}}^{\leqslant p+1}_{A}(\mathfrak{h}[1])}\ .

(4.51)

Definition 4.52

Given an order
p
morphism
\tau\colon\mathfrak{h}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{g}}
of
L_{\infty}
-algebroids over
A
, the element

F^{p}(\tau)\ \in\ {\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}\left({\mathrm{Sym}}^{p+1}_{A}\big(\mathfrak{h}[1]\big),\,\ker(\rho_{\mathfrak{g}})[2]\right)

(4.53)

defined in (
4.51
) is the
curvature
of
\tau
.

The construction of the curvature
F^{p}
gives rise to a map

F^{p}\colon L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}(Q^{(p)}\mathfrak{h},{\mathfrak{g}})\longrightarrow{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}\left({\mathrm{Sym}}_{A}^{p+1}\big(\mathfrak{h}[1]\big)[-2],\ker(\rho_{\mathfrak{g}})\right)\ .

(4.54)

Furthermore, from every order
(p{+}1)
morphism
\tau^{\prime}\in L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}(Q^{(p+1)}\mathfrak{h},{\mathfrak{g}})
we can extract a map of dg
A
-modules

\displaystyle(\tau^{\prime}_{p+1},\mathrm{d}_{\operatorname{Hom}}\tau^{\prime}_{p+1})\colon\big({\mathrm{Sym}}_{A}^{p+1}(\mathfrak{h}[1])\big)[-2,-1]\longrightarrow\ker(\rho_{\mathfrak{g}})

(4.55)

which extracts the component
\tau^{\prime}_{p+1}\colon{\mathrm{Sym}}_{A}^{p+1}(\mathfrak{h}[1])\longrightarrow\ker(\rho_{\mathfrak{g}})[1]
of
\tau^{\prime}
together with its differential (taken in the hom complex).
This defines a map

\displaystyle\begin{split}\psi^{p}\colon L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}(Q^{(p+1)}\mathfrak{h},{\mathfrak{g}})&\longrightarrow{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}\left({\mathrm{Sym}}_{A}^{p+1}\big(\mathfrak{h}[1]\big)[-2,-1],\ker(\rho_{\mathfrak{g}})\right)\ ,\\
\tau^{\prime}&\longmapsto(\tau^{\prime}_{p+1},\mathrm{d}_{\operatorname{Hom}}\tau^{\prime}_{p+1})\ .\end{split}

(4.56)

The maps (
4.54
) and (
4.56
) fit into a commutative diagram of sets

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}(Q^{(p+1)}\mathfrak{h},{\mathfrak{g}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}\left({\mathrm{Sym}}_{A}^{p+1}\big(\mathfrak{h}[1]\big)[-2,-1],\ker(\rho_{\mathfrak{g}})\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}(Q^{(p)}\mathfrak{h},{\mathfrak{g}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}\left({\mathrm{Sym}}_{A}^{p+1}\big(\mathfrak{h}[1]\big)[-2],\ker(\rho_{\mathfrak{g}})\right)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi^{p}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F^{p}}

(4.57)

where the left-hand vertical morphism forgets the order
(p{+}1)
component of a morphism.
The commutative square (
4.57
) is obtained from the square (
4.47
) by taking morphisms to
{\mathfrak{g}}
(this uses that the
L_{\infty}
-algebroids on the left-hand side of (
4.47
) are free, together with Lemma
4.10
(3)).
In particular, by Lemma
4.46
it follows that the square (
4.57
) is cartesian.

With these preparations we can now give

Definition 4.58

Let
x\colon M\longrightarrow X
be a morphism from a smooth manifold to a dt stack.
Suppose that this morphism is equipped with a
p
-form connection
\tau
.
The
curvature
or
field strength
of
\tau
is the morphism of dg
C^{\infty}(M)
-modules

F^{p}(\tau)\colon\left({\mathrm{Sym}}_{C^{\infty}(M)}^{p+1}\big(TM[1]\big)\right)[-2]\longrightarrow\ker(\rho_{T(M/X)})\ .

(4.59)

Remark 4.60

Motivated by the theory of classical principal bundles, we can think of
\ker(\rho_{T(M/X)})
as the module of global sections of the adjoint bundle of
x
.
In this sense the curvature
F^{p}(\tau)
can be interpreted as a (derived)
(p{+}1)
-form with values in the adjoint bundle, generalising the classical picture.

\triangleleft

We record the relation between curvature and lifts to
(p{+}1)
-form connections in

Proposition 4.61

Let
x\colon M\longrightarrow X
be a morphism from a smooth manifold to a dt stack, and suppose that
\tau
is a
p
-form connection on
x
.
There is a one-to-one correspondence between the set of lifts of
\tau
to a
(p{+}1)
-form connection on
x
and the set of maps

\tau^{\prime}_{p+1}\colon\left({\mathrm{Sym}}_{C^{\infty}(M)}^{p+1}\big(TM[1]\big)\right)[-1]\longrightarrow\ker(\rho_{T(M/X)})

(4.62)

such that
\mathrm{d}_{\operatorname{Hom}}\tau^{\prime}_{p+1}=F^{p}(\tau)
.

Proof.

This is exactly the statement that the diagram (
4.57
) is a pullback.
∎

Example 4.63

For ordinary principal bundles, Definition
4.58
exactly recovers the usual definition of the curvature 2-form with values in the adjoint bundle.
Let
G
be a Lie group with Lie algebra
{\mathfrak{g}}
, and let
x\colon M\longrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}G
be a map in
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{M}\hskip-0.56917pt\mathrm{fd}})
.
We know from Example
4.26
that the Atiyah
L_{\infty}
-algebroid associated to the morphism
x\colon M\longrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}G
is the classical Atiyah Lie algebroid of the principal
G
-bundle
P\longrightarrow M
classified by
x
.
The morphism
x\colon M\longrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}G
further has a presentation by a morphism

g\colon{\check{C}}\,\pazocal{U}\,\longrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}G

of simplicial presheaves on
{\mathscr{M}\hskip-0.56917pt\mathrm{fd}}
, where
{\check{C}}\,\pazocal{U}
is the Čech nerve of a good open covering
\pazocal{U}=\{U_{a}\}_{a\in\varLambda}
of the manifold
M
.
In other words,
g
is a Čech 1-cocycle with values in
G
.

The Atiyah algebroid of
x
can then be expressed as

\displaystyle T(M/{\mathrm{B}}G)={\mathrm{At}}(P)

\displaystyle=\big\{(X,\xi)\ |\ X\in TM\ ,\ \xi=(\xi_{a}\colon U_{a}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{g}})_{a\in\varLambda}\ ,

(4.64)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\xi_{b}={\mathrm{Ad}}_{g_{ab}^{-1}}(\xi_{a})+g_{ab}^{*}\,\mu_{G}(X)\ ,\ \forall\,a,b\in\varLambda\big\}\ ,

(4.65)

where
{\mathrm{Ad}}\colon G\longrightarrow\mathsf{Aut}({\mathfrak{g}})
is the adjoint representation of
G
and
\mu_{G}
is its Maurer–Cartan 1-form.
The anchor map
\rho
is the projection

\rho(X,\xi)=X\ ,

and the bracket reads as

\big[(X,\xi),(Y,\eta)\big]=\big([X,Y],\,\pounds_{X}\eta-\pounds_{Y}\xi+[\xi,\eta]\big)\ .

(4.66)

Note that
\ker(\rho)
consists precisely of descent data for sections of the adjoint bundle of
P
.

A 1-form connection on
x
is then a morphism of
C^{\infty}(M)
-modules

\tau=({\mathrm{id}}_{TM},A)\colon TM\longrightarrow{\mathrm{At}}(P)\ ,\quad X\longmapsto\big(X,A(X)\big)\ .

(4.67)

We compute the curvature of
\tau
:
we find

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{\operatorname{Hom}}\,\tau(X_{1}\wedge X_{2})

\displaystyle=\tau\circ\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{CE}}(X_{1}\wedge X_{2})=-\tau\big([X_{1},X_{2}]\big)=-\big([X_{1},X_{2}],\,A([X_{1},X_{2}])\big)\ ,

(4.68)

and

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\,[\tau,\tau]_{{\operatorname{Hom}},2}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}\,[-]_{{\mathrm{At}}(P),2}\circ(\tau\otimes\tau)\circ{\mathsf{\Delta}}_{TM}(X_{1}\wedge X_{2})

(4.69)

\displaystyle=\Big([X_{1},X_{2}],\,\pounds_{X_{1}}\big(A(X_{2})\big)-\pounds_{X_{2}}\big(A(X_{1})\big)-\big[A(X_{1}),A(X_{2})\big]\Big)\ .

(4.70)

Thus since

\mathrm{d}A(X_{1},X_{2})=\pounds_{X_{1}}\big(A(X_{2})\big)-\pounds_{X_{2}}\big(A(X_{1})\big)-A\big([X_{1},X_{2}]\big)\ ,

we indeed see that

F^{1}(A)(X_{1},X_{2})=\mathrm{d}A(X_{1},X_{2})-\big[A(X_{1}),A(X_{2})\big]

(4.71)

recovers the classical expression for the curvature 2-form of the 1-form connection defined by
A
.

\triangleleft

Example 4.72

We revisit Example
4.32
, where we recovered the notion of 2-form connections on trivial bundles for a crossed module 2-group.
Here we obtain for the field strength

F^{2}(A^{(1)},A^{(2)})=\mathrm{d}A^{(2)}+[A^{(1)},A^{(2)}]\ ,

(4.73)

which is the well-known expression for the curvature of a crossed module 2-form connection (compare for instance
[
93
, Equation (5.1.7)]
).

\triangleleft

4.3
Functoriality of spaces of connections

We now discuss the functoriality of spaces of
p
-form connections on maps
x\colon M\longrightarrow X
from a smooth manifold to a dt stack.
This will imply, for instance, a canonical action of automorphisms of
x
on the space of
p
-form connections.
We first need some further background on
L_{\infty}
-algebroids and to see that
Q^{(p)}
induces an
\infty
-functor.

As before, let
A
be a cdga over
\mathbbm{R}
(for instance arising as the underlying cdga of a dg
C^{\infty}
-ring).
Recall that we denote by

L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\infty}\coloneqq L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}[W^{-1}]

(4.74)

the
\infty
-category obtained as the (
\infty
-categorical) localisation of
L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}
at the weak equivalences of
L_{\infty}
-algebroids over
A
.
This is canonically equivalent to the localisation of
L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}
at the
\infty
-morphisms which are weak equivalences.

Lemma 4.75

Let
{\mathfrak{g}}_{\bullet},\mathfrak{h}_{\bullet}\colon\mathbbm{N}\longrightarrow L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}
be functors which send each morphism in the poset
\mathbbm{N}
to a cofibration between cofibrant objects (equivalently,
{\mathfrak{g}}_{\bullet}
and
\mathfrak{h}_{\bullet}
are cofibrant in the Reedy model structure on
{\operatorname{Fun}}(\mathbbm{N},L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}})
).
Suppose that a morphism
f_{\bullet}\colon{\mathfrak{g}}_{\bullet}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{h}_{\bullet}
of diagrams induces weak equivalences of associated graded objects on the level of dg
\mathbbm{R}
-modules.
Then
f_{\bullet}
induces a weak equivalence
\operatorname{colim}_{\mathbbm{N}}f_{\bullet}\colon\operatorname{colim}_{\mathbbm{N}}{\mathfrak{g}}_{\bullet}\longrightarrow\operatorname{colim}_{\mathbbm{N}}\mathfrak{h}_{\bullet}
in
L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}
.

Proof.

A morphism
f\colon{\mathfrak{g}}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{h}
in
L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}
is a weak equivalence if and only if it is a quasi-isomorphism of the underlying dg
A
-modules, since by
[
69
, Theorem 3.1]
the semi-model structure on
L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}
is transferred along the free-forgetful adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F:({\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}})_{/T_{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}:U\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\perp}

(4.76)

As the model structure on
{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}
is itself transferred from that on
{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{\mathbbm{R}}^{\mathrm{dg}}
, it follows that
f
is a weak equivalence if and only if it is a quasi-isomorphism of the underlying dg
\mathbbm{R}
-modules.

The poset
\mathbbm{N}
is filtered, and hence homotopy sifted (see for instance
[
58
, Proposition 5.3.1.20]
).
Thus by
[
69
, Theorem 3.3]
the canonical morphism

\underset{\mathbbm{N}}{\operatorname{hocolim}}\,(U\circ{\mathfrak{g}}_{\bullet})\longrightarrow U\,\underset{\mathbbm{N}}{\operatorname{hocolim}}\,{\mathfrak{g}}_{\bullet}

(4.77)

is a weak equivalence of dg
A
-modules.
We obtain a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underset{\mathbbm{N}}{\operatorname{hocolim}}\,(U\circ{\mathfrak{g}}_{\bullet})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U\,\underset{\mathbbm{N}}{\operatorname{hocolim}}\,{\mathfrak{g}}_{\bullet}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underset{\mathbbm{N}}{\operatorname{colim}}\,(U\circ{\mathfrak{g}}_{\bullet})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U\,\underset{\mathbbm{N}}{\operatorname{colim}}\,{\mathfrak{g}}_{\bullet}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

(4.78)

showing that the canonical morphism
\operatorname{hocolim}_{\mathbbm{N}}{\mathfrak{g}}_{\bullet}\longrightarrow\operatorname{colim}_{\mathbbm{N}}{\mathfrak{g}}_{\bullet}
is a weak equivalence in
L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}
, for each Reedy cofibrant functor
{\mathfrak{g}}_{\bullet}\colon\mathbbm{N}\longrightarrow L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}
.
That is, the functor
U
commutes with colimits of diagrams of this type up to weak equivalence.
Consequently, to check whether
\operatorname{colim}_{\mathbbm{N}}f_{\bullet}
is a weak equivalence in
L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}
, it suffices to check whether
\operatorname{colim}_{\mathbbm{N}}U\,f_{\bullet}
is a weak equivalence in
{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}
, or equivalently in
{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{\mathbbm{R}}^{\mathrm{dg}}
.
This however is equivalent to
U\,f_{\bullet}
inducing quasi-isomorphisms on the associated graded objects.
∎

Remark 4.79

Suppose that
f_{\bullet}\colon{\mathfrak{g}}_{\bullet}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{h}_{\bullet}
is as in Lemma
4.75
, but suppose that there is a positive integer
N\in\mathbbm{N}
such that the restriction of
f_{\bullet}\colon{\mathfrak{g}}_{\bullet}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{h}_{\bullet}
to
\mathbbm{N}_{\geqslant N}
is a constant natural transformation between constant diagrams.
Then
\operatorname{colim}_{\mathbbm{N}}{\mathfrak{g}}_{\bullet}\cong{\mathfrak{g}}_{N}
and
\operatorname{colim}_{\mathbbm{N}}\mathfrak{h}_{\bullet}\cong\mathfrak{h}_{N}
.
It follows that
\operatorname{colim}_{\mathbbm{N}}f_{\bullet}=f_{N}
, and
f_{N}\colon{\mathfrak{g}}_{N}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{h}_{N}
is a weak equivalence in
L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}
whenever
f_{\bullet}
induces weak equivalences on associated graded objects in degrees
0,1,\ldots,N-1
.

\triangleleft

Lemma 4.80

For each
p\in\mathbbm{N}
, the functor

Q^{(p)}\colon L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}\longrightarrow L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}

(4.81)

preserves weak equivalences between
A
-cofibrant
L_{\infty}
-algebroids.

Proof.

Given an
A
-cofibrant
L_{\infty}
-algebroid
{\mathfrak{g}}
, the sequence of functors
\{Q^{(p)}\}_{p\in\mathbbm{N}}
produces a functor

Q^{(\bullet)}{\mathfrak{g}}\colon\mathbbm{N}\longrightarrow L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}\ ,\quad p\longmapsto Q^{(p)}{\mathfrak{g}}\ .

(4.82)

It follows from Lemma
4.46
that this functor is a Reedy cofibrant object of
{\operatorname{Fun}}(\mathbbm{N},L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}})
(see the proof of
[
69
, Proposition 5.17]
for details).
The associated graded of this sequence is
[
69
, Remark 5.20]

\mathrm{gr}(Q^{(p)}{\mathfrak{g}})=\Big(F\big({\mathrm{Sym}}^{p}_{A}({\mathfrak{g}}[1])[-1]\big),\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}\Big)\ ,

(4.83)

where
F\big({\mathrm{Sym}}^{p}_{A}({\mathfrak{g}}[1])[-1]\big)
is the free Lie algebroid on the dg
A
-module of degree
p
polynomials in
{\mathfrak{g}}[1]
, endowed with the extension of the differential on
{\mathfrak{g}}
by the Leibniz rule.

For each morphism
f\colon{\mathfrak{g}}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{h}
in
L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{(\infty)}
, we obtain a natural transformation

Q^{(\bullet)}f\colon Q^{(\bullet)}{\mathfrak{g}}\longrightarrow Q^{(\bullet)}\mathfrak{h}

(4.84)

in
{\operatorname{Fun}}(\mathbbm{N},L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}})
.
Suppose that
f
is a weak equivalence between
A
-cofibrant
L_{\infty}
-algebroids.
By Lemma
4.75
and Remark
4.79
it suffices to check that
Q^{(\bullet)}f
induces weak equivalences of dg
\mathbbm{R}
-modules on the associated gradeds in (
4.83
).
This follows by the same argument as in the proof of
[
69
, Proposition 5.22(a)]
.
∎

Remark 4.85

Let
\iota\colon L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}
be the canonical inclusion functor.
If
{\mathfrak{g}}
is an
A
-cofibrant
L_{\infty}
-algebroid over
A
, each morphism in the chain
Q^{(p)}{\mathfrak{g}}\longrightarrow Q^{(p+1)}{\mathfrak{g}}
is a cofibration in
L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}
.
Since
Q^{(1)}{\mathfrak{g}}=(F({\mathfrak{g}}),\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}})
is itself a cofibrant object in
L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}
, it follows that
Q^{(p)}{\mathfrak{g}}
is cofibrant for each
p\in\mathbbm{N}
.
Thus
Q^{(p)}\circ\iota
maps
A
-cofibrant
L_{\infty}
-algebroids over
A
to cofibrant
L_{\infty}
-algebroids; in particular, it restricts to the full subcategory
(L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}})_{\mathrm{cof}}\subset L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}
on the cofibrant objects.

\triangleleft

Proposition 4.86

The functors

Q^{(p)}\circ\iota\ ,\ Q\circ\iota\colon L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}\longrightarrow L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}\ ,\quad p\in\mathbbm{N}

(4.87)

induce
\infty
-functors

\overline{Q^{(p)}}\ ,\ \overline{Q}\colon L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\infty}\longrightarrow L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\infty}\ .

(4.88)

Proof.

By the results in this subsection thus far, the functors

Q^{(p)}\circ\iota\ ,\ Q\circ\iota\colon L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}\longrightarrow L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}\ ,\quad p\in\mathbbm{N}

(4.89)

canonically induce
\infty
-functors

\widetilde{Q^{(p)}}\ ,\ \widetilde{Q}\colon(L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}})_{\mathrm{cof}}[W^{-1}]\longrightarrow(L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}})_{\mathrm{cof}}[W^{-1}]\ .

(4.90)

By
[
26
, Theorem 7.5.18]
the inclusion
\jmath\colon(L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}})_{\mathrm{cof}}\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}
induces a canonical equivalence of
\infty
-categories

\overline{\jmath}\colon(L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}})_{\mathrm{cof}}[W^{-1}]\longrightarrow L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\infty}\ .

(4.91)

Thus we obtain canonical
\infty
-functors

\overline{Q^{(p)}}\coloneqq\overline{\jmath}\circ\widetilde{Q^{(p)}}\circ\overline{\jmath}^{-1}\colon L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\infty}\longrightarrow L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\infty}

(4.92)

as desired, and analogously for
\overline{Q}\coloneqq\overline{\jmath}\circ\widetilde{Q}\circ\overline{\jmath}^{-1}
.
∎

We can now state the functoriality of our spaces of connections.
Let
M
be a smooth manifold and
X\in{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}})
a dt stack.
We obtain a commutative diagram of
\infty
-categories

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textstyle{\int}\,{\mathrm{Con}}_{p}(-)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{M}^{\infty}\big)_{\overline{Q^{(p)}}TM/}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{S}_{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}})(M,X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{M}^{\infty}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T(M/-)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Con}}_{p}(-)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Hom}}(\overline{Q^{(p)}}TM,-)}

(4.93)

Here the rightmost vertical morphism
\mathscr{S}_{*}\longrightarrow\mathscr{S}
is the universal left fibration.
The
\infty
-functor

{\operatorname{Hom}}(\overline{Q^{(p)}}TM,-)\colon L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{M}^{\infty}\longrightarrow\mathscr{S}

(4.94)

classifies the left fibration

\big(L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{M}^{\infty}\big)_{\overline{Q^{(p)}}TM/}\longrightarrow L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{M}^{\infty}\ ,

(4.95)

and so the right-hand square is a pullback square (and also a homotopy pullback square in the Joyal model structure; see for instance
[
26
, Corollary 5.3.6]
).
The left-hand vertical arrow is the left fibration classified by the composition of the bottom horizontal arrows, i.e. the
\infty
-functor

\displaystyle{\mathrm{Con}}_{p}(-)\colon{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}})(M,X)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathscr{S}\ ,

(4.96)

\displaystyle(x\colon M\longrightarrow X)

\displaystyle\longmapsto{\mathrm{Con}}_{p}(x)=L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{M}^{\infty}\big(\overline{Q^{(p)}}TM,\,T(M/X)\big)\ .

(4.97)

Since the codomain of this left fibration is an
\infty
-groupoid, it is even a Kan fibration.

Example 4.98

For
X={\mathrm{B}}G
the classifying object of a Lie group
G
, we obtain

\int\,{\mathrm{Con}}_{1}(-)\simeq{\mathscr{B}\hskip-0.56917pt\mathrm{un}}_{\nabla}(M;G)\ ,

(4.99)

the groupoid of principal
G
-bundles with (not necessarily flat) connection on
M
.

One of the main results of the remainder of this paper is an explicit proof that also for
X={\mathrm{B}}^{n}{\mathrm{U}}(1)
and any
p\in\mathbbm{N}
, there is a canonical equivalence of
spaces

\int{\mathrm{Con}}_{p}(-)\simeq{\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}}^{n-1}_{\nabla|p}(M)\ ,

(4.100)

where the right-hand side is the space of
(n{-}1)
-gerbes with
p
-form connection on the manifold
M
(if
p>n
, then
{\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}}^{n-1}_{\nabla|p}(M)={\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}}^{n-1}_{\flat}(M)
is the space of
(n{-}1)
-gerbes on
M
with
flat
connection).

\triangleleft

As a consequence of the above and Example
4.23
, we can now give a definition of a space of
G_{\bullet}
-bundles with
p
-form connection from first principles, for
any
group object
G_{\bullet}
in
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}})
with deformation theory and any manifold
M
.

Definition 4.101

Let
M
be a smooth manifold and
G_{\bullet}\in{\operatorname{Fun}}({{\mathbbe{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{op}},{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}}))
a group object whose underlying object
G=G_{1}
in
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}})
is a dt stack.
For each
p\in\mathbbm{N}
, the
\infty
-groupoid of
G_{\bullet}
-principal
\infty
-bundles on
M
with
p
-form connection
is

{\mathscr{B}\hskip-0.56917pt\mathrm{un}}_{\nabla|p}(M;G_{\bullet})\coloneqq\int\,{\mathrm{Con}}_{p}(-)\ .

(4.102)

Remark 4.103

The fact that we have constructed
{\mathrm{Con}}_{p}(-)
as an
\infty
-functor automatically encodes the action of bundle morphisms (in particular gauge transformations) on the space of
p
-form connections on any
G_{\bullet}
-bundle
P\longrightarrow M
, enabling the study of the higher gauge theory of such connections.

\triangleleft

5
\infty
-groupoids of connections on higher
{\mathrm{U}}(1)
-bundles

The goal of this section is to prove that the notion of higher-form connections we introduced in Definition
4.21
reproduces the entire
\infty
-groupoids of connections on higher gerbes, i.e.
{\mathrm{B}}^{n}{\mathrm{U}}(1)
-principal
\infty
-bundles.
While the connections on a classical principal bundle form a set, it has been observed in
[
16
]
that the connections on a higher bundle form a higher groupoid.
Below we start by computing two equivalent presentations of the Atiyah
L_{\infty}
-algebroid of any given higher gerbe (Corollary
5.13
and Theorem
5.27
).
We then recall the definition of the space of
p
-form connections on an
(n{-}1)
-gerbe via Čech–Deligne cocycles and state the main result of this section in Theorem
5.55
:
The Čech–Deligne picture of
p
-form connections on
(n{-}1)
-gerbes is equivalent to that following from Definition
4.21
.
This is remarkable because the former makes no reference to derived geometry, and so is entirely independent of the geometric methods and approach to connections we developed in the first part of this paper.

5.1
Atiyah
L_{\infty}
-algebroids of higher
{\mathrm{U}}(1)
-bundles

Our goal will now be to calculate the
L_{\infty}
-algebroid associated to a higher
{\mathrm{U}}(1)
-bundle

x\colon M\longrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}^{n}{\mathrm{U}}(1)

on a smooth manifold
M
.
Such a bundle is also called an
(n{-}1)
-gerbe
on
M
.
We will carry out the computation in a few steps, using the fact that the bottom-right square in the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{B}}^{n}{\mathrm{U}}(1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ast}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{B}}^{n+1}\mathbbm{Z}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{B}}^{n+1}\mathbbm{R}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x_{\mathrm{top}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

(5.1)

is a homotopy pullback square of smooth stacks. Here

[x_{\mathrm{top}}]\ \in\ \pi_{0}\,{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{M}\hskip-0.56917pt\mathrm{fd}})(M,{\mathrm{B}}^{n+1}\mathbbm{Z})\cong\mathrm{H}^{n+1}(M;\mathbbm{Z})

is the integral cohomology class classifying the
(n{-}1)
-gerbe.

Theorem
3.2
implies that taking the associated
L_{\infty}
-algebroids preserves homotopy pullbacks.
Our strategy will therefore be to compute the
L_{\infty}
-algebroids associated to the three corners of the square (
5.1
), as well as the morphisms between them, and then compute the homotopy pullback in the
\infty
-category of
L_{\infty}
-algebroids.
Let us point out that the homotopy pullback diagram we will construct is only going to live in the
\infty
-category
L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{M}^{\mathrm{dg}}[W^{-1}]=L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{M}^{\infty}
; its morphisms will be presented by
\infty
-morphisms of
L_{\infty}
-algebroids.

We start by identifying the
L_{\infty}
-algebroid associated to the bottom-right object in (
5.1
).

Proposition 5.2

Let
0\colon M\longrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}^{n+1}\mathbbm{R}
be the zero map. The associated
L_{\infty}
-algebroid is given by

T(M/{\mathrm{B}}^{n+1}\mathbbm{R})\simeq TM\oplus C^{\infty}(M)[n]\ ,

(5.3)

with the only non-trivial brackets given by the usual commutator bracket on
TM
and by
[X,f]=\pounds_{X}(f)
for
X\in TM
and
f\in C^{\infty}(M)
.

Proof.

Let
{\mathfrak{g}}
be a complete
L_{\infty}
-algebroid over
M
and let
M\longrightarrow Y
be the associated map to a qcdt stack (via Theorem
3.2
). Then there are natural equivalences of spaces

\displaystyle L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{M}^{{\mathrm{cpl}},\infty}\big({\mathfrak{g}},T(M/{\mathrm{B}}^{n+1}\mathbbm{R})\big)

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}})_{M/}\big(Y,{\mathrm{B}}^{n+1}\mathbbm{R}\big)

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathrm{DK}}\Big({\mathrm{hofib}}\big(\Gamma(Y,{\mathscr{O}}_{Y})\longrightarrow C^{\infty}(M)\big)[n+1]\Big)

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathrm{DK}}\Big({\mathrm{hofib}}\big({\mathrm{CE}}^{*}({\mathfrak{g}})\longrightarrow C^{\infty}(M)\big)[n+1]\Big)\ .

Here the first and last equivalences follow from Theorem
3.2
. The second equivalence follows from Examples
2.59
and
2.63
.

The last homotopy fibre can be identified with the
reduced
Chevalley–Eilenberg complex
\overline{{\mathrm{CE}}}{}^{*}({\mathfrak{g}})
, and so the last term corresponds under the Dold–Kan correspondence to the cochain complex

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z^{n+1}\overline{{\mathrm{CE}}}{}^{*}({\mathfrak{g}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{{\mathrm{CE}}}{}^{n}({\mathfrak{g}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{{\mathrm{CE}}}{}^{n-1}({\mathfrak{g}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots\ .}

Unraveling the definition of
\infty
-morphisms of
L_{\infty}
-algebroids, we see that the degree
(n{+}1)
cocycles in the reduced Chevalley–Eilenberg complex of an
L_{\infty}
-algebroid
{\mathfrak{g}}
correspond precisely to the
\infty
-morphisms
{\mathfrak{g}}\,\relbar\joinrel\leadsto\,TM\oplus C^{\infty}(M)[n]
. Combining this with the simplicial resolution from Theorem
D.108
, one finds that

{\mathrm{DK}}\big(\overline{{\mathrm{CE}}}{}^{*}({\mathfrak{g}})[n+1]\big)\simeq L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{M}^{{\mathrm{cpl}},\infty}\big({\mathfrak{g}},TM\oplus C^{\infty}(M)[n]\big)

(5.4)

is a model for the
\infty
-categorical mapping space we are interested in. It then follows from the Yoneda lemma that
T(M/{\mathrm{B}}^{n+1}\mathbbm{R})\simeq TM\oplus C^{\infty}(M)[n]
.
∎

Lemma 5.5

The
L_{\infty}
-algebroids associated to
M\longrightarrow\ast
and
M\longrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}^{n+1}\mathbbm{Z}
each coincide with the tangent bundle
TM
.

Proof.

We have already proven the first case in Example
3.4
. For the second case, notice that
{\mathrm{B}}^{n+1}\mathbbm{Z}
is a discrete, or constant, stack (we will expand on this further in the paragraphs following this proof). In particular,
{\mathrm{B}}^{n+1}\mathbbm{Z}(U)={\mathrm{B}}^{n+1}\mathbbm{Z}(U_{\mathrm{red}})
for any inf-manifold
U
. It readily follows that the formal moduli problem associated to
M\longrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}^{n+1}\mathbbm{Z}
is the terminal one, so that the corresponding
L_{\infty}
-algebroid is given by
TM
.
∎

Consequently, the homotopy pullback square (
5.1
) gives rise to a homotopy pullback square of
L_{\infty}
-algebroids

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\big(M/{\mathrm{B}}^{n}{\mathrm{U}}(1)\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TM}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TM}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TM\oplus C^{\infty}(M)[n]}

Note that this diagram a priori only lives in the
\infty
-category
L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{M}
. By our computation of the mapping spaces of this
\infty
-category in terms of
\infty
-morphisms, we can present the bottom and right map by
\infty
-morphisms of
L_{\infty}
-algebroids. It remains to identify these two
\infty
-morphisms more explicitly and then calculate the homotopy pullback.

The proof of Proposition
5.2
shows that there is a bijection

\operatorname{Ho}(L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{M}^{\mathrm{dg}})\big(TM,\,TM\oplus C^{\infty}(M)[n]\big)\cong\overline{\mathrm{H}}{}^{n+1}_{\mathrm{dR}}(M)

between the set of
\infty
-morphisms
TM\,\relbar\joinrel\leadsto\,TM\oplus C^{\infty}(M)[n]
and the set of degree
(n{+}1)
reduced de Rham cohomology classes (i.e. the cohomology of
\Omega^{\geqslant 1}(M)
). Therefore it will now suffice to identify the two de Rham cohomology classes associated to the bottom and vertical map in (
5.1
).

To achieve this we will need to take a slight formal detour. First, let
\Gamma\colon{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}})\longrightarrow\mathscr{S}
be the global sections functor, evaluating a sheaf at the final object
*
(represented by the one-point smooth manifold). This functor admits a fully faithful left adjoint
\mathrm{cst}
, which takes
constant sheaves
, and this in turn has a further left adjoint
\Pi_{\infty}
taking
shapes
, or
homotopy types
.
Altogether we obtain a double adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{\infty}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\perp}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{cst}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\perp}

(5.6)

Using the adjoint pairs (
2.52
), these functors can also be described as follows: for any homotopy type
K
, the
\infty
-sheaf
\mathrm{cst}(K)
is given by
\mathrm{cst}(K)(U)=\mathrm{cst}(K)(U_{\mathrm{red}})
, where on the right hand side we take the constant sheaf on
K
on the site of ordinary smooth manifolds. Likewise,
\Pi_{\infty}(X)=\Pi_{\infty}(X_{\mathrm{red}})
is the shape of the reduction of
X
, i.e. the shape of its restriction to the site of smooth manifolds. In other words, both functors
\mathrm{cst}
and
\Pi_{\infty}
are insensitive to any derived or infinitesimal structure.

By a slight abuse of notation, we will denote the composition
\mathrm{cst}\circ\Pi_{\infty}
again by
\Pi_{\infty}
; it will be clear from the context whether the output produced by the functor is a single space or a sheaf of spaces on
{\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}}
.
Any object
X\in{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}})
comes naturally endowed with a map
X\longrightarrow\Pi_{\infty}(X)
with the following universal property: for any map
X\longrightarrow\mathrm{cst}(K)
to a constant sheaf, there exists a unique map

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{\infty}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{cst}(K)}

as indicated.

Lemma 5.7

Let
X\in{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}})
, and consider the natural map
X\longrightarrow\Pi_{\infty}(X)
. The natural map
\Pi_{\infty}(X)^{\wedge}_{X}\longrightarrow\ast^{\wedge}_{X}=X_{\mathrm{dR}}
of formal completions (Definition
3.7
) is an equivalence of sheaves.

Proof.

Since
\Pi_{\infty}(X)
is a constant sheaf, it satisfies
\Pi_{\infty}(X)(U)\simeq\Pi_{\infty}(X)(U_{\mathrm{red}})
for all inf-manifolds
U
. We then compute

\Pi_{\infty}(X)^{\wedge}_{X}(U)=\Pi_{\infty}(X)(U)\times_{\Pi_{\infty}(X)(U_{\mathrm{red}})}X(U_{\mathrm{red}})\simeq X(U_{\mathrm{red}})\simeq X_{\mathrm{dR}}(U)\ ,

as required.
∎

In particular, for any sheaf
X\in{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}})
there is a natural sequence of maps

X\longrightarrow X_{\mathrm{dR}}\longrightarrow\Pi_{\infty}(X)\ .

Lemma 5.8

Let
M
be a smooth manifold. The restriction along the canonical map
M_{\mathrm{dR}}\longrightarrow\Pi_{\infty}(M)
induces a natural quasi-isomorphism of function algebras

\Gamma(\Pi_{\infty}(M),{\mathscr{O}}_{\Pi_{\infty}(M)})\xrightarrow{\ \simeq\ }\Gamma(M_{\mathrm{dR}},{\mathscr{O}}_{M_{\mathrm{dR}}})\ .

Consequently, the restriction map

{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}})(\Pi_{\infty}(M),{\mathrm{B}}^{n+1}\mathbbm{R})\longrightarrow{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}})(M_{\mathrm{dR}},{\mathrm{B}}^{n+1}\mathbbm{R})

(5.9)

is an equivalence for all
n\geqslant 0
.

This is a version of the de Rham Theorem relating singular and de Rham cohomology.

Proof.

Both
X\longmapsto X_{\mathrm{dR}}
and
X\longmapsto\Pi_{\infty}(X)
preserve homotopy colimits of
\infty
-sheaves, and
\Gamma(-,{\mathscr{O}})
sends such homotopy colimits to homotopy limits. Since every smooth manifold can be written as a homotopy colimit of an open cover by contractibles, this reduces the assertion to the case where
M
is contractible. But in that case
\Pi_{\infty}(M)\simeq\ast
, so that
\Gamma(\Pi_{\infty}(M),{\mathscr{O}}_{\Pi_{\infty}(M)})\simeq\mathbbm{R}
is the initial cdga, while
\Gamma(M_{\mathrm{dR}},{\mathscr{O}}_{M_{\mathrm{dR}}})\simeq\Omega^{*}(M)\simeq\mathbbm{R}
by the usual de Rham Theorem and Example
3.4
. In particular, any map between these cdgas is a quasi-isomorphism.
The second part of the lemma follows from the first part and Example
2.63
.
∎

For a smooth manifold
M
and
p\in\mathbbm{N}
, we write
\Omega^{p}_{\mathrm{cl}}(M)
for the abelian group of closed
p
-forms on
M
.

Proposition 5.10

Let
M
be a smooth manifold and consider the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ast}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{B}}^{n+1}\mathbbm{Z}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{B}}^{n+1}\mathbbm{R}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x_{\mathrm{top}}}

of
\infty
-sheaves. The associated diagram in
L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{M}
is given (up to homotopy) by

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TM}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TM}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TM\oplus C^{\infty}(M)[n]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H}

where the right vertical
\infty
-morphism corresponds to
0\in\Omega_{\mathrm{cl}}^{n+1}(M)
and the bottom
\infty
-morphism corresponds to any closed
(n{+}1)
-form
H\in\Omega_{\mathrm{cl}}^{n+1}(M)
representing the integral cohomology class
[x_{\mathrm{top}}]
.

Proof.

The left-bottom part of the diagram can be further decomposed as the sequence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{\mathrm{dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{\infty}(M)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{B}}^{n+1}\mathbbm{Z}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{B}}^{n+1}\mathbbm{R}\ .}

Since by assumption the map
\Pi_{\infty}(M)\longrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}^{n+1}\mathbbm{Z}
classifies the cohomology class
[x_{\mathrm{top}}]
, Lemma
5.8
implies that the homotopy class of the composite map
M_{\mathrm{dR}}\longrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}^{n+1}\mathbbm{R}
is classified by the de Rham cohomology class associated to
x_{\mathrm{top}}
.

Now note that there are isomorphisms

\displaystyle\pi_{0}\,L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{M}^{\infty}\big(T(M/{\mathrm{B}}^{n+1}\mathbbm{Z}),\ T(M/{\mathrm{B}}^{n+1}\mathbbm{R})\big)

(5.11)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\cong\overline{\mathrm{H}}{}^{n+1}_{\mathrm{dR}}(M)\cong\pi_{0}\Big({\mathscr{S}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}})\big(M_{\mathrm{dR}},{\mathrm{B}}^{n+1}\mathbbm{R}\big)\times^{h}_{{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.0819pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}})(M,{\mathrm{B}}^{n+1}\mathbbm{R})}\{0\}\Big)\ ,

(5.12)

where the middle term is the reduced de Rham cohomology (i.e. the cohomology of
\Omega^{\geqslant 1}(M)
—in these degrees there is no difference). The first isomorphism is the isomorphism appearing in the proof of Proposition
5.2
(using Lemma
5.5
), and the second isomorphism follows from Example
3.4
. The induced morphism

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TM=T(M/{\mathrm{B}}^{n+1}\mathbbm{Z})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T(M/{\mathrm{B}}^{n+1}\mathbbm{R})=TM\oplus C^{\infty}(M)[n]}

can therefore be described by any representative
H
of the image of
[x_{\mathrm{top}}]
in de Rham cohomology.

For the right-hand vertical map one argues similarly, but in this case one simply needs a representative for the
zero
cohomology class.
∎

This now leads to the first main result of this section, namely an identification of the Atiyah
L_{\infty}
-algebroid
