T(M/{\mathrm{B}}^{n}{\mathrm{U}}(1))
through

Corollary 5.13

Let
M
be a smooth manifold and
x\colon M\longrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}^{n}{\mathrm{U}}(1)
a morphism of stacks (i.e. the classifying map of an
(n{-}1)
-gerbe on
M
). The associated
L_{\infty}
-algebroid is given by

T\big(M/{\mathrm{B}}^{n}{\mathrm{U}}(1)\big)\simeq\big(TM\oplus C^{\infty}(M)[n-1],\,H\big)\ ,

(5.14)

whose underlying complex is
TM\oplus C^{\infty}(M)[n-1]
with zero differential, whose anchor map is the canonical projection onto
TM
, whose binary bracket is given by the Lie bracket on vector fields and by the Lie derivative
[X,f]=\pounds_{X}(f)
for all
X\in TM
and
f\in C^{\infty}(M)[n-1]
, and whose
(n{+}1)
-ary bracket is given on
X_{1},\dots,X_{n+1}\in TM
by

[X_{1},\dots,X_{n+1}]=H(X_{1},\dots,X_{n+1})\ ,

(5.15)

for any closed
(n{+}1)
-form
H\in\Omega^{n+1}_{\mathrm{cl}}(M)
on
M
which represents the cohomology class in
\mathrm{H}^{n+1}(M,\mathbbm{Z})
defined by
x
.
All other brackets are zero.

Proof.

One has to compute the homotopy pullback of the diagram of
L_{\infty}
-algebroids and
\infty
-morphisms appearing in Proposition
5.10
. This can be done explicitly, cf.
[
69
]
.
∎

The Atiyah
L_{\infty}
-algebroid
T(M/{\mathrm{B}}^{n}{\mathrm{U}}(1))
in Corollary
5.13
depends on the choice of a closed
(n{+}1)
-form
H
, and we abbreviate

\displaystyle{\mathrm{At}}_{n-1}(H):=\big(TM\oplus C^{\infty}(M)[n-1],\,H\big)\ .

(5.16)

The form
H
can be thought of as the curvature form of a full
n
-form connection on the
(n{-}1)
-gerbe classified by the morphism
x\colon M\longrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}^{n}{\mathrm{U}}(1)
(see Example
5.57
below).
We conclude this section by providing an equivalent model of
T(M/{\mathrm{B}}^{n}{\mathrm{U}}(1))
as a dg Lie algebroid.
It will not depend on a choice of an
(n{+}1)
-form, but only on a presentation of the abstract morphism
x\colon M\longrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}^{n}{\mathrm{U}}(1)
via a choice of a good open covering
\pazocal{U}
of
M
and a
{\mathrm{U}}(1)
-valued Čech
n
-cocycle
g
subordinate to
\pazocal{U}
.

Remark 5.17

Let
{\mathscr{C}\mathrm{art}}
be the category of cartesian spaces
\mathbbm{R}^{k}
,
with
k\in\mathbbm{N}_{0}
, and smooth maps. The morphism
x\colon M\longrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}^{n}{\mathrm{U}}(1)
can be presented by Čech data as follows:
there are canonical equivalences of
\infty
-categories

{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{M}\hskip-0.56917pt\mathrm{fd}})\simeq{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{C}\mathrm{art}})\simeq L_{W}{\operatorname{Fun}}({\mathscr{C}\mathrm{art}}^{\mathrm{op}},{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{et}_{\hskip-0.85355pt\Delta}})\ ,

(5.18)

where the first equivalence is induced by the canonical inclusion
{\mathscr{C}\mathrm{art}}\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,{\mathscr{M}\hskip-0.56917pt\mathrm{fd}}
(see for instance
[
19
, Theorem B.8]
for a presentation, or
[
2
, Corollary A.5.6]
for an
\infty
-categorical proof) and the last
\infty
-category is the
\infty
-localisation of the local projective model structure on
{\operatorname{Fun}}({\mathscr{C}\mathrm{art}}^{\mathrm{op}},{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{et}_{\hskip-0.85355pt\Delta}})
.

If we let
\pazocal{U}=\{U_{a}\}_{a\in\varLambda}
be a good open covering of the smooth manifold
M
and
{\check{C}}\,\pazocal{U}
its simplicial Čech nerve, then it follows that the morphism
x\colon M\longrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}^{n}{\mathrm{U}}(1)
can be presented by a Čech cocycle
g\colon{\check{C}}\,\pazocal{U}\longrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}^{n}{\mathrm{U}}(1)
.

\triangleleft

In the following we fix an
(n{-}1)
-gerbe
x\colon M\longrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}^{n}{\mathrm{U}}(1)
on
M
, together with a presentation by a Čech cocycle

g\colon{\check{C}}\,\pazocal{U}\longrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}^{n}{\mathrm{U}}(1)\ .

We denote the Čech differential by
\check{\delta}:\Omega^{q}({\check{C}}_{k}\,\pazocal{U})\longrightarrow\Omega^{q}({\check{C}}_{k+1}\,\pazocal{U})
, for
q,k\in\mathbbm{N}_{0}
.

Definition 5.19

The
Atiyah
L_{\infty}
-algebroid
{\check{C}\mathrm{At}}(g)
of
g
is given as follows:
the non-vanishing part of its underlying cochain complex reads as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\infty}({\check{C}}_{0}\,\pazocal{U})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\infty}({\check{C}}_{1}\,\pazocal{U})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\infty}({\check{C}}_{n-2}\,\pazocal{U})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{n-1}(g)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-1)^{n}\,\check{\delta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-1)^{n-1}\,\check{\delta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-1)^{3}\,\check{\delta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(0,(-1)^{2}\,\check{\delta})}

(5.20)

where

E_{n-1}(g)\coloneqq\big\{(X,f)\in TM\oplus C^{\infty}({\check{C}}_{n-1}\,\pazocal{U})\ \big|\ \mathrm{d}\log(g)(X)-\check{\delta}f=0\big\}

(5.21)

is situated in degree zero.
Its anchor map is the canonical projection onto
TM
, while its only non-trivial bracket is the binary bracket which is given by

\displaystyle\big[(X,f)\,,\,(Y,h)\big]

\displaystyle=\big([X,Y]\,,\,\pounds_{Y}f-\pounds_{X}h\big)\ ,

\displaystyle\forall\,(X,f)\,,\,(Y,h)\in{\check{C}\mathrm{At}}(g)_{0}\ ,

(5.22)

\displaystyle\big[(X,f)\,,\,h\big]

\displaystyle=\pounds_{X}h\ ,

\displaystyle\forall\,(X,f)\in{\check{C}\mathrm{At}}(g)_{0}\,,\,h\in{\check{C}\mathrm{At}}(g)_{k}\,,\,-(n-1)\leqslant k\leqslant-1\ ,

(5.23)

\displaystyle[f,h]

\displaystyle=0\ ,

\displaystyle\forall\,f\in{\check{C}\mathrm{At}}(g)_{k}\,,\,h\in{\check{C}\mathrm{At}}(g)_{l}\,,\,-(n-1)\leqslant l,k\leqslant-1\ .

(5.24)

In particular,
{\check{C}\mathrm{At}}(g)
is even a fibrant dg Lie algebroid on
M
.

Remark 5.25

The kernel of the anchor map
{\check{C}\mathrm{At}}(g)\longrightarrow TM
is the abelian dg Lie algebroid with zero anchor map and underlying complex

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\infty}({\check{C}}_{0}\,\pazocal{U})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\infty}({\check{C}}_{1}\,\pazocal{U})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\infty}({\check{C}}_{n-2}\,\pazocal{U})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ker(\check{\delta})\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-1)^{n}\,\check{\delta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-1)^{n-1}\,\check{\delta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-1)^{3}\,\check{\delta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-1)^{2}\,\check{\delta}}

(5.26)

This complex is quasi-isomorphic to
C^{\infty}(M)[n-1]
.

\triangleleft

There is a notion of
p
-form connections on a Čech cocycle
g\colon{\check{C}}\,\pazocal{U}\longrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}^{n}{\mathrm{U}}(1)
which predates and is independent of the formalism for
p
-form connections developed in the present paper.
Giving an
n
-form connection
\pazocal{A}
of this type on
g
is equivalent to enhancing
g
to a full Čech–Deligne differential cocycle (we review this in more detail in Section
5.2
below).
The second main result of this section is the strictification of
T(M/{\mathrm{B}}^{n}{\mathrm{U}}(1))
to a dg Lie algebroid on
M
through

Theorem 5.27

Let
H\in\Omega^{n+1}_{\mathrm{cl}}(M)
be a closed
(n{+}1)
-form whose class
[H]\in\mathrm{H}^{n+1}_{\mathrm{dR}}(M)
agrees with the image of
[g]\in\mathrm{H}^{n+1}(M;\mathbbm{Z})
in de Rham cohomology.
Any enhancement of
g
to a full Čech–Deligne differential cocycle
(g,\pazocal{A})
with curvature
H
canonically induces an equivalence of
L_{\infty}
-algebroids on
M
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varPhi_{g,\pazocal{A}}\colon{\mathrm{At}}_{n-1}(H)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\check{C}\mathrm{At}}(g)\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

(5.28)

We prove Theorem
5.27
in Appendix
C
.

Remark 5.29

Theorem
5.27
for
n=1
also appears in
[
37
, Proposition 5.1.3]
and for
n=2
in
[
37
, Proposition 5.2.6]
. The novelty in our approach is a universal explicit computation and strictification of the Atiyah
L_{\infty}
-algebroid of an
(n{-}1)
-gerbe for all
n\geqslant 1
.

\triangleleft

5.2
\infty
-groupoids of
p
-form connections: Čech–Deligne vs. derived geometric

Given a Lie group
G
and a principal
G
-bundle
P\longrightarrow M
, the connections on
P
form a
set

{\mathrm{Con}}(P)
.
This set carries an affine action of the space of 1-forms on
M
valued in the adjoint bundle of
P
, but it does not exhibit any higher structure.
Higher
{\mathrm{U}}(1)
-principal bundles
\pazocal{G}
over a manifold
M
and their connections have a description in terms of Čech–Deligne cocycles.
Using this description, it has already been observed in
[
16
]
that the
p
-form connections on
\pazocal{G}
form an
\infty
-groupoid which, in general, does have non-trivial morphisms and higher morphisms; that is, we should have the following expectation, which is a feature of higher gauge theory:

In general, the collection of
p
-form connections on a given higher principal bundle is not a set, but a higher groupoid with its own internal morphisms.

We emphasise that the description of
p
-form connections on
(n{-}1)
-gerbes in terms of Čech–Deligne differential cocycles is entirely unrelated to the formalism we have developed in this paper.
The main upshot of our new derived deformation theoretic approach is that, in contrast to the Čech–Deligne picture, it straightforwardly generalises to generic types of principal
\infty
-bundles on manifolds in
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}})
.
In this section we prove that for higher gerbes
x\colon M\longrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}^{n}{\mathrm{U}}(1)
on
M
the derived geometric approach reproduces the full homotopy type of
p
-form connections on
x
as defined independently via Čech–Deligne differential cocycles.

We first recall the definition of
p
-form connections used in
[
16
]
.
It extends the geometric picture on differential cohomology promoted in
[
41
,
39
,
76
]
.
For
0\leqslant p\leqslant n\in\mathbbm{N}_{0}
, consider the complex of sheaves of abelian groups

(5.30)

where the sheaf
{\mathrm{U}}(1)
of smooth
{\mathrm{U}}(1)
-valued functions is situated in degree
-n
.
We define a simplicial presheaf on
{\mathscr{C}\mathrm{art}}\subset{\mathscr{M}\hskip-0.56917pt\mathrm{fd}}
as

{\mathrm{B}}^{n-p}\,{\mathrm{B}}_{\nabla}^{p}{\mathrm{U}}(1)\coloneqq{\mathrm{DK}}\circ{\mathrm{b}}^{n-p}\,{\mathrm{b}}_{\nabla}^{p}{\mathrm{U}}(1)\ ,

(5.31)

where
{\mathrm{DK}}
is the Dold–Kan functor.

Let
M
be a smooth manifold and
\pazocal{U}=\{U_{a}\}_{a\in\varLambda}
a good open cover of
M
.
The Kan complex of
(n{-}1)
-gerbes with
p
-form connection on
M
relative to
\pazocal{U}
is defined as the simplicially enriched hom

{\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}}^{n-1}_{\nabla|p}(\pazocal{U})=\underline{{\operatorname{Fun}}({\mathscr{C}\mathrm{art}}^{\mathrm{op}},{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{et}_{\hskip-0.85355pt\Delta}})}\big({\check{C}}\,\pazocal{U},{\mathrm{B}}^{n-p}\,{\mathrm{B}}_{\nabla}^{p}{\mathrm{U}}(1)\big)\ ,

(5.32)

where
{\check{C}}\,\pazocal{U}
denotes the Čech nerve of the good open cover
\pazocal{U}
of
M
, and where the underline indicates that we are taking the enriched hom object.
Changing the good open covering does not change the homotopy type of this space, since we can equivalently describe
{\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}}^{n-1}_{\nabla|p}(\pazocal{U})
as computing the mapping space
\mathrm{Map}(M,{\mathrm{B}}^{n-p}\,{\mathrm{B}}_{\nabla}^{p}{\mathrm{U}}(1))
in the local projective model structure on
{\operatorname{Fun}}({\mathscr{C}\mathrm{art}}^{\mathrm{op}},{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{et}_{\hskip-0.85355pt\Delta}})
.
Taking the colimit over all good open covers
\pazocal{U}
of
M
, one can promote
{\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}}^{n-1}_{\nabla|p}
to a simplicial homotopy sheaf of Kan complexes on
{\mathscr{M}\hskip-0.56917pt\mathrm{fd}}
(see
[
19
, Appendix B]
).
Its value on
M
is the Kan complex of
(n{-}1)
-gerbes with
p
-form connection on
M
.

For each
0\leqslant m\leqslant p\leqslant n\in\mathbbm{N}_{0}
there are canonical projection morphisms

{\mathrm{b}}^{n-p}\,{\mathrm{b}}_{\nabla}^{p}{\mathrm{U}}(1)\longrightarrow{\mathrm{b}}^{n-m}\,{\mathrm{b}}_{\nabla}^{m}{\mathrm{U}}(1)\ ,

(5.33)

which forget the forms of highest degrees.
Since the functors
{\mathrm{DK}}
and
\underline{{\operatorname{Fun}}({\mathscr{C}\mathrm{art}}^{\mathrm{op}},{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{et}_{\hskip-0.85355pt\Delta}})}({\check{C}}\,\pazocal{U},-)
are right Quillen functors,
5
5

5

The latter functor is right Quillen from the projective model structure on simplicial presheaves on
{\mathscr{C}\mathrm{art}}
, localised at Čech nerves of good open coverings, to the Kan–Quillen model structure on simplicial sets.

they induce Kan fibrations

{\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}}^{n-1}_{\nabla|p}(\pazocal{U})\longrightarrow{\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}}^{n-1}_{\nabla|m}(\pazocal{U})\ .

(5.34)

In the extreme case
p=n
, we also write

{\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}}^{n-1}_{\nabla}(\pazocal{U}):={\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}}^{n-1}_{\nabla|n}(\pazocal{U})

and refer to this as the Kan complex of
(n{-}1)
-gerbes on
M
with (full) connection relative to
\pazocal{U}
. At the opposite extreme
p=0
, we also write

{\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}}^{n-1}(\pazocal{U}):={\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}}^{n-1}_{\nabla|0}(\pazocal{U})

and refer to this as the Kan complex of
(n{-}1)
-gerbes on
M
relative to
\pazocal{U}
(without connection).

Definition 5.35

(
[
16
, Definition 10.22]
)

Let
\pazocal{G}
be an
(n{-}1)
-gerbe on
M
, presented by a morphism
g\colon{\check{C}}\,\pazocal{U}\longrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}^{n}{\mathrm{U}}(1)
.
For
0\leqslant p\leqslant n\in\mathbbm{N}_{0}
, the Kan complex of
p
-form connections on
\pazocal{G}
is the fibre

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Con}}_{p}(g)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}}^{n-1}_{\nabla|p}(\pazocal{U})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta^{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}}^{n-1}(\pazocal{U})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{g\}}

(5.36)

Since all vertices in the cospan underlying the pullback square (
5.36
) are Kan complexes and the right-hand vertical map is a Kan fibration, the square (
5.36
) is even homotopy cartesian in
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{et}_{\hskip-0.85355pt\Delta}}
.
This makes the homotopy type of
{\mathrm{Con}}_{p}(g)
independent of the choice of the cover
\pazocal{U}
and cocycle
g
.

We can also compute
{\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}}^{n-1}_{\nabla|p}(\pazocal{U})
as follows:
define the complexes

{\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}}^{n-1,{\operatorname{ch}}}_{\nabla|p}(\pazocal{U}):=\tau_{\leqslant 0}\circ{\mathrm{Tot}}^{\times}\circ N\big({\mathrm{b}}^{n-p}\,{\mathrm{b}}_{\nabla}^{p}{\mathrm{U}}(1)({\check{C}}\,\pazocal{U})\big)\ ,

(5.37)

where
N
takes the double complex associated to a cosimplicial complex and
{\mathrm{Tot}}^{\times}
is the total complex functor (based on products, rather than direct sums).
The canonical projections
{\mathrm{b}}^{n-p}\,{\mathrm{b}}_{\nabla}^{p}{\mathrm{U}}(1)\longrightarrow{\mathrm{b}}^{n-m}\,{\mathrm{b}}_{\nabla}^{m}{\mathrm{U}}(1)
then induce morphisms

\pi^{n,{\operatorname{ch}}}_{p,m}\colon{\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}}^{n-1,{\operatorname{ch}}}_{\nabla|p}(\pazocal{U})\longrightarrow{\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}}^{n-1,{\operatorname{ch}}}_{\nabla|m}(\pazocal{U})\ ,

(5.38)

for each
0\leqslant m\leqslant p\leqslant n\in\mathbbm{N}_{0}
.
There are canonical isomorphisms

{\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}}^{n-1}_{\nabla|p}(\pazocal{U})\cong{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{\mathbbm{Z}}^{\mathrm{dg}}\big(C_{*}(\Delta),{\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}}^{n-1,{\operatorname{ch}}}_{\nabla|p}(\pazocal{U})\big)\ ,

(5.39)

where we have used that the Dold–Kan functor
{\mathrm{DK}}
is canonically isomorphic to taking maps in
{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{\mathbbm{Z}}^{\mathrm{dg}}
out of the simplicial chains on the standard simplices
[
51
]
.

In this setting, we can describe the
m
-simplices of
{\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}}^{n-1}_{\nabla|p}(\pazocal{U})
explicitly as follows:
for each injective morphism

\varphi\colon[l]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[m]

in
{{\mathbbe{\Delta}}}
, they consist of a tuple

\pazocal{A}_{\varphi}=\big(g_{\varphi},A_{\varphi}^{(1)},\ldots,A_{\varphi}^{(\ell(\varphi))}\big)\ ,

(5.40)

where

\displaystyle g_{\varphi}

\displaystyle\colon{\check{C}}_{n-l}\,\pazocal{U}\longrightarrow{\mathrm{U}}(1)\ ,

(5.41)

\displaystyle A^{(k)}_{\varphi}

\displaystyle\in\Omega^{k}({\check{C}}_{n-(l+k)}\,\pazocal{U})\ ,\quad k=1,\ldots,\ell(\varphi):=\min(n-l,p)\ .

(5.42)

Here we set

\displaystyle g_{\varphi}

\displaystyle=1\ \quad

if

\displaystyle\quad 0

\displaystyle>n-l\ ,

(5.43)

\displaystyle A^{(k)}_{\varphi}

\displaystyle=0\ \quad

if

\displaystyle\quad k

\displaystyle>n-l\ .

(5.44)

These data further satisfy

\displaystyle\begin{split}\check{\delta}\,g_{\varphi}&=\prod_{r=0}^{l}\,g_{\varphi\circ\partial_{r}}^{(-1)^{r}}\ ,\\[4.0pt]
\mathrm{d}\log g_{\varphi}+(-1)^{l+1}\check{\delta}A^{(1)}_{\varphi}&=\sum_{r=0}^{l}\,(-1)^{r}\,A^{(1)}_{\varphi\circ\partial_{r}}\ ,\\[4.0pt]
\mathrm{d}A^{(k-1)}_{\varphi}+(-1)^{l+k}\,\check{\delta}A^{(k)}_{\varphi}&=\sum_{r=0}^{l}\,(-1)^{r}\,A^{(k)}_{\varphi\circ\partial_{r}}\ ,\quad k=2,\ldots,\ell(\varphi)\ ,\\[4.0pt]
\mathrm{d}A^{(\ell(\varphi))}_{\varphi}&=\sum\limits_{r=0}^{l}\,(-1)^{r}\,A^{(m)}_{\varphi\circ\partial_{r}}\ ,\quad l\geqslant 1\ ,\ p>n-l\ .\end{split}

(5.45)

In the cases where
p\leqslant n-l
there is no condition on
\mathrm{d}A^{(\ell(\varphi))}_{\varphi}
.

We describe the simplicial structure using the generating morphisms of
{{\mathbbe{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{op}}
:
let
\pazocal{A}
denote an
m
-simplex in
{\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}}^{n-1}_{\nabla|p}(\pazocal{U})
.
Then
d_{i}\pazocal{A}
is the
(m{-}1)
-simplex which associates to each injective morphism
\varphi^{-}\colon[l]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[m-1]
the data

(d_{i}\pazocal{A})_{\varphi^{-}}=\pazocal{A}_{\partial^{i}\circ\varphi^{-}}\ .

(5.46a)

The
(m{+}1)
-simplex
s_{i}\pazocal{A}
associates to an injective morphism
\varphi^{+}\colon[q]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[m+1]
the data

(s_{i}\pazocal{A})_{\varphi^{+}}=\begin{cases}\ \pazocal{A}_{\sigma^{i}\circ\varphi^{+}}\ ,&\text{if}\ \sigma^{i}\circ\varphi^{+}\ \text{is injective}\ ,\\[4.0pt]
\ 0\ ,&\text{otherwise}\ .\end{cases}

(5.46b)

Consequently, the
m
-simplices of
{\mathrm{Con}}_{p}(g)
are in bijection with those morphisms of chain complexes

\pazocal{A}\colon C_{*}(\Delta^{m})\longrightarrow{\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}}^{n-1,{\operatorname{ch}}}_{\nabla|p}(\pazocal{U})

(5.47)

such that the composition

\pi^{n,{\operatorname{ch}}}_{p,0}\circ\pazocal{A}\colon C_{*}(\Delta^{m})\longrightarrow{\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}}^{n-1,{\operatorname{ch}}}(\pazocal{U})

(5.48)

satisfies the conditions:

•

\pi^{n,{\operatorname{ch}}}_{p,0}\circ\pazocal{A}
maps each degree zero generator of the graded
\mathbbm{Z}
-module underlying
C_{*}(\Delta^{m})
to the element
g\in{\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}}^{n-1,{\operatorname{ch}}}(\pazocal{U})_{0}
, and

•

\pi^{n,{\operatorname{ch}}}_{p,0}\circ\pazocal{A}
maps each generator in degree
-l
of the graded
\mathbbm{Z}
-module underlying
C_{*}(\Delta^{m})
to the element
1\in{\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}}^{n-1,{\operatorname{ch}}}(\pazocal{U})_{-l}
.

In other words, an
m
-simplex of
{\mathrm{Con}}_{p}(g)
is an assignment of a tuple
\pazocal{A}_{\varphi}=\big(g_{\varphi},A^{(1)}_{\varphi},\ldots,A^{(p)}_{\varphi}\big)
to each injective morphism
\varphi\colon[l]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[m]
exactly as in (
5.40
), but with the additional requirement

g_{\varphi}=\begin{cases}\ g\ ,&l=0\ ,\\[4.0pt]
\ 1\ ,&l\geqslant 1\ .\end{cases}

(5.49)

Remark 5.50

We also extend the definition of
{\mathrm{Con}}_{p}(g)
to
p>n
:
in this case, the data of
m
-simplices is as for
p\leqslant n
, but with the additional condition

\mathrm{d}A^{(n)}_{\varphi}=0

(5.51)

whenever
\varphi\colon[0]\longrightarrow[m]
presents a vertex of
\Delta^{m}
.
In particular, it follows that

{\mathrm{Con}}_{p}(g)={\mathrm{Con}}_{q}(g)\qquad\forall\,p,q>n\ .

(5.52)

It thus makes sense to define

{\mathrm{Con}}_{\flat}(g)\coloneqq{\mathrm{Con}}_{p}(g)\ ,

(5.53)

the space of
flat connections
on the
(n{-}1)
-gerbe
\pazocal{G}
, where
p
is any integer with
p>n
.

\triangleleft

Remark 5.54

This description of
m
-simplices in
{\mathrm{Con}}_{p}(g)
based on the functors
{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{\mathbbm{Z}}^{\mathrm{dg}}(C_{*}(\Delta),-)
is a much larger model than that produced by the smaller model of the functor
{\mathrm{DK}}
(see for instance
[
43
, Section II.4]
).
However, we emphasise again that the two models produce isomorphic simplicial sets:
much of the information in the families
\{\pazocal{A}_{\varphi}\,|\,\varphi\colon[l]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[m]\}
is redundant.
To exemplify this, observe that in the larger model a 1-simplex is specified by data assigned to each of its vertices, and its edge.
In the smaller model determined by
{\mathrm{DK}}
, we instead specify data for only one vertex and the edge.
Let us denote these schematically by
c_{0}
and
c_{01}
, respectively.
The data
c_{1}
assigned to the second vertex is then already determined to be
c_{1}=c_{0}\pm\mathrm{d}c_{01}
.

\triangleleft

The main result of this section is Theorem
5.55
below, which states that the entire space of
p
-form connections on any higher gerbe produced by our derived geometric formalism is weakly homotopy equivalent the space of
p
-form connections produced by the Čech–Deligne description of higher gerbes with connections.
As the Čech–Deligne formalism makes no reference to derived techniques or deformation theory, this relates two genuinely independent definitions of connections on
(n{-}1)
-gerbes.

Theorem 5.55

Suppose that
M
is a smooth manifold,
\pazocal{U}=\{U_{a}\}_{a\in\varLambda}
is a good open cover of
M
, and
g\colon{\check{C}}\,\pazocal{U}\longrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}^{n}{\mathrm{U}}(1)
is a Čech
n
-cocycle.
Let
x\colon M\longrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}^{n}{\mathrm{U}}(1)
be the object in
\mathrm{St}^{\mathrm{qcdt}}_{M/}
presented by
g
.
Then for each
p\in\mathbbm{N}_{0}
there is a canonical equivalence

{\mathrm{Con}}_{p}(g)\simeq{\mathrm{Con}}_{p}\big({\check{C}\mathrm{At}}(g)\big)

(5.56)

between the space of
p
-form connections on
g
defined via Čech–Deligne cocycles and the space of
p
-form connections on
x
in the derived geometric sense of Definition
4.21
.

Recall that by Corollary
5.13
and Theorem
5.27
, the
L_{\infty}
-algebroid
{\check{C}\mathrm{At}}(g)
on the right-hand side is indeed a model for the relative tangent complex
T\big(M/{\mathrm{B}}^{n}{\mathrm{U}}(1)\big)
.
The proof of Theorem
5.55
is computationally involved and is presented in two appendices:
in Appendix
D
we first develop techniques for computing mapping spaces of
L_{\infty}
-algebroids.
In particular, we construct computationally convenient fibrant simplicial resolutions of a class of
L_{\infty}
-algebroids which are close to being abelian (they arise from Lie actions of
TM
on chain complexes).
We believe that generalisations of these constructions to more general
L_{\infty}
-algebroids should exist.
The construction of the weak homotopy equivalence in Theorem
5.55
is contained in Appendix
E
.

Example 5.57

We compute the curvature of an
n
-form connection on an
(n{-}1)
-gerbe by combining Equation (
4.51
) and Diagram (
E.6
).
Concretely, consider Diagram (
E.6
) with
r=0
and in the left-hand column add the order
(n{+}1)
term.
By computations analogous to those in Appendix
E
, we obtain the curvature
F^{n}(\pazocal{A})
of an
n
-form connection

\pazocal{A}=(A^{(1)},\ldots,A^{(n)})

on an
(n{-}1)
-gerbe.
Only terms involving an entry of degree zero contribute to the brackets in (
4.51
), and all higher-order brackets are zero since the target
L_{\infty}
-algebroid is a dg Lie algebroid.

With a hat denoting a dropped argument, we obtain

\displaystyle F^{n}(\pazocal{A})(X_{0},\dots,X_{n})

\displaystyle=\sum_{0\leqslant i<j\leqslant n}\,(-1)^{i+j}\,A^{(n)}\big([X_{i},X_{j}],X_{0},\ldots,\widehat{X_{i}},\ldots,\widehat{X_{j}},\ldots,X_{n}\big)

(5.58)

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{i=0}^{n}\,(-1)^{i}\,\big[\big(X_{i},A^{(1)}(X_{i})\big)\,,\,A^{(n)}(X_{0},\ldots,\widehat{X_{i}},\ldots,X_{n})\big]

(5.59)

\displaystyle=\sum_{0\leqslant i<j\leqslant n}\,(-1)^{i+j}\,A^{(n)}\big([X_{i},X_{j}],X_{0},\ldots,\widehat{X_{i}},\ldots,\widehat{X_{j}},\ldots,X_{n})

(5.60)

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{i=0}^{n}\,(-1)^{i}\,\pounds_{X_{i}}\big(A^{(n)}(X_{0},\ldots,\widehat{X_{i}},\ldots,X_{n})\big)

(5.61)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}A^{(n)}(X_{0},\ldots,X_{n})=H(X_{0},\dots,X_{n})\ .

(5.62)

This is also in agreement with the notion of curvature from the Čech–Deligne theory of differential cocycles.
We again emphasise that the notion of curvature of an
n
-form connection we introduced in Section
4.2
is defined in an entirely independent way of this classical curvature, and instead recovers it from fundamental principles.

\triangleleft

6
Higher Courant algebroids and symmetries of higher
{\mathrm{U}}(1)
-bundles

In this final section we discuss the relation between higher
{\mathrm{U}}(1)
-bundles, Atiyah
L_{\infty}
-algebras and higher Courant algebroids.
A higher Courant algebroid has a canonical extension to an
L_{\infty}
-algebra which we argue can be thought of as encoding the infinitesimal symmetries of a
{\mathrm{U}}(1)
-
n
-bundle with
(n{-}1)
-form connection, i.e. symmetries which preserve all of the connection data except the highest form degree part (Conjecture
6.87
); we prove this rigorously for
n=2
in Example
6.99
. We compare these
L_{\infty}
-algebroids to the Atiyah
L_{\infty}
-algebras which encode the infinitesimal symmetries preserving only the underlying
(n{-}1)
-gerbe (Theorem
6.54
and Conjecture
6.90
). The relevant objects do not have deformation theory, and a detailed study of their global moduli problem naturally leads to a weakened notion of
p
-form connections in terms of certain splittings of maps of
L_{\infty}
-algebras. In particular, we show that a weak
1
-form connection on a
{\mathrm{U}}(1)
-bundle with connection is simply the original connection with the restriction that it be flat (Example
6.93
), while a weak
1
-form connection on a
1
-gerbe with
1
-form connection is the same as an enhanced curving in the terminology of
[
86
]
(Example
6.99
).

6.1
Higher Courant brackets and their
L_{\infty}
-structures

We consider higher (and lower) analogues of (split) exact Courant algebroids
[
48
,
34
,
95
,
14
]
. Let
M
be a smooth manifold and
n\in\mathbbm{N}
. Consider the vector bundle

\displaystyle\pazocal{E}_{n-1}\coloneqq TM\oplus\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{n-1}\,T^{*}M

(6.1)

endowed with the symmetric fibrewise pairing

\displaystyle\left\langle\,\cdot\ ,\,\cdot\,\right\rangle:\pazocal{E}_{n-1}\times\pazocal{E}_{n-1}\longrightarrow\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{n-2}\,T^{*}M

(6.2)

given by a symmetrization of contractions between vectors and
(n{-}1)
-forms as

\displaystyle\left\langle(X,\alpha)\ ,\,(Y,\beta)\right\rangle\coloneqq\iota_{X}\beta+\iota_{Y}\alpha\ .

(6.3)

We further endow the
C^{\infty}(M)
-module of sections of
\pazocal{E}_{n-1}
with the
Dorfman bracket

\displaystyle[\![(X,\alpha)\ ,\,(Y,\beta)]\!]\coloneqq\big([X,Y]\ ,\,\pounds_{X}\beta-\iota_{Y}\,\mathrm{d}\alpha\big)\ .

(6.4)

With a suitable smooth bracket-preserving bundle map
\rho:\pazocal{E}_{n-1}\longrightarrow TM
, the Dorfman bracket obeys Jacobi and Leibniz identities

\displaystyle[\![e_{1},[\![e_{2},e_{3}]\!]]\!]

\displaystyle=[\![[\![e_{1},e_{2}]\!],e_{3}]\!]+[\![e_{2},[\![e_{1},e_{3}]\!]]\!]\ ,

(6.5)

\displaystyle[\![e_{1},f\,e_{2}]\!]

\displaystyle=f\,[\![e_{1},e_{2}]\!]+\big(\pounds_{\rho(e_{1})}\,f\big)\,e_{2}\ ,

(6.6)

\displaystyle[\![f\,e_{1},e_{2}]\!]

\displaystyle=f\,[\![e_{1},e_{2}]\!]-\big(\pounds_{\rho(e_{2})}\,f\big)\,e_{1}+\mathrm{d}f\wedge\left\langle e_{1},e_{2}\right\rangle\ ,

(6.7)

as well as a compatibility condition between the pairing and the bracket

\displaystyle\pounds_{\rho(e_{3})}\left\langle e_{1},e_{2}\right\rangle=\left\langle[\![e_{3},e_{1}]\!]+\tfrac{1}{2}\,\mathrm{d}\left\langle e_{3},e_{1}\right\rangle,e_{2}\right\rangle+\left\langle e_{1},[\![e_{3},e_{2}]\!]+\tfrac{1}{2}\,\mathrm{d}\left\langle e_{3},e_{2}\right\rangle\right\rangle\ ,

(6.8)

for all
e_{1},e_{2},e_{3}\in\Gamma(M,\pazocal{E}_{n-1})=TM\oplus\Omega^{n-1}(M)
and
f\in C^{\infty}(M)
.

When the anchor map
\rho
is the projection to
TM
, we call the vector bundle
\pazocal{E}_{n-1}
endowed with these structure maps the
standard higher Courant algebroid
on
M
. In this case it is also possible to twist the Dorfman bracket by a closed
(n{+}1)
-form
H\in\Omega_{\rm cl}^{n+1}(M)
to the
H
-twisted Dorman bracket

\displaystyle[\![(X,\alpha)\ ,\,(Y,\beta)]\!]_{H}\coloneqq[\![(X,\alpha)\ ,\,(Y,\beta)]\!]+(0,\iota_{X}\,\iota_{Y}H)\ .

(6.9)

The decomposition of the Dorfman bracket into its respective antisymmetric and symmetric parts

\displaystyle[\![e_{1},e_{2}]\!]=[\![e_{1},e_{2}]\!]_{\rm C}+\big(0\,,\,\tfrac{1}{2}\,\mathrm{d}\left\langle e_{1},e_{2}\right\rangle\big)

(6.10)

defines the
Courant bracket

\displaystyle[\![(X,\alpha)\ ,\,(Y,\beta)]\!]_{\rm C}\coloneqq\big([X,Y]\ ,\,\pounds_{X}\beta-\pounds_{Y}\alpha-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\mathrm{d}(\iota_{X}\beta-\iota_{Y}\alpha)\big)\ .

(6.11)

Unlike the Dorfman bracket, the Courant bracket is antisymmetric but it violates the Jacobi identity by a coboundary-like term. In other words, neither the Dorfman nor Courant brackets define Lie structures on
\pazocal{E}_{n-1}
. Below we will discuss how higher Courant algebroids can instead be viewed as
L_{\infty}
-algebras.

The geometric subgroup preserving the Dorfman bracket, the pairing and the anchor is the semi-direct product

\displaystyle{\mathsf{Sym}}(\pazocal{E}_{n-1}):=\mathsf{Diff}(M)\ltimes\Omega^{n}_{\rm cl}(M)

(6.12)

of diffeomorphisms of
M
with
gauge transformations

\mathrm{e}^{\,B}:\Gamma(M,\pazocal{E}_{n-1})\longrightarrow\Gamma(M,\pazocal{E}_{n-1})
parametrised by closed
n
-forms
B\in\Omega_{\rm cl}^{n}(M)
acting as

\displaystyle\mathrm{e}^{\,B}(X,\alpha)=(X,\alpha+\iota_{X}B)\ .

(6.13)

Example 6.14

Higher Courant algebroids appear in a number of supergravity contexts:

(1)

The standard case
n=2
is the
generalised tangent bundle

\displaystyle\pazocal{E}_{1}=TM\oplus T^{*}M\ .

(6.15)

It represents an isotropic splitting of the exact sequence of vector bundles

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow T^{*}M\xrightarrow{\ \rho^{*}\ }\pazocal{E}_{1}\xrightarrow{\ \rho\ }TM\longrightarrow 0

(6.16)

and gives rise to an exact Courant algebroid with Ševera class
[H]\in\mathrm{H}^{3}_{\mathrm{dR}}(M)
represented by the closed
3
-form
H

[
78
]
, called the NSNS flux in string theory. Gauge transformations are called
B
-field transformations by the Kalb–Ramond 2-form
B
. Type II supergravity can be entirely formulated in terms of the generalised geometry of exact Courant algebroids
[
31
]
.

(2)

The case
n=3
is the
exceptional tangent bundle

\displaystyle\pazocal{E}_{2}=TM\oplus\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{2}\,T^{*}M

(6.17)

of four-dimensional compactifications of M-theory
[
49
,
27
]
. It
represents an isotropic splitting of the exact sequence of vector bundles

\displaystyle T^{*}M\otimes T^{*}M\longrightarrow\pazocal{E}_{2}\xrightarrow{\ \rho\ }TM\longrightarrow 0\ ,

(6.18)

giving the extension of string charges to membrane charges, with closed 4-form
G
-flux
H
and
C
-field gauge transformations by the 3-form
B
.

(3)

For
n\geqslant 3
,
\pazocal{E}_{n-1}
is the higher (twisted) Courant algebroid of
\mathrm{GL}^{+}(n{+}2,\mathbbm{R})
generalised geometry that appears in
(n{+}1)
-dimensional compactifications of type IIB supergravity
[
49
]
.

\triangleleft

Remark 6.19

Higher Courant algebroids arise from higher
{\mathrm{U}}(1)
-bundles in the following way. Let
(g,A^{(1)},\dots,A^{{(n-1)}})
be a Čech–Deligne differential cocycle of an
(n{-}1)
-gerbe
x:M\longrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}^{n}{\mathrm{U}}(1)
with
(n{-}1)
-form connection on a smooth manifold
M
with a good open covering
\pazocal{U}=\{U_{a}\}_{a\in\varLambda}
, for
n\geqslant 2
(see Section
5.2
). Over each open set
U_{a}
we put the standard higher Courant algebroid
TU_{a}\oplus\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{n-1}\,T^{*}U_{a}
and glue them together on overlaps
U_{a}\cap U_{b}
, for all
a,b\in\varLambda
, using the automorphism

(X,\alpha)\longmapsto\big(X,\alpha+\iota_{X}\,\mathrm{d}A_{ab}^{{(n-1)}}\big)\ ,

which is a gauge transformation because
\mathrm{d}A^{{(n-1)}}\in\Omega_{\rm cl}^{n}(\check{C}_{1}\,\pazocal{U})
.
Applying the de Rham differential
\mathrm{d}
to the relation

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}A^{(n-2)}-(-1)^{n}\,\check{\delta}A^{(n-1)}=0

(6.20)

implies the cocycle condition

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\,\check{\delta}A^{(n-1)}=0

(6.21)

in
\Omega_{\rm cl}^{n}(\check{C}_{2}\,\pazocal{U})
, which in turn gives rise to an extension

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{n-1}\,T^{*}M\longrightarrow\pazocal{E}_{n-1}\xrightarrow{\ \rho\ }TM\longrightarrow 0

(6.22)

of
TM
in the category of vector bundles.

Now enlarge the data
(g,A^{(1)},\dots,A^{{(n-1)}})
by
A^{{(n)}}\in\Omega^{n}(\check{C}_{0}\,\pazocal{U})
to get an
n
-form connection whose curvature
H
represents the class
[g]\in\mathrm{H}^{n+1}(M;\mathbbm{Z})
classifying the
{\mathrm{U}}(1)
-
n
-bundle
x:M\longrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}^{n}{\mathrm{U}}(1)
. Then from the equation

\check{\delta}A^{(n)}=-(-1)^{n}\,\mathrm{d}A^{(n-1)}

we get a Lagrangian splitting

TM\longrightarrow\pazocal{E}_{n-1}\ ,\qquad X\longmapsto\big(X,\iota_{X}A^{(n)}\big)

of the exact sequence (
6.22
), making the vector bundle
\pazocal{E}_{n-1}\cong TM\oplus\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{n-1}\,T^{*}M
into a standard higher Courant algebroid with the
H
-twisted Dorfman bracket.

In the case
n=2
, i.e. the twisted generalised tangent bundle from Example
6.14
(1)
, this is the well-known Hitchin construction of an exact Courant algebroid from a gerbe with connective structure in the case that the Ševera class
[H]
is integral
[
48
]
.

\triangleleft

We proceed to discuss how to canonically associate to any higher Courant algebroid
\pazocal{E}_{n-1}
an
n
-term
L_{\infty}
-algebra, or equivalently a Lie
n
-algebra, extending the
H
-twisted Courant bracket. This
L_{\infty}
-structure was worked out by Zambon in
[
95
, Propositions 8.1 and 8.4]
, generalising the
n=2
result of Roytenberg and Weinstein
[
75
,
74
,
42
]
who show that the sections of an
H
-twisted Courant algebroid form a 2-term
L_{\infty}
-algebra. To this end, recall that the
Bernouilli numbers

B_{k}\in\mathbbm{Q}
for
k\geqslant 0
are defined by the coefficients in the power series expansion

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\,B_{k}\,\frac{t^{k}}{k!}:=\frac{t}{\mathrm{e}^{\,t}-1}=1-\frac{t}{2}+\frac{t^{2}}{6\cdot 2!}-\frac{t^{4}}{30\cdot 4!}+\frac{t^{6}}{42\cdot 6!}-\frac{t^{8}}{30\cdot 8!}+\cdots\ .

(6.23)

We summarise Zambon’s construction as

Proposition 6.24

Let
(\pazocal{E}_{n-1},\left\langle\,\cdot\ ,\,\cdot\,\right\rangle,[\![\,\cdot\ ,\,\cdot\,]\!]_{H})
be an
H
-twisted higher Courant algebroid on a smooth manifold
M
for
n\geqslant 2
. There is an
n
-term
L_{\infty}
-algebra
{\rm Cour}_{n-1}(H)
over
TM
with underlying complex

\displaystyle C^{\infty}(M)[n-1]\xrightarrow{\ \mathrm{d}\ }\Omega^{1}(M)[n-2]\xrightarrow{\ \mathrm{d}\ }\cdots\xrightarrow{\ \mathrm{d}\ }\Omega^{n-2}(M)[1]\xrightarrow{\ \mathrm{d}\ }\Gamma(M,\pazocal{E}_{n-1})=TM\oplus\Omega^{n-1}(M)\ ,

(6.25)

where
\mathrm{d}
is the de Rham differential. For
v_{i}=(e_{i},\xi_{i})
with
e_{i}=(X_{i},\alpha_{i})\in\Gamma(M,\pazocal{E}_{n-1})
and
\xi_{i}\in\Omega^{\leqslant n-2}(M):=\bigoplus_{r=0}^{n-2}\,\Omega^{r}(M)
, the non-zero higher brackets are given as follows:

•

Binary brackets:

\displaystyle[e_{1},e_{2}]=[\![e_{1},e_{2}]\!]_{{\rm C},H}\quad,\quad[e_{1},\xi_{2}]=\tfrac{1}{2}\,\pounds_{X_{1}}\,\xi_{2}\ .

(6.26)

•

Ternary brackets:

\displaystyle[e_{1},e_{2},e_{3}]

\displaystyle=-T_{H}(e_{1},e_{2},e_{3}):=-\tfrac{1}{3!}\,\big(\left\langle[\![e_{1},e_{2}]\!]_{{\rm C},H}\ ,\,e_{3}\right\rangle+\left\langle[\![e_{3},e_{1}]\!]_{{\rm C},H}\ ,\,e_{2}\right\rangle+\left\langle[\![e_{2},e_{3}]\!]_{{\rm C},H}\ ,\,e_{1}\right\rangle\big)\ ,

(6.27)

\displaystyle[\xi_{1},e_{2},e_{3}]

\displaystyle=-\tfrac{1}{3!}\,\big(\tfrac{1}{2}\,(\iota_{X_{2}}\,\pounds_{X_{3}}\,\xi_{1}-\iota_{X_{3}}\,\pounds_{X_{2}}\,\xi_{1})+\iota_{[X_{2},X_{3}]}\,\xi_{1}\big)\ .

(6.28)

•

k
-ary brackets for all odd
5\leqslant k\leqslant n+1
:

\displaystyle[v_{1},\dots,v_{k}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{k}\,(-1)^{i}\,\mu_{k}\big((\xi_{i},\alpha_{i}),X_{1},\dots,\widehat{X_{i}},\dots,X_{k}\big)+(-1)^{\frac{k+1}{2}}\,k\,B_{k-1}\ \iota_{X_{k}}\cdots\iota_{X_{1}}\,H\ ,

(6.29)

where

\displaystyle\mu_{k}\big((\xi_{1},\alpha_{1}),X_{2},\dots,X_{k}\big)

(6.30)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt=(-1)^{\frac{k+1}{2}}\,\frac{12\,B_{k-1}}{(k-1)\,(k-2)}

(6.31)

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt\,\times\sum_{2\leqslant i<j\leqslant k}\,(-1)^{i+j+1}\ \iota_{X_{k}}\cdots\widehat{\iota_{X_{j}}}\cdots\widehat{\iota_{X_{i}}}\cdots\iota_{X_{1}}\big([\xi_{1},X_{i},X_{j}]-T_{0}(\alpha_{1},X_{i},X_{j})\big)\ ,

(6.32)

and the hat indicates omission of the corresponding term.

Gauge transformations
\mathrm{e}^{\,B}:\Gamma(M,\pazocal{E}_{n-1})\longrightarrow\Gamma(M,\pazocal{E}_{n-1})
induce strict
L_{\infty}
-isomorphisms
{\mathrm{id}}+\iota_{\rho(-)}B:{\rm Cour}_{n-1}(H)\longrightarrow{\rm Cour}_{n-1}(H)

[
95
, Proposition 8.5]
.

6.2
Higher derived brackets

For later use below, let us take a brief detour to review a general construction of
L_{\infty}
-algebras due to Fiorenza–Manetti
[
36
]
and Getzler
[
42
]
, which plays a central role in the construction of Zambon. Consider a pair consisting of a dgla and a sub-dgla

\mathfrak{h}\subseteq{\mathfrak{g}}\ .

In this situation, the homotopy fibre of the inclusion
\mathfrak{h}\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,{\mathfrak{g}}
of the underlying complexes is quasi-isomorphic to
{\mathfrak{g}}/\mathfrak{h}[-1]
. Our goal is to endow the complex
{\mathfrak{g}}/\mathfrak{h}[-1]
with an
L_{\infty}
-structure that models the homotopy fibre in the
\infty
-category of
L_{\infty}
-algebras
L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}}^{\infty}_{\mathbbm{R}}
.

To this end, let us fix a compatible decomposition of graded vector spaces

{\mathfrak{g}}\cong\mathfrak{h}\,\oplus\,{\mathfrak{g}}/\mathfrak{h}\ .

The differential on
{\mathfrak{g}}
then assumes the form

\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{h}}&\mathsf{D}\\
0&\mathrm{d}_{{\mathfrak{g}}/\mathfrak{h}}\end{pmatrix}

for some intertwining map
\mathsf{D}\colon{\mathfrak{g}}/\mathfrak{h}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{h}
satisfying
\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}\circ\mathsf{D}=-\mathsf{D}\circ\mathrm{d}_{{\mathfrak{g}}/\mathfrak{h}}
. Finally, let us write

\pi_{\mathfrak{h}}\colon{\mathfrak{g}}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{h}\qquad,\qquad\pi_{{\mathfrak{g}}/\mathfrak{h}}\colon{\mathfrak{g}}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{g}}/\mathfrak{h}

for the induced projections; here
\pi_{{\mathfrak{g}}/\mathfrak{h}}
preserves the differential and does not depend on the choice of splitting.

Let us now recall that in the model category of dglas, a path object for a dgla
{\mathfrak{g}}
is given by the dgla
{\mathfrak{g}}[t,\mathrm{d}t]
of polynomial differential forms on the
1
-simplex with values in
{\mathfrak{g}}
. Using this, we obtain the rather big model for the homotopy fibre of
\mathfrak{h}\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,{\mathfrak{g}}
in dglas given by

{\mathrm{hofib}}_{\mathrm{PL}}(\mathfrak{h}\subseteq{\mathfrak{g}})=\big\{x(t)+y(t)\,\mathrm{d}t\in{\mathfrak{g}}[t,\mathrm{d}t]\ \big|\ x(0)=0\ ,\,x(1)\in\mathfrak{h}\big\}\ .

Here the subscript
PL
indicates that we are using polynomial differential forms.

This model for the homotopy fibre comes equipped with a natural deformation retract

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{g}}/\mathfrak{h}[-1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{hofib}}_{\mathrm{PL}}(\mathfrak{h}\subseteq{\mathfrak{g}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}

where

\displaystyle i(x)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{D}(x)\,t+x\,\mathrm{d}t\ ,

\displaystyle r\big(x(t)+y(t)\,\mathrm{d}t\big)

\displaystyle=\pi_{{\mathfrak{g}}/\mathfrak{h}}\Big(\int_{0}^{1}\,y(s)\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)\ ,

\displaystyle h\big(x(t)+y(t)\,\mathrm{d}t\big)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\,y(s)\,\mathrm{d}s-t\,\pi_{{\mathfrak{g}}/\mathfrak{h}}\Big(\int_{0}^{1}\,y(s)\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)\ .

Note that the inclusion
i
is well defined because
\mathsf{D}(x)\,t
vanishes at
t=0
and is contained in
\mathfrak{h}
when
t=1
. Likewise, the contracting homotopy
h
is well-defined because
h\big(x(t)+y(t)\,\mathrm{d}t\big)
vanishes at
t=0
and its value at
t=1
is

\int_{0}^{1}\,y(s)\,\mathrm{d}s-\pi_{{\mathfrak{g}}/\mathfrak{h}}\Big(\int_{0}^{1}\,y(s)\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)=\pi_{\mathfrak{h}}\Big(\int_{0}^{1}\,y(s)\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)\ \in\ \mathfrak{h}\ .

Using
\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}\big(\int_{0}^{t}\,y(s)\,\mathrm{d}s\big)=-\int_{0}^{t}\,\big(\mathrm{d}_{{\mathfrak{g}}}y(s)\big)\,\mathrm{d}s
by the Koszul sign rule, one readily verifies
r\,i={\mathrm{id}}
,
{\mathrm{id}}-i\,r=\mathrm{d}\,h+h\,\mathrm{d}
and
h^{2}=0
.

We can thus apply the Homotopy Transfer Theorem to obtain an
L_{\infty}
-structure on
{\mathfrak{g}}/\mathfrak{h}[-1]
and an enhancement of the maps
i
and
r
to
\infty
-quasi-isomorphisms

i_{\infty}\colon{\mathfrak{g}}/\mathfrak{h}[-1]\longrightarrow{\mathrm{hofib}}_{\mathrm{PL}}(\mathfrak{h}\subseteq{\mathfrak{g}})\qquad\text{and}\qquad r_{\infty}\colon{\mathrm{hofib}}_{\mathrm{PL}}(\mathfrak{h}\subseteq{\mathfrak{g}})\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{g}}/\mathfrak{h}[-1]\ .

The brackets on
{\mathfrak{g}}/\mathfrak{h}[-1]
are defined combinatorially by summing over rooted trees
[
63
]
, where

•

each
k
-ary vertex is labelled by the
k
-ary bracket of
{\mathrm{hofib}}_{\mathrm{PL}}(\mathfrak{h}\subseteq{\mathfrak{g}})
(for
k\geqslant 2
),

•

each leaf is labelled by the inclusion
i
,

•

each internal edge is labelled by the homotopy
h
, and

•

the root is labelled by the restriction
r
.

The resulting compositions then define an operation on
{\mathfrak{g}}/\mathfrak{h}[-1]
.

As discussed in
[
36
, Section 5]
, this greatly simplifies in the present situation because
{\mathrm{hofib}}_{\mathrm{PL}}(\mathfrak{h}\subseteq{\mathfrak{g}})
has only a binary bracket and

[\mathrm{im}(h),\mathrm{im}(h)]\subseteq\ker(h)\cap\ker(r)\ ,

since
\mathrm{im}(h)
consists of
0
-forms while
h
and
r
vanish on
0
-forms. Up to isomorphism the only rooted tree that remains is therefore the “left comb” (with automorphism group the degree
2
symmetric group
\Sigma_{2}
), so that the
k
-ary bracket on
{\mathfrak{g}}/\mathfrak{h}[-1]
is given by

\displaystyle[x_{1},\dots,x_{k}]_{{\mathfrak{g}}/\mathfrak{h}[-1]}=\frac{1}{2}\,\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{k}}\,\pm\,r\big[h\big[h\big[\cdots h\big[i(x_{\sigma(1)}),i(x_{\sigma(2)})\big],i(x_{\sigma(3)})\big],\dots\big],i(x_{\sigma(k)})\big]\ .

(6.33)

Here the brackets on the right-hand side are the Lie brackets in
{\mathrm{hofib}}_{\mathrm{PL}}(\mathfrak{h}\subseteq{\mathfrak{g}})
, and
\pm
is the Koszul sign of the degree
k
permutation
\sigma
acting on
x_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes x_{k}\in({\mathfrak{g}}/\mathfrak{h})^{\otimes k}
(without the shift!). In other words, if the degrees of the elements in
{\mathfrak{g}}/\mathfrak{h}[-1]
are shifted by one, then the bracket becomes graded-symmetric, as it should be.

Recall that
i(x)=\mathsf{D}(x)\,t+x\,\mathrm{d}t
and that
\mathrm{im}(h)
consists of
0
-forms in
t
. Since
h
and
r
vanish on
0
-forms, the formula (
6.33
) simplifies to

[x_{1},\dots,x_{k}]_{{\mathfrak{g}}/\mathfrak{h}[-1]}=\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{k}}\,\pm\,r\big[h\big[h\big[\cdots h\big[\mathsf{D}(x_{\sigma(1)})\,t,x_{\sigma(2)}\,\mathrm{d}t\big],x_{\sigma(3)}\,\mathrm{d}t\big],\dots\big],x_{\sigma(n)}\,\mathrm{d}t\big]\ .

One can make this expression more explicit in terms of the Lie bracket
[-,-]_{\mathfrak{g}}
on
{\mathfrak{g}}
. To this end, let us introduce the notation

\{x_{1},\dots,x_{k}\}=\pi_{{\mathfrak{g}}/\mathfrak{h}}\big[\big[\cdots\big[[x_{1},x_{2}]_{\mathfrak{g}},x_{3}\big]_{\mathfrak{g}},\dots\big]_{\mathfrak{g}},x_{k}\big]_{\mathfrak{g}}

for elements
x_{1},\dots,x_{k}\in{\mathfrak{g}}
.

Proposition 6.34

Let
\mathfrak{h}\subseteq{\mathfrak{g}}
be the inclusion of a sub-dgla, together with a splitting of graded vector spaces
{\mathfrak{g}}\cong\mathfrak{h}\oplus{\mathfrak{g}}/\mathfrak{h}
as above. Then the transferred
L_{\infty}
-structure on
{\mathfrak{g}}/\mathfrak{h}[-1]
is given by

\displaystyle[x_{1},\dots,x_{k}]_{{\mathfrak{g}}/\mathfrak{h}[-1]}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{k}}\,\pm\ \sum_{i=1}^{k-1}\ \sum_{1<k_{1}<k_{2}<\dots<k_{i}=k}\,\frac{(-1)^{i-1}}{k_{1}!\,(k_{2}-k_{1}+1)!\cdots(k_{i}-k_{i-1}+1)!}

\displaystyle\qquad\,\times\big\{\big\{\cdots\big\{\{\mathsf{D}(x_{\sigma(1)}),x_{\sigma(2)},\dots,x_{\sigma(k_{1})}\},x_{\sigma(k_{1}+1)},\dots,x_{\sigma(k_{2})}\big\},\dots,x_{\sigma(k_{i})}\big\}\ ,

where
\pm
is the Koszul sign of the permutation
\sigma
acting on
x_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes x_{k}\in({\mathfrak{g}}/\mathfrak{h})^{\otimes k}
.

Proof.

This is a standard result, see e.g.
[
90
]
. The homotopy
h
consists of two terms:

(a)

A term that takes the primitive.

(b)

A term that integrates over
[0,1]
and then applies
-t\,\pi_{{\mathfrak{g}}/\mathfrak{h}}
.

We therefore obtain a sum of
2^{k-1}
terms, corresponding to the sum over
1<k_{1}<\dots<k_{i-1}<k
where we take part (b) of the homotopy
h
. Each of these times we apply
-\pi_{{\mathfrak{g}}/\mathfrak{h}}
, yielding the iteration of brackets and the overall sign
(-1)^{i-1}
. On the other hand, each time we apply (a) in between, we pass from a coefficient
t^{l}/l!
to coefficient
t^{l+1}/(l+1)!
, and this gets reset to a linear term in
t
when we apply (b). This leads to the formula for the denominator.
∎

Let us mention two particular cases of relevance to us in which the formula of Proposition
6.34
simplifies.

Corollary 6.35

Suppose that
{\mathfrak{g}}\cong\mathfrak{h}\oplus{\mathfrak{g}}/\mathfrak{h}
is as in Proposition
6.34
.

(1)

If the bracket of
{\mathfrak{g}}
vanishes on
{\mathfrak{g}}/\mathfrak{h}
, then

[x_{1},\dots,x_{k}]_{{\mathfrak{g}}/\mathfrak{h}[-1]}=\frac{1}{k!}\,\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{k}}\,\pm\,\pi_{{\mathfrak{g}}/\mathfrak{h}}\big[\big[\cdots\big[\big[\mathsf{D}(x_{\sigma(1)}),x_{\sigma(2)}\big]_{\mathfrak{g}},x_{\sigma(3)}\big]_{\mathfrak{g}},\dots\big]_{\mathfrak{g}},x_{\sigma(k)}\big]_{\mathfrak{g}}\ .

(2)

If
\big[{\mathfrak{g}}/\mathfrak{h},\ {\mathfrak{g}}/\mathfrak{h}+\mathsf{D}({\mathfrak{g}}/\mathfrak{h})\big]_{{\mathfrak{g}}}\subseteq{\mathfrak{g}}/\mathfrak{h}
, then

[x_{1},\dots,x_{k}]_{{\mathfrak{g}}/\mathfrak{h}[-1]}=-\frac{B_{k-1}}{(k-1)!}\,\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{k}}\,\pm\,\big[\big[\cdots\big[\big[\mathsf{D}(x_{\sigma(1)}),x_{\sigma(2)}\big]_{\mathfrak{g}},x_{\sigma(3)}\big]_{\mathfrak{g}},\dots\big]_{\mathfrak{g}},x_{\sigma(k)}\big]_{\mathfrak{g}}\ ,

where
B_{k-1}
are the Bernouilli numbers.

Proof.

In the first case, any iteration of brackets
\{\{x,\dots\},y,\dots\}
vanishes by assumption. The only remaining term is therefore
\{x_{1},\dots,x_{k}\}
, that is, the term corresponding to
i=1
.

In the second case, the assumptions imply that we can replace
\pi_{\mathfrak{h}/{\mathfrak{g}}}[-,-]_{\mathfrak{g}}
by
[-,-]_{\mathfrak{g}}
in all formulas. In particular, all iterated brackets are equal to the iterated bracket

\big[\big[\cdots\big[\big[\mathsf{D}(x_{\sigma(1)}),x_{\sigma(2)}\big]_{\mathfrak{g}},x_{\sigma(3)}\big]_{\mathfrak{g}},\dots\big]_{\mathfrak{g}},x_{\sigma(k)}\big]_{\mathfrak{g}}\ .

It therefore suffices to identify the coefficients
C_{k}
appearing in the first line of the equation in Proposition
6.34
. Separating out the summand with
i=1
, these coefficients are given by

C_{k}=\frac{1}{k!}+\sum_{i=2}^{k-1}\ \sum_{1<k_{1}<k_{2}<\dots<k_{i}=k}\,\frac{(-1)^{i-1}}{k_{1}!\,(k_{2}-k_{1}+1)!\cdots(k_{i}-k_{i-1}+1)!}\quad,\quad k\geqslant 2\ .

Let us furthermore define
C_{1}=-1
(which is incompatible with this formula).

Using this, we obtain a recurrence relation for the
C_{k}
as follows. Rewriting the summation over
1<k_{1}<k_{2}<\dots<k_{i}=k
as a sum over
1<r+1<r+l_{1}<\dots<r+l_{j}=k
with
j=i-1
, we obtain

\displaystyle C_{k}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{k!}-\sum_{r=1}^{k-2}\,\frac{1}{(r+1)!}\ \sum_{j=1}^{k-r-1}\ \sum_{1<l_{1}<l_{2}<\dots<l_{j}=k-r}\,\frac{(-1)^{j-1}}{l_{1}!\,(l_{2}-l_{1}+1)!\cdots(l_{j}-l_{j-1}+1)!}

\displaystyle=-\frac{C_{1}}{k!}-\sum_{r=1}^{k-2}\,\frac{1}{(r+1)!}\,C_{k-r}=-\sum_{r=1}^{k-1}\,\frac{1}{(r+1)!}\,C_{k-r}\ .

The proof is completed by recalling that the Bernouilli numbers may be completely determined from their standard recursion formula

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{m}\,{m+1\choose k}\,B_{k}=\delta_{m,0}\ ,

(6.36)

for
m\geqslant 0
, whence
C_{k}=-\frac{B_{k-1}}{(k-1)!}
. (Alternatively, one can use the same argument as in the proof of
[
36
, Lemma 5.4]
.)
∎

Example 6.37

Suppose that
{\mathfrak{g}}
is a dgla, and let
{\mathfrak{g}}^{\geqslant 0}
be the sub-dgla of elements of non-negative degree. Then
{\mathfrak{g}}/{\mathfrak{g}}^{\geqslant 0}={\mathfrak{g}}^{<0}
and
\mathsf{D}({\mathfrak{g}}^{<0})\subseteq{\mathfrak{g}}^{0}
. We are therefore in the setting of Corollary
6.35
(2)
; the resulting
L_{\infty}
-structure on
{\mathfrak{g}}^{<0}[-1]
coincides with the higher derived bracket construction of Getzler
[
42
]
, up to multiplying the
k
-ary bracket by
(-1)^{k-1}
.
This
L_{\infty}
-structure depends only on
{\mathfrak{g}}^{\leqslant 0}
: if
{\mathfrak{g}}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{k}
induces an isomorphism in non-positive degrees, then there is a natural isomorphism
{\mathfrak{g}}^{<0}[-1]\cong\mathfrak{k}^{<0}[-1]
.

\triangleleft

6.3
Projection to the Atiyah
L_{\infty}
-algebra

Our goal now is to apply the construction of Section
6.2
to relate Zambon’s
L_{\infty}
-algebra
{\rm Cour}_{n-1}(H)
to the Atiyah
L_{\infty}
-algebras
\mathrm{At}_{n-1}(H)=\big(TM\oplus C^{\infty}(M)[n-1],H\big)
over
TM
which underlie the
L_{\infty}
-algebroids associated with
{\mathrm{U}}(1)
-
n
-bundles that we introduced in Section
5.1
. For this, it is instructive to first recall the standard representative case at the lowest level, discussed already in Examples
4.26
and
4.63
.

Example 6.38

For
n=1
, the central extension

\displaystyle\pazocal{E}_{0}=TM\oplus{\mathbbm{R}}

(6.39)

of the tangent bundle
TM
represents a splitting of the short exact sequence of vector bundles

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow\mathbbm{R}\xrightarrow{\ j\ }\pazocal{E}_{0}\xrightarrow{\ \rho\ }TM\longrightarrow 0\ ,

(6.40)

where
\mathbbm{R}
denotes the trivial line bundle over
M
(regarded as a bundle of abelian Lie algebras).
Fixing a closed 2-form
H
(a 2-cocycle of the Lie algebroid
TM
), the
H
-twisted Dorfman bracket

\displaystyle[\![(X,f)\ ,\,(Y,g)]\!]_{H}=\big([X,Y]\ ,\,\pounds_{X}g-\pounds_{Y}f-H(X,Y)\big)

(6.41)

makes the space of sections
\Gamma(M,\pazocal{E}_{0})=TM\oplus C^{\infty}(M)
into a Lie algebra. Hence
\pazocal{E}_{0}
is a Lie algebroid with anchor
\rho
given by the projection to
TM
. The gauge symmetries of the bracket, induced by closed 1-forms
B
, are automorphisms of the exact sequence (
6.40
) which preserve the Lie algebra structure. These can be thought of as taking tensor products of
\pazocal{E}_{0}
with a trivial
{\mathrm{U}}(1)
-bundle equipped with a flat connection
B
.

In the case where the twisting 2-form
H
defines an integer cohomology class
[H]\in\mathrm{H}^{2}(M;\mathbbm{Z})
, Hitchin discusses in
[
48
]
how this can be interpreted in terms of the Atiyah Lie algebroid
{\mathrm{At}}(P)
associated to a principal
{\mathrm{U}}(1)
-bundle
\pi:P\longrightarrow M
through the Atiyah exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow\mathbbm{R}\xrightarrow{\ j\ }TP/{\mathrm{U}}(1)\xrightarrow{\ \pi_{*}\ }TM\longrightarrow 0\ .

(6.42)

A choice of splitting of this sequence gives an isomorphism
TP/{\mathrm{U}}(1)\cong TM\oplus\mathbbm{R}
, which may be used to induce a Lie algebroid structure on
TM\oplus\mathbbm{R}
from that of
TP/{\mathrm{U}}(1)
. The splitting is equivalent to a choice of
1
-form connection
\tau:TM\longrightarrow{\mathrm{At}}(P)=TP/{\mathrm{U}}(1)
on
P
(cf. Example
4.63
and Remark
6.19
), whose curvature
H=F^{1}(\tau)
measures the failure of
\tau
from being a Lie algebra homomorphism. The Lie bracket induced by this choice of splitting coincides with (
6.41
), and in this case there is an isomorphism

\displaystyle{\mathrm{At}}(P)\ \cong\ {\mathrm{At}}_{0}(H)=\mathrm{Cour}_{0}(H)\ .

(6.43)

The gauge transformations with
[B]\in\mathrm{H}^{1}(M;\mathbbm{Z})
arise from gauge transformations of the underlying
{\mathrm{U}}(1)
-bundle
P
.

\triangleleft

For
n\geqslant 2
, Example
6.38
together with Remark
6.19
suggest an interpretation of an
H
-twisted higher Courant algebroid as a ‘higher Atiyah Lie algebroid’ associated to an
(n{-}1)
-gerbe equipped with an
(n{-}1)
-form connection. In the following we make this statement precise by generalising and extending the
n=2
constructions of
[
37
, Section 5]
.

Let
M
be a smooth manifold and let
\Omega^{*}(M)
be its de Rham algebra. Let us write
\mathrm{Der}(\Omega^{*}(M))
for the dgla of derivations of
\Omega^{*}(M)
. This is given in degree
k
by the set of degree-preserving
\mathbbm{R}
-linear maps
Q\colon\Omega^{*}(M)\longrightarrow\Omega^{*}(M)[k]
such that

Q(\alpha\wedge\beta)=Q(\alpha)\wedge\beta+(-1)^{k\,|\alpha|}\,\alpha\wedge Q(\beta)\ .

The Lie bracket is the commutator bracket and the differential
\delta
is given by the commutator with the de Rham differential:

[Q_{1},Q_{2}]=Q_{1}\,Q_{2}-(-1)^{|Q_{1}|\,|Q_{2}|}\,Q_{2}\,Q_{1}\qquad,\qquad\delta(Q)=[\mathrm{d},Q]=\mathrm{d}\circ Q-(-1)^{|Q|}\,Q\circ\mathrm{d}\ .

Observe that
\mathrm{Der}(\Omega^{*}(M))
is concentrated in degrees
\geqslant-1
: derivations of degree
\leqslant-2
vanish on
0
-forms and
1
-forms, and hence on all of
\Omega^{*}(M)
. The derivations of degree
-1
are all of the form
\iota_{X}
, the contraction along a vector
X\in TM
on
M
. The differential sends each such
\iota_{X}
to the derivation
\pounds_{X}
, the Lie derivative along
X
.

By definition,
\mathrm{Der}(\Omega^{*}(M))
acts on
\Omega^{*}(M)
. We can therefore form the semi-direct product dgla

\mathrm{Der}(\Omega^{*}(M))\ltimes\Omega^{*}(M)[-n]

with bracket and differential

\big[(Q_{1},\alpha_{1})\ ,\,(Q_{2},\alpha_{2})\big]=\big([Q_{1},Q_{2}]\ ,\,Q_{1}(\alpha_{2})-(-1)^{|Q_{2}|\,|\alpha_{1}|}\,Q_{2}(\alpha_{1})\big)\qquad,\qquad\delta(Q,\alpha)=\big([\mathrm{d},Q]\ ,\,\mathrm{d}\alpha\big)\ .

Here
|\alpha|
is the cohomological degree of
\alpha
, which differs (because of the shift) from its form degree.

The closed
(n{+}1)
-form
H
defines an element of degree
1
in this dgla. It is a Maurer–Cartan element, since
\delta(H)=\mathrm{d}H=0
and
[H,H]=0
. We can therefore twist the differential by
H
to get the differential

\delta_{H}(Q,\alpha)=\big([\mathrm{d},Q]\ ,\,\mathrm{d}\alpha-(-1)^{|Q|}\,Q(H)\big)\ .

Let us denote the resulting dgla by

{\mathfrak{g}}_{H}=\big(\mathrm{Der}(\Omega^{*}(M))\ltimes\Omega^{*}(M)[-n]\ ,\,\delta_{H}\ ,\,[-,-]\big)\ .

For each
0\leqslant p\leqslant n-1
, let us consider the graded subspace

F^{p+1}{\mathfrak{g}}_{H}=\mathrm{Der}(\Omega^{*}(M))^{\geqslant 0}\oplus\Omega^{\geqslant p+1}(M)[-n]

consisting of all derivations of degree
\geqslant 0
and all forms of form degree
\geqslant p+1
. This is closed under the differential and the bracket, so
F^{p+1}{\mathfrak{g}}_{H}\subseteq{\mathfrak{g}}_{H}
is a sub-dgla. Using the isomorphism
\mathrm{Der}(\Omega^{*}(M))^{-1}\cong TM
which identifies a vector field
X
with the contraction
\iota_{X}
, the shifted quotient
{\mathfrak{g}}_{H}/F^{p+1}{\mathfrak{g}}_{H}[-1]
is isomorphic to the non-positively graded complex

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\infty}(M)[n-1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TM}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

(6.44a)

for
p=0
, to

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\infty}(M)[n-1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega^{1}(M)[n-2]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega^{p}(M)[n-p-1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TM}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{d}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{d}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{d}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

(6.44b)

for
0<p<n-1
, and to

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\infty}(M)[n-1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega^{1}(M)[n-2]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega^{n-2}(M)[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TM\oplus\Omega^{n-1}(M)\cong\Gamma(M,\pazocal{E}_{n-1})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{d}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{d}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{d}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{d}}

(6.44c)

for
p=n-1
. Note that the complexes (
6.44a
) and (
6.44c
) are precisely the complexes underlying the
L_{\infty}
-algebras
{\mathrm{At}}_{n-1}(H)
and
{\rm Cour}_{n-1}(H)
, respectively.

As graded vector spaces, there is a canonical splitting
{\mathfrak{g}}_{H}\cong F^{p+1}{\mathfrak{g}}_{H}\oplus{\mathfrak{g}}_{H}/F^{p+1}{\mathfrak{g}}_{H}
. The map
\mathsf{D}\colon{\mathfrak{g}}_{H}/F^{p+1}{\mathfrak{g}}_{H}\longrightarrow F^{p+1}{\mathfrak{g}}_{H}
is given by

\mathsf{D}(X)=\pounds_{X}-(-1)^{n+1}\,\iota_{X}H\qquad,\qquad\mathsf{D}(\alpha)=\mathrm{d}\alpha

for
X\in TM
and
\alpha\in\Omega^{p}(M)
. The map
\mathsf{D}
vanishes on forms of degree
<p
. We can therefore apply Proposition
6.34
to obtain an
L_{\infty}
-structure on
({\mathfrak{g}}_{H}/F^{p+1}{\mathfrak{g}}_{H})[-1]
.

Proposition 6.45

Let
n\geqslant 2
and
0\leqslant p\leqslant n-1
.
The complex (
6.44
) admits an
L_{\infty}
-structure with non-trivial higher brackets given on
X_{i}\in TM
and
\alpha\in\Omega^{\leqslant p}(M):=\bigoplus_{r=0}^{p}\,\Omega^{r}(M)
as follows:

•

Binary brackets:

\displaystyle[X_{1},X_{2}]

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}[X_{1},X_{2}]_{TM}+\iota_{X_{1}}\,\iota_{X_{2}}H&\text{if }\ p=n-1\ ,\\[4.0pt]
{[X_{1},X_{2}]_{TM}}&\text{if }\ p<n-1\ ,\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle[X_{1},\alpha]

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\pounds_{X_{1}}\alpha&\text{if }\ \alpha\in\Omega^{\leqslant p-1}(M)\ ,\\[4.0pt]
\pounds_{X_{1}}\alpha+\iota_{X_{1}}\,\mathrm{d}\alpha&\text{if }\ \alpha\in\Omega^{p}(M)\ .\end{array}\right.

•

k
-ary brackets for all odd
3\leqslant k\leqslant p+2
:

\displaystyle[X_{1},\dots,X_{k-1},\alpha]

\displaystyle=(-1)^{k}\,B_{k-1}\,\iota_{X_{1}}\cdots\iota_{X_{k-1}}\,\mathrm{d}\alpha-\frac{B_{k-1}}{k-1}\,\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}\,\iota_{X_{1}}\cdots\widehat{\iota_{X_{i}}}\dots\iota_{X_{k-1}}\,\pounds_{X_{i}}\alpha

\displaystyle\quad\,+\frac{2\,B_{k-1}}{(k-1)\,(k-2)}\,\sum_{1\leqslant i<j\leqslant k-1}\,\iota_{X_{1}}\cdots\widehat{\iota_{X_{i}}}\cdots\widehat{\iota_{X_{j}}}\cdots\iota_{X_{k-1}}\,\iota_{[X_{i},X_{j}]}\,\alpha\ .

•

k
-ary brackets for
n-p+1\leqslant k\leqslant n+1
:

\displaystyle[X_{1},\dots,X_{k}]=k!\ C_{k,p}\ \iota_{X_{1}}\cdots\iota_{X_{k}}H\ ,

(6.50)

where

C_{k,p}=\sum_{i=1}^{k+p-n}\ \sum_{n-p<k_{1}<k_{2}<\dots<k_{i}=k}\,\frac{(-1)^{i-1}}{k_{1}!\,(k_{2}-k_{1}+1)!\cdots(k_{i}-k_{i-1}+1)!}\ .

Proof.

The Lie bracket of
{\mathfrak{g}}_{H}
has the properties

\displaystyle[\mathrm{Der}(\Omega^{*}(M)),\mathrm{Der}(\Omega^{*}(M))]

\displaystyle\subseteq\mathrm{Der}(\Omega^{*}(M))\ ,

\displaystyle[\mathrm{Der}(\Omega^{*}(M)),\Omega^{*}(M)]

\displaystyle\subseteq\Omega^{*}(M)\ ,

\displaystyle[\Omega^{*}(M),\Omega^{*}(M)]

\displaystyle=0\ .

In particular, any Lie word involving two differential forms is zero. Likewise, any Lie word in derivations
\pounds_{X_{i}}
and
\iota_{X_{i}}
of degree
\leqslant 0
will vanish as soon as it contains two contractions
\iota_{X_{i}}
and
\iota_{X_{j}}
(for degree reasons). The only non-trivial brackets that can appear as part of the formulas from Proposition
6.34
are hence of the form

\displaystyle\{\pounds_{X_{1}},\iota_{X_{2}}\}

\displaystyle=\iota_{[X_{1},X_{2}]}\ ,

\displaystyle\{\alpha,\iota_{X_{1}},\dots,\iota_{X_{k}}\}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{k\,|\alpha|}\,\iota_{X_{1}}\cdots\iota_{X_{k}}\alpha

\displaystyle\text{if }\ \alpha\in\Omega^{\leqslant p+k}(M)[-n+1]\ ,

\displaystyle\{\pounds_{X_{1}},\alpha,\iota_{X_{2}},\dots,\iota_{X_{k}}\}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{(k-1)\,|\alpha|}\,\iota_{X_{2}}\cdots\iota_{X_{k}}\,\pounds_{X_{1}}\alpha

\displaystyle\text{if }\ \alpha\in\Omega^{\leqslant p+k-1}(M)[-n+1]\ ,

\displaystyle\{\pounds_{X_{1}},\iota_{X_{2}},\alpha,\iota_{X_{3}},\dots,\iota_{X_{k}}\}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{(k-1)\,|\alpha|}\,\iota_{X_{3}}\cdots\iota_{X_{k}}\,\iota_{[X_{1},X_{2}]}\,\alpha

\displaystyle\text{if }\ \alpha\in\Omega^{\leqslant p+k-1}(M)[-n+1]\ .

Consequently, only a few iterated brackets can appear in the formula for the
k
-ary bracket. These are of the form

\displaystyle\big\{\cdots\big\{\big\{\mathrm{d}\alpha,\iota_{X_{1}},\dots\big\},\dots,\iota_{X_{k}}\big\},\dots,\iota_{X_{k}}\big\}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{k\,(n-p)}\,\iota_{X_{1}}\cdots\iota_{X_{k}}\,\mathrm{d}\alpha

\displaystyle\text{if }\alpha\in\Omega^{p}(M)[-n+1]\ ,

\displaystyle\big\{\cdots\big\{\big\{\iota_{X_{1}}H,\iota_{X_{2}},\dots,\iota_{X_{k_{1}}}\big\},\dots\big\},\dots,\iota_{X_{k}}\big\}

\displaystyle=\iota_{X_{1}}\cdots\iota_{X_{k}}H

\displaystyle\text{if }k_{1}\geqslant n+1-p\ ,

\displaystyle\big\{\cdots\big\{\big\{\pounds_{X_{1}},\alpha,\iota_{X_{2}}\dots\big\},\dots\big\},\dots,\iota_{X_{k}}\big\}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{(k-1)\,|\alpha|}\,\iota_{X_{2}}\cdots\iota_{X_{k}}\,\pounds_{X_{1}}\alpha\ ,

\displaystyle\big\{\cdots\big\{\big\{\pounds_{X_{1}},\iota_{X_{2}},\alpha,\dots\big\},\dots\big\},\dots,\iota_{X_{k}}\big\}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{(k-1)\,|\alpha|}\,\iota_{X_{3}}\cdots\iota_{[X_{1},X_{2}]}\,\alpha\ ,

\displaystyle\big\{\cdots\big\{\big\{\pounds_{X_{1}},\iota_{X_{2}}\big\},\alpha,\dots\big\},\dots,\iota_{X_{k}}\big\}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{(k-1)\,|\alpha|}\,\iota_{X_{3}}\cdots\iota_{[X_{1},X_{2}]}\,\alpha\ ,

\displaystyle\{\pounds_{X_{1}},\iota_{X_{2}}\}

\displaystyle=\iota_{[X_{1},X_{2}]}\ ,

where all remaining elements indicated by “
\dots
” are of the form
\iota_{X_{j}}
for
X_{j}\in TM
and
\alpha\in\Omega^{\leqslant p}(M)
. Plugging these into Proposition
6.34
yields the result.
∎

Definition 6.51

For
n\geqslant 2
and
0\leqslant p\leqslant n-1
, we write
\mathrm{Cour}_{n-1}^{\leqslant p}(H)
for the
L_{\infty}
-algebra of Proposition
6.45
.

Lemma 6.52

\mathrm{Cour}_{n-1}^{\leqslant n-1}(H)\cong\mathrm{Cour}_{n-1}(H)
and
\mathrm{Cour}_{n-1}^{\leqslant 0}(H)\cong\mathrm{At}_{n-1}(H)
.

Proof.

Zambon defines
\mathrm{Cour}_{n-1}(H)
using Getzler’s higher derived bracket construction (Example
6.37
) applied to the dgla of shifted poly-derivations

\Big({\mathrm{Sym}}_{\Omega^{*}(M)}\big(\mathrm{Der}(\Omega^{*}(M))[n]\big)\Big)[-n]\ ,

twisted by the Maurer–Cartan element
H\in\Omega^{*}(M)
. In degrees
\leqslant 0
, this dgla is isomorphic to the sub-dgla
\mathrm{Der}(\Omega^{*}(M))\oplus\Omega^{*}(M)[-n]
of poly-derivations of weight
\leqslant 1
in the symmetric algebra. By Example
6.37
, this implies
\mathrm{Cour}_{n-1}^{\leqslant n-1}(H)\cong\mathrm{Cour}_{n-1}(H)
.

On the other hand, when
p=0
the
L_{\infty}
-structure from Proposition
6.45
only has one non-trivial higher bracket (for degree reasons), which is
[X_{1},\dots,X_{n+1}]=H(X_{1},\dots,X_{n+1})
.
∎

By construction, the
L_{\infty}
-algebras of Definition
6.51
fit into a diagram of the form

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Cour}_{n-1}^{\leqslant n-1}(H)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Cour}_{n-1}^{\leqslant p}(H)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{hofib}}_{\textrm{PL}}(F^{n}{\mathfrak{g}}_{H}\subseteq{\mathfrak{g}}_{H})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{hofib}}_{\textrm{PL}}(F^{p+1}{\mathfrak{g}}_{H}\subseteq{\mathfrak{g}}_{H})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{hofib}}_{\textrm{PL}}(F^{1}{\mathfrak{g}}_{H}\subseteq{\mathfrak{g}}_{H})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\infty}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

where the bottom horizontal maps are induced by the inclusions

. In particular, there exists a sequence of

and

. At the linear level, the map
\mathrm{Cour}_{n-1}^{\leqslant p}(H)\longrightarrow\mathrm{Cour}_{n-1}^{\leqslant p-1}(H)
is the projection sending the

-forms to zero.

Notably, when combined with Lemma

this construction establishes

For

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TM}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{d}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

Remark 6.57

Theorem
6.54
generalises the
n=2
result of
[
37
, Proposition 5.2.3]
, which connects the Courant algebroid with the Atiyah Lie
2
-algebroid.

\triangleleft

6.4
Global moduli problems

We would now like to expound the view that the
L_{\infty}
-algebra associated to a higher Courant algebroid, detailed in Proposition
6.24
, encodes the infinitesimal symmetries of an
(n{-}1)
-gerbe with
(n{-}1)
-form connection, as suggested by Remark
6.19
and through its relation to the Atiyah
L_{\infty}
-algebra in Theorem
6.54
. For this, we seek an equivalence akin to Theorem
5.13
. Note, however, that
\mathrm{Cour}^{\leqslant p}_{n-1}(H)
does
not
define an
L_{\infty}
-algebroid for
p>0
, and so this problem should be tackled in a global setting of underlying
L_{\infty}
-algebras. In order to achieve this within the framework of the present paper, in this section we take a slight detour to analyse some examples of moduli problems from the global perspective discussed in Section
3.1
.

For a smooth manifold
M
and stack
X\in{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}})
, we will also refer to a morphism
x\colon M\longrightarrow X
as an
X
-structure on
M
; this is convenient when thinking of
X
as a classifying stack for some geometric data on
M
.
For some stacks
X\in{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}})
there are different notions of moduli stack of
X
-structures on
M
, depending on how we define an
X
-structure on products
U\times M
of
M
with a parameter space
U
.
For example, to define smooth families of differential forms or connections on
M
parametrised by
U
, we can either use all differential forms on
U\times M
, or we can restrict to the vertical differential forms, i.e. those forms which vanish as soon as they are evaluated on a tangent vector to the parameter space
U
(this is closely related to concretification
[
76
,
17
]
).
Importantly, it may happen that only one of these moduli stacks has deformation theory.

Example 6.58

Let

\Omega^{p}\colon{\mathscr{M}\hskip-0.56917pt\mathrm{fd}}^{\mathrm{op}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{et}}

be the sheaf assigning to each smooth manifold its set of
p
-forms.
For any fixed manifold
M
, consider the sheaf
{\operatorname{Hom}}_{v}(M,\Omega^{p})/\mathsf{Diff}(M)
, whose value on
U
is the action groupoid of
\mathsf{Diff}(M)(U)
on the set of
vertical

p
-forms on
U\times M
.

We know from Example
2.95
that
\Omega^{p}
and the stack of differential forms on
M
do not have deformation theory when we define a
U
-family of differential forms on
M
to be a form on
U\times M
.
However, Example
2.92
shows that
{\operatorname{Hom}}_{v}(M,\Omega^{p})

does
have deformation theory.
We can thus encode a
p
-form on
M
as a morphism

\omega\colon*\longrightarrow{\operatorname{Hom}}_{v}(M,\Omega^{p})\,\big/\,\mathsf{Diff}(M)

and ask for the relative tangent complex of this morphism.
Note that this deformation problem does not correspond to a formal moduli problem over
M
in the sense of Theorem
3.2
, for instance.

\triangleleft

In order to compute the tangent stacks of moduli problems of this form, we use the following results:
let
A\in{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}})
be an abelian group object, and suppose that it is endowed with a
\mathsf{Diff}(M)
-action which is compatible with the group structure of
A
, thus defining a semi-direct product group
\mathsf{Diff}(M)\ltimes A
.
Consider a morphism

a\colon*\longrightarrow A\,\big/\,\mathsf{Diff}(M)\ .

We define a group object
{\mathsf{Sym}}(a)
as the homotopy pullback

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{Sym}}(a)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Diff}(M)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Diff}(M)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Diff}(M)\ltimes A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi\longmapsto(\phi,\,a^{-1}\,(\phi^{-1})^{*}a)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi\longmapsto(\phi,1)}

(6.59)

Via the canonical equivalence between maps
b\longrightarrow c
and
1\longrightarrow b^{-1}\,c
in group objects in spaces, where
1
denotes a unit element, we readily see

Lemma 6.60

If
A=X^{M}
for some group object
X
, and
a=x^{\dashv}
is the morphism corresponding to a morphism
x\colon M\longrightarrow X
with deformation theory, then
{\mathsf{Sym}}(a)
agrees with the smooth
\infty
-group
{\mathsf{Sym}}(x)=\mathsf{Diff}_{/X}(M,M)
of symmetries of
x
introduced in Remark
3.27
.

Proposition 6.61

In the situation of (
6.59
), suppose that
A
has deformation theory and let
E
be a cochain complex which describes the
L_{\infty}
-algebra of
A
; since
A
is abelian, all of its brackets vanish.
Then the differential graded Lie algebra over
TM
associated to a morphism
a\colon*\longrightarrow A/\mathsf{Diff}(M)
is
6
6

6

The subscript
pb
refers to the pullback square (
6.59
).

{\mathfrak{sym}}(a)=\big(TM\oplus E[-1],\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{pb}},[-,-]\big)\ ,

(6.62)

whose differential and bracket are defined as follows:
let

a^{-1}\,\mathrm{d}a\colon TM\longrightarrow TM\ltimes E

denote the map induced on dglas by the right vertical morphism of group objects in (
6.59
).

With
|\rho|\in\mathbbm{Z}
denoting the degree of an element
\rho\in E[-1]
in the shifted complex, the differential reads as

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{pb}}(\rho)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}_{E[-1]}\rho

\displaystyle\in\ (E[-1])^{|\rho|+1}\ ,

\displaystyle|\rho|\neq-1,0\ ,

(6.63)

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{pb}}(\rho)

\displaystyle=(0,\mathrm{d}_{E[-1]}\rho)

\displaystyle\in\ TM\oplus E^{-1}\ ,

\displaystyle|\rho|=-1\ ,

(6.64)

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{pb}}(X,\rho)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}_{E[-1]}\rho+a^{-1}\,\mathrm{d}a(X)

\displaystyle\in\ E^{0}\ ,

\displaystyle|\rho|=0\ .

(6.65)

The brackets read as

\displaystyle\big[(X,\rho),(Y,\mu)\big]

\displaystyle=\big([X,Y],X\mu-Y\rho\big)\ ,

\displaystyle\forall\ (X,\rho),(Y,\mu)\in TM\oplus(E[-1])^{0}\ ,

(6.66)

\displaystyle\big[(X,\rho),\mu\big]

\displaystyle=X\mu\ ,

\displaystyle\forall\ (X,\rho)\in TM\oplus(E[-1])^{0}\ ,\ \mu\in(E[-1])^{n}\ ,\,n\neq 0\ .

(6.67)

Proof.

The cartesian square (
6.59
) in the
\infty
-category of group objects in
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}})
induces a cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{B}}{\mathsf{Sym}}(a)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{B}}\mathsf{Diff}(M)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{B}}\mathsf{Diff}(M)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{B}}\mathsf{Diff}(M)\ltimes A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi\longmapsto(\phi,\,a^{-1}\,(\phi^{-1})^{*}a)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi\longmapsto(\phi,1)}

(6.68)

in
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}})^{*/}_{\geqslant 0}
, the
\infty
-topos of pointed connected objects in
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{h}}_{\infty}({\mathscr{I}\mathrm{nf}\hskip-0.09995pt\mathscr{M}\mathrm{fd}})
.
Forming the relative tangent complex with respect to the basepoint map of each vertex, we obtain a homotopy cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{sym}}(a)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TM}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TM}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TM\ltimes E}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\longmapsto(X,\,a^{-1}\,\mathrm{d}a(X))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\longmapsto(X,0)}

(6.69)

of dglas.
Here
{\mathfrak{sym}}(a)
is the dgla associated to
{\mathsf{Sym}}(a)
, which we aim to compute.

We can now make
{\mathfrak{sym}}(a)
explicit by computing this homotopy pullback of dglas.
To that end, we replace the bottom horizontal morphism by a fibration:
consider the factorisation of the morphism
TM\longrightarrow TM\ltimes E
as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TM}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TM\oplus{\mathrm{Cone}}({\mathrm{id}}_{E})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TM\ltimes E}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({\mathrm{id}}_{TM},0)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{pr}_{TM\ltimes E}}

(6.70)

where
\mathrm{pr}
is the canonical projection.
The brackets on
TM\oplus{\mathrm{Cone}}({\mathrm{id}}_{E})
are given by the
TM
-action on
E
and on
E[-1]
.
Then we can compute
{\mathfrak{sym}}(a)
as a strict pullback of dglas, i.e. the diagram of dglas

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{sym}}(a)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TM}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TM\oplus{\mathrm{Cone}}({\mathrm{id}}_{E})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TM\ltimes E}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\longmapsto(X,\,a^{-1}\,\mathrm{d}a(X))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{pr}_{TM\ltimes E}}

(6.71)

is cartesian. One now checks by a direct computation that
{\mathfrak{sym}}(a)
is the dgla described in the statement of Proposition
6.61
.
∎

Example 6.72

We return to the example of deforming a map (cf. Example
6.58
)

\omega\colon*\longrightarrow\Omega^{p}_{v}(M)\,\big/\,\mathsf{Diff}(M)\ .

In this case
A={\operatorname{Hom}}_{v}(M,\Omega^{p})
, and so likewise
E={\operatorname{Hom}}_{v}(M,\Omega^{p})
since
A
is even a vector space.
By Proposition
6.61
we obtain

{\mathfrak{sym}}(\omega)=\big(TM\xrightarrow{\ \pounds_{(-)}\omega\ }\Omega^{p}(M)\ ,[-,-]\big)\ ,

(6.73)

with
TM
lying in degree zero, and the brackets given by the action of
TM
on itself and on
\Omega^{p}(M)
via the Lie derivative.
The 2-term dgla
{\mathfrak{sym}}(\omega)
can be viewed as the derived stabiliser of
\omega
under the Lie algebra action of
TM
on
\Omega^{p}(M)
.
In particular

\mathrm{H}^{0}\big({\mathfrak{sym}}(\omega)\big)=\big\{X\in TM\,\big|\,\pounds_{X}\omega=0\big\}

(6.74)

is the Lie algebra of the stabiliser subgroup of
\omega
in
\mathsf{Diff}(M)
.

\triangleleft

In the remainder of this section, we will look at other examples which suggest that there is an interesting notion of

generalised higher connections
worthy of further investigation. These are order
p
splittings of the map
{\mathfrak{sym}}(x)\longrightarrow TM
for

x\colon*\longrightarrow\mathrm{Moduli}(M,X)

(6.75)

a point in a moduli stack of
X
-structures on
M
which is not of the form
{\operatorname{Hom}}(M,X)/\mathsf{Diff}(M)
of an internal hom where
X
is a qcdt stack.

6.5
Infinitesimal symmetries and weak connections

Building on the concept of vertical families of
p
-forms on
M
, one can construct moduli stacks of
(n{-}1)
-gerbes with
p
-form connection on
M
.
We denote these stacks by
\underline{{\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}}}^{n-1}_{\nabla|p}(M)_{v}
.
These moduli stacks further come endowed with a canonical action of
\mathsf{Diff}(M)
via pullback; see
[
16
]
for the details of such a construction.
The fact that the classifying stacks
{\mathrm{B}}^{n-p}\,{\mathrm{B}}^{p}_{\nabla}{\mathrm{U}}(1)
for
(n{-}1)
-gerbes with
p
-form connections are abelian group objects is passed on to the moduli stacks
\underline{{\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}}}^{n-1}_{\nabla|p}(M)_{v}
.
Furthermore, the
\mathsf{Diff}(M)
-action is compatible with this abelian group structure.
Thus we can form the semi-direct product
\mathsf{Diff}(M)\ltimes\underline{{\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}}}^{n-1}_{\nabla|p}(M)_{v}
and apply Proposition
6.61
.
We will now describe the resulting Lie algebra.

Consider an
(n{-}1)
-gerbe with
p
-form connection on
M
given as an element
[g,\pazocal{A}]\in\underline{{\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}}}^{n-1}_{\nabla|p}(M)_{v}(*)
. This defines a morphism

[g,\pazocal{A}]\colon*\longrightarrow\underline{{\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}}}^{n-1}_{\nabla|p}(M)_{v}\,\big/\,\mathsf{Diff}(M)\ .

(6.76)

We fix a good open cover
\pazocal{U}
of
M
and a representative
(g,\pazocal{A})
of the class
[g,\pazocal{A}]
.
That is,
(g,\pazocal{A})
is a Čech–Deligne cocycle on
M
with respect to
\pazocal{U}
, as spelled out in Section
5.2
.

In the notation of Proposition
6.61
we then have

E^{l}=\begin{cases}\ 0\ ,&l<-n\ ,\\[4.0pt]
\ \displaystyle\bigoplus\limits_{k=0}^{l}\,\Omega^{k}({\check{C}}_{n+l-k}\,\pazocal{U})\ ,&-n\leqslant l\leqslant-n+p\ ,\\[12.0pt]
\ \displaystyle\bigoplus\limits_{k=0}^{p}\,\Omega^{k}({\check{C}}_{n+l-k}\,\pazocal{U})\ ,&-n+p<l<0\ ,\\[12.0pt]
\ \ker(\mathrm{D})\ ,&l=0\ ,\\[4.0pt]
\ 0&l\geqslant 0\ ,\end{cases}

(6.77)

with differential

\mathrm{d}_{E}=\mathrm{D}=(-1)^{l+k}\,\check{\delta}+\mathrm{d}

given as the differential on the totalisation of the Čech–de Rham double complex associated to the cover
\pazocal{U}
and the complex of sheaves of abelian groups

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega^{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega^{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega^{p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{d}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{d}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{d}}

(6.78)

The morphism denoted
a^{-1}\,\mathrm{d}a
in Proposition
6.61
reads as

[g,\pazocal{A}]^{-1}\,\mathrm{d}[g,\pazocal{A}]\colon TM\longrightarrow TM\ltimes E\ ,\quad X\longmapsto\big(X,\,\mathrm{d}\log g(X),\,\pounds_{X}A^{{(1)}},\ldots,\pounds_{X}A^{{(p)}}\big)\ .

(6.79)

Proposition
6.61
thus computes the dgla which captures the deformations of the point (
6.76
).
In particular, this dgla is concentrated in degrees
-n+1,\ldots,-1,0,1
, has underlying complex
TM\oplus E[-1]
with
E
described in (
6.77
), and brackets given by the Lie derivative.
Its differential is the differential of
E[-1]
, with the additional term
[g,\pazocal{A}]^{-1}\,\mathrm{d}[g,\pazocal{A}]
in degree zero.
