\displaystyle[\phi_{g,\pazocal{A}},\phi_{g,\pazocal{A}}]_{{\operatorname{Hom}},2}(X_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge X_{n+1})=2\,\sum_{i=1}^{n+1}\,(-1)^{i-1}\,\pounds_{X_{i}}\big(A^{(n)}(X_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge\widehat{X_{i}}\wedge\cdots\wedge X_{n+1})\big)\ .

(C.47)

Together this yields

\displaystyle\Big(\mathrm{d}_{\operatorname{Hom}}\phi_{g,\pazocal{A}}+\sum_{l\geqslant 2}\,\frac{1}{l!}\,[\phi_{g,\pazocal{A}},\dots,\phi_{g,\pazocal{A}}]_{{\operatorname{Hom}},l}\Big)(X_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge X_{n+1})=(\check{\delta}H-\mathrm{d}A^{(n)})(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})=0\ .

(C.48)

This uses precisely that
H
is the curvature of the
(n{-}1)
-gerbe connection
\pazocal{A}
.

Finally, we compute

\displaystyle\Big(\mathrm{d}_{\operatorname{Hom}}\phi_{g,\pazocal{A}}+\sum_{l\geqslant 2}\,\frac{1}{l!}\,[\phi_{g,\pazocal{A}},\dots,\phi_{g,\pazocal{A}}]_{{\operatorname{Hom}},l}\Big)(X_{1}\vee f)\ .

(C.49)

We find

\displaystyle(\mathrm{d}_{\operatorname{Hom}}\phi_{g,\pazocal{A}})(X_{1}\vee f)=\check{\delta}(\pounds_{X_{1}}f)=\mathrm{d}\,\check{\delta}(f)(X_{1})\ ,

(C.50)

and

\displaystyle[\phi_{g,\pazocal{A}},\phi_{g,\pazocal{A}}]_{{\operatorname{Hom}},2}(X_{1}\vee f)

\displaystyle=[-]_{{\check{C}\mathrm{At}}(g),2}\circ(\phi_{g,\pazocal{A}}\otimes\phi_{g,\pazocal{A}})\circ{\mathsf{\Delta}}(X_{1}\vee f)

(C.51)

\displaystyle=[-]_{{\check{C}\mathrm{At}}(g),2}\circ(\phi_{g,\pazocal{A}}\otimes\phi_{g,\pazocal{A}})(X_{1}\otimes f+(-1)^{n}\,f\otimes X_{1})

(C.52)

\displaystyle=[-]_{{\check{C}\mathrm{At}}(g),2}\big((X_{1},A^{(1)}(X_{1}))\otimes(-\check{\delta}f)+(-1)^{n}\,(-\check{\delta}f)\otimes(X_{1},A^{(1)}(X_{1}))\big)

(C.53)

\displaystyle=-2\,\pounds_{X_{1}}\,\check{\delta}f

(C.54)

\displaystyle=-2\,\mathrm{d}\,\check{\delta}(f)(X_{1})\ .

(C.55)

These two contributions cancel each other, and we obtain

\displaystyle\Big(\mathrm{d}_{\operatorname{Hom}}\phi_{g,\pazocal{A}}+\sum_{l\geqslant 2}\,\frac{1}{l!}\,[\phi_{g,\pazocal{A}},\dots,\phi_{g,\pazocal{A}}]_{{\operatorname{Hom}},l}\Big)(X_{1}\vee f)=0\ .

(C.56)

All higher degree terms vanish since
{\check{C}\mathrm{At}}(g)[1]
is concentrated in degrees ranging from
-n
to
-1
.
This completes the proof that
\phi_{g,\pazocal{A}}
satisfies the Maurer–Cartan equation (
C.8
).

Finally, we have to check that the
\infty
-morphism
\phi_{g,\pazocal{A}}
is indeed a weak equivalence of
L_{\infty}
-algebroids over
C^{\infty}(M)
.
By definition (see for instance
[
54
, Definition 3.10]
) this is the case if and only if the linear part of the morphism

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TM}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{n-1}(g)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\infty}({\check{C}}_{n-2}\,\pazocal{U})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\infty}({\check{C}}_{1}\,\pazocal{U})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\infty}(M)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\infty}({\check{C}}_{0}\,\pazocal{U})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({\mathrm{id}}_{TM},A^{(1)})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(0,(-1)^{2}\,\check{\delta})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-1)^{3}\,\check{\delta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-1)^{n-1}\,\check{\delta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{\delta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-1)^{n}\,\check{\delta}}

(C.57)

is a quasi-isomorphism of cochain complexes.

First, we claim that the morphism
({\mathrm{id}}_{TM},A^{(1)})
induces an isomorphism on
\mathrm{H}^{-1}
(recall that the complexes in (
C.57
) are shifted by one degree).
Let
(X,f)
and
(X^{\prime},f^{\prime})
be elements of
E_{n-1}(g)
.
These are cohomologous if and only if
X=X^{\prime}
and there exists some
h\in C^{\infty}({\check{C}}_{n-2}\,\pazocal{U})
such that
\check{\delta}h=f^{\prime}-f
.

Consider the maps

\displaystyle[{\mathrm{id}}_{TM},A^{(1)}]

\displaystyle\colon TM\longrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{-1}\big(E_{n-1}(g)\big)\ ,

\displaystyle\quad X

\displaystyle\longmapsto\big[X,A^{(1)}(X)\big]\ ,

(C.58)

\displaystyle\mathrm{pr}_{TM}

\displaystyle\colon\mathrm{H}^{-1}\big(E_{n-1}(g)\big)\longrightarrow TM\ ,

\displaystyle\quad[Y,f]

\displaystyle\longmapsto Y\ .

(C.59)

These are isomorphisms:
we directly see that
\mathrm{pr}_{TM}\circ[{\mathrm{id}}_{TM},A^{(1)}]
is the identity on
TM
, and

[{\mathrm{id}}_{TM},A^{(1)}]\circ\mathrm{pr}_{TM}[Y,f]=\big[Y,A^{(1)}(Y)\big]=[Y,f]+\big[0,A^{(1)}(Y)-f\big]\ .

(C.60)

Recall that the Čech cohomology of the sheaf of smooth real-valued functions
C_{M}^{\infty}
satisfies

\check{\mathrm{H}}^{p-1}\big(M;C_{M}^{\infty}\big)=0

(C.61)

for all
p\geqslant 1
, and that it may be computed using any good open cover
\pazocal{U}
of the manifold
M
.
Since moreover

\check{\delta}\big(A^{(1)}(Y)-f\big)=(\check{\delta}A^{(1)})(Y)-\check{\delta}f=\mathrm{d}\log(g)(Y)-\mathrm{d}\log(g)(Y)=0\ ,

(C.62)

it follows that
[{\mathrm{id}}_{TM},A^{(1)}]\circ\mathrm{pr}_{TM}
is the identity on cohomology.

In degrees
-2,\ldots,-(n-1)
the morphism of cochain complexes (
C.57
) is an isomorphism on cohomology groups by (
C.61
).
Finally, it also induces an isomorphism of cohomology groups in degree
-n
; this follows from the fact that the Čech complex
C_{*}(\pazocal{U},C^{\infty}_{M})
is an acyclic resolution of the sheaf
C^{\infty}_{M}
on
M
.
∎

Appendix D
A simplicial resolution for dg abelian extensions of
L_{\infty}
-algebroids

Let
A
be a cdga over
\mathbbm{R}
with differential
\mathrm{d}_{A}
, and
{\mathfrak{g}}
a dg Lie algebroid over
A
with differential
\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}
, bracket
[-,-]_{\mathfrak{g}}
and anchor map
\rho\colon{\mathfrak{g}}\longrightarrow T_{A}
.
In this appendix we construct an explicit fibrant simplicial resolution of
{\mathfrak{g}}
in
L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}^{\mathrm{dg}}_{A}
, under certain abelianness assumptions on
{\mathfrak{g}}
(see Theorem
D.108
below).
Essentially, the Atiyah
L_{\infty}
-algebroid of any higher bundle whose structure group is an
\mathbbm{E}_{\infty}
-group satisfies this assumption.
The fibrant simplicial resolution we construct may be of independent interest, but in this paper it will serve as a key ingredient in the proof of Theorem
5.55
which we give in Appendix
E
.

Recall the notion of a dg Lie algebroid representation on a dg
A
-module (see for instance
[
69
, p. 29]
).

Lemma D.1

For each
l\in\mathbbm{Z}
, setting

[-,-]\colon{\mathfrak{g}}\otimes_{\mathbbm{R}}\ker(\rho)[l]\longrightarrow\ker(\rho)[l]\ ,\quad(x,v)\longmapsto[x,v]_{\mathfrak{g}}

(D.2)

defines a dg Lie algebroid representation of
{\mathfrak{g}}
on
\ker(\rho)[l]
, satisfying

[a\,x,v]=a\,[x,v]\qquad\text{and}\qquad[x,a\,v]=(-1)^{|a|\,|x|}\,a\,[x,v]+\pounds_{\rho(x)}(a)\,v\ ,

(D.3)

for all
a\in A
,
x\in{\mathfrak{g}}
and
v\in\ker(\rho)[l]
.

Proof.

Since
\ker(\rho)\subset{\mathfrak{g}}
is a Lie ideal, the action map
[-,-]\colon{\mathfrak{g}}\otimes_{\mathbbm{R}}\ker(\rho)[l]\longrightarrow\ker(\rho)[l]
is well-defined.
Compatibility of the action with the differential and the
A
-module structure follow from the fact that
{\mathfrak{g}}
is a dg Lie algebroid over
A
.
The degree shift on
\ker(\rho)
does not enter in these conditions, since the differential and the
A
-action only have to be commuted with elements
x\in{\mathfrak{g}}
, i.e. the entries of the first argument of the bracket
[-,-]
.
∎

Let
{\operatorname{ch}}_{\mathbbm{R}}\colon L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}^{\mathrm{dg}}_{A}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}^{\mathrm{dg}}_{\mathbbm{R}}
denote the forgetful functor.
7
7

7

The notation reminds us that we keep only the underlying cochain complex.

We define a simplicial object in
{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}
as the pullback

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Hom}}_{T_{A}}\big(C_{*}(\Delta;\mathbbm{R}),{\operatorname{ch}}_{\mathbbm{R}}({\mathfrak{g}})\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathbbm{R}}\big(C_{*}(\Delta;\mathbbm{R}),{\operatorname{ch}}_{\mathbbm{R}}({\mathfrak{g}})\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{c}\,{\operatorname{ch}}_{\mathbbm{R}}(T_{A})\cong{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathbbm{R}}\big(\mathsf{c}\,C_{*}(\Delta^{0};\mathbbm{R}),{\operatorname{ch}}_{\mathbbm{R}}(T_{A})\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathbbm{R}}\big(C_{*}(\Delta;\mathbbm{R}),{\operatorname{ch}}_{\mathbbm{R}}(T_{A})\big)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\rho}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{*}}

(D.4)

of simplicial objects in
{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}^{\mathrm{dg}}_{A}
, where
\mathsf{c}
denotes the constant diagram functor.

Explicitly, we find the following:
first, at the level of underlying graded modules

{\operatorname{grmod}}_{\mathbbm{R}}\big(C_{*}(\Delta^{n};\mathbbm{R})\big)=\bigoplus_{l\in\mathbbm{N}_{0}}\ \bigoplus_{[l]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[n]}\,\mathbbm{R}[l]\ ,

(D.5)

that is,

C_{-l}(\Delta^{n};\mathbbm{R})=\bigoplus_{[l]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[n]}\,\mathbbm{R}\ ,

(D.6)

where the sum runs over all injective morphisms
[l]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[n]
in
{{\mathbbe{\Delta}}}
.
We denote a pure element in the summand indexed by
\psi\colon[l]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[n]
as a pair
(\lambda,\psi)
, where
\lambda\in\mathbbm{R}
.
Note that by our convention to treat all differentials as having degree
+1
, the complexes of simplicial chains are concentrated in non-positive degrees.

At the level of underlying graded
\mathbbm{R}
-modules, we thus obtain

\displaystyle{\operatorname{grmod}}_{\mathbbm{R}}\,{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathbbm{R}}\big(C_{*}(\Delta^{n};\mathbbm{R}),{\operatorname{ch}}_{\mathbbm{R}}({\mathfrak{g}})\big)

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{l\in\mathbbm{N}_{0}}\ \bigoplus_{[l]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[n]}\,{\operatorname{grmod}}_{\mathbbm{R}}\,{\operatorname{ch}}_{\mathbbm{R}}({\mathfrak{g}})[-l]

(D.7)

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{l\in\mathbbm{N}_{0}}\ \bigoplus_{[l]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[n]}\,{\operatorname{grmod}}_{\mathbbm{R}}\,{\operatorname{ch}}_{\mathbbm{R}}\big({\mathfrak{g}}[-l]\big)\ .

(D.8)

We denote the pure elements of these graded modules as pairs
(x,\varphi)
, where
\varphi\colon[l]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[n]
and
x\in{\mathfrak{g}}
.
The degree of a pure element
(x,\varphi)
is

\big|(x,\varphi)\big|=|x|+l\ .

(D.9)

We also refer to
l\in\mathbbm{N}_{0}
as the
weight
of
(x,\varphi)
.
The element
(x,\varphi)
represents the morphism of
\mathbbm{R}
-modules

C_{-l}(\Delta^{n};\mathbbm{R})\longrightarrow{\operatorname{ch}}_{\mathbbm{R}}({\mathfrak{g}})\ ,\qquad(\lambda,\psi)\longmapsto\begin{cases}0\ ,&\psi\neq\varphi\ ,\\[4.0pt]
(\lambda\,x,\varphi)\ ,&\psi=\varphi\ .\end{cases}

(D.10)

The pairs
(x,\varphi)
generate the graded
\mathbbm{R}
-module
{\operatorname{grmod}}_{\mathbbm{R}}\,{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathbbm{R}}(C_{*}(\Delta^{n};\mathbbm{R}),{\operatorname{ch}}_{\mathbbm{R}}({\mathfrak{g}}))
.

The differential on
{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathbbm{R}}(C_{*}(\Delta^{n};\mathbbm{R}),{\operatorname{ch}}_{\mathbbm{R}}({\mathfrak{g}}))
is the usual differential on the hom complex, but we will need to describe it explicitly.
It will be useful to denote a morphism
\varphi\colon[l]\longrightarrow[n]
in
{{\mathbbe{\Delta}}}
by its ordered sequence of values; that is, we write

\varphi=\big(0\leqslant\varphi(0)<\cdots<\varphi(l)\leqslant n\big)\ .

(D.11)

The differential acts on a pair
(x,\varphi)=(x,0\leqslant\varphi(0)<\cdots<\varphi(l)\leqslant n)
as

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathrm{d}(x,\varphi)&=(\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}x,\varphi)-(-1)^{|x|+l}\,\sum_{r=0}^{l+1}\,(-1)^{r}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\psi\colon[l+1]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[n]\\
\psi\circ\partial_{r}=\varphi\end{subarray}}\,(x,\psi)\\[4.0pt]
&=(\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}x,\varphi)\\
&\qquad\,-\sum_{r=0}^{l+1}\,(-1)^{r+|x|+l}\ \sum_{j=\varphi(r-1)+1}^{\varphi(r)-1}\big(x,\varphi(0)<\cdots<\varphi(r-1)<j<\varphi(r)<\cdots<\varphi(l+1)\big)\ ,\end{split}

(D.12)

where we adopt the convention
\varphi(-1)=0
and
\varphi(l+1)=n
.

The simplicial structure takes the following form:
a morphism
\alpha\colon[m]\longrightarrow[n]
acts as

{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathbbm{R}}\big(\alpha,{\operatorname{ch}}_{\mathbbm{R}}({\mathfrak{g}})\big)(x,\varphi)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\psi\colon[l]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[m]\\
\alpha\circ\psi=\varphi\end{subarray}}\,(x,\psi)\ .

(D.13)

Indeed, one can check that for each
(\lambda,\psi)\in C_{-l}(\Delta^{m};\mathbbm{R})
,

\Big({\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathbbm{R}}\big(\alpha,{\operatorname{ch}}_{\mathbbm{R}}({\mathfrak{g}})\big)(x,\varphi)\Big)(\lambda,\psi)=(x,\varphi)(\lambda,\alpha\circ\psi)=\begin{cases}0\ ,&\alpha\circ\psi\neq\varphi\ ,\\[4.0pt]
(\lambda\,x,\varphi)\ ,&\alpha\circ\psi=\varphi\ .\end{cases}

(D.14)

The simplicial graded vector space underlying
{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathbbm{R}}(C_{*}(\Delta;\mathbbm{R}),{\operatorname{ch}}_{\mathbbm{R}}({\mathfrak{g}}))
further carries an
A
-module structure.
It is given as

\displaystyle A\otimes_{\mathbbm{R}}{\operatorname{grmod}}_{\mathbbm{R}}\,{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathbbm{R}}\big(C_{*}(\Delta;\mathbbm{R}),{\operatorname{ch}}_{\mathbbm{R}}({\mathfrak{g}})\big)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow{\operatorname{grmod}}_{\mathbbm{R}}\,{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathbbm{R}}\big(C_{*}(\Delta;\mathbbm{R}),{\operatorname{ch}}_{\mathbbm{R}}({\mathfrak{g}})\big)\ ,

(D.15)

\displaystyle a\otimes_{\mathbbm{R}}(x,\varphi)

\displaystyle\longmapsto a\,(x,\varphi)=(a\,x,\varphi)\ .

(D.16)

One checks the compatibility with the differential,

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\big(a\,(x,\varphi)\big)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}(a\,x,\varphi)

(D.17)

\displaystyle=\big(\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}(a\,x),\varphi\big)-\sum_{r=0}^{l+1}\,(-1)^{r+|a|+|x|+l}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\psi\colon[l+1]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[n]\\
\psi\circ\partial_{r}=\varphi\end{subarray}}\,(a\,x,\psi)

(D.18)

\displaystyle=\big(\mathrm{d}_{A}a\,x+(-1)^{|a|}\,a\,\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}x,\varphi\big)-\sum_{r=0}^{l+1}\,(-1)^{r+|a|+|x|+l}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\psi\colon[l+1]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[n]\\
\psi\circ\partial_{r}=\varphi\end{subarray}}\,(a\,x,\psi)

(D.19)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}_{A}a\,(x,\varphi)+(-1)^{|a|}\,a\,\mathrm{d}(x,\varphi)\ .

(D.20)

That is,
{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathbbm{R}}(C_{*}(\Delta;\mathbbm{R}),{\operatorname{ch}}_{\mathbbm{R}}({\mathfrak{g}}))
is even a simplicial dg
A
-module.

We can now describe the simplicial dg
A
-module in the top-left corner in (
D.4
) as follows:
its underlying graded
\mathbbm{R}
-module in simplicial degree
n\in\mathbbm{N}_{0}
is the subcomplex

{\operatorname{Hom}}_{T_{A}}\big(C_{*}(\Delta^{n};\mathbbm{R}),{\operatorname{ch}}_{\mathbbm{R}}({\mathfrak{g}})\big)\subset{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathbbm{R}}\big(C_{*}(\Delta^{n};\mathbbm{R}),{\operatorname{ch}}_{\mathbbm{R}}({\mathfrak{g}})\big)

(D.21)

with weight decomposition

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\operatorname{grmod}}_{\mathbbm{R}}\,{\operatorname{Hom}}_{T_{A}}\big(C_{*}(\Delta^{n};\mathbbm{R}),{\operatorname{ch}}_{\mathbbm{R}}({\mathfrak{g}})\big)&=\Big\{(x_{0},\ldots,x_{n})\in{\bigoplus\limits_{i=0}^{n}}\,{\mathfrak{g}}\,\Big|\,\rho(x_{0})=\cdots=\rho(x_{n})\in T_{A}\Big\}\\
&\qquad\,\oplus\ \bigoplus_{l\in\mathbbm{N}}\ \bigoplus_{[l]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[n]}\,{\operatorname{grmod}}_{\mathbbm{R}}\,{\operatorname{ch}}_{\mathbbm{R}}\big(\ker(\rho)\big)[-l]\\[4.0pt]
&\eqqcolon\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n,(0)}\ \oplus\ \bigoplus_{l\in\mathbbm{N}}\,\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n,(l)}=:\widehat{g}_{n}\ .\end{split}

(D.22)

Here we have introduced notation for the
weight
l
graded submodule

\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n,(l)}
, for
l\in\mathbbm{N}_{0}
.

We also write

\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n,(+)}\coloneqq\bigoplus_{l\in\mathbbm{N}}\,\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n,(l)}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n,(l)}=\bigoplus_{\varphi\colon[l]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[n]}\,\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n,(\varphi)}\ .

(D.23)

We emphasise that, in general, each of the graded
\mathbbm{R}
-modules
\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n,(l)}
may still be non-zero in each degree.
The anchor map of
\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n,(l)}
, i.e. the left vertical arrow in (
D.4
), vanishes on the positive weight component and acts as

\widehat{\rho}(x_{0},\ldots,x_{n})=\rho(x_{0})=\cdots=\rho(x_{n})

(D.24)

on the weight zero part.

We check that the differential restricts to this graded
\mathbbm{R}
-submodule:
since the anchor map
\rho
of
{\mathfrak{g}}
is a morphism of dg
A
-modules, the differential automatically restricts to the positive weight component.
It remains to consider its action on elements

\xi=\sum_{i=0}^{n}\,\big(x_{i},i\colon[0]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[n]\big)\ \in\ \widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n,(0)}\ .

(D.25)

Note that since the anchor map
\rho
preserves the
{\mathfrak{g}}
-degree, the condition
\rho(x_{i})=\rho(x_{j})
for all
i,j=0,\ldots,n
implies that
|x_{i}|=|x_{j}|
for all
i,j=0,\ldots,n
.
In particular,
\xi
is a pure element, i.e. it has a well-defined degree
|\xi|=|x_{i}|
, for any
i=0,\ldots,n
.

From (
D.12
) we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathrm{d}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}\xi&=\sum_{i=0}^{n}\,\mathrm{d}\big(x_{i},i\colon[0]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[n]\big)\\[4.0pt]
&=\sum_{i=0}^{n}\,\bigg(\big(\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}x_{i},i\colon[0]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[n]\big)\\
&\hskip 56.9055pt-\sum_{j=0}^{i-1}\,(-1)^{|x_{i}|}\,\big(x_{i},0\leqslant j<i\leqslant n\big)+\sum_{j=i+1}^{n}\,(-1)^{|x_{i}|}\,\big(x_{i},0\leqslant i<j\leqslant n\big)\bigg)\\[4.0pt]
&=\underbrace{\sum_{i=0}^{n}\,\big(\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}x_{i},i\colon[0]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[n]\big)}_{\in\,\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n,(0)}}\ -\ \underbrace{\bigoplus_{\varphi\colon[1]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[n]}\,\big(\underbrace{(-1)^{|x_{\varphi(1)}|}\,x_{\varphi(1)}-(-1)^{|x_{\varphi(0)}|}\,x_{\varphi(0)}}_{\in\,\ker(\rho)},\varphi\big)}_{\in\,\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n,(+)}}\ .\end{split}

(D.26)

Since
\widehat{\rho}
vanishes on the positive weight part, it also follows that
\widehat{\rho}\colon\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}\longrightarrow T_{A}
is a morphism of dg
\mathbbm{R}
-modules.

Remark D.27

We observe that the differential restricts to maps

\mathrm{d}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}\colon\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n,(l)}\longrightarrow\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n,(l)}\oplus\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n,(l+1)}\ .

(D.28)

It has a weight preserving component and a component which increases the weight by one.

\triangleleft

Definition D.29

A fibrant
L_{\infty}
-algebroid
{\mathfrak{g}}
over
A
is a
dg abelian extension of
T_{A}
if
\ker(\rho)
is an abelian
L_{\infty}
-algebroid, i.e. it has only trivial brackets.

Lemma D.30

Let
{\mathfrak{g}}
be a dg abelian extension of
T_{A}
.
Then the action of
{\mathfrak{g}}
on
\ker(\rho)
factors through
T_{A}
.
That is,
[x_{0},v]=[x_{1},v]
for each
x_{0},x_{1}\in{\mathfrak{g}}
with
\rho(x_{0})=\rho(x_{1})
and each
v\in\ker(\rho)
.

Proof.

This follows because the bracket on
\ker(\rho)
is trivial and
x_{1}-x_{0}\in\ker(\rho)
.
∎

For a dg abelian extension
{\mathfrak{g}}
of
T_{A}
we thus obtain an induced action of
T_{A}
on
\ker(\rho)
, which we denote by

\pounds_{(-)}(-)\colon T_{A}\otimes_{\mathbbm{R}}\ker(\rho)\longrightarrow\ker(\rho)\ ,\qquad(X,v)\longmapsto\pounds_{X}v\coloneqq[\hat{X},v]\ ,

(D.31)

where
\hat{X}\in{\mathfrak{g}}
is any element with
\rho(\hat{X})=X
.

From now on we assume that
{\mathfrak{g}}
is a dg abelian extension of
T_{A}
.
Under this assumption, we now endow the simplicial dg
A
-module
{\operatorname{Hom}}_{T_{A}}(C_{*}(\Delta;\mathbbm{R}),{\operatorname{ch}}_{\mathbbm{R}}({\mathfrak{g}}))
defined in (
D.4
) with the structure of a simplicial dg Lie algebroid over
A
.
Note that the splitting

{\operatorname{Hom}}_{T_{A}}\big(C_{*}(\Delta^{n};\mathbbm{R}),{\operatorname{ch}}_{\mathbbm{R}}({\mathfrak{g}})\big)=\bigoplus_{l\in\mathbbm{N}_{0}}\ \bigoplus_{\varphi\colon[l]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[n]}\,\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n,(\varphi)}

(D.32)

persists at the level of dg
A
-modules (in particular, the
A
-action changes degrees, but preserves weights and hence these dg
\mathbbm{R}
-submodules).

For each
n\in\mathbbm{N}_{0}
, we introduce a bracket

[-,-]_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}\colon{\operatorname{Hom}}_{T_{A}}\big(C_{*}(\Delta^{n};\mathbbm{R}),{\operatorname{ch}}_{\mathbbm{R}}({\mathfrak{g}})\big)\otimes_{\mathbbm{R}}{\operatorname{Hom}}_{T_{A}}\big(C_{*}(\Delta^{n};\mathbbm{R}),{\operatorname{ch}}_{\mathbbm{R}}({\mathfrak{g}})\big)\longrightarrow{\operatorname{Hom}}_{T_{A}}\big(C_{*}(\Delta^{n};\mathbbm{R}),{\operatorname{ch}}_{\mathbbm{R}}({\mathfrak{g}})\big)

(D.33)

as follows:

(1)

The bracket returns zero whenever both its arguments have non-zero
weight
:

[\xi,\zeta]_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}=0\ ,\qquad\forall\,\xi,\zeta\in\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n,(+)}\ .

(D.34a)

(2)

Given two elements

\xi=\sum_{i=0}^{n}\,\big(x_{i},i\colon[0]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[n]\big)\ \in\ \widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n,(0)}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\zeta=\sum_{i=0}^{n}\,\big(y_{i},i\colon[0]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[n]\big)\ \in\ \widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n,(0)}

(D.34b)

of weight zero, we set

[\xi,\zeta]_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}=\sum_{i=0}^{n}\,\big([x_{i},y_{i}]_{\mathfrak{g}},\,i\colon[0]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[n]\big)\ \in\ \widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n,(0)}\ .

(D.34c)

(3)

Finally, consider a pair of elements

\xi=\sum_{i=0}^{n}\,\big(x_{i},i\colon[0]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[n]\big)\ \in\ \widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n,(0)}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\big(y,\psi\colon[l]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[n]\big)\ \in\ \widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n,(l)}\ ,

(D.34d)

where
l>0
.
It follows from (
D.22
) that there exists
X\in T_{A}
such that
\rho(x_{i})=X
, for all
i=0,\ldots,n
.
We set

\big[\xi,(y,\psi)\big]_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}=\frac{1}{l+1}\,\sum_{i=0}^{l}\,\big([x_{\psi(i)},y]_{\mathfrak{g}},\,\psi\big)=(\pounds_{X}y,\psi)\ \in\ \widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n,(l)}\ ,

(D.34e)

where we have used Lemma
D.30
and the notation introduced in (
D.31
). We also write

\big[\xi,(y,\psi)\big]_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}=(\pounds_{\widehat{\rho}(\xi)}y,\psi)\ .

(D.34f)

We extend this bracket antisymmetrically and
\mathbbm{R}
-bilinearly to a morphism

[-,-]_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}\colon{\operatorname{Hom}}_{T_{A}}\big(C_{*}(\Delta^{n};\mathbbm{R}),{\operatorname{ch}}_{\mathbbm{R}}({\mathfrak{g}})\big)\otimes_{\mathbbm{R}}{\operatorname{Hom}}_{T_{A}}\big(C_{*}(\Delta^{n};\mathbbm{R}),{\operatorname{ch}}_{\mathbbm{R}}({\mathfrak{g}})\big)\longrightarrow{\operatorname{Hom}}_{T_{A}}\big(C_{*}(\Delta^{n};\mathbbm{R}),{\operatorname{ch}}_{\mathbbm{R}}({\mathfrak{g}})\big)

(D.35)

of graded
\mathbbm{R}
-modules.
For later use we record

\big[(y,\psi),\xi\big]_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}=(-1)^{(|y|+l)\,|\xi|+1}\,\big[\xi,(y,\psi)\big]_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}=\frac{(-1)^{l\,|\xi|}}{l+1}\,\sum_{i=0}^{l}\,\big([y,x_{\psi(i)}]_{\mathfrak{g}},\,\psi\big)=(-1)^{(|y|+l)\,|\xi|+1}\,(\pounds_{\widehat{\rho}(\xi)}y,\psi)\ ,

(D.36)

in the situation of (
D.34e
).

Remark D.37

We see from (
D.34e
) that the non-zero weight dg
\mathbbm{R}
-submodule is an ideal for the bracket
[-,-]_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}
, for each
n\in\mathbbm{N}_{0}
.

\triangleleft

Lemma D.38

For each
n\in\mathbbm{N}_{0}
, the bracket (
D.34
) endows the dg
\mathbbm{R}
-module
{\operatorname{Hom}}_{T_{A}}(C_{*}(\Delta^{n};\mathbbm{R}),{\operatorname{ch}}_{\mathbbm{R}}({\mathfrak{g}}))
with the structure of a dg Lie algebra over
\mathbbm{R}
.

Proof.

The bracket is (graded) antisymmetric and bilinear by construction.

Leibniz rule.
We check the interaction of the bracket with the differential
\mathrm{d}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}
by going through the three cases in (
D.34
).
As
\mathrm{d}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}
does not lower the weight of an element by Remark
D.27
, we see that

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}[\xi,\zeta]_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}

\displaystyle=0\ ,

(D.39)

\displaystyle[\mathrm{d}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}\xi,\zeta]_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}+(-1)^{|\xi|}\,[\xi,\mathrm{d}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}\zeta]_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}

\displaystyle=0\ ,

(D.40)

whenever
\xi
and
\zeta
are elements of
\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n,(+)}
.

Next we consider elements

\xi=\sum_{i=0}^{n}\,\big(x_{i},i\colon[0]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[n]\big)\ \in\ \widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n,(0)}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\zeta=\sum_{i=0}^{n}\,\big(y_{i},i\colon[0]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[n]\big)\ \in\ \widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n,(0)}\ ,

(D.41)

and using (
D.12
) compute

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}[\xi,\zeta]_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}\,\bigg(\sum_{i=0}^{n}\big([x_{i},y_{i}]_{\mathfrak{g}},\,i\colon[0]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[n]\big)\bigg)

(D.42)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{n}\,\bigg(\big([\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}x_{i},y_{i}]_{\mathfrak{g}}+(-1)^{|\xi|}\,[x_{i},\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}y_{i}]_{\mathfrak{g}},\,i\colon[0]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[n]\big)

(D.43)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt-(-1)^{|\xi|+|\zeta|}\,\sum_{j=0}^{i-1}\,\big([x_{i},y_{i}]_{\mathfrak{g}},\,0\leqslant j<i\leqslant n\big)

(D.44)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+(-1)^{|\xi|+|\zeta|}\,\sum_{j=i+1}^{n}\,\big([x_{i},y_{i}]_{\mathfrak{g}},\,0\leqslant i<j\leqslant n\big)\bigg)

(D.45)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{n}\,\big([\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}x_{i},y_{i}]_{\mathfrak{g}}+(-1)^{|\xi|}\,[x_{i},\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}y_{i}]_{\mathfrak{g}},\,i\colon[0]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[n]\big)

(D.46)

\displaystyle\qquad\,+\sum_{0\leqslant i<j\leqslant n}\,(-1)^{|\xi|+|\zeta|}\,\big([x_{i},y_{i}]_{\mathfrak{g}}-[x_{j},y_{j}]_{\mathfrak{g}},\,0\leqslant i<j\leqslant n\big)\ ,

(D.47)

where we have used (
D.26
) and that
{\mathfrak{g}}
is a dg Lie algebra over
\mathbbm{R}
.

We also compute one half of the right-hand side of the Leibniz identity:

\displaystyle[\mathrm{d}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}\xi,\zeta]_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,r=0}^{n}\,\big[\mathrm{d}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}\big(x_{i},i\colon[0]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[n]\big)\,,\,\big(y_{r},r\colon[0]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[n]\big)\big]_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}

(D.48)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,r=0}^{n}\,\bigg(\Big[\big(\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}x_{i},i\colon[0]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[n]\big)\,,\,\big(y_{r},r\colon[0]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[n]\big)\Big]_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}

(D.49)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt-\sum_{j=0}^{i-1}\,(-1)^{|\xi|}\,\Big[\big(x_{i},0\leqslant j<i\leqslant n\big)\,,\,\big(y_{r},r\colon[0]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[n]\big)\Big]_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}

(D.50)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\sum_{j=i+1}^{n}\,(-1)^{|\xi|}\,\Big[\big(x_{i},0\leqslant i<j\leqslant n\big)\,,\,\big(y_{r},r\colon[0]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[n]\big)\Big]_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}\bigg)

(D.51)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{n}\,\big([\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}x_{i},y_{i}]_{\mathfrak{g}},i\colon[0]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[n]\big)

(D.52)

\displaystyle\qquad\,-\sum_{i=0}^{n}\ \sum_{j=0}^{i-1}\,(-1)^{|\xi|+|\zeta|}\,\frac{1}{2}\,\big([x_{i},y_{j}]_{\mathfrak{g}}+[x_{i},y_{i}]_{\mathfrak{g}},0\leqslant j<i\leqslant n\big)

(D.53)

\displaystyle\qquad\,+\sum_{i=0}^{n}\ \sum_{j=i+1}^{n}\,(-1)^{|\xi|+|\zeta|}\,\frac{1}{2}\,\big([x_{i},y_{i}]_{\mathfrak{g}}+[x_{i},y_{j}]_{\mathfrak{g}},0\leqslant i<j\leqslant n\big)

(D.54)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{n}\,\big([\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}x_{i},y_{i}]_{\mathfrak{g}},i\colon[0]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[n]\big)

(D.55)

\displaystyle\qquad\,+\sum_{0\leqslant i<j\leqslant n}\,\frac{(-1)^{|\xi|+|\zeta|}}{2}\,\big([x_{i},y_{i}]_{\mathfrak{g}}+[x_{i},y_{j}]_{\mathfrak{g}}-[x_{j},y_{i}]_{\mathfrak{g}}-[x_{j},y_{j}]_{\mathfrak{g}},0\leqslant i<j\leqslant n\big)\ .

(D.56)

The degree
|\zeta|
which appears in the sign factor in the third step here arises since we compute a bracket of the type (
D.34e
), but with the order of arguments reversed:
the first argument is of weight one and the second of weight zero.
To compute the bracket in this order, we recall the formula (
D.9
) for the degree in
{\operatorname{Hom}}_{T_{A}}(C_{*}(\Delta^{n};\mathbbm{R}),{\operatorname{ch}}_{\mathbbm{R}}({\mathfrak{g}}))
and compute

\displaystyle\sum_{r=0}^{n}\,\Big[\big(x_{i},0\leqslant i<j\leqslant n\big)\,,\,\big(y_{r},r\colon[0]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[n]\big)\Big]_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}

(D.57)

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt=\sum_{r=0}^{n}\,(-1)^{(|\xi|+1)\,|\zeta|+1}\,\Big[\big(y_{r},r\colon[0]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[n]\big)\,,\,\big(x_{i},0\leqslant i<j\leqslant n\big)\Big]_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}

(D.58)

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt=(-1)^{(|\xi|+1)\,|\zeta|+1}\,\tfrac{1}{2}\,\big([y_{i},x_{i}]_{\mathfrak{g}}+[y_{j},x_{i}]_{\mathfrak{g}},0\leqslant i<j\leqslant n\big)

(D.59)

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt=(-1)^{|\zeta|}\,\tfrac{1}{2}\,\big([x_{i},y_{i}]_{\mathfrak{g}}+[x_{i},y_{j}]_{\mathfrak{g}},0\leqslant i<j\leqslant n\big)\ .

(D.60)

Noting that the degree of
\zeta=\sum_{i=0}^{n}\,(y_{i},i\colon[0]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[n])\in\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n,(0)}
is
|\zeta|=|y_{i}|
, for each
i=0,\ldots,n
, we also compute the other half of the right-hand side of the Leibniz identity:

\displaystyle(-1)^{|\xi|}\,[\xi,\mathrm{d}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}\zeta]_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{n}\,(-1)^{|\xi|}\,\big([x_{i},\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}y_{i}]_{\mathfrak{g}},i\colon[0]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[n]\big)

(D.61)

\displaystyle\qquad\,-\sum_{i=0}^{n}\ \sum_{j=0}^{i}\,\frac{(-1)^{|\xi|+|\zeta|}}{2}\,\big([x_{j},y_{i}]_{\mathfrak{g}}+[x_{i},y_{i}]_{\mathfrak{g}},0\leqslant j<i\leqslant n\big)

(D.62)

\displaystyle\qquad\,+\sum_{i=0}^{n}\ \sum_{j=i+1}^{n}\,\frac{(-1)^{|\xi|+|\zeta|}}{2}\,\big([x_{i},y_{i}]_{\mathfrak{g}}+[x_{j},y_{i}]_{\mathfrak{g}},0\leqslant i<j\leqslant n\big)

(D.63)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{n}\,(-1)^{|\xi|}\,\big([x_{i},\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}y_{i}]_{\mathfrak{g}},i\colon[0]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[n]\big)

(D.64)

\displaystyle\qquad\,+\sum_{0\leqslant i<j\leqslant n}\,\frac{(-1)^{|\xi|+|\zeta|}}{2}\,\big([x_{i},y_{i}]_{\mathfrak{g}}+[x_{j},y_{i}]_{\mathfrak{g}}-[x_{i},y_{j}]_{\mathfrak{g}}-[x_{j},y_{j}]_{\mathfrak{g}},0\leqslant i<j\leqslant n\big)\ .

(D.65)

Combining the above results, we indeed find that the Leibniz rule

\mathrm{d}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}[\xi,\zeta]_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}=[\mathrm{d}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}\xi,\zeta]_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}+(-1)^{|\xi|}\,[\xi,\mathrm{d}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}\zeta]_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}

(D.66)

is satisfied, for all

\xi=\sum_{i=0}^{n}\,\big(x_{i},i\colon[0]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[n]\big)\ \in\ \widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n,(0)}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\zeta=\sum_{i=0}^{n}\,\big(y_{i},i\colon[0]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[n]\big)\ \in\ \widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n,(0)}\ .

(D.67)

It remains to check the Leibniz rule for pairs of the form

\xi=\sum_{i=0}^{n}\,\big(x_{i},i\colon[0]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[n]\big)\ \in\ \widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n,(0)}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\big(y,\varphi\colon[l]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[n]\big)\ \in\ \widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n,(l)}\ ,

(D.68)

with
l>0
.
We find

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}\big[\xi,(y,\varphi)\big]_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{l+1}\,\sum_{i=0}^{l}\,\mathrm{d}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}\big([x_{\varphi(i)},y]_{\mathfrak{g}},\,\varphi\big)

(D.69)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{l+1}\,\sum_{i=0}^{l}\,\big(\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}[x_{\varphi(i)},y]_{\mathfrak{g}},\varphi\big)

(D.70)

\displaystyle\qquad\,-\frac{(-1)^{|\xi|+|y|+l}}{l+1}\,\sum_{i=0}^{l}\ \sum_{r=0}^{l+1}\,(-1)^{r}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\psi\colon[l+1]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[n]\\
\psi\circ\partial_{r}=\varphi\end{subarray}}\,\big([x_{\varphi(i)},y]_{\mathfrak{g}},\,\psi\big)

(D.71)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{l+1}\,\sum_{i=0}^{l}\,\big([\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}x_{\varphi(i)},y]_{\mathfrak{g}}+(-1)^{|x_{i}|}\,[x_{\varphi(i)},\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}y]_{\mathfrak{g}},\varphi\big)

(D.72)

\displaystyle\qquad\,-\frac{(-1)^{|\xi|+|y|+l}}{l+1}\,\sum_{i=0}^{l}\ \sum_{r=0}^{l+1}\,(-1)^{r}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\psi\colon[l+1]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[n]\\
\psi\circ\partial_{r}=\varphi\end{subarray}}\,\big([x_{\varphi(i)},y]_{\mathfrak{g}},\,\psi\big)

(D.73)

\displaystyle=(\pounds_{\mathrm{d}_{\operatorname{Hom}}\widehat{\rho}(\xi)}\,y+(-1)^{|\xi|}\,\pounds_{\widehat{\rho}(\xi)}\,\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}y,\varphi)

(D.74)

\displaystyle\qquad\,-(-1)^{|\xi|+|y|+l}\,\sum_{r=0}^{l+1}\,(-1)^{r}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\psi\colon[l+1]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[n]\\
\psi\circ\partial_{r}=\varphi\end{subarray}}\,(\pounds_{\widehat{\rho}(\xi)}\,y,\,\psi\big)\ .

(D.75)

In the last step we have used the notation from (
D.34f
).

On the other hand, using (
D.34e
) and (
D.34a
) we find

\displaystyle\big[\mathrm{d}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}\xi,(y,\varphi)\big]_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{n}\,\big[(\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}x_{i},i)\,,\,(y,\varphi)\big]_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}=\frac{1}{l+1}\,\sum_{i=0}^{l}\,\big([\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}x_{\varphi(i)},y]_{\mathfrak{g}},\,\varphi\big)=(\pounds_{\mathrm{d}_{\operatorname{Hom}}\widehat{\rho}(\xi)}\,y,\varphi)\ ,

(D.76)

where in the first step we have used that the bracket vanishes on
\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n,(+)}\otimes_{\mathbbm{R}}\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n,(+)}
.
Using (
D.12
), we also compute

\displaystyle(-1)^{|\xi|}\,\big[\xi,\mathrm{d}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}(y,\varphi)\big]_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{|\xi|}\,\big[\xi,(\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}y,\varphi)\big]_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}-(-1)^{|\xi|+|y|+l}\,\sum_{r=0}^{l+1}\,(-1)^{r}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\psi\colon[l+1]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[n]\\
\psi\circ\partial_{r}=\varphi\end{subarray}}\,\big[\xi,(y,\psi)\big]_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}

(D.77)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{|\xi|}\,\sum_{i=0}^{l}\,\frac{1}{l+1}\,\big([x_{\varphi(i)},\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}y]_{\mathfrak{g}},\varphi\big)

(D.78)

\displaystyle\qquad\,-(-1)^{|\xi|+|y|+l}\,\sum_{r=0}^{l+1}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\psi\colon[l+1]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[n]\\
\psi\circ\partial_{r}=\varphi\end{subarray}}\ \sum_{i=0}^{l+1}\,\frac{(-1)^{r}}{l+2}\,\big([x_{\psi(i)},y]_{\mathfrak{g}},\psi\big)

(D.79)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{|\xi|}\,(\pounds_{\widehat{\rho}(\xi)}\,\mathrm{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}y,\varphi)-(-1)^{|\xi|+|y|+l}\,\sum_{r=0}^{l+1}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\psi\colon[l+1]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[n]\\
\psi\circ\partial_{r}=\varphi\end{subarray}}\,(-1)^{r}\,(\pounds_{\widehat{\rho}(\xi)}\,y,\psi)\ .

(D.80)

Thus we see that the Leibniz rule is satisfied in this case as well.

Jacobi identity.
The Jacobi identity on triples
\xi_{0},\xi_{1},\xi_{2}\in\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}
of weight zero follows readily from (
D.34c
) and the Jacobi identity for
[-,-]_{\mathfrak{g}}
.
By (
D.34a
) and (
D.34e
), the Jacobi identity is trivially satisfied on triples of elements in
\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}
where at least two elements lie in the positive weight submodule
\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n,(+)}
.
It thus remains to check the Jacobi identity on elments
\xi,\zeta\in\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n,(0)}
and
(y,\varphi)\in\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n,(+)}
.
We compute

\displaystyle\big[\xi,[\zeta,(z,\varphi)]_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}\big]_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}

\displaystyle=\big[\xi,(\pounds_{\widehat{\rho}(\zeta)}z,\varphi)\big]_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}=\big(\pounds_{\widehat{\rho}(\xi)}(\pounds_{\widehat{\rho}(\zeta)}z),\varphi\big)\ ,

(D.81)

\displaystyle\big[[\xi,\zeta]_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}},(z,\varphi)\big]_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}

\displaystyle=\Big[\sum_{i=0}^{n}\,\big([x_{i},y_{i}]_{\mathfrak{g}},i\big),(z,\varphi)\Big]_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}=(\pounds_{[\widehat{\rho}(\xi),\widehat{\rho}(\zeta)]}\,z,\varphi)\ ,

(D.82)

\displaystyle(-1)^{|\zeta|\,|\xi|}\,\big[\zeta,[\xi,(z,\varphi)]_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}\big]_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{|\zeta|\,|\xi|}\,\big(\pounds_{\widehat{\rho}(\zeta)}(\pounds_{\widehat{\rho}(\xi)}z),\varphi\big)\ .

(D.83)

The Jacobi identity then follows from that of
[-,-]_{\mathfrak{g}}
; it implies that the action map
{\mathfrak{g}}\otimes_{\mathbbm{R}}\ker(\rho)\longrightarrow\ker(\rho)
is compatible with the bracket on
{\mathfrak{g}}
, that is

\pounds_{[\widehat{\rho}(\xi),\widehat{\rho}(\zeta)]}\,z=\pounds_{\widehat{\rho}(\xi)}\,\pounds_{\widehat{\rho}(\zeta)}z-(-1)^{|\xi|\,|\zeta|}\,\pounds_{\widehat{\rho}(\zeta)}\,\pounds_{\widehat{\rho}(\xi)}z\ ,

(D.84)

as required.
∎

Lemma D.85

The
A
-module structure on
\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}
inherited from that on
{\mathfrak{g}}
via the pullback (
D.4
) is compatible with the anchor map and brackets on
\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}
, for each
n\in\mathbbm{N}_{0}
.
That is, for each
n\in\mathbbm{N}_{0}
, the dgla
\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}
over
\mathbbm{R}
carries the structure of a dg Lie algebroid over
A
.

Proof.

The
A
-action reads as

a\,(y,\varphi)=(a\,y,\varphi)\qquad,\qquad a\,\xi=a\,\sum_{i=0}^{n}\,\big(x_{i},i\colon[0]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[n]\big)=\sum_{i=0}^{n}\,\big(a\,x_{i},i\colon[0]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[n]\big)\ .

(D.86)

Here the
A
-action inside the brackets is the original
A
-action on
{\mathfrak{g}}
.
Since
\widehat{\rho}=0
in positive weight, there is no compatibility condition to check in that case.
In weight zero we check that

\displaystyle\widehat{\rho}(a\,\xi)

\displaystyle=\widehat{\rho}\Big(\sum_{i=0}^{n}\,\big(a\,x_{i},i\colon[0]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[n]\big)\Big)=a\,\rho(x_{0})=a\,\widehat{\rho}(\xi)\ .

(D.87)

It remains to check that the bracket on
\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}
satisfies the anchored Leibniz rule with respect to the
A
-action.
In the case where both arguments of the bracket have positive weight, the bracket is zero and there is nothing to check.
Thus let
a\in A
,
\xi\in\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n,(0)}
and
(y,\varphi)\in\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n,(l)}
.
Then

\displaystyle\big[\xi,a\,(y,\varphi)\big]_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}

\displaystyle=\big(\pounds_{\widehat{\rho}(\xi)}(a\,y),\varphi\big)

(D.88)

\displaystyle=(a\,\pounds_{\widehat{\rho}(\xi)}y+\pounds_{\widehat{\rho}(\xi)}(a)\,y,\varphi)

(D.89)

\displaystyle=a\,(\pounds_{\widehat{\rho}(\xi)}y,\varphi)+\pounds_{\widehat{\rho}(\xi)}(a)\,(y,\varphi)\ ,

(D.90)

where we have used that the bracket
[-,-]_{\mathfrak{g}}
on
{\mathfrak{g}}
satisfies the anchored Leibniz rule with respect to the
A
-action on
{\mathfrak{g}}
.
We also check that

\displaystyle\big[(y,\varphi),a\,\xi\big]_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{(|a|+|\xi|)\,(|y|+l)+1}\,(\pounds_{a\,\widehat{\rho}(\xi)}y,\varphi)

(D.91)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{(|a|+|\xi|)\,(|y|+l)+1}\,(a\,\pounds_{\widehat{\rho}(\xi)}y,\varphi)

(D.92)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{(|a|+|\xi|)\,(|y|+l)+1}\,a\,(\pounds_{\widehat{\rho}(\xi)}y,\varphi)

(D.93)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{|a|\,(|y|+l)}\,a\,\big[(y,\varphi),\xi\big]_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}\ ,

(D.94)

where we have used
\widehat{\rho}(y,\varphi)=0
.

Finally, we consider the case of two elements
\xi,\zeta\in\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n,(0)}
.
We compute

\displaystyle[\xi,a\,\zeta]_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{n}\,\big([x_{i},a\,y_{i}]_{\mathfrak{g}},\,i\colon[0]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[n]\big)

(D.95)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{n}\,\big(a\,[x_{i},y_{i}]_{\mathfrak{g}}+\pounds_{\rho(x_{i})}(a)\,y_{i},\,i\colon[0]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[n]\big)

(D.96)

\displaystyle=a\,[\xi,\zeta]_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}+\pounds_{\widehat{\rho}(\xi)}(a)\,\zeta\ .

(D.97)

Here we have used
\rho(x_{0})=\cdots=\rho(x_{n})=\widehat{\rho}(\xi)
.
∎

Lemma D.98

The dg Lie algebroids
\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}
, for
n\in\mathbbm{N}_{0}
, assemble into a simplicial dg Lie algebroid over
A
.
That is, the structure of a dg Lie algebroid over
A
on each
\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}
, for
n\in\mathbbm{N}_{0}
, is compatible with the simplicial structure maps induced by the pullback (
D.4
).

Proof.

Let
\alpha\colon[m]\longrightarrow[n]
be a morphism in
{{\mathbbe{\Delta}}}
.
We denote the morphism in
{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}
induced by
\alpha
as
\alpha^{*}\colon\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}\longrightarrow\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{m}
(it is described explicitly in (
D.13
)).
From the description of the
A
-action it is evident that
\alpha^{*}
is a morphism of
A
-modules.
Furthermore,
\alpha^{*}
is compatible with the differential by construction of the cochain complex underlying
\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}
as a pullback in
{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{\mathbbm{R}}^{\mathrm{dg}}
(see (
D.4
); however, this compatibility can also be seen directly, since
\alpha^{*}
acts on
\varphi
by postcomposition and the differential by precomposition).

Let
\xi=\sum_{i=0}^{n}\,(x_{i},i)
be an element in
\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n,(0)}
. It will be helpful to observe that

\alpha^{*}\xi=\alpha^{*}\bigg(\sum_{i=0}^{n}\,(x_{i},i)\bigg)=\sum_{i=0}^{n}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\colon[0]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[m]\\
\alpha(j)=i\end{subarray}}\,(x_{i},j)=\sum_{i=0}^{n}\ \sum_{j\in\alpha^{-1}(i)}\,(x_{i},j)=\sum_{j=0}^{m}\,(x_{\alpha(j)},j)\ .

(D.99)

Using this identity, we can check the compatibility of
\alpha^{*}
with brackets between elements of weight zero and of positive weight.
We compute

\displaystyle\big[\alpha^{*}\xi,\alpha^{*}(y,\varphi)\big]_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{m}}

\displaystyle=\bigg[\sum_{j=0}^{m}\,(x_{\alpha(j)},j),\,\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\psi\colon[l]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[m]\\
\alpha\circ\psi=\varphi\end{subarray}}\,(y,\psi)\bigg]_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{m}}

(D.100)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\psi\colon[l]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[m]\\
\alpha\circ\psi=\varphi\end{subarray}}\ \sum_{j=0}^{m}\,\big[(x_{\alpha(j)},j),(y,\psi)\big]_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{m}}

(D.101)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\psi\colon[l]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[m]\\
\alpha\circ\psi=\varphi\end{subarray}}\,(\pounds_{\widehat{\rho}(\alpha^{*}\xi)}y,\psi)

(D.102)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\psi\colon[l]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[m]\\
\alpha\circ\psi=\varphi\end{subarray}}\,(\pounds_{\widehat{\rho}(\xi)}y,\psi)

(D.103)

\displaystyle=\alpha^{*}\big[\xi,(y,\varphi)\big]_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}\ ,

(D.104)

where we used
\widehat{\rho}(\alpha^{*}\xi)=\widehat{\rho}(\xi)
, which follows either directly or by construction of
\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}
via the pullback in (
D.4
).

Next we check the compatibility of
\alpha^{*}
with brackets between two elements of weight zero.
On the one hand, we compute

\displaystyle\big[\alpha^{*}\xi,\alpha^{*}\zeta\big]_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{m}}=\bigg[\sum_{j=0}^{m}\,(x_{\alpha(j)},j),\,\sum_{s=0}^{m}\,(y_{\alpha(s)},s)\bigg]_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{m}}=\sum_{j=0}^{m}\,\big([x_{\alpha(j)},y_{\alpha(j)}]_{\mathfrak{g}},\,j\big)\ .

(D.105)

On the other hand, we obtain

\displaystyle\alpha^{*}[\xi,\zeta]_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n}}=\alpha^{*}\bigg(\sum_{i=0}^{n}\,\big([x_{i},y_{i}]_{\mathfrak{g}},\,i\big)\bigg)=\sum_{j=0}^{m}\,\big([x_{\alpha(j)},y_{\alpha(j)}]_{\mathfrak{g}},\,j\big)\ ,

(D.106)

as required.
∎

To summarise, we have shown

Proposition D.107

For each dg abelian extension
{\mathfrak{g}}
of
T_{A}
, the simplicial dg
A
-module defined by the pullback (
D.4
) carries the structure of a simplicial dg Lie algebroid
\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}
over
A
, with brackets as defined in (
D.34
).

Next we will enhance this result to

Theorem D.108

For each dg abelian extension
{\mathfrak{g}}
of
T_{A}
, the simplicial dg Lie algebroid
\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}
is a fibrant simplicial resolution of
{\mathfrak{g}}
in the semi-model structure on
L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}
.

Corollary D.109

Let
\mathfrak{h}
be an
A
-cofibrant
L_{\infty}
-algebroid over
A
.
Suppose that
{\mathfrak{g}}
is a dg abelian extension of
T_{A}
.
Then the derived space of morphisms
\mathfrak{h}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{g}}
in
L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}
can be computed as

\mathbf{R}{\operatorname{Hom}}_{L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}}(\mathfrak{h},{\mathfrak{g}})\cong L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}(Q\mathfrak{h},\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}})\cong L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}(\mathfrak{h},\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}})\ ,

(D.110)

where the isomorphisms are in the homotopy category
\operatorname{Ho}{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{et}_{\hskip-0.85355pt\Delta}}
.

Proof.

This follows by combining Remark
4.17
, Proposition
D.107
and Theorem
D.108
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
D.108
.

We need to check that

(1)

the functor
\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}\colon{{\mathbbe{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{op}}\longrightarrow L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{{\mathrm{dg}}}
is Reedy fibrant, and

(2)

the canonical morphism
\mathsf{c}\,{\mathfrak{g}}\longrightarrow\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}
from the constant simplicial object on
{\mathfrak{g}}
is an objectwise weak equivalence.

We start with (1), the Reedy fibrancy.
By
[
69
, Theorems 3.1 and 3.3]
there is a sequence of Quillen adjunctions

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{\mathbbm{R}}^{\mathrm{dg}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{\mathbbm{R}}^{\mathrm{dg}})_{/T_{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}})_{/T_{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\perp}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\perp}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\perp}

(D.111)

where the right adjoints are forgetful functors.
Here
{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{\mathbbm{R}}^{\mathrm{dg}}
is endowed with the projective model structure, and each of the other (semi-)model structures is transferred from this projective model structure along the respective right adjoints.
However, the leftmost right adjoint does not preserve final objects.

It thus suffices to check that the underlying functor

U\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}\colon{{\mathbbe{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{op}}\longrightarrow({\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{\mathbbm{R}}^{\mathrm{dg}})_{/T_{A}}

is Reedy fibrant.
This is the functor sending
[n]\in{{\mathbbe{\Delta}}}
to the morphism

{\operatorname{ch}}_{\mathbbm{R}}(\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}})={\operatorname{Hom}}_{T_{A}}\big(C_{*}(\Delta^{n};\mathbbm{R}),{\operatorname{ch}}_{\mathbbm{R}}({\mathfrak{g}})\big)\longrightarrow T_{A}

(D.112)

which appears as the left-hand vertical arrow in the pullback square (
D.4
).
We thus have to check that the matching map

(U\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}})_{n}\longrightarrow M_{n}U\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}\times_{M_{n}\mathsf{c}\,{\mathrm{id}}_{T_{A}}}{\mathrm{id}}_{T_{A}}

(D.113)

is a fibration in
({\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{\mathbbm{R}}^{\mathrm{dg}})_{/T_{A}}
, for each
n\in\mathbbm{N}_{0}
.

The indexing category for cospans is connected, and so the functor
({\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{\mathbbm{R}}^{\mathrm{dg}})_{/T_{A}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{\mathbbm{R}}^{\mathrm{dg}}
preserves and reflects pullbacks.
Since the model structure on
({\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{\mathbbm{R}}^{\mathrm{dg}})_{/T_{A}}
is transferred from that on
{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{\mathbbm{R}}^{\mathrm{dg}}
, we equivalently need to check that the matching map

{\operatorname{ch}}_{\mathbbm{R}}(\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{n})\longrightarrow M_{n}{\operatorname{ch}}_{\mathbbm{R}}(\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}})\times_{M_{n}\mathsf{c}\,T_{A}}T_{A}

(D.114)

is a fibration in
{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{\mathbbm{R}}^{\mathrm{dg}}
, for each
n\in\mathbbm{N}_{0}
.
In other words, we are checking that the left-hand vertical arrow in (
D.4
) is a Reedy fibration in
{\operatorname{Fun}}({{\mathbbe{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{op}},{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{\mathbbm{R}}^{\mathrm{dg}})
.

The stability of fibrations under pullbacks will imply this, provided we can show that the right-hand vertical morphism in (
D.4
) is a Reedy fibration in
{\operatorname{Fun}}({{\mathbbe{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{op}},{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{\mathbbm{R}}^{\mathrm{dg}})
.
There are canonical isomorphisms

M_{n}{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathbbm{R}}\big(C_{*}(\Delta;\mathbbm{R}),{\operatorname{ch}}_{\mathbbm{R}}({\mathfrak{g}})\big)\cong{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathbbm{R}}\big(L_{n}C_{*}(\Delta;\mathbbm{R}),{\operatorname{ch}}_{\mathbbm{R}}({\mathfrak{g}})\big)

(D.115)

where the matching object
M_{n}
is computed for a
{{\mathbbe{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{op}}
-shaped diagram, and the latching object
L_{n}
is computed for the
{{\mathbbe{\Delta}}}
-shaped diagram
C_{*}(\Delta;\mathbbm{R})\colon{{\mathbbe{\Delta}}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{\mathbbm{R}}^{\mathrm{dg}}
.
Property (1) will now follow if we can show that the pullback product map induced by the commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathbbm{R}}\big(C_{*}(\Delta^{n};\mathbbm{R}),{\operatorname{ch}}_{\mathbbm{R}}({\mathfrak{g}})\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathbbm{R}}\big(L_{n}C_{*}(\Delta;\mathbbm{R}),{\operatorname{ch}}_{\mathbbm{R}}({\mathfrak{g}})\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathbbm{R}}\big(C_{*}(\Delta^{n};\mathbbm{R}),{\operatorname{ch}}_{\mathbbm{R}}(T_{A})\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathbbm{R}}\big(L_{n}C_{*}(\Delta;\mathbbm{R}),{\operatorname{ch}}_{\mathbbm{R}}(T_{A})\big)}

(D.116)

is a fibration, for each
n\in\mathbbm{N}_{0}
.

Since
{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{\mathbbm{R}}^{\mathrm{dg}}
is a symmetric monoidal model category, this will follow if the anchor map
\rho\colon{\mathfrak{g}}\longrightarrow T_{A}
is a fibration and the canonical morphism
C_{*}(\Delta^{n};\mathbbm{R})\longrightarrow L_{n}C_{*}(\Delta;\mathbbm{R})
is a cofibration in
{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{\mathbbm{R}}^{\mathrm{dg}}
.
The first condition is satisfied by the assumption that
{\mathfrak{g}}
is fibrant.
For the second condition, recall that there is a sequence of Quillen adjunctions

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{et}_{\hskip-0.85355pt\Delta}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Fun}}({{\mathbbe{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{op}},{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{b}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{\mathbbm{Z},\leqslant 0}^{\mathrm{dg}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{\mathbbm{Z}}^{\mathrm{dg}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{\mathbbm{R}}^{\mathrm{dg}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\perp}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbbm{Z}[-]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\perp}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varGamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\perp}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{\leqslant 0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\perp}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)\otimes_{\mathbbm{Z}}\mathbbm{R}}

(D.117)

The composition of the left adjoints is the singular chains functor with coefficients in
\mathbbm{R}
,

C_{*}(-;\mathbbm{R})\colon{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{et}_{\hskip-0.85355pt\Delta}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{\mathbbm{R}}^{\mathrm{dg}}\ ,

(D.118)

where we view the singular chain complex as an unbounded chain complex.
It can be written as a composition of left Quillen functors and so is a left Quillen functor itself.
The cosimplicial diagram
\Delta\colon{{\mathbbe{\Delta}}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{et}_{\hskip-0.85355pt\Delta}}
,
[n]\longmapsto\Delta^{n}
is Reedy cofibrant and, moreover, a Reedy cofibrant resolution of the
0
-simplex
\Delta^{0}
in
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{et}_{\hskip-0.85355pt\Delta}}

[
47
, Corollaries 15.9.11 and 15.9.12]
.
By
[
47
, Proposition 15.4.1]
it follows that the functor
C_{*}(\Delta;\mathbbm{R})\colon{{\mathbbe{\Delta}}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{\mathbbm{R}}^{\mathrm{dg}}
is Reedy cofibrant.
That is, the latching map
C_{*}(\Delta^{n};\mathbbm{R})\longrightarrow L_{n}C_{*}(\Delta;\mathbbm{R})
is a cofibration in
{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{\mathbbm{R}}^{\mathrm{dg}}
, for each
n\in\mathbbm{N}_{0}
.
This completes the proof of (1), i.e. that
\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}\colon{{\mathbbe{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{op}}\longrightarrow L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}
is Reedy fibrant.

It remains to show (2), i.e. that the canonical morphism
\mathsf{c}\,{\mathfrak{g}}\longrightarrow\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}
is an objectwise weak equivalence of simplicial diagrams in
L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}
.
Since the semi-model structure on
L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{A}^{\mathrm{dg}}
is transferred from the projective model structure on
{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{\mathbbm{R}}^{\mathrm{dg}}
, it suffices to check this at the level of the underlying dg
\mathbbm{R}
-modules.
We again use properties of the pullback square (
D.4
).
The bottom horizontal morphism in that diagram is an objectwise weak equivalence of functors
{{\mathbbe{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{op}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{\mathbbm{R}}^{\mathrm{dg}}
.
We have shown above that the right-hand vertical morphism in (
D.4
) is a Reedy fibration.
8
8

8

In fact, for this part of the proof it suffices that it is a fibration in the projective model structure on
{\operatorname{Fun}}({{\mathbbe{\Delta}}}^{\mathrm{op}},{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{\mathbbm{R}}^{\mathrm{dg}})
, which holds since
{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{\mathbbm{R}}^{\mathrm{dg}}
is a monoidal model category and the anchor map
\rho\colon{\mathfrak{g}}\longrightarrow T_{A}
is a fibration by assumption.

Since every object in
{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{\mathbbm{R}}^{\mathrm{dg}}
is fibrant, the square (
D.4
) is homotopy cartesian in
{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{\mathbbm{R}}^{\mathrm{dg}}
.
We infer that the top horizontal morphism in (
D.4
) is an objectwise weak equivalence as well.

Now consider the augmentation of the diagram (
D.4
) given by

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{c}\,{\operatorname{ch}}_{\mathbbm{R}}({\mathfrak{g}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Hom}}_{T_{A}}\big(C_{*}(\Delta;\mathbbm{R}),{\operatorname{ch}}_{\mathbbm{R}}({\mathfrak{g}})\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathbbm{R}}\big(C_{*}(\Delta;\mathbbm{R}),{\operatorname{ch}}_{\mathbbm{R}}({\mathfrak{g}})\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{c}\,{\operatorname{ch}}_{\mathbbm{R}}(T_{A})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathbbm{R}}\big(C_{*}(\Delta;\mathbbm{R}),{\operatorname{ch}}_{\mathbbm{R}}(T_{A})\big)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\sim)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\rho}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

(D.119)

The left-hand bent arrow is the anchor map of
{\mathfrak{g}}
, and the top bent arrow arises from the collapsing map
\Delta\longrightarrow\mathsf{c}\,\Delta^{0}
.
The dashed arrow is induced by the universal property of the pullback.
It is an objectwise weak equivalence in
{\mathscr{M}\mathrm{od}}_{\mathbbm{R}}^{\mathrm{dg}}
by the two-out-of-three property.
∎

Appendix E
Proof of Theorem
5.55

Proof of Theorem
5.55
.

As a consequence of Remark
4.17
and Example
4.20
we can compute the space
{\mathrm{Con}}_{p}({\check{C}}{\mathrm{At}}(g))
as

{\mathrm{Con}}_{p}\big({\check{C}}{\mathrm{At}}(g)\big)\simeq L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{M}\big(Q^{(p)}TM,\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}\big)\ ,

(E.1)

where
\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}
is any fibrant simplicial resolution of
{\check{C}}{\mathrm{At}}(g)
in
L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{M}^{\mathrm{dg}}
.
In particular, since
{\check{C}}{\mathrm{At}}(g)
is a dg abelian extension of
TM
, we can use the simplicial resolution of
{\check{C}}{\mathrm{At}}(g)
derived in Theorem
D.108
.

Thus, from now on, let
{\mathfrak{g}}={\check{C}}{\mathrm{At}}(g)
and let
\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}
be that fibrant simplicial resolution of
{\mathfrak{g}}
.
We will show that there is a canonical isomorphism of simplicial sets

{\mathrm{Con}}_{p}(g)\cong L_{\infty}{\mathscr{A}\mathrm{gd}}_{M}\big(Q^{(p)}TM,\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}\big)\ .

(E.2)

The kernel of the anchor map
\rho:{\check{C}}{\mathrm{At}}(g)\longrightarrow TM
is given by

\ker(\rho)=\tau^{\leqslant 0}\big(C^{\infty}({\check{C}}_{*}\,\pazocal{U}),(-1)^{n}\,\check{\delta}\big)\ .

(E.3)

Recall the notation

from Definition
5.19
and that the elements of

(see Lemma
4.10
).

We first consider the case
p\leqslant n
and compute the set of
l
-simplices, i.e. the set

(E.4)

using the description of
\infty
-morphisms of

-algebroids in Remark
2.13
and the paragraphs preceding it.
To that end, we set

E_{n-1,l}(g)\coloneqq\bigg\{\bigoplus_{i=0}^{l}\,(X_{i},f_{i})\,\bigg|\,(X_{i},f_{i})\in E_{n-1}(g)\ ,\ X_{i}=X_{j}\ \forall\,i,j\in[l]\bigg\}\ .

.

Figure 1:
Maps from
\overline{{\mathrm{Sym}}}{}_{\mathbbm{R}}^{\leqslant p}(TM[1])
to the cochain complex underlying the dg Lie algebroid

of the fibrant simplicial resolution

of

.

We will be referring to Diagram (

) throughout this proof.
The vector spaces in the right-hand column are obtained from (
D.22
).
Note that, whilst each column of this diagram is a complex, we do not aim to construct a cochain morphism, but rather a Maurer–Cartan element in the mapping
L_{\infty}
-algebra; that is, Diagram (
E.6
) is
not
a commutative diagram.
Each horizontal arrow is labelled by a
C^{\infty}(M)
-linear map, which decomposes as a sum of maps into each component of the direct sum in its codomain, that is,

A^{(q)}=\bigoplus_{r\in\mathbbm{N}_{0}}\ \bigoplus_{\varphi\colon[r]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[l]}\,A^{(q)}_{\varphi}\ .

(E.7)

Reassembling these data by
\varphi
, instead of by form degree
q
, yields tuples
\pazocal{A}_{\varphi}=(A^{(1)}_{\varphi},\ldots,A^{(p-r)}_{\varphi})
indexed by all injective maps with codomain
[l]
in
{{\mathbbe{\Delta}}}
.
Our goal is to show that these tuples are precisely the same as the tuples of differential forms making up
l
-simplices in
{\mathrm{Con}}_{p}(g)
as detailed in Section
5.2
.

Remark
2.13
(2) amounts to the top horizontal map having the identity on
TM
in its first component, as indicated.
It remains to compute the conditions on the linear map imposed by the Maurer–Cartan condition in Remark
2.13
(1).
We check this at each level in
\overline{{\mathrm{Sym}}}{}^{\leqslant p}_{\mathbbm{R}}(TM[1])
.
Note that

A^{(q)}_{\varphi}=0\qquad\text{whenever}\quad n-(q+r)<0\ ,

(E.8)

where
\varphi\colon[r]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[l]
.

The fact that the weight zero component of the top non-trivial horizontal morphism
({\mathrm{id}}_{TM},A^{(1)})
in (
E.6
) is required to take values in
E_{n,l}(g)
is equivalent to the condition

-\check{\delta}A^{(1)}_{i}(X)+\mathrm{d}\log(g)=0\ ,\qquad\forall\,i\in\{0,\ldots,l\}\,,\,X\in TM\ .

(E.9)

\text{\Large$\wedge$}_{\mathbbm{R}}^{1}TM
.
Let
X_{1}\in TM
be a vector field.
It is sent to an element of the form

\bigoplus_{i=0}^{l}\,\big(X_{1},A^{(1)}_{i}(X_{1})\big)\ \oplus\ \bigoplus_{r\geqslant 1}\ \bigoplus_{\varphi\colon[r]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[l]}\,A^{(1)}_{\varphi}(X_{1})\ .

(E.10)

Here the coproduct and the differential in
\overline{{\mathrm{Sym}}}{}^{\leqslant p}_{\mathbbm{R}}(TM[1])
act as zero.
If
\mathrm{d}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{l}}
denotes the differential on
\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{l}
, we thus have to ensure that

\mathrm{d}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{l}}\bigg(\bigoplus_{i=0}^{l}\big(X_{1},A^{(1)}_{i}(X_{1})\big)\ \oplus\ \bigoplus_{r\geqslant 1}\ \bigoplus_{\varphi\colon[r]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[l]}\,A^{(1)}_{\varphi}(X_{1})\bigg)=0\ .

(E.11)

Using the explicit expression (
D.12
) for the differential, we evaluate the left-hand side as

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{l}}\bigg(\bigoplus_{i=0}^{l}\big(X_{1},A^{(1)}_{i}(X_{1})\big)\ \oplus\ \bigoplus_{r\geqslant 1}\ \bigoplus_{\varphi\colon[r]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[l]}A^{(1)}_{\varphi}(X_{1})\bigg)

(E.12)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt=-\sum_{i=0}^{l}\ \sum_{s=0}^{1}\,(-1)^{s}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\psi\colon[1]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[l]\\
\psi\circ\partial_{s}=i\end{subarray}}\,\big(A^{(1)}_{i}(X_{1}),\psi\big)+\sum_{r\geqslant 1}\ \sum_{\varphi\colon[r]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[l]}\,\big((-1)^{r+1}\,\check{\delta}A^{(1)}_{\varphi}(X_{1}),\varphi\big)

(E.13)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\qquad-\sum_{r\geqslant 1}\ \sum_{\varphi\colon[r]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[l]}\ \sum_{s=0}^{r+1}\,(-1)^{s}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\psi\colon[r+1]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[l]\\
\psi\circ\partial_{s}=\varphi\end{subarray}}\,\big(A^{(1)}_{\varphi}(X_{1}),\psi\big)

(E.14)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt=\sum_{r\geqslant 1}\ \sum_{\psi\colon[r]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[l]}\,\big((-1)^{r+1}\,\check{\delta}A^{(1)}_{\psi}(X_{1}),\psi\big)-\sum_{r\geqslant 0}\ \sum_{\varphi\colon[r]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[l]}\ \sum_{s=0}^{r+1}\,(-1)^{s}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\psi\colon[r+1]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[l]\\
\psi\circ\partial_{s}=\varphi\end{subarray}}\big(A^{(1)}_{\varphi}(X_{1}),\psi\big)

(E.15)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\overset{(\ast)}{=}\sum_{r\geqslant 1}\ \sum_{\psi\colon[r]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[l]}\,\big((-1)^{r+1}\,\check{\delta}A^{(1)}_{\psi}(X_{1}),\psi\big)-\sum_{r\geqslant 1}\ \sum_{\psi\colon[r]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[l]}\ \sum_{s=0}^{r+1}\,(-1)^{s}\ \big(A^{(1)}_{\psi\circ\partial_{s}}(X_{1}),\psi\big)

(E.16)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt=\sum_{r\geqslant 1}\ \sum_{\psi\colon[r]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[l]}\,\bigg((-1)^{r+1}\,\check{\delta}A^{(1)}_{\psi}(X_{1})-\sum_{s=0}^{r+1}\,(-1)^{s}\,A^{(1)}_{\psi\circ\partial_{s}}(X_{1})\,,\,\psi\bigg)\ .

(E.17)

In the step labelled
(\ast)
we have reorganised the second term (with the quadruple sum) as follows:
fix
r\in\{0,\ldots,l\}
.
Prior to step
(\ast)
there is a triple sum over all injective maps
\varphi\colon[r]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[l]
, all
s\in[r{+}1]
and all those injective maps
\psi\colon[r{+}1]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[l]
such that
\psi
extends the map
\varphi
along
\partial_{s}\colon[r]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[r{+}1]
.
We assemble all terms in this triple sum where a fixed map
\psi\colon[r{+}1]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[l]
appears.
These are precisely those terms in the original sum where
\varphi=\psi\circ\partial_{s}
.
In this case, the map
\varphi
is completely determined by
\psi
and
s
.
Finally, observe that each injective morphism
\psi\colon[r{+}1]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[l]
appears in the original sum (given
\psi
, consider any term indexed by
\varphi=\psi\circ\partial_{s}
, for any
s\in\{0,\ldots,r+1\}
).
In other words, the indexing sets of the original and new sum are each canonically isomorphic to the set of all commutative diagrams of injective maps

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[r{+}1]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[r]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[l]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial_{s}}

(E.18)

At the same time, in the new sum we have shifted the index
r
by one, i.e. we perform the sum over
(r\geqslant 1,\psi\colon[r]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[p])
instead of
(r\geqslant 0,\psi\colon[r{+}1]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[p])
.

We thus read off that the condition (
E.11
) is equivalent to the equations

(-1)^{r+1}\,\check{\delta}A^{(1)}_{\psi}(X_{1})-\sum_{s=0}^{r+1}\,(-1)^{s}\,A^{(1)}_{\psi\circ\partial_{s}}(X_{1})=0\ ,\qquad\forall\,\psi\colon[r]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[l]\,,\,r\geqslant 1\ .

(E.19)

\text{\Large$\wedge$}_{\mathbbm{R}}^{m}TM\ ,\ 2<m<p
.
Let
X_{1},\ldots,X_{m}\in TM
be vector fields on
M
.
We compute

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{l}}\bigg(\bigoplus_{r\in\mathbbm{N}_{0}}\ \bigoplus_{\varphi\colon[r]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[l]}\,A^{(m)}_{\varphi}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{m})\bigg)

(E.20)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt=\sum_{r\in\mathbbm{N}_{0}}\ \sum_{\psi\colon[r]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[l]}\,\big((-1)^{m+r}\,\check{\delta}A^{(m)}_{\psi}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{m}),\psi\big)

(E.21)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\qquad-\sum_{r\in\mathbbm{N}_{0}}\ \sum_{\varphi\colon[r]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[l]}\ \sum_{s=0}^{r+1}\,(-1)^{s}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\psi\colon[r+1]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[l]\\
\psi\circ\partial_{s}=\varphi\end{subarray}}\,\big(A^{(m)}_{\varphi}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{m}),\psi\big)

(E.22)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt=\sum_{\psi\colon[0]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[l]}\,\big((-1)^{m}\,\check{\delta}A^{(m)}_{\psi}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{m}),\psi\big)

(E.23)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\qquad+\sum_{r\geqslant 1}\ \sum_{\psi\colon[r]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[l]}\,\bigg((-1)^{m+r}\,\check{\delta}A^{(m)}_{\psi}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{m})-\sum_{s=0}^{r+1}\,(-1)^{s}\,A^{(m)}_{\psi\circ\partial_{s}}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{m}),\,\psi\bigg)\ ,

(E.24)

where we have used the same reorganisation of the quadruple sum as in the computation for
\text{\Large$\wedge$}_{\mathbbm{R}}^{1}TM
above.

Next we compute

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{r\in\mathbbm{N}_{0}}\ \bigoplus_{\varphi\colon[r]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[l]}\,A^{(m-1)}_{\varphi}\circ\delta_{\mathrm{CE}}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{m})

(E.25)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt=\sum_{r\in\mathbbm{N}_{0}}\ \sum_{\varphi\colon[r]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[l]}\ \sum_{1\leqslant i<j\leqslant m}\,(-1)^{i+j-1}\,\Big(A^{(m-1)}_{\varphi}\big([X_{i},X_{j}]_{TM},X_{1},\ldots,\widehat{X_{i}},\ldots,\widehat{X_{j}},\ldots,X_{m}\big),\,\varphi\Big)\ .

(E.26)

Finally, we compute the bracket term in the Maurer–Cartan equation

\displaystyle\big[A^{(m)},A^{(m)}\big]_{{\operatorname{Hom}},2}(X_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge X_{m})

(E.27)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt=[-]_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{l}}\circ(A^{(m)}\otimes A^{(m)})\bigg(\sum_{\sigma\in\mathrm{Sh}(1,m-1)}\,\epsilon(\sigma)\,X_{\sigma(1)}\otimes(X_{\sigma(2)}\wedge\cdots\wedge X_{\sigma(m)})

(E.28)

\displaystyle\hskip 170.71652pt+\sum_{\sigma\in\mathrm{Sh}(m-1,1)}\,\epsilon(\sigma)\,(X_{\sigma(1)}\wedge\cdots\wedge X_{\sigma(m-1)})\otimes X_{\sigma(m)}\bigg)

(E.29)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt=\sum_{\sigma\in\mathrm{Sh}(1,m-1)}\,\epsilon(\sigma)\,\Big[\big(X_{\sigma(1)},\,A^{(1)}(X_{\sigma(1)})\big)\,,\,A^{(m-1)}(X_{\sigma(2)}\wedge\cdots\wedge X_{\sigma(m)})\Big]_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{l}}

(E.30)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\qquad+\sum_{\sigma\in\mathrm{Sh}(m-1,1)}\ \epsilon(\sigma)\,\Big[A^{(m-1)}(X_{\sigma(1)}\wedge\cdots\wedge X_{\sigma(m-1)})\,,\,\big(X_{\sigma(m)},\,A^{(1)}(X_{\sigma(m)})\big)\Big]_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{l}}

(E.31)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt=2\,\sum_{\sigma\in\mathrm{Sh}(1,m-1)}\,\epsilon(\sigma)\,\Big[\big(X_{\sigma(1)}\,,\,A^{(1)}(X_{\sigma(1)})\big)\,,\,A^{(m-1)}(X_{\sigma(2)}\wedge\cdots\wedge X_{\sigma(m)})\Big]_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{l}}

(E.32)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt=2\,\sum_{\sigma\in\mathrm{Sh}(1,m-1)}\ \epsilon(\sigma)

(E.33)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\qquad\times\sum_{i=0}^{l}\ \sum_{r\in\mathbbm{N}_{0}}\ \sum_{\varphi\colon[r]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[l]}\,\Big[\big(X_{\sigma(1)},\,(A^{(1)}_{i}(X_{\sigma(1)}),\,i)\big)\,,\,\big(A^{(m-1)}_{\varphi}(X_{\sigma(2)}\wedge\cdots\wedge X_{\sigma(m)}),\,\varphi\big)\Big]_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{l}}

(E.34)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt=2\,\sum_{\sigma\in\mathrm{Sh}(1,m-1)}\,\epsilon(\sigma)\ \sum_{r\in\mathbbm{N}_{0}}\ \sum_{\varphi\colon[r]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[l]}\,\Big(\pounds_{X_{\sigma(1)}}\big(A^{(m-1)}_{\varphi}(X_{\sigma(2)}\wedge\cdots\wedge X_{\sigma(m)})\big)\,,\,\varphi\Big)

(E.35)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt=2\,\sum_{r\in\mathbbm{N}_{0}}\ \sum_{\varphi\colon[r]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[l]}\ \sum_{i=1}^{m}\,(-1)^{i-1}\,\Big(\pounds_{X_{i}}\big(A^{(m-1)}_{\varphi}(X_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge\widehat{X_{i}}\wedge\cdots\wedge X_{m})\big)\,,\,\varphi\Big)\ .

(E.36)

Altogether we obtain

\displaystyle\big(\mathrm{d}_{\operatorname{Hom}}A^{(m)}+\tfrac{1}{2}\,\big[A^{(m)},A^{(m)}\big]_{{\operatorname{Hom}},2}\big)(X_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge X_{m})

(E.37)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt=\sum_{i=0}^{l}\,\bigg((-1)^{m}\,\check{\delta}A^{(m)}_{i}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{m})

(E.38)

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt-\sum_{1\leqslant s<t\leqslant m}\,(-1)^{s+t-1}\,A^{(m-1)}_{i}\big([X_{s},X_{t}]_{TM},X_{1},\ldots,\widehat{X_{s}},\ldots,\widehat{X_{t}},\ldots,X_{m}\big)

(E.39)

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt+\sum_{t=1}^{m}\,(-1)^{t+1}\,\pounds_{X_{t}}\big(A^{(m-1)}_{i}(X_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge\widehat{X_{t}}\wedge\cdots\wedge X_{m})\big),\,i\bigg)

(E.40)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\quad\,+\sum_{r\geqslant 1}\ \sum_{\psi\colon[r]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[l]}\,\bigg((-1)^{m+r}\,\check{\delta}A^{(m)}_{\psi}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{m})-\sum_{s=0}^{r+1}\,(-1)^{s}\,A^{(m)}_{\psi\circ\partial_{s}}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{m})

(E.41)

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\qquad-\sum_{1\leqslant i<j\leqslant m}\,(-1)^{i+j-1}\,A^{(m-1)}_{\psi}\big([X_{i},X_{j}]_{TM},X_{1},\ldots,\widehat{X_{i}},\ldots,\widehat{X_{j}},\ldots,X_{m}\big)

(E.42)

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\qquad+\sum_{i=1}^{m}\,(-1)^{i+1}\,\pounds_{X_{i}}\big(A^{(m-1)}_{\psi}(X_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge\widehat{X_{i}}\wedge\cdots\wedge X_{m})\big),\,\psi\bigg)

(E.43)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt=\sum_{i=0}^{l}\,\bigg(\big((-1)^{m}\,\check{\delta}A^{(m)}_{i}+\mathrm{d}A^{(m-1)}_{i}\big)(X_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge X_{m}),\,i\bigg)

(E.44)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\quad+\sum_{r\geqslant 1}\ \sum_{\psi\colon[r]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[l]}\,\bigg(\Big((-1)^{m+r}\,\check{\delta}A^{(m)}_{\psi}+\mathrm{d}A^{(m-1)}_{\psi}-\sum_{s=0}^{r+1}\,(-1)^{s}\,A^{(m)}_{\psi\circ\partial_{s}}\Big)(X_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge X_{m}),\,\psi\bigg)\ .

(E.45)

\text{\Large$\wedge$}_{\mathbbm{R}}^{2}TM
.
This case is analogous to the previous computation upon replacing the bracket (
D.34e
) with the bracket (
D.34c
).

In summary, we obtain

\mathrm{d}_{\operatorname{Hom}}A^{(m)}+\tfrac{1}{2}\,\big[A^{(m)},A^{(m)}\big]_{{\operatorname{Hom}},2}=0\ ,

(E.46)

that is,
A^{(m)}
is an order
p
morphism
TM\longrightarrow\widehat{g}_{l}
of
L_{\infty}
-algebroids on
M
if and only if

(-1)^{m+r}\,\check{\delta}A^{(m)}_{\psi}+\mathrm{d}A^{(m-1)}_{\psi}=\begin{cases}\ 0\ ,&\psi\colon[0]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[l]\ ,\\[4.0pt]
\ \displaystyle\sum\limits_{s=0}^{r+1}\,(-1)^{s}\,A^{(m)}_{\psi\circ\partial_{s}}\ ,&\psi\colon[r]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[l]\ ,\ r\geqslant 1\ .\end{cases}

(E.47)

In other words, there is a canonical bijection between
l
-simplices of
{\mathrm{Con}}_{p}(g)
, defined via Čech–Deligne cocycles, and
l
-simplices of
{\mathrm{Con}}_{p}({\check{C}}{\mathrm{At}}(g))
, defined via our derived geometric formalism.
Furthermore, these isomorphisms
{\mathrm{Con}}_{p}({\check{C}}{\mathrm{At}}(g))_{l}\longrightarrow{\mathrm{Con}}_{p}(g)_{l}
are compatible with the simplicial structure maps, as is evident from (
D.13
), (
5.46a
) and (
5.46b
).
We thus obtain an isomorphism in
{\mathscr{S}\mathrm{et}_{\hskip-0.85355pt\Delta}}
as claimed.

Finally, if
p\geqslant n
we obtain an additional non-trivial condition at
\text{\Large$\wedge$}_{\mathbbm{R}}^{n+1}TM
.
However, we observe that
A^{(n)}_{\varphi}=0
whenever
\varphi\colon[r]\,\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\,[l]
with
r>0
and
A^{(m)}=0
whenever
m>n
(since the target complex is truncated).
In this case, the second and third computations from the case
\text{\Large$\wedge$}_{\mathbbm{R}}^{m}TM
above yield the additional condition

\mathrm{d}A^{(n)}_{i}=0\ ,\quad\forall\,i\in[l]\ .

(E.48)

That is, in that situation the vertices of the
l
-simplex
\pazocal{A}_{\varphi}
are
flat

n
-form connections.
∎
